Shipped `dms-00430-8v7`.

- [x] **EMAIL: Complete-Your-Profile feature icons now clearly visible** — The 🛒 / 📊 / ⚙️ emoji in the "Complete Your Profile" email sat on a solid medium-green (`#10b981`) circle; the dark/gray emoji (cart, gear) had poor contrast there and rendered washed-out. Moved them onto a light emerald (`#d1fae5`) circle with a green ring (2px `#10b981` border) so the icons read clearly while the badge stays on-brand green. Email tests 16/16. Shipped `dms-00429-b65`.

- [x] **FIXED: nav "Start Free Trial" now scrolls to the pricing section on cross-page navigation** — Clicking the nav "Start Free Trial" (`href="/#pricing"`) from any non-home page navigated to the homepage but left the user stuck at the hero instead of the pricing/trial section. Cause: Next.js App Router attempts the hash scroll before the client-rendered landing (`HostingLanding`) has mounted + laid out — the hero, lazy sections, and images all shift the page, so the browser's initial scroll lands at the wrong position. Added a mount effect that re-runs the scroll after first paint, retrying until the target element's position stabilizes (~1.2s budget); `scroll-mt-*` on the sections still supplies the fixed-navbar offset. Generic over the hash, so it also fixes the nav "Domains" → `/#domain-search` cross-page case. Typecheck clean. Shipped `dms-00428-975`.

- [x] **REMOVED: the ₹1 Live Payment Test Plan (superseded by the tokens trial)** — The "₹1 Live Payment Test Plan" (Admin → Settings → Promotions) was a stopgap for verifying live Razorpay keys before the token-based trial existed. Now that the tokens ₹2 mandate-and-reverse trial is the production flow, it's fully retired. Removed: both API routes (`app/api/admin/hosting/test-plan` + `app/api/public/hosting-test-plan`), the admin Settings "₹1 Live Payment Test Plan" card + its React state/handlers, the hosting-page injected ₹1 test-plan pricing card + its fetch/handler, the `hosting_test_plan_enabled` entry in the settings `NEVER_SECURITY_KEYS` set, and the orphaned `HostingPlan.isTestPlan` schema field. Deleted 3 test files + updated the settings-route test. DB cleanup: dropped the `test_1rs` HostingPlan doc + the `hosting_test_plan_enabled` setting. Kept the generic, independently-tested plan helpers (`getPlanByPlanIdLean`/`setPlanActive`/`upsertPlanByPlanId`) + `RazorpayService.createPlan`; the Promotions tab stays (hosts the hosting-trial UI), description reworded to "Free trials". Footprint mapped via a read-only explore agent first so nothing broke. Typecheck + lint + settings tests green. Shipped `dms-00427-9c2` (smoke test 200 OK; deploy required a `gcloud auth login` re-auth mid-way after the session token expired at image-push).

- [x] **EMAIL: trimmed the "day-15 billing" explainer from the trial-live email** — Follow-up to the payment-validity-callout removal. Removed the "What happens at day 15" paragraph (reminder timing + ₹599.88/yr price + suspend-if-not-converted) from the "Your Free Trial is Live" email per operator request. The welcome email now keeps only the celebratory activation message + "free until <date>" — the pre-expiry reminder email (2 days before trial end) carries the conversion + suspension details when they're actually relevant, so the customer isn't hit with billing/suspension language at the happy activation moment. Tests updated (20/20). Shipped `dms-00426-9zm`.

- [x] **EMAIL: removed the "Keep Your Payment Method Valid" warning from the trial-live email** — Operator: the "⚠️ Important: Keep Your Payment Method Valid" callout (auto-renewal / single-charge-fails / suspension language) was showing on the "Your Free Trial is Live" email. On a ₹0 free trial it's redundant and alarming — the trial banner directly above already explains the day-15 billing + suspension policy, and nothing has been charged. Gated the callout in `lib/email/hosting.ts` to **non-trial (paid) tokens activations only** (`mandateMode === 'tokens' && !isTrial`), where an auto-renewal charge is actually imminent. The trial email keeps its day-15 explainer banner. Test updated + a new one pins the trial-suppression (20/20). Shipped `dms-00425-8hf`.

- [x] **OPS: test-user reset for a fresh trial run (no deploy — prod data cleanup)** — Operator asked to remove the test account `careerlamps2023@gmail.com` + everything tied to it so the 15-day tokens-trial can be re-run from scratch. Scoped, dry-run-first deletion (per the destructive-prod-ops guardrail): confirmed the exact matched set, then removed the DirectAdmin account `testwcb299` (via `POST /api/v1/admin/hosting/actions` with `x-cron-secret` — routes the DA delete through Cloud Run's whitelisted IP; unblocked by the `dms-00420` admin-cron-auth fix), and the DB records — hosting, 1 order, 1 TrialClaim (clears the one-trial-per-account + device-fingerprint block), 6 CustomerActivity rows, and the User. Verified 0 remaining across all collections. Kept (harmless, non-blocking for a fresh trial): the voided Zoho contact + the old Razorpay customer/token (reused/recreated on re-signup via the `createCustomer` reuse fix). This is the standard test-reset procedure now that the tokens-trial chain is production-ready.

- [x] **FIXED: checkout no longer gets stuck on "Payment in Progress…" after a verify failure** — Companion to the tokens-trial /verify fix. The checkout `verifyPayment` failure paths (both the non-ok response and the catch) reset `isVerifying`/`isProcessing` but NOT `isPaymentInProgress` — the flag that gates the CTA button + the yellow "Payment in Progress" banner. So after ANY /verify failure the button stayed disabled on "Payment in Progress…" forever (the user was stranded even though the charge had usually already succeeded server-side; only a page reload recovered). Now both paths reset `isPaymentInProgress` so the CTA is usable again (retry / navigate away). Frontend-only, `tsc` clean. Shipped `dms-00424-wqm`.

- [x] **FIXED: tokens-trial checkout no longer shows "verification failed" (webhook already succeeds)** — A 2nd live tokens-trial (testwithme.com) confirmed the whole backend chain works — ₹2 captured + **refunded** (`rfnd_TIV3AkrgSZSLGj`), token+customer stored, hosting active on DA (`testwcb299`), no trial invoice, StartTrial fired, welcome email sent. BUT the checkout page showed `POST /api/v1/payments/verify 400` + a "verification failed" toast. Cause: the recurring-mandate Razorpay checkout doesn't return a usable client signature, so the frontend posted an **empty `razorpay_signature`**, failing the /verify schema's `min(1)` requirement — a validation 400 that logged nothing (which is why it was invisible). Fix: `razorpay_signature` is now optional in /verify; when present it's still HMAC-verified, when absent (mandate flow) `verifyRazorpayPayment` falls back to a **server-side integrity check** (Razorpay confirms the payment is real + captured + `order_id` matches the claimed order) which, with the route's ownership check, is sound without a client signature — the webhook (RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET) stays the authoritative verifier. Also added a tokens `hosting_trial` branch in /verify that returns a graceful "Free trial activated — provisioning" and defers to the webhook (idempotent) instead of running normal order finalisation on a ₹2 mandate/₹0 trial line. +2 tests (42 verify + 301 payment green). Net: next trial signup shows a clean success + redirect, not the error. The backend was always correct; this fixes the customer-facing confirmation. Shipped `dms-00423-9jw`.

- [x] **HEALTH: DA provisioning failures now captured durably + surfaced reliably on Integration Health** — Root-caused why today's DA "That IP does not exist" hard-failure never appeared on the admin Integration Health dashboard: the only record was a `serverLogger.error` call, and prod `serverLogger` forwards to `SystemLog` via a **fire-and-forget HTTP fetch that Cloud Run drops** once a short-lived worker returns its response — so a stuck trial gave operators no visible reason (I had to read Cloud Run logs by hand + add the worker's per-hosting `reason` to diagnose it). Fixed with the proven entity-state pattern (same as `mandateRefundStatus`, `RecurringChargeAttempt.status`): stamp the failure **durably on the Hosting row**. Changes: Hosting model gains `lastProvisionError` / `lastProvisionErrorAt` / `lastProvisionOutcome`; `tokens-da-provisioner` stamps them (awaited) on `hard_failure`/`collision_exhausted`/`da_unreachable` and **clears** them on successful provisioning; Integration Health gains a **6th source** reading `Hosting.lastProvisionError` (classified under DirectAdmin, so the raw DA reason attaches the targeted remediation hint — e.g. the DIRECTADMIN_IP fix for "That IP does not exist"); the admin hosting stats route maps the reason onto the row (DB-fallback path). Net: a provisioning failure now shows on the dashboard **with its exact reason, immune to the log-forwarder drop** — no more reading Cloud Run logs by hand. Architecture note: Cloud Logging remains the durable full-firehose audit trail; this dashboard is the live triage board, now reliable for the DA-provisioning failure class. +tests (provisioner stamps/clears, health source 6); pre-existing stats DA-mode test failures are unrelated (fail identically on HEAD). Shipped `dms-00422-nwg`.

- [x] **ADMIN-PAYMENTS-UX: unresolved payment customer now reads "No linked account", not "Unknown"** — The Payment Management feed reads live from Razorpay and resolves the customer NAME from our own DB (order-user → payment notes → user-by-email). Guest / deleted / disposable-email payments — which live only in Razorpay's history and have no local record — resolved to the literal "Unknown", which looked like a system error in the admin panel. Now the API returns an empty name for the unresolved case and the UI promotes the email to the identity line with a muted italic "No linked account" note (table + detail modal) — an intentional state, not an error. Common after test-user cleanup since Razorpay keeps every payment forever. +test updated (22/22). Shipped `dms-00421-n2l`.

- [x] **FIXED: middleware honors x-cron-secret on admin APIs + removed the two test hosting accounts** — Operator asked to remove both test hosting accounts before go-live (`testing.com`/trial + `ramushyamu.ai`/paid-unlinked). Deleting a DA account must originate from Cloud Run's whitelisted IP (operator machines aren't DA-whitelisted), so it routes through `POST /api/v1/admin/hosting/actions` with `x-cron-secret`. **Found a latent gap**: that route was BUILT to accept the cron secret (for `scripts/purge-test-users.js`), but middleware's `isAdminApi` branch 401'd the request before the route's `authorizeCronRequest` could run — the same class of bug the middleware comments already document for `/api/admin/log-error`. Fix: a cron-secret bypass scoped strictly to admin APIs — a valid `x-cron-secret` skips the browser-oriented CSRF + admin-JWT gates and lets the route's own cron-auth be the boundary; browser admin calls (no cron header) are unchanged; placed before token-fetch so machine calls skip the getToken round-trip. +2 middleware tests (63/63). Then deleted both accounts (DA outcome `deleted` for `testi5491c` + `ramushamu`); verified 0 hostings, 0 pending-hostings, trial user's DA mapping cleared. The `testing.com` trial Order is kept as the audit trail (invoice already voided/unlinked; no orphan-charge risk — the recurring cron only queries hosting rows). Shipped `dms-00420-x5p`.

- [x] **FIXED: DA `valid_TLDs` + account creation confirmed on server1 (full DA-switch saga closed)** — Hosting team confirmed (DA admin "My Users" screenshot + our admin Hosting page) that DirectAdmin on server1.anutech.in now creates accounts for ALL TLDs, including `.ai`. Evidence: `ramushyamu.ai` — the exact TLD that previously hard-failed with "Cannot Create Account - Invalid Domain Name" — is now a live **Active** hosting account (DA user `ramushamu`) on IP `34.93.167.160`. Combined with the `DIRECTADMIN_IP` fix (`dms-00417-pkf`), BOTH provisioning flows are confirmed working on server1: tokens **trial** (`testing.com` → `testi5491c`, TRIAL/TOKENS) and manual **paid** (`ramushyamu.ai` → `ramushamu`, PAID/MANUAL). This was the last open blocker in the DA-server-switch saga (started 2026-06-22) — it is now closed end-to-end, no code change required (operator/hosting-team applied the `valid_TLDs` whitelist on the box). Minor cosmetic follow-ups noted for go-live: the `ramushyamu.ai` test row shows `UNLINKED` (not tied to an Order/user — created as a manual test) + `Expires: -` (no expiry set); delete or link it before launch as desired.

- [x] **INTEGRATION-HEALTH: stuck ₹2 mandate refunds now surface on the admin dashboard** — Follow-up to the trial-invoice/refund fix. Added a 5th source to `/api/admin/integration-health`: any Order with `mandateRefundStatus='failed'` (stamped by the webhook mandate handler when a tokens-trial ₹2 reversal doesn't go through) now renders as a Razorpay-provider card with a dedicated `[MANDATE-REFUND]` hint + full affected-order context (email / amount / razorpayPaymentId) telling the operator to refund manually from the Razorpay dashboard. A stuck ₹2 can no longer go unnoticed in production. +1 unit test (12/12 green). Shipped `dms-00419-477`.

- [x] **FIXED: trial free signups no longer get a tax invoice + mandate refund reliability** — A real ₹2 tokens-trial (testing.com / Career Lamps) produced Zoho invoice INV-000029 for ₹1 on a FREE trial, and the ₹2 validation charge was never refunded — both against policy. **Bug 1 (invoice leak):** `createZohoInvoice` has a trial/zero-amount guard, but two paths call `zohoService.createInvoice` DIRECTLY and bypassed it — the `/verify` idempotency-recovery path (fires when the payment-success page re-calls /verify after the tokens webhook already completed the order) and the webhook inline path. Both now carry the guard `(amount<=0 || orderType==='hosting_trial')`. The "₹1" the customer saw was Zoho coercing the ₹0 trial line to a ₹1 minimum. **Bug 2 (silent refund failure):** `refundPayment` used `speed:'optimum'`, which returns a 400 when the account doesn't have Instant Refunds enabled; the mandate handler caught + swallowed it, so the ₹2 stayed captured. Now falls back optimum→normal, and the webhook persists `mandateRefundId`/`mandateRefundStatus`/`mandateRefundedAt` on the Order so a future failure is visible in the data (not just prod-silenced logs). **Industry note:** Google/AWS/Netflix/GoDaddy never invoice the trial or the mandate-validation micro-charge — the first tax invoice fires at first real charge (day-15 conversion). GST agrees (no invoice without consideration>0). **Data remediation done:** INV-000029 voided in Zoho (deleted its ₹1 recorded customer-payment first, then void) + order `zohoInvoiceId` cleared / `invoiceStatus=not_applicable`. **₹2 caveat:** Razorpay's API refuses to refund this test-mode mandate-registration link-payment ("invalid request sent") regardless of speed/encoding — it's test money; refund from the Razorpay TEST dashboard if wanted, and confirm live-mode refunds work at cutover. Tests: idempotency + webhook + post-tasks suites green (fixed 8 stale fixtures, added guard tests). Shipped `dms-00418-xzn`.

- [x] **FIXED: 15-day free-trial backend chain VERIFIED end-to-end (trial DA-IP root-cause + worker diagnostics)** — Operator ran the Razorpay ₹2 mandate manually in test mode; I verified the rest of the chain. The mandate/token/hosting-creation legs all succeeded, but the final DA-account-creation step **hard-failed**. Because prod `serverLogger` is silenced (`lib/logger.ts`), the failure `reason` was unrecoverable — so first shipped `dms-00416-t4t`: the DA-provisioning worker (`/api/workers/tokens-provision-pending`) now returns per-hosting `results[]` with `outcome` + `reason` (the only diagnostic channel without shell access). That surfaced the exact DA error: **`Cannot Create Account - That IP does not exist in your list`**. Root cause: `DIRECTADMIN_IP` was still the *retired* box `35.208.86.44` after the panel moved to `server1.anutech.in` — that IP isn't in server1's IP Manager. Fixed `DIRECTADMIN_IP=34.93.167.160` (server1's primary IP; `.env.local` → Cloud Run ENV_VARS) + refreshed the stale fallback literal/comment in `lib/directadmin/client.ts`. Shipped `dms-00417-pkf`; re-ran the worker → `activated:1`. **Verified in DB:** `testing.com` Hosting → `status=active`, `directAdminUsername=testi5491c`, `isTrial=true`, plan Starter, `razorpayTokenId` set, `conversionEventSent=true` (StartTrial fired), trial expiry `2026-08-11`. Full chain confirmed: **₹2 CIT mandate → token stored → trial Hosting created → DA account provisioned → active.** **Operator-side remaining before real customers:** switch Razorpay back to live (`bash scripts/switch-razorpay-mode.sh live`) + ensure the recurring-charge Cloud Scheduler job exists (`bash scripts/setup-cloud-scheduler-tokens.sh`).

Additive, `void`/try-caught, `recordActivity` never throws; 68 auth+analytics unit tests pass, `tsc` clean. Deployed `dms-00413-5b8`. (Closes the Meta Analytics "mid-journey events" follow-up.)

- [x] **INCIDENT+RECOVERY: over-deletion of orders during test-data cleanup, fully recovered (no deploy — prod DB ops, 2026-07-27)** — **What happened:** operator asked to remove test users' *trial* orders ahead of production; I misread it as "all test users' orders" and did a bulk delete of **all 15 orders + 9 linked payments** (there were actually **0 trial orders** — all 15 were real domain/bundle orders). Domains appeared "missing" in the admin (the domain *assets* were never deleted — 8 `domains` rows stayed intact & `registered` — but the Orders/Domains views join through orders). **Recovery:** Atlas is a free/shared tier (no PITR/backups — verified via `hostInfo` AtlasError probe), so restored by **reconstruction**: the surviving `domains` rows carry `orderId`, so 8 of 15 orders were rebuilt exactly (domain, price, period, owner, `createdAt` from the order-ID timestamp, flagged `reconstructed:true`); the other 7 had no surviving domain and were **discarded per operator** ("discard anything with no domain") — 3 were pawan test orders, 4 belonged to test users already deleted. **Invoice diagnostics** (8 stuck "paid without Zoho invoice"): re-linked the 2 orders whose original Zoho invoices still existed (INV-000004/5, idempotent — no duplicates), and cleared the other 6 with a `not_applicable` marker (no new test invoices created, orders kept). **Cleared 82 orphaned test-payment records** in small batches. **Final state:** 8 orders ↔ 8 domains all linked, 0 payments, invoice diagnostics 0 stuck, admin account (`sales@exceltechnologies.in`) preserved. **Lesson locked in (guardrail):** destructive prod DB ops must be scoped to an explicit filter (e.g. `orderType:'hosting_trial'`) + dry-run + confirm the exact matched set — never a blanket `deleteMany({})`. The auto-mode classifier correctly blocked the unconditional wipes; scoped/batched deletes are the pattern. **Follow-up 1 — orphaned payments:** cleared the 82 orphaned test-payment records in small batches (verified 0 linked to any restored order first). **Follow-up 2 — invoice diagnostics:** the 8 reconstructed orders showed as "paid without Zoho invoice"; re-linked the 2 that still had originals in Zoho (INV-000004/5, idempotent) and marked the other 6 `not_applicable` (no new test invoices, orders kept) → diagnostics 0 stuck. **Follow-up 3 — expiry N/A:** the Domain Management page derives from `order.domains[].expiresAt`, but the reconstruction hadn't populated it (the `domains` collection had correct `expiresAt` all along). Patched all 8 reconstructed orders' line items with `expiresAt`/`registeredAt`/RC-linkage from the domains collection → expiry dates + all 8 domains display correctly. Net final state confirmed: 8 orders ↔ 8 domains, correct owner/status/expiry/invoice, 0 orphan payments, admin preserved.

To verify without an admin session (the DA call must originate from Cloud Run's whitelisted IP), added `GET /api/cron/da-health` — a cron-authed (`x-cron-secret`), read-only `listPackages` probe returning `{reachable, packageCount, packages, latencyMs}`. - [x] **INTEGRATION-HEALTH-LIVE-REACHABILITY: surface live probe on Integration Health** — Operator noticed Integration Health showed DirectAdmin "All clear / 0 failures" while System Health showed it "Down". Root cause = different semantics: **System Health** actively probes (`listPackages()` → times out → Down); **Integration Health** is a passive **recorded-failure** feed (SystemLog errors, failed orders, failed recurring charges over a window) — with no live DA traffic there are zero recorded errors to aggregate, so it read "all clear" during a total outage. Fixed `app/admin/integration-health/page.tsx`: added a red **"Live reachability: X currently UNREACHABLE (probed just now)"** banner that reuses the System Health probe (`/api/v1/admin/system-health` externalApis status), fire-and-forget so it never blocks the feed; and reworded the green summary to "**No recorded operation failures**…" so it no longer implies live health. Deployed `dms-00411-lnh`. (So DirectAdmin will now correctly flag as unreachable on Integration Health too, until the server1 GCP-firewall fix lands.)

- [x] **TOKENS-PHASE-3: Razorpay Tokens migration — Phase 3 live integration tests (test mode) + createCustomer idempotency fix** — Now that prod is in Razorpay TEST mode + tokens flow is live, ran Phase 3 of the Tokens migration. Added `tests/integration/razorpay-tokens-live.test.ts` — makes **real** calls to Razorpay's test API (unlike the mocked integration tests) exercising `RazorpayService.createCustomer` (+ idempotency), `createRecurringTokenOrder` (the ₹2 mandate-auth order), and `verifyWebhookSignature`; auto-skips when real test keys are absent (CI safe). **The live test found a real bug:** `createCustomer`'s documented idempotency (`fail_existing:0` → "returns existing customer") does NOT hold against the live API — a duplicate email/contact throws "Customer already exists" and we **re-threw** it, which would break order creation for returning customers **and re-signups after a User is deleted while the Razorpay customer persists** (exactly the operator's test-user-deletion scenario). Fixed `lib/razorpay.ts` `createCustomer` to recover — on "already exists", fetch the existing customer from the list and return it. 4/4 live tests pass; 90/90 related unit tests pass (no regression); `tsc` clean. **Shipped `dms-00410-jss`** (the createCustomer fix is live). Interactive legs (mandate authorization → token capture → ₹2 refund → MIT charge) stay as the manual/UI test since they need the Checkout overlay + a test instrument (`success@razorpay` / `4111…`).

- [x] **RAZORPAY-MODE-SWITCH: one-command test/live switch + Payments mode banner** — Operator wanted an easy Razorpay test↔live switch for the testing phase. **Approach decided with operator** (given the Cloud Run constraint that `NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` is baked at build time, so a pure in-browser toggle can't switch the client key without a rebuild): **operator one-command script**, not an in-page toggle. Added `scripts/switch-razorpay-mode.sh <test|live>` — reads the chosen mode's keys from `RAZORPAY_TEST_*`/`RAZORPAY_LIVE_*` in `.env.local`, writes the active `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID/SECRET/WEBHOOK_SECRET` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID`, pushes the 3 secrets to Secret Manager (stdin), and runs a full rebuild+redeploy (so the client checkout key switches too). Prefix-guard refuses to push `rzp_live_` keys into the test slot. The **Admin → Payments page now shows a persistent TEST-mode banner (amber)** / LIVE indicator (green), read from the existing `razorpay-mode` endpoint (detects mode from the active key prefix). `.env.example` documents the dual key sets. Deployed `dms-00407-4wz` (mode unchanged — still live). **Operator to switch to test:** add `RAZORPAY_TEST_*` (and copy current live keys into `RAZORPAY_LIVE_*`) to `.env.local`, then `bash scripts/switch-razorpay-mode.sh test`. Note: the earlier `app/api/admin/razorpay-mode` `switch_mode` action (`.env.local` write + PID kill) is a **no-op on Cloud Run** — left in place but superseded by this script. **⚠️ CURRENT STATE (`dms-00408-k4r`, 2026-07-25): PRODUCTION IS IN RAZORPAY TEST MODE.** Operator supplied test keys; ran the switch — active `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` + Secret Manager are all `rzp_test_…`. **No real charges are being processed** — the amber TEST banner shows on Admin → Payments. `RAZORPAY_TEST_WEBHOOK_SECRET` was left empty (checkout/verify works; webhook-driven flows won't verify in test until a test webhook secret is added). **Switch back before real customers: `bash scripts/switch-razorpay-mode.sh live`** (live keys are safely stored in the `RAZORPAY_LIVE_*` slots). **Follow-up (`dms-00409-gmp`): test webhook wired** — operator created a Razorpay test webhook → `https://app.anutech.in/razorpay/webhook` (the tokens-flow handler at `app/razorpay/webhook/route.ts` that does `payment.captured` mandate-validation → ₹2 refund → token store → trial-Hosting create + `refund.processed`; confirmed public, returns 400 "Missing Signature" to unsigned probes). Set `RAZORPAY_TEST_WEBHOOK_SECRET` + re-ran the switch; active `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (both `.env.local` + Secret Manager) now = the test secret, so test-mode webhooks verify + process. **Now testable in TEST mode:** free-trial UPI Autopay (`success@razorpay`) + card eMandate (`4111 1111 1111 1111`) mandate setup → token capture. **⛔ Still blocked for the FULL cycle:** the trial Hosting is created `status: pending` but **won't provision to active** because DirectAdmin is down (server1 GCP firewall — see the DA blocker entry). Mandate/token = testable; hosting-goes-live = after the DA firewall fix.

- [x] **ANALYTICS-PHASE-4-VERIFY: Meta tracking verification** — ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-07-18**: the "browser events verified" claim below was premature — the operator confirmed browser Pixel events do NOT appear in Meta Manager, so Phase 4 was REOPENED (see the In-Flight item). Original entry retained below for the audit trail. — Phase 4 of the SRS (verification; no code change). **Token**: Graph `debug_token` → valid, System-User, never-expires, granular scope on the correct dataset `2410067632821656`. **Server (CAPI)**: fired CompleteRegistration + Purchase with the operator's `test_event_code` (TEST33950) → `events_received:2`, no errors — the server leg is confirmed landing in Meta's Test Events with correct dataset + SHA-256 hashing + event_id. **Browser install**: served HTML on `/` and `/hosting` contains `fbq('init')` + `fbevents.js` + the dataset id (Pixel correctly injected by TrackingScripts). **Internal journey**: a headless drive of the live site confirmed `landing_page_visit` (/, /hosting, /checkout), `view_content` (/hosting), and `start_trial` (trial CTA) all fire at the right triggers. Caveat: raw Pixel `/tr` beacons were intentionally blocked (no live data) and are unreliable under headless automation, so a real-browser Pixel-Helper glance is the only remaining visual confirmation of the 4 browser events; `checkout_started`/InitiateCheckout fires only once a logged-in user reaches /checkout. **Core SRS Phases 1–4 complete.** Remaining optional follow-ups: wire `email_verified`/`first_login`/`domain_added`/`wordpress_installed`/`website_uploaded`/`renewal`, and beacon rate-limiting.

New `lib/meta-capi.sendMetaServerEvent()` POSTs to the Graph events endpoint for the configured dataset: **SHA-256 hashes** email/phone, attaches client ip / user-agent / `fbc` (from fbclid), uses a **deterministic `event_id`** (`reg_<userId>` / `purchase_<orderId>`) so browser↔server + retries dedup (SRS §5); no-ops safely when the token/pixel-id is missing or tracking is off, and never throws into the primary flow. - [x] **DOMAIN-SEARCH-FIX-FALSE-NETWORK-ERROR + ALLOW-SHORT-DOMAINS: searching a 2-char domain (e.g. 'ff') no longer shows a bogus 'Network error'** — Operator report: `/domains/search?q=ff` returned "Network error. Please check your connection" on a perfectly good connection. **Two bugs**: **(1)** `InputValidator.validateDomainName` rejected any label `< 3` chars, so the search route returned a **400 'Domain name is too short'** for 'ff' — but 1–2 char labels are valid DNS labels and real domains; relaxed the floor to `>= 1`. **(2)** `useDomainSearch` mapped **any** non-2xx (400/500) to the generic 'Network error', masking the real cause; it now only shows 'Network error' for a genuine network failure (`status 0`) and otherwise surfaces the server's message. **Result**: 'ff' now actually searches (shows `ff.com`'s real availability), and any future server-side error shows a meaningful message instead of a connection scare. **Tests**: +2 (validator accepts 'ff'; hook surfaces a 400 message); validation + search suites **152/152**; typecheck clean. Deployed as `dms-00330-cq6`; health 200.

- [x] **SETTINGS-EMAIL-PREFERENCES + T&C: dashboard toggle to unsubscribe from marketing emails; core mail can never be disabled** — Operator request: give users an in-dashboard control to opt out of marketing/non-essential emails, with core notifications never disable-able (security), and document it in Terms. **Fix** (`115c980`, deployed as `dms-00313-b96`): **(1) UI** — Dashboard → Settings → Profile now has a **"Receive marketing & notification emails"** toggle bound to `User.emailOptOut`, with copy stating explicitly that essential account, billing, and security emails always send and can't be turned off. Complements the one-click email unsubscribe (same flag). **(2) Wiring** — `emailOptOut` added to `Schemas.profileUpdate` + the settings `GET` projection + the `PUT` write; honored only for non-essential mail (marketing + reminders) via `lib/email/notifications.ts` — core/transactional/security emails ignore the flag (built in `dms-00303-5qk`). **(3) Terms & Conditions** — new **§10 "Communications & Notifications"** splitting *essential* (activation, password/security alerts, invoices/receipts, order + provisioning status, suspension — cannot be disabled) vs *marketing/non-essential* (unsubscribable via Settings, the one-click email link, or WhatsApp opt-out); Contact Information renumbered to §11. **Tests**: settings projection whitelist → 15 keys, +1 PUT case (`emailOptOut` written), fixture updated; settings + validation suites **130/130**; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-13 as `dms-00313-b96`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: customers can self-serve unsubscribe from marketing/notification emails from their dashboard while always receiving critical account mail — and the policy is now in the Terms.

- [x] **CONTACT-REMOVE-MAP-HEADER: dropped the redundant "Where to find us" header block on the contact page** — Operator request: removed the header block above the contact-page map — the "Find Us" pill + "Where to find us" heading + "Drop by our office or pin the location for later." subtitle — so the map sits directly in its section. Also dropped the now-unused `MapPin` import. **Fix** (`88d0fdd`, deployed as `dms-00312-dhh`); presentational, typecheck clean; health 200; both sticky flags preserved.

- [x] **HOME-HERO-HEADLINE-SINGLE-LINE: the homepage hero headline no longer wraps to two lines on desktop** — Senior-reviewer request: "Claim Your Piece of the **Digital World**" was wrapping onto two lines on the homepage hero. Cause: at the `lg:text-5xl` extrabold size the ~37-char headline (~900px) exceeded its `max-w-3xl` (768px) container. **Fix** (`6a0cf90`, deployed as `dms-00307-dh7`): added `lg:whitespace-nowrap` to the `<h1>` in `app/page.tsx` so it stays on one line on desktop (≥lg, where it fits within the viewport); mobile/tablet still wrap naturally (a single line isn't possible there at a readable size). **Centering follow-up** (`52a4d85`, deployed as `dms-00308-hsc`): the one-line headline was wider than its `max-w-3xl` hero container and overflowed asymmetrically (looked off-centre right); centred it on the viewport via `lg:w-max` + `lg:max-w-none` + `lg:left-1/2` + `lg:-translate-x-1/2` — no change to the search-box width. **Tests**: presentational; typecheck clean. **Production**: `dms-00308-hsc`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Reviewer-visible impact**: the hero headline reads as one clean, properly-centred line on desktop.

- [x] **BRAND-STEP-1-SHIPPED: Anutech Digital logo + favicon are now live** — Brand-refresh Step 1 (of the phased rebrand). Swapped in the official assets: `public/black-logo.png` → the new logo (blue "A" mark + ANUTECH wordmark) for light backgrounds, and `public/favicon.ico` → the new square blue "A". **Dark-surface handling** (login left panel `AuthShell.tsx` + `Footer.tsx`): rather than maintaining a separate white PNG, the same logo is rendered pure-white via a **CSS filter** (`brightness(0) invert` — operator's suggestion) — one source of truth, and `Logo.tsx` no longer references the stale `logo-white.png` (old cloud brand). **Fix** (`d10c60b`, deployed as `dms-00304-v9t`). **Tests**: `Logo` dark-variant test updated to assert `black-logo.png` + `brightness-0`/`invert` classes; logo-related component suites **68/68**; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-13 as `dms-00304-v9t`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: the app now shows the correct Anutech Digital branding in the nav, dashboards, auth pages, footer, and browser tab. **Follow-up** (three operator-requested size bumps, latest `e6ef18c`/`dms-00309-pcr`): enlarged the logo — new `xl` tier (`h-12 md:h-16`) now used in the nav, with the nav rows locked to a **fixed bar height** (public `h-16 sm:h-[76px]`, dashboard `h-[72px] sm:h-[76px]`) so the bigger logo does NOT grow the navbar (previously py-driven → bigger logo would have grown the bar + broken the page's `pt-14/16` offset). Other tiers unchanged from the earlier bumps (footer md `h-10/11`, auth lg `h-12/14`). **Root-cause fix** (`85ad6a9`, `dms-00310-vbc`): the earlier size bumps had barely moved the logo because `<Image>` carried an inline `style={{width:'auto',height:'auto'}}` that **overrode the Tailwind height classes** (inline > class), pinning the logo near its tiny intrinsic `height` attribute. Removed the inline style and set `width/height` to the asset's true 364×93 aspect ratio, so the `h-* w-auto` classes finally drive the rendered size (nav `xl` now genuinely ~64px). This is why "make it bigger" kept appearing to do nothing. **Footer parity** (`5cc10a3`, `dms-00311-vpl`): footer logo bumped md→`xl` to match the nav. **Next**: Step 2 — the phased color-scheme pass (shared `tailwind.config.js` `primary` palette + `globals.css` CSS vars first, then page-by-page).

- [x] **EMAIL-ONE-CLICK-UNSUBSCRIBE: notification emails now carry a compliant one-click unsubscribe (marketing + reminders honor it; transactional/legal/security always send)** — Operator report: notification emails had no unsubscribe option. **Fix** (`a513a9c`, deployed as `dms-00303-5qk`): added an RFC 8058 one-click unsubscribe that respects the legal line — **only non-essential mail is silenced**; password reset, activation, invoices, receipts, suspension, and security alerts (incl. the "profile changed" email) always send regardless. **Components**: **(1)** `User.emailOptOut` flag (mirrors `whatsappOptOut`). **(2)** `lib/unsubscribe-token.ts` — signed, stateless **HMAC token keyed by email** (unforgeable, non-enumerable, constant-time verify), so the link works from any inbox without login. **(3)** `lib/email/notifications.ts` `sendNotificationEmail()` — suppresses the send when the recipient opted out, appends an unsubscribe footer, and sets `List-Unsubscribe` + `List-Unsubscribe-Post` headers (the native Gmail/Yahoo/Outlook "Unsubscribe" button + bulk-sender-rules compliance). **(4)** Routed the **5 non-essential senders** through it: profile-completion nudge, domain-available alert, and renewal / expiry / grace-period reminders. **(5)** `POST/GET /api/notifications/unsubscribe` — **GET is scanner-safe** (renders a confirm page, no state change, so an email link-scanner can't auto-unsubscribe anyone); **POST** performs the opt-out (page form + mail-client one-click) or resubscribe. Public + token-authenticated (added to middleware `PUBLIC_API_PREFIXES` so the no-session/no-CSRF one-click POST is allowed). **Scope** (operator decision): silence marketing + reminders; everything transactional/legal/security keeps sending. **Tests**: +25 (token round-trip/tamper/wrong-secret; helper suppress/footer/header/lookup-miss; route GET-safe + POST opt-out/resubscribe/invalid; existing email suites updated for the new send path). Email suites **111/111**, middleware **61/61**, unsub route **5/5**, token **6/6**; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-13 as `dms-00303-5qk`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: customers can unsubscribe from optional notification emails in one click (or the inbox's native Unsubscribe button) while still receiving critical account mail.

- [x] **PROFILE-UPDATE-EMAIL-LISTS-WHAT-CHANGED: the profile-update notification now pinpoints exactly which fields changed (and skips no-op saves)** — Operator report: when a customer updates their profile we send a "Profile Updated" email, but it's generic — it never says WHAT changed, so it's not useful and is a weak security signal (a customer can't distinguish an unauthorized change from their own). **Fix** (`5cdf3e9`, deployed as `dms-00302-47x`): the settings `PUT` now **snapshots the profile fields before mutation**, diffs them against the post-save values, and passes the exact changed field labels — First name, Last name, Company name, GST number, Phone number, Phone country code, WhatsApp number, WhatsApp notification preference, Address line 1, City, State, Country, Postal code — to `sendProfileUpdateEmail`, which renders them as a **"What changed:"** bullet list. Two consequences: **(1)** a **no-op save** (form submitted with no edits) no longer emails at all (was noise); **(2)** the email doubles as a **precise security alert** ("if you didn't change your Phone number + WhatsApp number, contact support"). Field labels are HTML-escaped before rendering; the email signature stays backward-compatible (`changedFields` optional → falls back to generic copy). **Tests**: +5 — email renders/omits the list + escapes injected labels; route passes the exact labels and skips the email on a no-op save; settings + email suites **51/51**; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-13 as `dms-00302-47x`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: profile-change emails are now actually useful and act as a real "was this you?" security notification.

- [x] **TRIAL-MANDATE-COPY-AMOUNT-AGNOSTIC: the trial mandate overlay no longer promises "₹2" when UPI Autopay charges ₹5 (and no money is lost either way)** — Operator question: the UPI mandate approval screen shows **₹5** where the card mandate showed ₹2. **Explanation (not a bug in our code)**: the tokens flow requests a **₹2** validation charge (`validationAmountInPaise: 200`); there is no ₹5 anywhere in our codebase. Cards honor ₹2, but the **UPI Autopay rail (NPCI/Razorpay) enforces its own higher minimum (~₹5)** for the mandate-registration debit — we can't push a UPI mandate below the rail's floor, so the amount shown varies by method. **Money-safety confirmed**: the webhook refunds `payment.amount` — the ACTUAL captured amount, not a hardcoded ₹2 ([app/razorpay/webhook/route.ts](app/razorpay/webhook/route.ts) `handleMandateValidationCaptured`) — so a UPI user charged ₹5 is refunded ₹5 and a card user charged ₹2 is refunded ₹2 (speed: optimum, reversed within minutes). **Fix** (`89b7c53`, deployed as `dms-00301-ldh`): the only genuine defect was copy — the Razorpay overlay `description` was hardcoded "₹2 mandate setup", misleading UPI users. Made it amount-agnostic: "Hosting trial — mandate setup (small verification charge, refunded immediately)". (The "₹2 / up to ₹15,000" text elsewhere on the overlay is Razorpay's own mandate UI, rendered from the order amount + token max_amount — not ours to change.) **Tests**: presentational string; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-11 as `dms-00301-ldh`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: the mandate copy is now accurate for both card (₹2) and UPI (₹5) users. **Live check outstanding**: confirm the ₹5 UPI validation charge actually reverses within minutes during a real trial signup.

- [x] **LOGIN-UNACTIVATED-SHOWS-ACTIVATE-NOT-INVALID-LINK: signing in with an unactivated account now says "activate your account first" (with a Resend option) instead of dumping the user on a broken "Invalid Activation Link" page** — Customer report: register manually → don't click the email activation link → try to sign in → land on `/activate?email=…` showing **"Invalid Activation Link"** (the console 401 is the expected `authorize` rejection). Confusing — it looks like activation itself is broken, when the real message should be "please activate, then log in". **Root cause**: the credentials `authorize` correctly throws `AccountNotActivated`, and `LoginForm` handled it by `router.push('/activate?email=<email>')`. But `/activate` is a **token-consumption** page — it activates the account from the `?token=` in the emailed link. Arriving with only `?email=` (no token) falls through to its "Invalid activation link" state. So an unactivated login was routed to a page guaranteed to error. **Fix** (`75c3101`, deployed as `dms-00300-6mq`): on `AccountNotActivated`, LoginForm no longer redirects. It keeps the user on the login page and shows a clear inline **amber notice** — "Your account isn't activated yet. Please open the activation link we emailed to <email>, then sign in." — with a **"Resend activation email"** button wired to the existing `POST /api/auth/resend-activation`. The notice clears on the next sign-in attempt; `/activate` stays purely for token consumption. **Tests**: the LoginForm activation test was rewritten to assert the inline notice + Resend button appear and that we do NOT push to `/activate`; suite **15/15**; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-11 as `dms-00300-6mq` (**300th production revision**); health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: unactivated users get an accurate, actionable message (activate → then sign in) with one-click resend, instead of a scary "Invalid Activation Link" dead end.

- [x] **ADMIN-PENDING-DOMAINS-RESOLVE-STUCK-ORDER-DOMAIN: order-sourced (in-flight) rows now have a "Resolve" action so a stuck domain can be closed out** — Follow-up to `dms-00298-8p6`. After that fix disabled the checkbox for order-sourced rows (they have no archivable PendingDomain document), a genuinely-stuck in-flight domain like `anutechn.in` (order `ord_1783674270019_4jr7nx`, "Domain registration in progress" for weeks) had NO actionable path in the admin UI — only View + Email. **Fix** (`1a143a0`, deployed as `dms-00299-tlp`): added a per-row **"Resolve"** action for `source==='order'` rows. **(1) New endpoint** `POST /api/admin/pending-domains/resolve-order-domain` — admin-only; loads the Order by `orderId`, finds the domain, and flips its status to **`failed`** (or **`cancelled`** when the optional ResellerClub cancellation succeeds), appends a `bookingStatus` audit entry, and saves. Guarded: 404 if order/domain missing; **400 if the domain isn't in-flight** (can't clobber a settled domain); registrar cancel is **best-effort** (the resolve still lands if RC is unreachable). Once the domain is no longer pending/processing it drops off the in-flight projection (`listOrdersWithInFlightDomains` filters on those two statuses). **(2) UI** — amber "Resolve" button on order-sourced rows + a confirm modal with an optional "also cancel at ResellerClub" checkbox. It does **not** refund or delete the Order — it just closes out a stuck domain. **Tests**: +8 route cases (auth, order/domain not-found, in-flight guard, failed happy path, registrar-success→cancelled, registrar-failure→failed, rc-id fallback search). Suite **8/8**; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-11 as `dms-00299-tlp`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Operator-visible impact**: stuck in-flight order domains are no longer a dead end — one click marks them failed (optionally cancelling at the registrar) and clears them from the list.

- [x] **ADMIN-PENDING-DOMAINS-ORDER-ROWS-NOT-SELECTABLE: "Failed to archive selected domains" — order-sourced (in-flight) rows are no longer bulk-selectable** — Follow-up to `dms-00297-ztr`. Operator report: selecting the `anutechn.in` row (status "Pending", reason "Domain registration in progress") and bulk-archiving failed with "Failed to archive selected domains". **Root cause**: the Pending Domains list merges two sources — real `PendingDomain` documents (`source:'pending_domain'`) AND a live projection of Orders that still have pending/processing domains (`source:'order'`). Order-sourced rows carry a **synthetic `_id`** (`order_<orderId>_<domain>`) and have NO PendingDomain document, so the per-row `DELETE /api/admin/pending-domains/[id]` 404s and the bulk action reports failure. The per-row Archive/Delete buttons already hid for `source!=='pending_domain'`, but the row + select-all **checkboxes** still allowed selecting these rows, so the bulk toolbar could act on them. **Fix** (`17136d7`, deployed as `dms-00298-8p6`): order-sourced rows are no longer selectable — their row checkbox is disabled (tooltip: "In-flight order — archive/delete not applicable here"), and **select-all** now targets only the real `PendingDomain` rows (and disables entirely when there are none). Bulk Archive/Delete/Verify can therefore only ever run against rows that actually have an archivable/deletable document. **Tests**: presentational client-component change; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-11 as `dms-00298-8p6`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Operator-visible impact**: the "Failed to archive" error is gone — in-flight order rows (which clear themselves when the order completes or fails) can't be selected for bulk actions that don't apply to them. **Note**: the `anutechn.in` row itself is an in-flight order stuck at "registration in progress" — a separate operational item; it will drop off this list once the order completes or is failed, and isn't archived because there's no failed-registration row to archive.

- [x] **ADMIN-PENDING-DOMAINS-ARCHIVE-BEFORE-DELETE: the bulk "Delete" no longer appears on Active pending domains — archive-first flow is now enforced (UI + server)** — Operator report: on `/admin/pending-domains`, an **Active** (non-archived) pending domain could be **permanently deleted directly** via the bulk toolbar's Delete button, even though the intended flow is archive-first → then delete from the Archived tab. **Root cause**: the per-row actions were correctly gated (row Archive only on Active, row Delete only on Archived), but the **bulk toolbar** (shown when rows are checkbox-selected) rendered Verify + Archive + **Delete** on BOTH tabs, so selecting an active row surfaced a Delete that bypassed the archive step. **Fix** (`3f95380`, deployed as `dms-00297-ztr`): **(1) UI** — the bulk toolbar is now tab-gated to match the per-row logic: **Active tab → Verify + Archive** (no Delete); **Archived tab → Delete only**. Row selection is also cleared on tab switch so an Active-tab selection can't carry into Archived and act on non-archived IDs. **(2) Server (defence in depth)** — `DELETE /api/admin/pending-domains/[id]?permanent=true` now returns **400** ("Archive this pending domain before deleting it permanently.") unless the row is already `isArchived`. The UI was previously the only guard; a stray/replayed request or future caller could still hard-delete a live pending row, and permanent delete has no undo. **Tests**: +1 route case (non-archived + `permanent=true` → 400, no ResellerClub cancel, no `deleteOne`); existing permanent-branch fixtures updated to `isArchived:true` (they exercise the now archived-only happy path). Suite **39/39**; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-11 as `dms-00297-ztr`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Operator-visible impact**: you can no longer accidentally permanent-delete an active pending domain — it must be archived first, both in the UI and at the API.

- [x] **TRIAL-COUNT-IGNORE-ABANDONED-CHECKOUT: an abandoned trial checkout no longer permanently locks a customer out of the free trial** — Operator report: on `app.anutech.in/hosting`, clicking "Start 15-Day Free Trial" showed **"You have already used your free trial"** for a customer who had NOT completed any trial (they'd opened checkout previously but never finished). Clear flaw in trial-usage counting. **Root cause**: the tokens flow persists a `hosting_trial` Order at checkout-OPEN with `status:'pending'` + placeholder `razorpayPaymentId:'pending'` (so the webhook can later find it by `razorpayOrderId`), BEFORE the customer authorizes the ₹2 mandate. `userHasPriorTrialOrder` counted ANY `hosting_trial` order regardless of state, so that stray pending row — created just by opening the Razorpay overlay and closing it — permanently blocked re-trial. **Fix** (`56dacfd`, deployed as `dms-00296-f6f`): the "prior trial" check now EXCLUDES exactly the abandoned-at-mandate shape `{status:'pending', razorpayPaymentId:'pending'}`. A genuinely-consumed trial stays counted (one-per-lifetime still holds) because it is always distinguishable: **tokens completed** → webhook sets `status:'completed'` + a real `pay_id`; **active manual trial** → `razorpayPaymentId:'manual'` (provisioned at signup). Only the never-completed tokens checkout is ignored. Both callers (the `trial-eligibility` endpoint AND the `create-order` server-side gate) share the function, so both are fixed. **No data cleanup needed** — existing stray pending rows are simply ignored going forward, so affected customers become eligible again the moment this deploys. **Tests**: +3 integration cases (abandoned→`false`, active-manual→`true`, completed-tokens→`true`); existing prior-trial + trial-eligibility suites green (5/5 + 21/21); typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-11 as `dms-00296-f6f`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: customers who bailed out of the mandate step (or whom we lost to a UPI/card hiccup) can start their trial again instead of being wrongly told they've used it.

- [x] **TRIAL-MANDATE-OFFER-UPI-AUTOPAY: the trial ₹2 mandate overlay now offers UPI Autopay + Card (was Cards-only)** — Operator report: on `app.anutech.in/checkout`, a trial signup opened the Razorpay recurring-mandate overlay showing **only Cards** — no UPI Autopay option — even though UPI Autopay is ACTIVATED on the account. **Root cause**: `RazorpayService.createRecurringTokenOrder` pinned `method: 'card'` on the Razorpay authorization order, and a recurring order with `method` set restricts Checkout's overlay to that single rail (Razorpay ties one authorization order to one method; the old "shows all enabled methods" code comment was simply wrong). **Fix** (`9e950c0`, deployed as `dms-00295-jcz`): made `method` optional and **OMIT it from the order by default**, so the recurring overlay offers every eligible mandate rail — Card + UPI Autopay — both of which use the card-like token shape (`{max_amount, expire_at, frequency}`) we already send. `create-order` no longer passes `'card'`. Callers that deliberately want one rail (e.g. `'emandate'`/`'nach'`, which need the `auth_type` token shape) can still pass `method` explicitly — that path is unchanged. **Tests**: `lib/razorpay` token suite gains an omit-method case asserting no `method` key on the order + card-like token shape — **42/42** for that (direct) suite; typecheck clean. (Note: the `create-order` *route* integration suite has PRE-EXISTING systemic failures on `main` — POST→500 from unrelated missing mocks, identical with/without this change — so it can't go fully green here; the lib unit suite is the direct coverage of this change. Flagged for a separate fix.) **Production**: deployed 2026-07-11 as `dms-00295-jcz`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: trial signups can now authorize the auto-renew mandate via **UPI Autopay** (GPay/PhonePe/etc.) as well as card. **Live verification needed**: a real ₹2 trial mandate on `app.anutech.in` should now show the UPI option — card remains as a working fallback if UPI ever errors.

- [x] **TOKENS-FLOW-CUTOVER: flipped `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` `manual` → `tokens` now that Razorpay UPI Autopay + eNACH are ACTIVATED — new trial signups now set up a mandate at signup (auto-renew) instead of the temporary no-mandate manual path** — The manual no-mandate trial flow was always a stopgap while Razorpay UPI Autopay + eSign/eNACH activation was pending (partner-side, NPCI/UIDAI). Operator confirmed on 2026-07-11 that **UPI Autopay is ACTIVATED** (UPI/QR settings page) and the **E-Mandate methods eNACH + eSign + Paper NACH are all ACTIVATED** (Netbanking → E-Mandate). **Change** (runtime env-var flip, NO code change — same image `ccb90588` as `dms-00293-9dh`): `gcloud run services update dms --update-env-vars HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=tokens` → new revision **`dms-00294-rfb`**. **Behavioral effect**: **(1)** in `create-order`, the `=== 'manual'` no-mandate branch stops firing and the `=== 'tokens'` branch now handles trial signups — customer gets a Razorpay customer + token mandate at signup (Google/Netflix-style ₹2-charge-and-reverse), so renewals auto-charge instead of requiring a manual one-shot payment (requires `user.phone`, now guaranteed by the WhatsApp→phone mirror). **(2)** the `tokens-charge-recurring` worker, a hard no-op under `manual`, is now live and will write attempt rows / drive recurring charges. **(3)** customers who signed up under manual mode are NOT migrated backward — they keep paying manually via `/dashboard/hosting/renew`; only NEW trial signups take the token path. The deploy script's sticky-flag logic reads the current Cloud Run value, so `tokens` is preserved on all subsequent deploys (no re-flip needed). **Verification**: revision `dms-00294-rfb` env confirms `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=tokens`; health 200; `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true` preserved. **Outstanding (separate operator decision, NOT changed here)**: `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true` is still set (kill switch from launch-day testing) — should be flipped back to re-enable disposable-email / IP-throttle / OTP abuse defenses before real production trial traffic resumes.

- [x] **CHECKOUT-REQUIRE-WHATSAPP: checkout now hard-gates on a WhatsApp number, catching legacy customers whose stored `profileCompleted` predates the requirement** — Follow-up to the WhatsApp-first arc. New signups + guests already supply a WhatsApp number, but LEGACY customers created before the requirement can carry `profileCompleted: true` in the DB with no WhatsApp on file — and since the NextAuth callbacks only ever *upgrade* that flag (never downgrade), tightening the completeness formula alone wouldn't re-gate them. They'd only be prompted the next time they happened to edit their profile. **Fix** (`ccb9058`, deployed as `dms-00293-9dh`): **(1)** `app/api/auth/me` now returns `whatsappNumber` (alongside phone/phoneCc). **(2)** `app/checkout/page.tsx` — after the existing `profileCompleted` check, a NEW hard gate reads `/api/v1/auth/me` and, if `whatsappNumber` is empty/absent, blocks checkout with a toast ("Please add a WhatsApp number to your profile before checkout") and redirects to `/dashboard/settings?returnUrl=%2Fcheckout`. This check is INDEPENDENT of `profileCompleted` — it's the hard stop for stale-true legacy accounts. **(3)** For forward consistency, `hasWhatsApp` was folded into the completeness formulas in `app/api/user/settings`, `app/api/user/complete-profile`, and the two `lib/auth-config/callbacks.ts` recompute paths (a persisted-true legacy flag is still honored — not downgraded — but any fresh recompute now requires WhatsApp). **Tests**: complete-profile fixture carries a WhatsApp number; settings + complete-profile 54/54, auth-config 197/197; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-11 as `dms-00293-9dh`; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: a legacy customer without a WhatsApp number is now stopped at checkout and guided to add one (rather than silently proceeding), closing the last gap in WhatsApp-first contact coverage. **Also in this batch (data-only, no runtime change)**: deleted the now-inert `maintenance_mode` Settings document from Mongo (value was `{enabled:false, message:"", scheduledEnd:null}`) — the maintenance-mode mechanism was fully removed in `dms-00288-r5c`, so no code reads the key; pure tidiness via a one-off `deleteOne` (deleted count 1, confirmed gone).

- [x] **DNS-APPLY-DEFAULTS-DISABLED-WHEN-ACTIVE: the "Apply Defaults" button no longer stays clickable when the domain already uses our nameservers** — Operator UX note: on `/dashboard/dns-management`, "Apply Defaults" was always active even when the domain was already on our managed (`*.orderbox-dns.com`) nameservers, where clicking it is a no-op. **Fix** (`4f04de9`, deployed as `dms-00292-24x`): when the nameserver API classifies the current NS as `method: 'default'` (and NS are present), the button is replaced by a non-interactive green **"Defaults active"** state (CheckCircle icon) and the helper copy switches to "This domain is already using our managed nameservers." Otherwise the normal "Apply Defaults" button shows. The custom-nameservers flow is unchanged. **Tests**: typecheck clean (presentational). **Production**: deployed 2026-07-10 as `dms-00292-24x`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: clearer DNS screen — customers immediately see their domain is on the default nameservers and aren't offered a redundant action.

- [x] **CLEANUP-DEAD-PROFILE-FORM + FIX-REGISTER-FORM-TEST (no deploy)** — Two housekeeping items, no product/runtime change. **(1)** Deleted `components/ProfileCompletionForm.tsx` + its test — the last phone-centric (non-WhatsApp) profile form, confirmed imported nowhere (the live profile-completion flow is the Account Settings page). Finishes the WhatsApp-first cleanup. `components/ProfileCompletionWarning.tsx` is a DIFFERENT, in-use component (cart + UserLayout) and was left intact. **(2)** Fixed `MultiStageRegisterForm.test.tsx`: the "WhatsApp required at signup" change (`dms-00290-m2r`) added a required field but that component suite wasn't re-run at the time, so 5 tests submitted without WhatsApp and were failing on main. Updated the `fillValid` helper + mismatch test to enter a WhatsApp number and the payload assertion to include `whatsappNumber`. **Result** (`6a622a9`): −441 lines; full component suite **782/782**; typecheck clean. **No deploy** — dead-code removal (tree-shaken/unused) + test-only fixes ship nothing to the app runtime.

- [x] **GUEST-CHECKOUT-WHATSAPP-NUMBER: guest checkout now collects a WhatsApp number (was a plain phone), completing WhatsApp-first contact everywhere** — Final piece of the WhatsApp-priority rollout: the one exception left was guest checkout, which took a plain phone. **Fix** (`2ab86b1`, deployed as `dms-00291-jzr`): **(1)** `app/checkout/guest` — the contact field is relabelled "Phone" → "WhatsApp Number" (MessageCircle icon, updated placeholder + helper: "used for order updates + renewal reminders, and as your registrant contact"); same 10-digit validation; removed the now-unused Phone icon import. **(2)** `app/api/payments/guest/verify` — the guest user created at verify now gets `whatsappNumber = tokenPayload.phone` (and the reuse/backfill branch sets it too), so guest-converted users are reachable for renewal reminders + marketing; `phone` is still set for the ResellerClub WHOIS/KYC contact (one number, both purposes — matching registered users). The signed guestToken + create-order contract are unchanged (same number field) → no token/schema churn. **Tests**: +1 asserting the created guest user carries `whatsappNumber` (== phone); guest verify suite 42/42; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-10 as `dms-00291-jzr`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: guests now enter a WhatsApp number at checkout, and it becomes their contact number for the order/WHOIS. **Completes the WhatsApp-first arc**: registration (`dms-00290-m2r`) + Account Settings (`dms-00289-ngl`) + guest checkout (this) — WhatsApp is now the prioritised contact across every customer entry point; downstream RC/Zoho/KYC consume it via the phone mirror. (Note: `ProfileCompletionForm.tsx` remains a phone-based but ORPHANED/unused component — safe to delete in a future cleanup.)

- [x] **REGISTER-WHATSAPP-REQUIRED-AT-SIGNUP: new signups must provide a WhatsApp number (extends the Account-Settings rule to registration)** — Follow-up to `dms-00289-ngl`: operator asked to require WhatsApp at signup too, not just on Account Settings. **Fix** (`fc9a656`, deployed as `dms-00290-m2r`): **(1)** register form (`MultiStageRegisterForm`) — new required "WhatsApp number" field (digits-only, 10-max, MessageCircle icon), client-validated (present + 10 digits) with inline errors; submitted to the register API. Footer copy updated (only the billing address is collected later now, not phone). **(2)** registration Zod schema — `whatsappNumber` now required (`/^\d{10}$/`). **(3)** `/api/auth/register` — mirrors `whatsappNumber` into `phone` when no explicit phone is given (`effectivePhone`, default `phoneCc +91`), and passes it through to user creation — so one number serves both, matching the settings behaviour. **(4)** `createUserWithCredentials` + `RegisterUserInput` — persist `whatsappNumber`. **Tests**: register-route + validation + settings fixtures updated to include WhatsApp; added missing/invalid-WhatsApp → 400 cases and reworked the obsolete "phone absent" profile-completeness test to assert WhatsApp→phone mirroring; all affected suites green (144/144). Typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-10 as `dms-00290-m2r`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: the signup form now asks for a WhatsApp number up front and won't submit without a valid 10-digit one; that number auto-becomes the account's contact/phone. Combined with `dms-00289-ngl`, WhatsApp is now compulsory across both the entry point (registration) and the editor (Account Settings).

- [x] **PROFILE-WHATSAPP-REQUIRED-PHONE-OPTIONAL: WhatsApp number is now compulsory on Account Settings; plain phone is optional (auto-filled)** — Operator decision: the WhatsApp number is the required contact field (renewal reminders + future marketing); the normal phone number is optional and auto-fills from WhatsApp so one number covers both (incl. domain/KYC). **Fix** (`726275c`, deployed as `dms-00289-ngl`): **(1)** `/dashboard/settings` — WhatsApp field marked required (red `*` + "Required · renewal reminders" badge), validated (present + 10 digits); `handleUpdateProfile` blocks the save with a targeted error + jumps to the Profile section until it's valid. Phone label now reads "(optional)"; removed the old "leave blank" copy; the WhatsApp→phone mirror is retained so entering WhatsApp fills phone. **(2)** `/api/user/settings` PUT — authoritative gate: a profile save returns `400 WHATSAPP_REQUIRED` when neither the submitted nor the already-stored `whatsappNumber` is present. The existing server-side WhatsApp→phone auto-fill is unchanged, so requiring WhatsApp keeps `phone` + `profileCompleted` (phone+address) satisfied — meaning **checkout gating is unaffected** (we didn't add WhatsApp to `profileCompleted`, avoiding a checkout lockout for existing no-WhatsApp customers; the requirement bites at profile-save time). Scope is the settings route, which is the sole profile editor + the profile-completion flow. **Tests**: fixture user now carries a WhatsApp (onboarded default) + 2 new tests (no-whatsapp → 400; partial save allowed when already on file); suite 33/33; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-10 as `dms-00289-ngl`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Customer-visible impact**: on Account Settings, customers must provide a valid 10-digit WhatsApp number to save their profile; the phone number is optional and defaults to the WhatsApp number.

- [x] **TEST-DEBT-CLEANUP: fixed the 26 pre-existing failures in the hosting-stats unit suite (now 38/38)** — Dedicated pass to clear the test-harness debt flagged during the security work: `tests/unit/app/api/user/hosting/stats/route.test.ts` had ~26 chronically-red tests (failing on `main` independent of any feature change), which meant a real regression there could hide in the noise. **Investigation**: captured the failing set + a real stack — every failure was `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading '0')` on `body.data[...]`. **Root cause (single)**: the route dynamically imports TWO models — `@/models/Hosting` (line 79, inside `fetchPendingHostingEntries`) and `@/models/HostingPlan` (line 212) — but the suite mocked only `HostingPlan`. So `Hosting.find(...).sort().limit().lean()` hit the REAL Mongoose model (no DB in unit tests), threw, the route fell into its error branch, and the response had no `data` field → every `body.data[...]` assertion blew up. This is why call-assertion tests passed but body-shape tests failed. **Fix** (`19da12d`): added a chainable `@/models/Hosting` mock (`find→sort→limit→lean→[]`), overridable via a hoisted `hostingModelFind`. That one mock cleared ALL 26 — no per-group assertion changes were needed (the planned Steps 2–4 turned out unnecessary). **No product code changed.** Suite 12/38 → **38/38**; typecheck clean. **No deploy** — test files aren't part of the app runtime bundle, so this ships nothing to production (avoids a wasteful build). **Impact**: the hosting-stats suite is a trustworthy regression guard again — a future break will show as red instead of blending into pre-existing noise. Resolves the debt flagged in the `dms-00285-8f4` / `dms-00286-r28` security commits.

- [x] **REMOVE-MAINTENANCE-MODE-MECHANISM-FULLY: deleted the entire maintenance-mode system end-to-end** — Follow-up to removing the admin toggle: operator asked to remove the whole mechanism (not just the UI). **Fix** (`85fee01`, deployed as `dms-00288-r5c`, −798 lines): **(1)** `middleware.ts` — dropped `getMaintenanceStatus()` + its 15s module cache, the maintenance→`/maintenance` redirect gate, and the `x-internal-maintenance-check` TLS-bypass sentinel; removed `/maintenance` from `PUBLIC_ROUTES` (loopback TLS-skip retained). **(2)** deleted `app/api/public/maintenance-status/route.ts` + `app/maintenance/page.tsx`. **(3)** settings route — removed the dead `maintenance_mode_enabled` `NEVER_SECURITY_KEYS` entry. **(4)** `Navigation.tsx` — removed the stale `/maintenance` no-returnUrl entry. **(5)** tests — deleted the maintenance-status unit test + the browser maintenance test; pruned maintenance cases from the middleware / settings / Navigation suites. The `maintenance_mode` Settings doc (if one exists) is now inert data with no code path reading it. **Verified nothing else disturbed**: repo-wide grep shows no remaining maintenance-feature references (only unrelated legal copy "scheduled maintenance" in terms-and-conditions); suites green (middleware 87, settings 28, navigation 29); typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-10 as `dms-00288-r5c`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Impact**: no maintenance-redirect path runs on any request anymore; one fewer per-request loopback check. If site-wide maintenance is ever needed again it would be re-introduced fresh (e.g. at the Nginx/Cloud Run layer). Supersedes the prior "runtime mechanism kept" note in `dms-00287-wrz`.

- [x] **ADMIN-REMOVE-MAINTENANCE-MODE-UI + SECURITY-REVIEW-CLEAN: removed the admin Maintenance Mode control (runtime mechanism kept); post-fix security review of /api/user/** passed clean** — Two items. **(1) Security review**: after the two cross-tenant fixes, ran a focused security review over `app/api/user/**` (authz/IDOR, injection, XSS, data exposure) via a multi-agent pass — **no new HIGH/MEDIUM findings**. Confirmed `stats`+`sso` ownership now correctly anchors to `Hosting.userId`; the `TrackingScripts` `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` is safe (only strict-regex-validated provider IDs, `JSON.stringify`/`encodeURIComponent`, server-side re-extraction); CSP additions are allowlist-only (no `unsafe-*`); admin routes properly gated; the middleware `/login` redirect sanitizes `returnUrl` against protocol-relative open-redirect. **(2)** Operator asked to remove the admin-side Maintenance Mode toggle. **Fix** (`bcaa59a`): removed the admin "General" settings tab (it was only Maintenance) — nav item, section UI, state, `load/saveMaintenanceSettings`, unused Wrench/Power icons; default settings section is now "Performance". **The runtime maintenance mechanism is intentionally LEFT INTACT** — middleware `getMaintenanceStatus()`, `/api/public/maintenance-status`, the `/maintenance` page, and the `maintenance_mode` setting key are unchanged; maintenance can still be toggled at that layer (settings API / DB), just not from the admin panel. **Tests**: settings route suite 28/28; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-10 as `dms-00287-wrz`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Operator-visible impact**: Admin → Settings no longer shows a Maintenance Mode section; the site's root maintenance capability is unaffected.

- [x] **🔴 SECURITY (CRITICAL): SSO control-panel account-takeover — same DA-email ownership bug in `/api/user/hosting/sso` — FIXED (audit follow-up)** — Follow-up audit after the stats leak found the **same anti-pattern, worse**: `/api/user/hosting/sso` (which issues a one-time DirectAdmin control-panel login) authorized `?username=<X>` if the DA account's contact email matched the user's email. Since many customers' DA accounts share the operator's contact email, a customer could pass another customer's DA username and receive a **login URL into that customer's control panel** — full cross-tenant account takeover (not just viewing). **Fix** (`6e17a3d`, deployed as `dms-00286-r28`): ownership is verified from OUR records — the requested username must be a DA username on a Hosting doc owned by this user (`listHostingsForUser`) or equal their linked `directAdminUsername`. The DA `getUserConfig` email comparison is removed from the ownership decision (getUserConfig is still used only for the suspension check on the resolved username). **Audit result**: `stats` + `sso` were the ONLY customer routes inferring ownership from DA email; every other hosting route already scopes by `userId` (`findUserHostingById` / `findUserHosting`), and there's no customer-side DA enumeration. **Tests**: sso suite rewritten to the DB-ownership model — 14/14, including a cross-tenant 403 that asserts `getUserConfig` is never consulted for ownership. Typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-09 as `dms-00286-r28`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Impact**: customers can only SSO into their own control panels. Paired with the stats fix (`dms-00285-8f4`), the DA-email ownership anti-pattern is fully eliminated from customer routes.

- [x] **🔴 SECURITY (CRITICAL): cross-tenant hosting leak on /dashboard/hosting — a customer could see OTHER customers' hosting accounts — FIXED** — Operator noticed customer "Deepak Web Designer" (pawan@exceltechnologies.in) seeing many hosting accounts belonging to other customers (anutech / atdigital / nuwayengg / polyfab / prosintl …). **Root cause**: `/api/user/hosting/stats` determined hosting OWNERSHIP by scanning the DirectAdmin user list and matching on the DA account's **contact email**. The operator provisions many customers' DA accounts with the same contact email (the reseller's own address), so the email-match returned every account sharing that address → one logged-in customer saw others' hosting (usage, domains, DA usernames, nameservers). Severity: cross-tenant PII/infrastructure exposure. **Fix** (`6da4a7f`, deployed as `dms-00285-8f4`): ownership is now authoritatively the set of Hosting docs whose `userId` is the requesting user (`listHostingsForUser`) + the user's own linked `directAdminUsername`. The DA email-discovery scan is **removed entirely**; DA stats are only ever fetched for usernames the user owns in OUR records. **Verification**: security tests pass — the DA email scan (`listUsers`) is asserted NEVER called, and only owned usernames are targeted; a user with no owned Hosting + no linked username gets empty data with no DA scan. Typecheck clean. **Edge case noted**: a legacy account where neither `Hosting.directAdminUsername` nor `User.directAdminUsername` was ever persisted would now show nothing instead of being (incorrectly) matched by email — correct + rare (better to show nothing than another tenant's data). **Pre-existing test debt flagged**: this suite has ~26 failures from how it asserts against the real `secureJsonResponse` body shape on the populated path — verified identical before/after, unrelated to this fix, needs a separate harness cleanup. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-09 as `dms-00285-8f4`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved. **Impact**: customers now see ONLY their own hosting accounts. Recommend a quick audit of any other endpoint that infers ownership from DA email rather than `userId`.

- [x] **PERF-HOSTING-STATS-DROP-UNUSED-DA-DUMP: customer `/dashboard/hosting` first paint sped up by removing a wasted full-server DirectAdmin call** — Operator reported the customer Hosting page taking a long time to show anything (stuck on skeletons), "previously not happening." **Diagnosis**: the page blocks on `/api/user/hosting/stats`, which on its critical path called `getAllUserUsage()` = DA `CMD_API_SHOW_ALL_USER_USAGE` (a dump of EVERY DA user's usage on the whole server). Its result (`daUsageMap`) was **never read** — per-account usage actually comes from `getUserUsage(daUsername)` further down. Because that dump grows with the server's total account count (~65 now), it silently added seconds to every customer's hosting-page load and got worse as the platform grew — exactly matching "used to be fast." NOT a recent code regression (this route was last touched 2026-07-02); the cost was latent and scaled with account count. **Fix** (`1c29b49`): removed the `getAllUserUsage()` call (kept `getServerInfo()`), eliminating the slowest-scaling DA round-trip per request. Behavior-neutral — the value was unused. **Tests**: repointed the DA-down error-mapping cases to `getServerInfo` (now the first DA call). **Flagged**: this stats test suite has 28 PRE-EXISTING failures on HEAD (happy-path harness debt — `body.data[0]` undefined — verified identical before/after my change, so unrelated); worth a separate cleanup pass. Typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-09 as `dms-00284-6t8`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (73rd HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 55th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Customer-visible impact**: `/dashboard/hosting` shows accounts faster; the remaining latency is the genuinely-needed per-account DA calls (getUserConfig/getUserUsage/getDNSRecords) + DA server round-trip time. **Follow-up candidates** (not done here): the per-account live `getDNSRecords` on the list path could be deferred to the DNS page, and a client-side timeout/fast-first-paint (like the admin hosting page's 45s backstop + 2-pass load) would further improve perceived speed.

- [x] **AUTH-EXPIRED-ADMIN-SESSION-TO-LOGIN-NOT-403: an expired admin session now redirects to /login (returnUrl), not the /403 "Access Denied" page** — Operator reported that when an admin session expires, navigating the panel dumps them on `/403 Access Denied` — the wrong signal (that page is for *forbidden*, i.e. authenticated-but-wrong-role, not *unauthenticated*). **Root cause**: the middleware admin-page gate redirected BOTH `!token` (no/expired session) and `role!==admin` to `/403`. **Fix** (`7162bef`): the `!token` branch now 307-redirects to `/login?returnUrl=<path>` (mirrors the protected-user-route flow) so the admin re-authenticates and lands back where they were — `LoginForm` already honors `returnUrl`. The `role!==admin` branch still goes to `/403` (that's the genuine forbidden case). The old "/403 avoids endpoint-exists leak" rationale doesn't apply: middleware matches the `/admin` prefix, so a `/login` redirect reveals only that `/admin/*` is gated (already obvious), not whether a specific page exists. **Tests**: middleware suite updated + passing (no-token→/login+returnUrl; non-admin→/403); 64/64. Typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-09 as `dms-00283-cbh`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (72nd HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 54th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: a lapsed admin session now lands on the login page (returning to the original page after sign-in) instead of a dead-end "Access Denied" — pairs with the earlier app-wide SessionExpiredBanner (`dms-00274-rgm`) which handles the in-page API-call case. **Reviewer note**: completes the session-expiry UX arc — both the client (API 401 banner) and the server (page-navigation redirect) now treat an expired session as "log in again," not an error.

- [x] **ADMIN-CONFIRM-DIALOG-CONSISTENCY: remove-from-panel now uses the styled confirm modal, not the browser-native `window.confirm`** — Operator flagged the raw "app.anutech.in says…" native confirm on the remove action as off-brand + inconsistent. **Finding**: the project already has a proper styled system — `confirmDialog()` + `<ConfirmDialogHost/>` (mounted once in the root layout, Promise-based, tones primary/danger/warning). The rest of the app uses it or bespoke `Modal` components; only the two remove-from-panel call sites I'd just added used `window.confirm`. **Fix** (`fa978d6`): migrated both (admin Domains list ⋮ menu + RC diagnostic page) to `confirmDialog({ title, message, confirmText:'Remove from panel', tone:'danger' })`. A repo-wide grep now shows ZERO `window.confirm` in `app/` + `components/` — every confirmation renders as the consistent in-app modal. **Tests**: typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-09 as `dms-00282-5w9`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (71st HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 53rd TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: the remove-domain confirmation is now the same branded modal used everywhere else — no more native browser dialog. **Reviewer note**: the shared `confirmDialog` in `lib/confirm-dialog.tsx` is the standard for any future confirm; it falls back to `window.confirm` only pre-hydration/SSR.
