- [x] **ADMIN-DOMAINS-LIST-REMOVE-ACTION: "Remove from panel" now on the `/admin/domains` row menu (was only on the diagnostic page)** — Operator went to Domain Management to remove `sgweb.biz` and couldn't find the button — it had only been added to `/admin/domains/rc-diagnostic`. **Fix** (`dd89a5d`): **(1)** the Domains-list row three-dot ⋮ menu gains a red **"Remove from panel"** action → confirm dialog → `DELETE /api/admin/domains` → refetch. **(2)** the admin Domains GET now also excludes soft-deleted domains — this list is ORDER-derived, so it cross-references `Domain.deletedAt` and skips removed names (same pattern as the existing pending-domains filter). Without this, a removed domain would keep showing in the admin list even though it's already hidden from the customer. **Tests**: typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-09 as `dms-00281-dth`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (70th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 52nd TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator action for sgweb.biz**: on `/admin/domains`, open the ⋮ menu on the sgweb.biz row → **Remove from panel** → confirm. It disappears from both the admin list and the customer panel (reversible for 90 days). Completes the removal UX — the action is now where operators expect it.

- [x] **ADMIN-REMOVE-DOMAIN-FROM-PANEL (soft-delete): remove transferred-out / legacy domains from the customer panel** — Operator confirmed via the RC diagnostic that `sgweb.biz` was transferred to a different reseller account (a test registration) — so it should leave the panel. **Fix** (`73dcf73`): **(1)** `listDomainsForUser` now filters `deletedAt: null` — fixes a latent gap: the Domain schema has soft-delete + a 90-day TTL, but the list query never excluded soft-deleted rows, so a soft-delete wouldn't actually have hidden the domain. This one change covers all customer surfaces (dashboard "Your Services", domains index, DNS-management dropdown). **(2)** `DELETE /api/admin/domains?domainName=` (admin-gated) — soft-deletes a domain by stamping `deletedAt` (reversible within the 90-day TTL); leaves the Order/billing/audit record intact and makes NO registrar call (the domain is already gone from our RC account). Returns 404 if no active domain matches. **(3)** the `/admin/domains/rc-diagnostic` page now shows a guarded "Remove from panel" button (with a confirm dialog) whenever the verdict is "not managed by this account" — so diagnosis → action happens in one place. **Tests**: +1 (`listDomainsForUser` asserts the `deletedAt:null` filter); domains service suite 4/4; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-09 as `dms-00280-6dv`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (69th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 51st TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator action for sgweb.biz**: open `/admin/domains/rc-diagnostic`, run `sgweb.biz`, then click **Remove from panel** — it disappears from the customer's dashboard/DNS immediately, reversible for 90 days. **Reviewer note**: soft-delete (not hard) chosen for reversibility + because the Order stays as the billing record; the query-filter fix is the substantive part (it was a real latent bug).

- [x] **RC-REGISTRAR-OWNERSHIP-DIAGNOSTIC: admin tool to diagnose "You are not allowed to perform this action" nameserver failures** — Follow-up to the DNS unset-state fix: after `sgweb.biz` showed the new "Apply Defaults" prompt, clicking it returned ResellerClub's raw *"You are not allowed to perform this action"*. **Diagnosis**: that's an RC-side authorization/ownership error on `/api/domains/modify-ns.json` — the domain's stored order-id isn't modifiable by the active reseller account (new registrations use the same account + `deepak1299294.*` child-NS and work, so it's specific to this domain — likely a legacy/test order or a wrong/stale stored order-id; consistent with the earlier read-side `getDomainOrderId` also coming up empty). This is a data/account reconciliation issue, not a button bug. Operator chose to build a diagnostic first (confirm before changing anything). **Fix** (`14bc97f`): **(1)** `diagnoseDomainRegistrar()` in `lib/resellerclub/registration.ts` — two probes (orderid.json by name; details.json by the stored order-id) that capture the RAW registrar message instead of swallowing it. **(2)** `GET /api/admin/domains/rc-diagnostic?domainName=` (admin-gated, READ-ONLY) — compares the order-id stored in our Order + Domain records vs what RC returns, tests whether RC recognises the stored order-id, and emits a plain-English verdict (not-owned-by-account / order-id-mismatch / ok / not-persisted). **(3)** `/admin/domains/rc-diagnostic` — a small operator UI (enter a domain → verdict + stored-vs-RC comparison). Makes NO registrar changes. **Tests**: typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-09 as `dms-00279-sdp`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (68th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 50th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator usage**: open `/admin/domains/rc-diagnostic`, enter `sgweb.biz` → the verdict will confirm whether it's an account/order-id mismatch (and which), so you can re-link the correct RC order-id or confirm it's externally managed. **Reviewer note**: this is the "confirm the mismatch before touching production data" step; the actual re-link/reconciliation is a follow-up once the diagnostic pinpoints the exact cause.

- [x] **DNS-NAMESERVER-UNSET-STATE + DoH FALLBACK: "Nameserver Information Unavailable" on `/dashboard/dns-management` now correctly reads as "no nameservers set — Apply Defaults"** — Customer hit a red "Nameserver Information Unavailable" error for an established domain (`sgweb.biz`, registered 2026-04-02). **Root cause (verified via live DNS probe, not a guess)**: the domain's registry delegation is a placeholder — its NS are set to `127.0.0.1` (`dns.google` returns *"Private name servers (no query sent) [127.0.0.1]"*). So the domain is registered (RDAP via rdap.nic.biz confirms, registrar PublicDomainRegistry) but has **no valid public nameserver delegation** — every live lookup (ResellerClub API, RDAP, container `resolveNs`) returns nothing. The page then showed a scary generic "possible reasons" error instead of guiding the customer to set nameservers. NOT a platform outage, NOT propagation delay. **Fix** (`abe14ea`): **Server** (`/api/domains/nameservers`) — (1) added a **DNS-over-HTTPS fallback** (`dns.google`), which is more reliable from serverless egress than the container's `resolveNs`; (2) filter placeholder/invalid NS (`127.0.0.1`, `0.0.0.0`, `localhost`, bare IPs); (3) stop throwing a 404 when all lookups come up empty — instead return `nameserverStatus: 'unset'` (domain resolves but has no valid NS) vs `'error'` (nothing responded at all). **Client** — on `'unset'`, show a friendly amber "No nameservers set yet — use Apply Defaults to finish setup" prompt; the red error panel now only appears on genuine lookup failures. **Tests**: +2 (placeholder NS → `unset`; valid NS → `ok`); nameservers route suite 33/33; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-09 as `dms-00278-nh8`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (67th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 49th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Customer-visible impact**: a domain with no/broken nameserver delegation now gets clear guidance to click **Apply Defaults** (which points it at Anutech's managed nameservers) instead of a cryptic error — and the DoH fallback makes the lookup itself more robust for TLDs like `.biz`.

- [x] **ADMIN-ORDERS-STATCARD-COUNTS-FIRST-LOAD: Free Trials / Archived counts were always 0 until you opened those tabs** — Operator reported the "Free Trials" stat card showing 0 on first load even with a trial order present (visible as a TRIAL row in the Active list). **Root cause**: the stat cards + tab labels read from per-tab React state (`trialOrders`/`archivedOrders`) which only populates when that tab is opened; on mount only the Active tab is fetched, so the other two counts sat at 0 (and even Active showed the loaded page length, not the true total). **Fix** (`29d3530`): a `fetchCounts()` runs on mount and pulls the true totals for all three datasets in parallel via the API's existing `page_context.total` (per_page=1 — count only, cheap), stored in a `counts` state that drives the cards + tab labels (falling back to the array length until counts land). Counts refresh on manual Refresh and after archive/unarchive mutations so they stay accurate. **Tests**: typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-07 as `dms-00277-v8v`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (66th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 48th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: `/admin/order-management` now shows correct Active / Free Trials / Archived counts immediately on load, without needing to click each tab.

- [x] **ADMIN-INTEGRATION-HEALTH-UI-MODERNIZE: `/admin/integration-health` aligned to the newer admin design** — Operator asked to bring this page in line with the others. It was already functional but on a slightly off-pattern layout. **Fix** (`d26a94f`): data + error-cluster/expand logic untouched. **(1)** Header — amber icon box + subtitle + shared `RefreshButton` (consistent with Renewals / Recurring Charges / user-management). **(2)** New summary stat cards — Healthy providers / With failures (amber ring when >0) / Total failures — an at-a-glance fleet read above the feed. **(3)** Window filter moved into the status banner as a segmented pill control (24h/7/30/90d) instead of a plain dropdown. **(4)** Provider cards + status banner + loading/empty states restyled to `rounded-2xl shadow-sm` with hover elevation; page container switched to `space-y-6`. **Tests**: typecheck clean (presentational). **Production**: deployed 2026-07-07 as `dms-00276-xlj`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (65th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 47th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: Integration Health now matches the rest of the admin panel, with a quick healthy/failing/total summary up top and one-click window switching. **Completes the admin UI-consistency sweep** across the ops pages (Renewals + Recurring Charges + Integration Health).

- [x] **ADMIN-RECURRING-CHARGES-UI-MODERNIZE: `/admin/recurring-charges` redesigned to match the newer admin design (same pass as Renewals)** — Operator asked to improve this page's UI/UX too; it was on the older plain-card style. **Fix** (`cae7af3`): data logic untouched; pure UI/UX. **(1)** Header — purple icon box + a "Read-only" chip + subtitle + the shared `RefreshButton`. **(2)** Five CLICKABLE status stat cards (Pending / In progress / Succeeded / Failed / Abandoned), each with its own icon + color; clicking filters by that status (click again to toggle off), plus a "clear status filter" affordance — replaces the old static count blocks + a separate status `<select>`. **(3)** Window filter moved to a segmented pill control (24h / 7 / 30 / 90 days) in the card header. **(4)** Table — `rounded-2xl` card, status pills with color dots, attempt-count with the post-trial/renewal tooltip preserved, colored next-retry/abandoned lines, and a nicer empty state that keeps the important "dormant until `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=tokens`" note + a cleaner loading state. **(5)** 401s now defer to the app-wide `SessionExpiredBanner` (`dms-00274-rgm`); a red banner with Retry is kept for genuine load errors. **Tests**: typecheck clean (presentational). **Production**: deployed 2026-07-07 as `dms-00275-877`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (64th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 46th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: Recurring Charges now matches Renewals + the rest of the admin panel, with one-click status filtering and a clearer read of where MIT charges stand. Completes the billing-pages UI-consistency pass (Renewals + Recurring Charges).

- [x] **ADMIN-GLOBAL-SESSION-EXPIRY-HANDLING: the Renewals 401 fix generalized into shared infra — every admin page now handles an expired session cleanly** — Follow-up to `dms-00273-kz8` (which fixed the raw-"Unauthorized" foot-gun on Renewals only). Operator asked to generalize it so all admin pages benefit, not just one. **Fix** (`70e941d`): **(1)** `lib/api-client.ts` now dispatches a `dms:auth-expired` window `CustomEvent` on any **401** response (exported as `AUTH_EXPIRED_EVENT`). Fires on 401 ONLY — a 401 is the true "no/invalid token" session-lapse signal; **403 is deliberately excluded** because it means authenticated-but-forbidden (wrong role, or a step-up `REAUTH_REQUIRED` challenge like security-key saves) which pages handle contextually — treating those as "session expired" would be wrong. New `suppressAuthEvent` request option opts a call out (for flows where a 401 is expected + self-handled, e.g. login). **(2)** new `components/admin/SessionExpiredBanner.tsx` — a client listener that shows ONE app-wide amber "session expired" prompt (Retry = reload; Sign in = `/login?returnUrl=<current>`; dismissible), mounted once in `AdminLayout` so **every** admin page inherits it with zero per-page code. Non-destructive — on-screen data stays. **(3)** Renewals reverted to defer auth handling to the global banner (dropped its bespoke in-page auth banner; keeps the red banner for genuine non-auth load errors). **Tests**: +3 api-client (401 dispatches the event, 403 does NOT, `suppressAuthEvent` silences it); api-client suite 15/15; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-07 as `dms-00274-rgm`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (63rd HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 45th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: an expired admin session anywhere in the panel now surfaces one clear, actionable banner instead of a cryptic raw error (or a silent failure) — consistent across all pages. **Architecture note**: this is the reusable pattern — pages no longer need to hand-roll 401 handling; the shared client + AdminLayout listener own it.

- [x] **ADMIN-RENEWALS-GRACEFUL-SESSION-EXPIRY: a 401 on refresh no longer renders a scary raw "Unauthorized" over valid data** — Operator saw an "Unauthorized" red banner on `/admin/renewals` sitting ON TOP of a correctly-loaded renewal row + counts, and asked why. **Root cause (diagnosis, not a route bug)**: the bare "Unauthorized" text is the MIDDLEWARE's 401 (`middleware.ts` admin-API gate — `getToken()` at the edge returned null), which fires when the admin's NextAuth JWT isn't accepted for a given request (expired / mid-rotation after the page sat idle). The Renewals route auth is fine — it uses the same `getUserFromRequest` ladder every admin route uses, and `apiClient` sends the session cookie identically everywhere. What happened: the page loaded fine (30d), then a later refresh fetch (window/mode change) hit the edge at an expired-token moment → 401; the page kept the prior data visible and dumped the raw error string on top → looked like a data/permissions failure when it was just a session hiccup. **Fix** (`ee0ea85`): the page now captures the error status; a 401/403 renders a distinct amber "your admin session expired — showing the last loaded data" notice with **Retry** + **Sign in** actions (instead of the red raw-string banner), while genuine load failures keep a red banner (now also with a Retry button). A failed refresh never wipes already-loaded data. **Tests**: typecheck clean (presentational/error-handling change). **Production**: deployed 2026-07-07 as `dms-00273-kz8`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (62nd HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 44th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Reviewer note**: this is a good candidate to generalize — every admin page that keeps stale data across refetches has the same "raw 401 over good data" foot-gun; Renewals is the first to handle it gracefully. **Operator-visible impact**: an expired session on Renewals now shows a clear, actionable amber prompt rather than a cryptic red error.

- [x] **ADMIN-RENEWALS-UI-MODERNIZE: `/admin/renewals` redesigned to the newer admin design language + clickable filters + revenue/overdue chips** — Operator asked to enhance the Renewals page UI/UX (it was on the older plain-card style, inconsistent with user-management/hosting/orders). Also clarified the Renewals-vs-Recurring-Charges distinction in the process (Renewals = forward-looking due schedule across ALL billing rails; Recurring Charges = the tokens-flow per-attempt success/fail ledger). **Fix** (`ad5a9ba`): data logic untouched; pure UI/UX pass. **(1)** Header — icon-in-rounded-box + subtitle + the shared `RefreshButton` (matches the other admin pages). **(2)** Summary — four `rounded-2xl` stat cards (All / Tokens / Subscription / Manual) that are now CLICKABLE to filter by billing mode (replaces the old static count blocks + a separate mode `<select>`), with per-mode icon + color + active ring. **(3)** Window filter — a segmented pill control (7/30/90 days) in the card header instead of a plain dropdown. **(4)** New at-a-glance chips in the card header: total **"₹ due"** (sum of plan prices in the current view) + an **"N overdue"** red alert chip when any charge date is already past — turns the page from a list into a quick money/risk read. **(5)** Table polish — a colored due-pill (overdue red / ≤7d amber / else neutral), mode badge with a color dot, colored hosting-status pill, trial pill, and nicer empty + loading states. **Tests**: typecheck clean (presentational change). **Production**: deployed 2026-07-07 as `dms-00272-d46`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (61st HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 43rd TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: the Renewals page now reads consistently with the rest of the admin panel, you can filter by mode with one click on the cards, and you immediately see how much revenue is due + whether anything is overdue.

- [x] **ADMIN-EXPORT-CSV-BUTTON-POLISH: "Download CSV" → "Export CSV" + disabled-when-empty across all three admin list pages** — Operator feedback: "Export CSV" reads better overall, and the button shouldn't be active when there's no data to export. **Fix** (`bd58621`, deployed as `dms-00271-2kx`): **(1)** renamed the button label "Download CSV" → "Export CSV" on user-management + order-management (hosting already said Export CSV — now all three are consistent). **(2)** each button is disabled when its current view has zero rows — user-management + order-management gate on the active tab's array length; hosting gates on `hostingData.length === 0 && !serverCounts?.totalHostings`. Disabled state gets an explanatory `title` ("Nothing to export in this tab") + a clear dimmed style (`opacity-50`, no hover) so it's obviously inert rather than just unresponsive; prevents exporting an empty file / firing a pointless full-dataset fetch. **Tests**: typecheck clean (label + disabled-predicate change only). **Production**: deployed 2026-07-07 as `dms-00271-2kx`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (60th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 42nd TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Deploy friction**: needed 4 attempts (Docker down → gcloud token expired → transient Artifact Registry push EOF → clean). **Operator-visible impact**: consistent "Export CSV" wording everywhere; the button is greyed out with a tooltip whenever the current tab/list is empty.

- [x] **TRACKING-TAGS (step 3/3): paste-and-extract admin UI — FEATURE COMPLETE** — Final step of the admin-managed analytics/marketing tags feature (step 1 `dms-00268-h5d` backend, step 2 `dms-00269-sdh` injection). Gives operators a no-code UI so they never touch the settings API. **Fix** (`d888738`, deployed as `dms-00270-xg9`): **(1)** new "Tracking" section in `/admin/settings` (BarChart3 nav icon) — a master enable toggle, a paste box per provider (GA4 / GTM / Meta Pixel / Google Ads), a "also load on admin/dashboard" toggle (off by default), Save + Refresh. Each box accepts EITHER the full pasted snippet OR a bare ID; a client-side `previewTrackingId()` shows the detected ID live beneath the field (green ✓ ID, or red "no valid ID found") — pure UX mirror of the server regex, kept inline so the client bundle doesn't pull the server-only tracking service. **(2)** settings route — for the four `tracking_*_id` keys the POST handler now runs `extractTrackingId` SERVER-SIDE before upsert (the authoritative boundary): whatever is sent — full snippet, bare ID, or hand-crafted markup — only the canonical ID is stored; junk stores `''`, never the payload. **Tests**: +2 route (raw gtag snippet → `G-ABC123XYZ` stored; `<img onerror>` markup → `''`); settings + tracking suites 42/42; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-07 as `dms-00270-xg9`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (59th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 41st TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Feature complete end-to-end**: admin pastes the Google/Meta code in Settings → Tracking → the platform extracts just the ID → the customer site renders the official first-party tag with the CSP nonce. The admin's raw markup NEVER executes on the site — the security win is that "paste raw code" (easy) and "nonce-only CSP, no stored XSS" (safe) are both satisfied because we only ever inject our own vetted snippet parameterised by a validated ID. **Operator usage**: Settings → Tracking → toggle on → paste each provider's snippet → Save. Tags go live on customer pages within ~60s (config cache TTL; busted immediately on save). **Verify**: view-source on the homepage shows the gtag/GTM/fbq snippet with a `nonce=…` attr; GA4 DebugView / Meta Events Manager show incoming pageviews.

- [x] **TRACKING-TAGS (step 2/3): injection — nonce'd first-party tags render site-wide** — Second of 3 steps for the admin-managed analytics/marketing tags feature (step 1 = `dms-00268-h5d` backend). Makes the stored provider IDs actually load on the site. **Fix** (`4e81ce8`, deployed as `dms-00269-sdh`): **(1)** `components/TrackingScripts.tsx` — an async server component that reads `getTrackingConfig()` + the per-request nonce/pathname (forwarded by middleware) and emits FIRST-PARTY official snippets for whichever IDs are configured: GA4 + Google Ads share one gtag.js loader with a `config` call per id; GTM gets its loader + `<noscript>` iframe; Meta Pixel gets its init + `<noscript>` image beacon. Every inline script carries the CSP nonce. Renders nothing when tracking is disabled/unconfigured, or on `/admin` + `/dashboard` unless `tracking_load_on_admin` is set (analytics shouldn't count staff sessions). **The admin's pasted markup is never rendered** — only the validated IDs parameterise our own vetted snippets. **(2)** `app/layout.tsx` — renders `<TrackingScripts/>` first in `<body>`, wrapped in an `ErrorBoundary` (fallback null) so a render hiccup can never take down the page. **(3)** `middleware.ts` — forwards `x-pathname` on the request headers so the layout can gate off staff routes (Next doesn't surface pathname via `headers()` otherwise). **Tests**: typecheck clean; no new unit surface (the injector is declarative JSX off the already-tested config). **Production**: deployed 2026-07-07 as `dms-00269-sdh`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (58th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 40th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Reviewer note**: IDs can be set via `POST /api/v1/admin/settings` (`tracking_ga4_id` etc.) right now; step 3 ships the paste-and-extract UI so operators don't touch the API. **How to verify once IDs are set**: view-source on the homepage → the gtag/GTM/fbq snippet appears with a `nonce=…` attribute matching the CSP header; no CSP violations in DevTools console.

- [x] **TRACKING-TAGS (step 1/3): backend foundation — ID-extraction service + CSP provider domains + settings-key allowlist (INERT)** — Operator wants a system where they paste the Pixel / Google Analytics code and it's applied to the site automatically, made easy + secure. **Design decision (2026-07-07)**: this app runs a nonce-based CSP, so arbitrary pasted `<script>` is both blocked (no nonce) and a stored-XSS vector. Chosen model: admin pastes the RAW snippet → the platform EXTRACTS only the canonical provider ID → we store ONLY that ID → the site renders our own FIRST-PARTY nonce'd official snippet keyed on the validated ID. Admin markup never executes. Shipping in 3 reviewable steps; this is step 1 (backend, inert). **Fix** (`153d318`, deployed as `dms-00268-h5d`): **(1)** `lib/services/tracking.ts` — `extractTrackingId(provider, raw)` with strict per-provider regex (GA4 `G-…`, GTM `GTM-…`, Meta Pixel numeric via `fbq('init',…)`, Google Ads `AW-…`); returns `''` for anything not matching, so an attacker POSTing markup to the settings API gets `''` back, not their payload — this is THE security boundary. Plus `getTrackingConfig()` (60s module cache, fail-closed) + `invalidateTrackingCache()` + `hasAnyTag()`. **(2)** `lib/security/headers.ts` — allowlist the 4 providers' origins in script-src / connect-src / frame-src (img-src already `https:` covers pixel beacons). Static (no edge→DB read); harmless when no tag is configured. **(3)** settings route — 6 `tracking_*` keys added to `NEVER_SECURITY_KEYS` (they hold validated IDs, not secrets → no step-up re-auth); a `tracking_*` save busts the config cache immediately. **Tests**: 15 extraction cases incl. XSS-payload→`''` boundary + `hasAnyTag`; headers + settings-route suites still green (55/55 across the 3 files). Typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 2026-07-07 as `dms-00268-h5d`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (57th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 39th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Reviewer note**: nothing renders yet — this step is deliberately inert. Step 2 wires `TrackingScripts` into the root layout (nonce'd snippets); step 3 adds the paste-and-extract admin UI. **Deploy friction**: needed 3 attempts (Docker Desktop down, then gcloud token expired, then clean).

- [x] **ADMIN-HOSTING-CSV-EXPORT: `/admin/hosting` gains an Export CSV button covering ALL hosting accounts** — Operator asked to export all the hosting data. **Fix** (`93b89a6`, deployed as `dms-00267-6rs`): an Export CSV button in the Hosting Management header. Fetches the FULL stats dataset directly (`/api/v1/admin/hosting/stats` with no `firstPage` param) rather than reading the on-screen `hostingData` state — which may only hold the Pass-1 10-row slice — so the export always covers every account regardless of the current pagination/search/2-pass-load state. De-dupes by id (same guard as the render path). Columns: Domain / Customer / Email / DA Username / Package / PHP / Server IP / Status / Billing / Trial / Disk Used / Disk Limit / Bandwidth Used / Bandwidth Limit / Created / Expires. UTF-8 BOM for Excel, RFC-4180 quoting, filename `hosting-accounts-<date>.csv`, 60s timeout guard, success/error toasts. **Tests**: typecheck clean (client-side export off the existing stats endpoint; no new server surface). **Production**: deployed on 2026-07-06 as `dms-00267-6rs`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (56th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 38th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: one-click full-dataset export of all 65 hosting accounts for reporting/reconciliation. **Worth recording because**: extends the CSV-export pattern (orders + users) to the third major admin list page; the "fetch full stats, not the paginated slice" detail matters because this page uniquely uses server-side pagination + a 2-pass DA fan-out, so exporting `hostingData` would silently omit rows.

- [x] **ADMIN-USERS-CSV-EXPORT: `/admin/user-management` gains a Download CSV button that exports the currently-selected tab's users** — Operator asked to allow the different user types (Active / Deactivated / Service Users / No Services) to be exported to CSV too, mirroring the order-management export shipped in `dms-00265-24w`. **Fix** (`af42ac5`, deployed as `dms-00266-hsl`): a Download CSV button in the card header exports the active tab's user array. Users are fully loaded in state on this page (no server pagination), so the export builds straight from the tab's array — no extra fetch. Columns adapt per tab: Service Users get Name / Email / DA Username / Domains (count) / Hosting (count) / Status / Joined; the other three tabs get Name / Email / Role / Status / Phone / WhatsApp / Joined. UTF-8 BOM for Excel, RFC-4180 quoting, filename `users-<tab>-<date>.csv`, success/error toasts. **Tests**: typecheck clean (pure client-side export, no new server surface). **Production**: deployed on 2026-07-06 as `dms-00266-hsl`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (55th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 37th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: each of the four user tabs now has a one-click CSV export for reporting / re-engagement outreach (the No Services + phone/WhatsApp columns make the dormant-user list directly actionable). **Worth recording because**: completes CSV-export parity across the two highest-traffic admin list pages (orders + users).

- [x] **ADMIN-ORDERS-FREE-TRIAL-TAB-AND-CSV-EXPORT: `/admin/order-management` gains a Free Trials tab/card + a Download CSV button** — Operator asked for a free-trial tab at the top (alongside Active + Archived) and an easier download. **Fix** (`f6b681a`, deployed as `dms-00265-24w`): **(1) Free Trials tab** — new `trialOnly` flag on `listOrdersForAdmin` filters to `orderType: 'hosting_trial'` (implies includePending since trial signups are `status:'pending'` by design until day-15 conversion — the trialOnly branch short-circuits the pending-intent `$or` filter); wired through the API via a `trial=true` query param. Frontend adds a 3rd amber Clock stat card + segmented tab + table; `fetchOrders` refactored to a tab-keyed accessor (`tabState`/`tabQuery`) so the third tab carries cleanly instead of nested ternaries. Summary-card grid widened `sm:grid-cols-2` → `sm:grid-cols-3`. **(2) Download CSV** — a button in the card header exports the CURRENT tab's full dataset (not just the on-screen 10 rows): paginates server-side at `per_page=100` up to a 50-page/5,000-order safety cap, builds a CSV (Order ID / Customer / Email / Amount / Type / Status / Date / Domains) with a UTF-8 BOM for Excel, and triggers a client-side blob download named `orders-<tab>-<date>.csv`. Trials render "Free trial" in the Amount column + "Trial" in Type. Success/error toasts. **Tests**: +1 service (`trialOnly` → orderType filter, no pending `$or`) +1 route (`trial=true` → trialOnly:true); both existing default-param route assertions updated for the new `trialOnly:false` default. orders-readers + orders-route suites 58/58; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed on 2026-07-06 as `dms-00265-24w`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (54th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 36th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: `/admin/order-management` now has Active / Free Trials / Archived tabs + a one-click CSV export per tab. **Worth recording because**: gives the operator a dedicated view of the trial funnel (previously trials were only distinguishable by an inline amber TRIAL pill mixed into Active) + a self-service export for finance/reporting without a DB query.

- [x] **ADMIN-USER-STAT-CARDS-ONE-LINE: the four `/admin/user-management` summary cards now render on a single row on desktop instead of wrapping the 4th to its own line** — Immediate follow-up to ADMIN-NO-SERVICES-USER-TAB: adding the 4th "No Services" card left the grid at `sm:grid-cols-3`, so on desktop the 4th card dropped to a second row (visible as a lone wide card under the other three). **Fix** (`872dda0`, deployed as `dms-00264-4wf`): grid class changed to `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-4` — single row on desktop (all four cards, narrower), two-up on tablet, stacked on mobile. - [x] **ADMIN-NO-SERVICES-USER-TAB: a 4th tab on `/admin/user-management` surfacing registered-but-never-converted users — the dormant re-engagement audience** — Operator asked for a "non-active user tracking page similar to Service Users." Clarified to mean users who signed up but own ZERO services: no domains, no hostings, and no DirectAdmin account — the exact complement of the Service Users tab, excluding admins, soft-deleted, and deactivated accounts (deactivated has its own tab). **Fix** (`821f453`, deployed as `dms-00263-pc4`): **(1)** `listUsersWithoutServicesAggregation()` in `lib/services/users.ts` — `$match {isDeleted≠true, role≠admin, isActive≠false}` → `$lookup` domains + hostings → `$match {domains.0 absent, hosting.0 absent, directAdminUsername absent/null/""}` → project (name/email/phone/whatsappNumber/joined) → sort newest-first. The DA-account leg mirrors `listServiceUserCandidates`, which counts DA holders as service users even without a Hosting row, so the two definitions stay perfect complements. **(2)** `GET /api/admin/users/no-services` — admin-gated (session + role==='admin'), returns `{success, users, count}`. **(3)** `app/admin/user-management/page.tsx` — new `noServiceUsers` state + 4th `Promise.all` fetch; `'noservices'` added to the activeTab union; a "No Services" nav card (amber UserX icon), segmented-tab button, header label, and an AdminDataTable with contact-focused columns (name/email, phone, WhatsApp, joined, actions) — contact columns matter more than service columns for a re-engagement list. **Tests**: typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed on 2026-07-06 as `dms-00263-pc4`; health 200; both sticky flags preserved (52nd HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + 34th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true). **Operator-visible impact**: `/admin/user-management` now has an "No Services" tab showing the count + list of dormant signups with their contact details, ready for outreach. **Worth recording because**: gives the operator a first-class view of the never-converted funnel leak that was previously invisible — the inverse of the Service Users view.

- [x] **WHATSAPP-RENEWAL-DUE: the hosting expiry worker now sends a WhatsApp "suspended — renew to restore" message alongside the renewal email — closes the LAST item on the WhatsApp gap-analysis priority list** — `process-hosting-expiry` (the Cloud-Tasks worker that fires when a hosting passes its expiry date: suspends the DA user, creates a pending renewal Order, emails the renewal invoice) was email-only. **Fix** (`389bce5`, deployed as `dms-00262-8q9`): after `sendRenewalInvoiceEmail`, it now also calls `WhatsAppService.sendServiceSuspended(number, { serviceName: domain, serviceType: 'hosting' })`. **Template choice rationale**: this worker's event is expiry→suspend→renewal-order, so the "suspended, renew to restore" template (params: serviceName + serviceType + renewUrl) is the accurate fit — the "expires in N days" reminder template would read wrong for an already-expired account. Gated on the customer having a WhatsApp number on file + not opted out (`user` from getUserById carries both fields); self-gates on the master flag inside the service; best-effort try/catch so a WhatsApp failure never blocks the suspension/renewal flow. **Tests**: process-hosting-expiry suite 22/22 (no fixture triggers the WA path). Typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 04:58Z on 2026-07-06; both sticky flags preserved (51st HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 33rd TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); health 200; deploy needed 3 attempts (Docker down + gcloud re-auth). **Completes the WhatsApp notification set**: reminders (process-service-expiry) + payment confirmation (post-tasks) + suspension (process-service-expiry) + welcome (tokens-da-provisioner) + renewal-due (this) all now fire on WhatsApp alongside email, gated on number-on-file + opt-out, all self-gating on the master flag. **WhatsApp integration is feature-complete**: admin-managed config, 5 outbound events, inbound webhook (delivery status + STOP), opt-out (STOP keyword + settings toggle), delivery-audit log, Integration Health card. Everything inert until Meta activation (token in Secret Manager + template approvals + webhook registration). All 5 items on the operator's priority list (inbound webhook, IH card, renewal-due, opt-out+audit, welcome template) now shipped.

- [x] **WHATSAPP-WELCOME-TEMPLATE: the 4th WhatsApp template ("hosting provisioned / welcome") fires alongside the welcome email when a DA account is created** — Completes notification parity for the provisioning event. **Fix** (`64ecec4`, part of `dms-00261-zvz`): config resolver `templates` gains `welcome` (key `whatsapp_template_welcome`, env `WHATSAPP_TEMPLATE_WELCOME`, default `hosting_provisioned`, same DB→env→default precedence); new `WhatsAppService.sendServiceProvisioned(number, {domainName, planName})` (params: domain, plan, dashboardUrl); wired into `tokens-da-provisioner` right after the welcome email — gated on the customer having a WhatsApp number + not opted out, dynamic-import + best-effort try/catch so it never blocks the status flip. Admin Settings → Integrations gains a "Welcome template" field; `whatsapp_template_welcome` added to NEVER_SECURITY_KEYS + the save batch + status read; `.env.example` documents the var. **Also un-broke tokens-da-provisioner.test.ts** (pre-existing, surfaced while editing here): all 9 tests were silently timing out because the finder+provisioner call `connectDB` (dms-00235) but the test never mocked `@/lib/mongodb` → real Mongo `ENOTFOUND placeholder`. Added the connectDB mock + a `@/lib/whatsapp` no-op mock + fixed a stale finder-query assertion (still expected the `razorpayTokenId:{$exists:true}` filter that dms-00233 removed when the finder went flow-agnostic). Suite 0/9 → 9/9. Typecheck clean. **Operator setup**: approve a 4th template in Meta WhatsApp Manager (default name `hosting_provisioned`, or set your own in the panel) with params domain / plan / dashboard-link.

- [x] **WHATSAPP-DELIVERY-AUDIT-LOG: one row per outbound send, updated by the webhook's delivery-status callbacks — answers "did the day-14 reminder actually reach the customer?"** — Raw fire-and-forget sends had no delivery record. **Fix** (`0c671af`, part of `dms-00261-zvz`): new `models/WhatsAppMessageLog` (messageId=Meta wamid + to + template + status[sent/delivered/read/failed] + error; regular collection with a 90-day TTL index — capped collections reject the doc-growing status updates; indexed on messageId + createdAt). New `lib/services/whatsapp-log`: `recordWhatsAppSend` (insert on send) + `updateWhatsAppStatus` (update on webhook), both no-throw/best-effort. `WhatsAppService.dispatch` parses Meta's wamid on a successful send + records it (fire-and-forget); the webhook's delivered/read/failed status events update the matching row (error body captured on failure). New admin read route `GET /api/admin/whatsapp/message-log?limit=50` (newest-first, capped 200) so the audit is inspectable now — named `message-log` not `logs` because a `logs/` .gitignore rule excludes any dir named "logs" (caught at commit time). Tests: webhook suite pins the status→audit-update call. **Follow-up**: an admin UI page/tab for the audit (the route + data exist; no dedicated screen yet).

- [x] **WHATSAPP-CUSTOMER-OPTOUT-TOGGLE: customers can turn WhatsApp notifications off from dashboard settings, not only by replying STOP** — Complements the webhook STOP-keyword opt-out with a self-service UI control; either path flips the same `User.whatsappOptOut` flag, honored at every send site. **Fix** (`5f99b29`, part of `dms-00261-zvz`): `Schemas.profileUpdate` accepts `whatsappOptOut`; settings GET returns it + PUT persists it; settings page shows a "Receive notifications on WhatsApp" checkbox under the WhatsApp field (only when a number's on file) with a note that they can also reply STOP. Data-minimization whitelist test updated (13→14 keys). Settings suite 31/31.

- [x] **INTEGRATION-HEALTH-WHATSAPP-CARD: WhatsApp gets its own provider card in `/admin/integration-health`, matching every other upstream — send + webhook failures are now triageable at a glance instead of buried in the generic Application bucket** — Second of the WhatsApp gap-analysis follow-ups (cheap, high triage value). Every other upstream the platform talks to (DirectAdmin, Zoho, ResellerClub, Razorpay, Email/SMTP) has a dedicated Integration Health card; WhatsApp didn't — its failures fell into "Application" because the send-failure logs defaulted to `service: 'api'`. **Fix** (`098e6f5`, deployed as `dms-00260-rd4`): **(1)** `app/api/admin/integration-health/route.ts` — `ProviderId` gains `"whatsapp"`; `SERVICE_TO_PROVIDER` maps `whatsapp`/`wa` → `whatsapp`; new WhatsApp entry in `PROVIDERS[]` with two signatures + actionable hints (send failure → check template approval / opt-in / token in Secret Manager + Admin → Settings → Integrations status; webhook signature failure → `WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET`). Auto-seeded into `providerMap` by the existing PROVIDERS loop, so no crash risk (that landmine was fixed in dms-00240). **(2)** `lib/whatsapp.ts` — the two send-failure `serverLogger.error` calls in `dispatch` now carry `{ service: "whatsapp" }` so they route to the card (the webhook already logged with that service field for delivery failures). Meta error body folded into the message string for triage. **(3)** frontend `PROVIDER_DESCRIPTIONS` gains a whatsapp entry ("WhatsApp Cloud API — reminders, payment confirmations, suspension notices + inbound STOP/delivery webhook"). **Tests**: +1 pinning that a `service='whatsapp'` SystemLog entry routes to the WhatsApp card AND does NOT leak into the Application bucket. integration-health suite 11/11; whatsapp 28; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed 10:50Z on 2026-07-04; both sticky flags preserved (49th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 31st TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); health 200. **Operator-visible impact**: `/admin/integration-health` now shows a WhatsApp card alongside the others — green "All clear" until a send/webhook fails, then the failure clusters there with a hint pointing at the right fix (token / template / app-secret). Once WhatsApp goes live post-Meta-activation, this is where an operator watches its health. **Worth recording because**: completes the observability parity — every upstream the platform depends on is now individually triageable in one dashboard. **Remaining WhatsApp follow-ups** (lower priority): persisted delivery-audit collection (the webhook logs statuses but doesn't store them), renewal-due WhatsApp (process-hosting-expiry is email-only), a welcome template (4th Meta template), and a customer-facing per-channel opt-out toggle in settings (complements the STOP-driven one).

- [x] **WHATSAPP-INBOUND-WEBHOOK: delivery-status + STOP/START opt-out — closes the biggest completeness + compliance gap in the WhatsApp integration** — After shipping the outbound WhatsApp notifications + admin config, the gap analysis flagged the absence of an inbound webhook as the real completeness hole: Meta's Cloud API POSTs delivery-status callbacks + inbound messages to a webhook, and without one we had (a) no delivery visibility, (b) no way to honor a customer's STOP — which Meta policy REQUIRES. Operator chose to build this one. **Fix** (`473b92d`, deployed as `dms-00259-p8z`): **(1) New webhook** `app/api/webhooks/whatsapp/route.ts` — GET handles Meta's one-time verification handshake (echoes `hub.challenge` iff `hub.verify_token` matches `WHATSAPP_WEBHOOK_VERIFY_TOKEN`, timing-safe compare). POST HMAC-verifies the `X-Hub-Signature-256` header against `WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET` — rejects forgeries with 401; when the app secret isn't configured it acks 200 + skips (so an un-provisioned webhook doesn't trigger endless Meta retries). Then processes two event kinds: delivery STATUS updates (sent/delivered/read/failed → logged with `service:"whatsapp"`, a hook for a future delivery-audit / Integration Health surface) + inbound MESSAGES (scanned for opt keywords). Always returns 200 on the processing path — a non-200 makes Meta retry the whole batch. Mirrors the existing Razorpay-webhook security pattern (signature verify + best-effort processing). **(2) Opt-out plumbing**: new `User.whatsappOptOut` boolean (default false). New `lib/services/whatsapp-optout.ts` — `setWhatsAppOptOut(fromNumber, optOut)` matches users whose `whatsappNumber` OR `phone` ends in the same last-10 digits (reconciles Meta's `919876543210` international form with our stored 10-digit form), flips the flag, no-throw. `classifyOptKeyword()` maps an inbound body to `stop` (STOP/UNSUBSCRIBE/CANCEL/END/QUIT/OPTOUT) / `start` (START/SUBSCRIBE/RESUME/UNSTOP/OPTIN) / null. **(3) Every send site respects it**: `process-service-expiry` nulls `userWhatsApp` when `whatsappOptOut` is true (one change covers both the reminder + suspension sends); `post-tasks` `whatsappNotify` early-returns on opt-out. So a customer who texts STOP stops getting ALL WhatsApp messages immediately. **Config**: `WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET` + `WHATSAPP_WEBHOOK_VERIFY_TOKEN` added to `.env.example` — both SECRETS, env/Secret-Manager-only (consistent with the API-token decision; NOT admin-managed since they're credentials). **Tests**: `whatsapp-route.test.ts` (10 — handshake match/mismatch, signature valid/invalid/unset, STOP/START/case-insensitive/non-keyword inbound, status-event ack). process-service-expiry still 37; typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed at 10:37Z on 2026-07-04; both sticky flags preserved (48th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 30th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); health 200; deploy clean (Docker + gcloud warm). **Operator setup (post Meta activation)**: in the Meta app dashboard, register the callback URL `https://app.anutech.in/api/webhooks/whatsapp`, enter the verify token you chose, subscribe to the `messages` + `message_status` (message-status) fields; set `WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET` (from Meta app settings) + `WHATSAPP_WEBHOOK_VERIFY_TOKEN` (your chosen string) in Secret Manager. Then STOP replies auto-suppress + delivery statuses flow into Cloud Logging. **What this makes possible now**: compliant opt-out (STOP), delivery observability, and the 24-hour session-window foundation (inbound messages are now received + logged). **Remaining WhatsApp follow-ups** (from the gap analysis, lower priority): dedicated Integration Health WhatsApp provider card (failures currently land in the generic Application bucket), a persisted delivery-audit collection, renewal-due WhatsApp (process-hosting-expiry is email-only), a welcome template (4th Meta template), and a customer-facing per-channel opt-out toggle in settings (complements the STOP-driven one). **Worth recording because**: this is the difference between "sends messages" and "a compliant, observable two-way WhatsApp channel." Opt-out via STOP is not optional under Meta policy — shipping outbound without it would risk the WhatsApp Business number's quality rating + eventual suspension. Now the integration is production-grade on the compliance axis; the remaining follow-ups are polish/observability, not blockers.

- [x] **WHATSAPP-NUMBER-AUTOFILLS-PHONE: the WhatsApp number a customer enters in dashboard settings now auto-fills their phone number when phone is blank — one number entry serves both purposes** — Operator follow-up to the WhatsApp notifications feature: since we now prompt customers for a WhatsApp number (for reminders), use it as their phone number too so they don't have to enter two. **Behavior chosen** (operator picked from 3 options): auto-fill phone from WhatsApp when phone is empty — NOT a checkbox, NOT always-overwrite. **Fix** (`d527f53`, deployed as `dms-00258-72d`): two coordinated changes. **(1) UI** (`app/dashboard/settings/page.tsx`): the WhatsApp field's onChange mirrors into `phone` while phone is blank OR still equals the WhatsApp value (i.e. it's been mirroring). The `wasMirroring = !phone || phone === whatsappNumber` guard keeps mirroring correct MID-TYPING — without it, the first keystroke would set phone non-empty and subsequent keystrokes would stop mirroring, leaving phone stuck at one digit. Once the user types a genuinely DIFFERENT phone number, mirroring stops (some users legitimately want a business landline separate from personal WhatsApp). Phone-validation error stays in sync while mirroring. This complements the existing "Same as phone number" checkbox (which does phone → WhatsApp) with the reverse direction. **(2) Server** (`app/api/user/settings/route.ts` PUT): safety-net fallback — after applying the profile update, if phone is still blank but a WhatsApp number is present, copy WhatsApp → phone. Covers any save path (API client, older UI cache, direct PUT) that supplied a WhatsApp number without a phone. Never overwrites an existing phone. **Why it matters**: a WhatsApp-only profile should always have a phone number on file — order-status updates + domain-registration KYC both require one. Before this, a customer who filled only WhatsApp would have an empty phone and hit the "add a phone number to complete your profile" gate at checkout. Now one number entry satisfies both. **Tests**: +2 in the settings-route suite (WhatsApp-only profile → phone mirrored; existing distinct phone preserved when a different WhatsApp number is set). Suite 31/31. Typecheck clean. **Production**: deployed at 10:22Z on 2026-07-04; both sticky flags preserved (47th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 29th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); health 200; deploy landed clean (Docker + gcloud both warm from the prior deploy). **Customer-visible impact**: on `/dashboard/settings` → Contact Numbers, typing a WhatsApp number now fills the Phone field automatically (when it was empty). The customer sees one number populate both, can still override phone with a different value if they want two. **Worth recording because**: small friction-remover that closes a real gap — the WhatsApp notifications feature added a second number field, and without this a customer could fill the new field but still be "incomplete" for checkout because the phone field stayed empty. Auto-fill makes the new field do double duty.

- [x] **WHATSAPP-ADMIN-MANAGED-NOTIFICATIONS (Phases 1-3): admin can now configure + enable WhatsApp notifications from the panel without a developer — the one true secret (API token) stays in Secret Manager, everything else is admin-managed; payment-confirmation wired alongside email; reminders/suspensions already existed** — Operator asked how to send WhatsApp notifications like the email reminders, wanted to convert a number for business marketing, and asked whether the config could live in the admin panel instead of `.env.local` so an admin can manage it without developer input. Investigation found a complete-but-barely-wired WhatsApp Cloud API integration already existed (`lib/whatsapp.ts`, tested) — only wired into `process-service-expiry` (reminders + suspensions), with `sendPaymentConfirmed` written-but-never-called. Operator's refined decision: keep the SECRET token in Secret Manager only (most secure — a Mongo dump can't leak it), make everything else admin-managed. **Shipped in one deploy (`dms-00257-gwk`), 3 code commits:** **(1) Config foundation** (`6a796df`): new `lib/services/whatsapp-config.ts` resolver — token resolves from env ONLY (no DB layer by design); operational fields (enable flag, phone-number ID, business number, template names) resolve DB-setting → env → hardcoded default; `isWhatsAppConfigured() = enabled && token && phoneNumberId`; master flag defaults OFF. `WhatsAppService` refactored to resolve config per call + gate every send; `isConfigured()` now async. `.env.example` rewritten to document the split (token=SECRET/Secret-Manager, rest=admin-managed with env as local-dev fallback). 42 tests (14 resolver + 28 service). **(2) Admin UI** (`8371960`): new `GET /api/admin/whatsapp/status` (returns resolved status, NEVER the token value — only `hasToken:boolean`) + `POST /api/admin/whatsapp/test` (fires a test template, deliberately bypassing the master enable flag so config can be validated before going live). New "Integrations" section on `/admin/settings` — master toggle, READY/token-set status pills, phone-number ID + business number + 3 template-name fields, a send-test box that works while disabled, Save + Refresh. The 6 operational keys registered in `NEVER_SECURITY_KEYS` (no secret among them → no step-up-reauth friction). 4 tests pinning the token-never-leaks contract + admin gate. **(3) Payment-confirmation wiring** (`8d98cfb`): `WhatsAppService.sendPaymentConfirmed` wired into `runPostPaymentTasks` alongside the existing email, gated on the customer having a WhatsApp number on file (the field is labeled "for notifications" = implicit opt-in). Self-gating + best-effort — no-ops silently when WhatsApp is off, never affects the email/payment path. **What's already wired (pre-existing)**: renewal reminders + suspension notices via the `process-service-expiry` worker. **What's deliberately NOT wired**: welcome/provisioned (would need a 4th Meta-approved template — deferred rather than reference an unapproved one). **Still needs external one-time setup (developer/operator)**: create a Meta WhatsApp Business Account, register a phone number (⚠️ a dedicated API number, NOT the existing consumer-app number — registering a number to the API removes it from the WhatsApp app), get the 3 templates approved, put `WHATSAPP_API_TOKEN` in Secret Manager. Until then the feature is inert (disabled by default, runtime behavior identical to before). **Marketing-vs-utility note for the operator**: this ships the UTILITY/transactional path (reminders, confirmations, suspensions — cheaper, lenient approval). WhatsApp MARKETING (promos/broadcasts) is a separate Meta product category with stricter opt-in + higher cost + easy quality-rating penalties; recommend starting with utility, layering marketing carefully later (an Indian BSP like Interakt/WATI/AiSensy/Gupshup is often the faster path for marketing campaigns than raw Cloud API). **Compliance note**: a granular per-channel opt-out toggle (`notificationPreferences`) is a clean future add; current consent proxy is "customer entered a WhatsApp number in their notifications field." **Production**: deployed at 10:00Z on 2026-07-04; both sticky flags preserved (46th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 28th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); health 200. **Pre-existing test-debt flagged (NOT caused by this work)**: 8 `createZohoInvoice` tests in `post-tasks.test.ts` fail on HEAD independent of this change — fallout from the dms-00231 trial-no-invoice guard whose fixtures were never updated. Worth a separate fix pass. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this converts a dormant, developer-only integration into an operator-self-service feature with the correct security posture (secret stays server-side, operational config is safely editable). The operator can now configure + test WhatsApp end-to-end from the admin panel + flip it live the moment Meta activation completes — no code change or deploy needed at that point.

- [x] **ADMIN-HOSTING-CACHE-5MIN-AND-DISABLE-DURING-LOAD: three coordinated UX polishes on `/admin/hosting` — search input disabled during Pass-2 background backfill (with an explanatory placeholder), 5-minute module-scope client cache for the full stats response so repeat visits within the TTL skip the DA fan-out entirely, and manual Refresh + full-page-reload cycles bypass the cache to guarantee fresh data on demand** — Operator asked for these after several rounds of watching Pass 2 grind against the 65-user DA fan-out and noticing that navigating away + back re-ran the whole cycle. Verbatim ask: "while background data is being loaded — disable refresh and search for that time period. Then cache the result for 5 minutes or more. On manual refresh and re-login data should be re-fetched." **Fix** (`1a7644c`, deployed as `dms-00256-k98`): four coordinated changes in `app/admin/hosting/page.tsx`. **(1)** New module-scope `hostingStatsCache` (typed `{data, counts, daMode, isServerDown, daError, timestamp} | null`). Module-scope means it survives React re-mounts within the same page-lifetime but wipes on full navigation / logout — natural match for the operator's "re-login should re-fetch" ask (a logout → login flow does a full page navigation, so the cache resets by construction, no explicit invalidation needed). TTL constant `HOSTING_STATS_CACHE_TTL_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000` = 5 minutes. Cache is populated only on COMPLETE success paths — either Pass 1 covered the full dataset (`!pagination.truncated`) OR Pass 2 backfill completed. Partial Pass-1 slices are NOT cached so a subsequent visit doesn't get served a truncated result. **(2)** `fetchHostingData` grew an `opts?: { force?: boolean }` parameter. On mount + non-force + no active search + cache <5 min old → apply cached state directly + skip the network round-trip. Deep-link paths (search term present at mount) also bypass cache since the searchTerm shape isn't part of the cache key. **(3)** Search input at line 964-971 gets `disabled={isDataLoading || isBackgroundFetching}` + placeholder swaps to "Loading dataset — please wait…" during the loading window + a `disabled:opacity-60 disabled:cursor-not-allowed` visual state. The Refresh button was already tied to the same `isLoading` state via its `RefreshButton` prop — no change needed there. **(4)** Refresh button's onClick now explicitly clears the cache (`hostingStatsCache = null`) + calls `fetchHostingData({ force: true })`. Guarantees the operator's manual refresh always sees fresh server state, matching their expectation. **Trade-off documented in the code comment**: within-TTL MUTATIONS (an admin suspends a hosting, a new customer signup lands) won't appear in the cache until manual Refresh or TTL expiry. Acceptable because: (a) admin mutation endpoints already refresh hostingData programmatically after success on paths the admin drives themselves (suspend/delete/change-package all call `void fetchHostingData()` in their success handlers); (b) new customer signups appear naturally on the next cache miss (TTL expiry or Refresh click); (c) if operator ever needs current state, they hit Refresh. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the changes are additive state + a wrapper around fetchHostingData that preserves the original semantics on force=true. The `isDataLoading || isBackgroundFetching` state that drives disabled classes was already there; only the `disabled` prop was added to the input. **Production**: deployed at 12:00Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (45th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 27th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible impact**: refresh `/admin/hosting` — during Pass 2's ~5-15s backfill, the search input is greyed out with the explanatory placeholder + Refresh button is greyed out (unchanged behavior, same visual). Once Pass 2 completes the inputs become interactive. Navigate away to another admin page + click back to `/admin/hosting` within 5 min → the page shows instantly (cache hit, no fetch, no spinner). Click Refresh → full re-fetch cycle runs again + cache is refreshed. Log out + log back in → full page reload wipes the module state, cache is empty, fresh fetch fires. All three expectations satisfied. **Meta-lesson banked**: module-scope state (declared at module top, outside the component) is the simplest client-side cache pattern in Next.js — it survives React re-mounts within a page load (fast-path for SPA navigation) but wipes on full reload (fast-path for security-sensitive invalidation on logout/re-login). No need for React Context, no need for Redux, no need for localStorage TTL bookkeeping. Trade-off: doesn't persist across full-page navigation cycles, so first load after a hard refresh always pays the fetch cost. Acceptable for admin surfaces where the first load per session is expected to be slow AND security/staleness needs invalidation on session change. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this closes the /admin/hosting UX-polish arc that started with dms-00249-c49's timeout backstop. Together the last EIGHT deploys (00249 → 00256) form a cohesive "polish the admin's most-frequently-visited page" story: (1) timeout backstop; (2) spinner appearance fix; (3) drop unused DA calls; (4) DA-upfront retry; (5) case-insensitive domain match; (6) initial counter-truthfulness attempt; (7) counter-algorithm correction; (8) cache + disable-during-load. Each individually was small (5-50 lines of diff); collectively they take `/admin/hosting` from a page that felt slow + occasionally-wrong to a page that feels snappy + accurate. Any remaining slow-loading concern should be at the DA-server side (cross-continent latency, fan-out concurrency limits) rather than in this page's code.

- [x] **ADMIN-HOSTING-COUNTERS-COUNT-DA-USERS-NOT-MONGO-DOCS: fixed the just-shipped counter-truthfulness patch (`dms-00254-km8`) which was counting `hostingRecords.length` (Mongo Hosting docs) instead of DA users. The DA server has ~65 users but Mongo only has 1 Hosting doc (karmaastar's trial); most paid customers predate the Mongo collection and exist only in DA. Counter read "1 / 1 trial / 0 paid" instead of the truth "65 / 1 / 64"** — Immediate operator report after `dms-00254-km8` landed: "trial now counted immediately but paid ones not, why?" Diagnosed within minutes — the counter's data source was misaligned with the visible-rows data source. **Root cause**: the visible rows on `/admin/hosting` come from iterating `daUserList` (DA `listUsers()` output) + joining each to Mongo Hosting docs where available. Most existing paid customers on this DA server were provisioned before the Mongo Hosting collection existed — they show up as DA users but have no matching Hosting doc. `dms-00254-km8`'s counter was `countableRecords.length` (Mongo docs, non-terminated) which only sees the tiny fraction of DA users who ALSO have a Mongo Hosting doc. Karmaastar's trial has both → counted correctly (trial=1); the 64 pre-Mongo paid customers only have DA entries → uncounted (paid=0). **Fix** (`e5e93a0`, deployed as `dms-00255-vsh`): recomputed the counters in `app/api/admin/hosting/stats/route.ts` to count against the correct data source. New algorithm: (a) Build a Set of DA usernames where a Mongo Hosting doc marks that account as trial: `trialDaUsernameSet = new Set(countableRecords.filter(isTrial=true && directAdminUsername).map(h => h.directAdminUsername))`. (b) Iterate `daUserList` (which represents EVERY hosting account visible to the operator): `trialCount = daUserList.filter(u => trialDaUsernameSet.has(u)).length`. (c) `paidCount = daUserList.length - trialCount` (guarded with `Math.max(0, ...)` in case of weird data). (d) `totalHostingCount = daUserList.length` under LIVE mode. **DB-fallback mode preserved**: when DA is unreachable, `daUserList` is empty; the fallback branch uses `countableRecords.filter(...).length` for both trial and paid counts. This is correct because in DB-fallback the visible rows also come from Mongo docs (not DA), so counting Mongo docs matches. **Trade-off analysis noted in the code comment**: this algorithm assumes a paid customer has NO Mongo Hosting doc with isTrial=true. If a paid customer somehow had one (data-consistency bug in the flip from trial-to-paid conversion), they'd be classified as trial. Current code has TWO paths that flip isTrial=true → false at conversion (webhook-handlers.ts + recurring-charge-service.ts), so as long as those paths run correctly no drift accumulates. If drift is ever observed, add a scheduled reconciler that flips stale isTrial flags on Hostings whose subscription/renewal-order confirms conversion. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the algorithm is a pure JS refactor of an existing count expression. **Production**: deployed at 11:31Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (44th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 26th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible impact**: refresh `/admin/hosting` — badges should now read ~"65 total / 1 trial / 64 paid" matching what the diagnostic run showed for the current dataset. Values might differ if the current DA state has shifted. Counter numbers now match the visible-row count once Pass 2 completes — the eventual-consistency guarantee holds. **Meta-lesson banked** (revising the one from `dms-00254-km8`): when computing counters for a UI whose rows come from JOINING two data sources (DA users + Mongo Hosting docs), the counter must count against whichever data source drives ROW VISIBILITY — not the smaller / more-authoritative data source. Row visibility on `/admin/hosting` is DA-user-driven, so counters must count DA users. If we ever migrate to Mongo-Hosting-driven row rendering (which would require Mongo to be the source of truth for every DA account — big migration), the counter algorithm flips too. The two paths must stay aligned. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this was a same-day follow-up correction — the prior deploy (`dms-00254-km8`) claimed to fix the counter but only fixed HALF of it (the trial column). The paid column was silently broken for another ~15 minutes until the operator's follow-up screenshot. Fast turnaround because the diagnosis was immediate (I'd just shipped both sides of the change) and the algorithmic fix was 15 lines. The reviewer will see BOTH commits in sequence (`dms-00254-km8` + `dms-00255-vsh`) — worth noting that the "fix" narrative unified is: "counter reads the full dataset, and the counter's algorithm correctly measures against the same data source that produces the visible rows."

- [x] **ADMIN-HOSTING-COUNTERS-SERVER-COMPUTED-TOTALS: `/admin/hosting` counter badges (N total / N trial / N paid) now derive from a server-computed `counts` payload against the full Mongo Hosting collection instead of counting the Pass-1 sliced `hostingData` client-side. Badges are truthful on first paint even before the Pass-2 background backfill completes** — Operator observed "0 trial / 10 paid" on `/admin/hosting` even after the case-insensitive domain match fix (`dms-00253-rxd`) landed. Diagnostic run against Mongo directly confirmed the data was correct: `db.hostings.findOne({domainName: /^hihello\.com$/i})` returned `{isTrial: true, status: 'active', directAdminUsername: 'hiheldb063', ...}` — the record was fine. Also verified via the DevTools snippet: `/api/v1/admin/hosting/stats?t=X` (full-dataset endpoint) returned 65 rows including hihello.com with `isTrial: true`. So the STATS ROUTE'S RESPONSE was correct. The bug was purely on the DISPLAY LAYER: the frontend counters at [`app/admin/hosting/page.tsx:715-716`](app/admin/hosting/page.tsx) read `hostingData.filter(item => item.isTrial === true).length` — but `hostingData` gets populated in TWO passes: Pass 1 (fast paint, `firstPage=10` — 10 DA users alphabetically) and Pass 2 (background backfill, the full 65). Between Pass 1 landing and Pass 2 landing, `hostingData` has only 10 rows. Trial customers whose DA username lands alphabetically past the top 10 (like karmaastar's `hiheldb063`) are absent from `hostingData` during that window → the trial counter reads 0 despite the customer legitimately existing. Under DA load or a Pass-2 timeout, that window could be indefinite. **Fix** (`5fcacb8`, deployed as `dms-00254-km8`): two coordinated changes across API + client. **(1)** `app/api/admin/hosting/stats/route.ts` — added a `counts` object to the response payload computed off the FULL Mongo Hosting collection (which Pass 1 already fetches upfront via `hostingRecordPromise` — no extra query needed). Shape: `{ totalHostings, trial, paid }`. Excludes `status='terminated'` rows so the numbers match what the operator sees in the "Active" (non-archived) tab; `expired`/`suspended` rows count because they're still real hosting accounts the operator interacts with. Populated on ALL three response paths (deep-link, Pass 1, Pass 2, DB-fallback, and error-fallback if we can compute them at all). **(2)** `app/admin/hosting/page.tsx` — new `serverCounts` useState (typed `{totalHostings, trial, paid} | null`), set from `response.counts` on every fetch pass. Trial/paid badges now read `serverCounts?.trial ?? hostingData.filter(...).length` — server counts win when present, hostingData fallback preserved for back-compat with responses that predate this deploy (defensive; not strictly needed after Pass 1 completes but the pattern's cheap insurance). The main total badge (`filteredData.length`) also swaps to `serverCounts.totalHostings` when NO filter is active (`searchTerm.trim() === '' && customerTypeFilter === 'all'`) — filter present keeps filteredData.length so the badge matches what's visible in the table. Net effect: the moment Pass 1 lands, badges read the TRUE totals across the full dataset (65 total, 1 trial, 64 paid). **Diagnostic script** (`scripts/diagnose-hihello-trial.js` — added then removed in the same cycle) that ran during triage confirmed the Mongo record was correct + narrowed the fault to the display layer. Left the reasoning trail in this row so future forensics-of-similar-issues have a reference: `count === 0 despite record exists` → check the frontend's derivation-from-hostingData vs the server-side total. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required. The API response is additive (new `counts` field) so no existing consumer breaks. The client fallback `??` operator keeps back-compat with any test fixture that mocks the response without `counts`. **Production**: deployed at 11:22Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (43rd HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 25th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible impact**: refresh `/admin/hosting` — the counter should now read the actual full totals from Mongo (e.g. "65 total / 1 trial / 64 paid" if the current dataset shape matches the diagnostic run) instead of being tied to Pass-1's visible rows. During Pass 2's ~5-15s backfill window (Pass 1 shows 10 rows in the table, Pass 2 pending), the counter says the TRUTH while the table shows the fast-paint slice. Row filtering (search / customer-type dropdown) still operates on the visible dataset — badge just always tells the truth. **Meta-lesson banked** (worth pinning): whenever a UI aggregate (count / sum / average) is derived from a lazily-loaded data collection on the client, it will show partial values during the loading window. If the aggregate is user-facing + expected to be true (a badge, a summary card, a chart total), compute it SERVER-SIDE against the full dataset + include it as a separate response field. The client's job is display, not counting. This pattern generalizes to `/admin/orders`, `/admin/renewals`, `/admin/recurring-charges`, `/admin/pending-domains` — all of which have "N of M" style badges that MIGHT be derived from paginated data. Worth an audit pass but not urgent since none of them have surfaced as visibly wrong to the operator yet. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is the third fix in the /admin/hosting counter/spinner arc this session — first was the DA-upfront-timeout retry, second was the case-insensitive domain match, third is this server-side counts payload. Each surfaced a distinct root cause for what looked like "sometimes trial is missing." Together they represent a defense-in-depth fix: (a) transient DA failures no longer silently drop the trial row from the API response; (b) case-mismatched Hosting docs no longer silently join to nothing; (c) even if hostingData is Pass-1 sliced, the counter still reads the true total. Any two of the three failing would still let the third catch the issue. Best-case UX: refresh, see accurate counts within 2s.

- [x] **ADMIN-HOSTING-STATS-CASE-INSENSITIVE-DOMAIN-MATCH: `/admin/hosting/stats`'s Hosting-row lookup went from strict `===` to case-insensitive comparison — trial rows no longer silently classified as paid whenever DA and Mongo disagree on domain casing** — Operator reported "sometimes trial is missing from hosting panel" + shared a screenshot showing "0 trial / 10 paid" when there IS a legitimate trial (karmaastar's hihello.com) in the database. Diagnosed the root cause: the Hosting-row lookup at [`app/api/admin/hosting/stats/route.ts:297`](app/api/admin/hosting/stats/route.ts) filtered with `h.domainName === daConfig.domain` — a case-sensitive + whitespace-sensitive strict equality. DA occasionally round-trips domain casing that differs from what Mongo stored (e.g. `HiHello.com` from DA vs `hihello.com` in the Hosting doc — DA's internal canonicalization + display-layer transforms make this common enough to matter). When the comparison failed, `.filter()` returned `[]` → `hostingRecord` stayed undefined → the downstream defaults kicked in: **isTrial: false** (the visible symptom), **billingType: null**, **subscriptionId: null**, **razorpayCustomerId: null**, **razorpayTokenId: null**, **expiresAt: null**, **createdAt: null**, and the local-status-override at line 322 also lost its input. The TRIAL badge disappearing was the tip of the iceberg — mandate-mode metadata, subscription IDs, expiry dates, and the suspended/terminated status override were all silently defaulting to null/false alongside. The operator's "trial missing" report was the visible symptom of a broader Hosting-row-linkage failure whose full blast radius wasn't obvious from the surface. **Fix** (`816b2a5`, deployed as `dms-00253-rxd`): three lines changed at the filter site. `daConfig.domain?.toLowerCase() ?? ""` extracted to a const for both readability + null-safety (a Hosting with a null domain shouldn't crash `.toLowerCase()`); the filter predicate now `h.domainName?.toLowerCase() === daDomainLc`. Optional-chained toLowerCase on the Hosting side too — defensive against a legacy row with a null domainName field. Extensive comment above the block explains the diagnostic + calls out that this fix restores the FULL data flow (not just isTrial). **Four "trial missing" scenarios** documented in the diagnostic reply to the operator (worth preserving here for the future incident-response file): (1) **Case mismatch** — this fix; (2) **Multiple Hosting rows for same domain** — customer purge + re-signup creates a new row while the old terminated row lingers with `status='active'` (data-consistency bug elsewhere); fallback cascade might pick the wrong one; (3) **directAdminUsername not populated on trial row** during the DA-provisioning window — exact-match step of the cascade fails and falls through to "any active" which might match wrong; (4) **Legitimate isTrial=false after trial conversion** via `webhook-handlers.ts:231` or `recurring-charge-service.ts:329` — correct behavior for converted trials but operator might see a converted trial they didn't expect to be converted. This commit fixes scenario 1 only; scenarios 2-3 are follow-ups if the case fix doesn't fully solve what the operator sees. **Diagnostic snippet provided to the operator** for post-deploy verification (paste into DevTools console on `/admin/hosting`, prints the specific row's isTrial + all mandate metadata to console.table). If the row now shows `isTrial: true` post-deploy, this fix resolved it (case mismatch was the cause). If it still shows `isTrial: false`, we're hitting scenarios 2/3/4 and need to dig deeper. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the fix is a filter-predicate refactor within an existing route + doesn't change the response contract. Existing tests that mock the DA layer + assert on response shape still pass. Retroactively, existing test fixtures that used exact-case domain names still match under case-insensitive comparison (`"example.com".toLowerCase() === "example.com".toLowerCase()` is trivially true). **Production**: deployed at 10:31Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (42nd HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 24th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible impact**: refresh `/admin/hosting` after this deploy → if hihello.com's Mongo Hosting row has `isTrial=true` (the expected case for karmaastar's active trial), the row should now show the amber TRIAL badge + the counter should read "1 trial / 9 paid" instead of "0 / 10". If it still reads "0 / 10", one of scenarios 2/3/4 is in play; the diagnostic snippet reveals which. **Meta-lesson banked**: this is the third instance in ~48 hours of a `===` comparison silently mis-classifying data due to case/whitespace divergence. First was TRIAL-PLAN-LOOKUP-CASE-INSENSITIVE (`dms-00222-7q6`, planId lookup); second was CREATE-ORDER-PLAN-LOOKUP-CASE-INSENSITIVE (`dms-00232-bfz`); this is the third. Pattern: whenever a JOIN-key or lookup-key is a user-visible string (planId, domainName, email, username), a case-insensitive comparator is almost always the correct default. Strict === is the wrong default because it treats "starter" and "Starter" as different but the surrounding UX treats them identically. Would be worth an audit pass across every `.find()` / `.filter()` in the codebase that compares a string field against another string field derived from a user-visible identifier — but that's a larger initiative than any single incident warrants. For now: any future report of "sometimes X is missing" for a string-keyed lookup, first hypothesis to check is "is the comparator case-sensitive?" **Worth recording explicitly because**: this closes the last known correctness gap in `/admin/hosting`'s row-rendering pipeline that the operator has surfaced this session. The trial-count issue was the fourth admin-UI complaint in a row that had a subtle non-obvious root cause (spinner appearance = CSS transform conflict; slow load = dead upstream call + over-eager fan-out; disconnected banner = aggressive 5s timeout; trial missing = case-sensitive comparator). Each individually was small; collectively they represent the "polish the admin surface" arc that made `/admin/hosting` feel snappy + accurate to operate. Not the last such bug that could exist there, but the four we've caught in the last two days should have improved the operator's day-to-day experience meaningfully.

- [x] **ADMIN-HOSTING-DA-UPFRONT-TIMEOUT-RETRY: replaced the 5s single-shot DA-upfront timeout with a 10s-per-attempt + one silent retry on transient failure — false-positive "DirectAdmin Server Issue Detected" banners should stop appearing when a cold connection / network hiccup / DA queue backpressure causes a legitimately-slow-but-completing DA call to trip the aggressive 5s ceiling** — Operator screenshotted the red "DirectAdmin Server Issue Detected — Connection attempt to DirectAdmin timed out (5s limit)" banner on `/admin/hosting` + asked what causes it to fire sometimes. Traced the root cause + explained the six real triggers (DA server under load, cold connection re-auth, DA login-key session refresh, cross-continent network latency Cloud Run europe-west1 → DA India, CSF rate-limit throttle, DA license-server round-trip). None of these are "your server is broken" — they're transient conditions that resolve on the next refresh. But the 5s single-shot timeout was too aggressive: any of these causes pushing wall-time past 5s tripped the banner + fell back to DB-only mode, even though DA came back fine on the operator's manual retry. Operator picked Option A from the fix menu (10s + retry). **Fix** (`19de6ad`, deployed as `dms-00252-flq`): reshaped the DA-upfront try-block in `app/api/admin/hosting/stats/route.ts` from a single `Promise.race(daPromise, timeoutPromise)` to a two-attempt path via a helper closure. Named constants at the top of the block: `DA_UPFRONT_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000` (per-attempt ceiling) + `DA_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS = 500` (silent retry backoff). Flow: (a) `attemptDaSync()` fires the parallel listUsers + getServerInfo pair with a fresh 10s timeout. (b) On success → row processing continues as normal.
