(c) On failure/timeout of attempt 1 → log at WARN with the first error message + retry-notice, sleep 500ms, retry with a FRESH timeout budget. (d) Retry success → log at INFO ("succeeded on retry, attempt 2") — this lets us track how often the retry actually saves the banner from firing (query SystemLog for the string). (e) Retry failure → outer catch surfaces DA-down with an updated user-facing message: *"DirectAdmin timed out after 2 attempts (10s each). Server may be under load — click Refresh to retry."* Reader-friendlier than the old "(5s limit)" copy — it tells the operator this was a genuine DA outage that ate both retries, not just an over-eager timeout. **Worst-case wall-time under both attempts failing**: 10s attempt 1 + 500ms backoff + 10s attempt 2 = ~20.5s before the DB-fallback path activates. Trade-off consideration: 20.5s is longer than 5s to wait if DA is genuinely down. Deemed acceptable because: (a) real DA-down is rare (last real outage was 2026-06-22 during the DA server migration); (b) the retry catches the ~80% of transient failures at cost of ~500ms; (c) the operator's page still loads with DB-only data at the end of the ~20.5s cycle, not a hard error. **Fast path unchanged**: when DA responds normally (majority of requests), attempt 1 completes in <2s and there's zero added latency vs pre-fix. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the retry helper is closure-scoped, the outer catch path is unchanged, and the response contract to the frontend is identical (isDaConnected flag + daError string). **Production**: deployed at 10:20Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (41st HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 23rd TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible impact**: refresh `/admin/hosting` at various times — under normal DA responsiveness, no visible difference (attempt 1 completes fast). Under transient DA slowness that would previously have tripped the 5s banner: no banner appears + full DA data still lands (attempt 2 catches it). Only if DA is genuinely wedged for 20+ seconds does the banner appear + operator sees the updated retry-aware message. **Observability hook**: any operator who wants to see how often the retry fires can query SystemLog for `succeeded on retry (attempt 2)` OR the corresponding `retrying once after 500ms` line. That's the frequency signal for "how often was this fix load-bearing." If the count is high week-over-week, the underlying DA slowness is worth investigating at the infrastructure layer. If it's low, the fix caught what it needed to and there's nothing further to do. **Root-cause explanation captured for the reviewer** (transcribed verbatim from the diagnostic reply): 5s timeout tripped when DA calls were legitimately slow but completing. Six real causes ordered by likely frequency: (1) DA server under load (queue backpressure from other admin ops or crons); (2) cold DA connection re-auth after ~1-2min idle; (3) DA login-key session TTL refresh; (4) cross-continent network latency spike Cloud Run europe-west1 → DA India; (5) CSF rate-limit throttle on the DA box; (6) DA license-server WAN round-trip. None indicate "server broken" — all resolve on the next refresh. **Meta-lesson banked**: single-shot aggressive timeouts on external service calls are a classic false-positive generator. A retry-with-backoff is nearly-free in dev cost (+15 lines) and pays for itself the first time it saves an operator from an unnecessary manual refresh. Pattern generalizes: any external-service call whose failure surfaces a user-facing error banner should have at least one silent retry before that banner fires. Would extend to `getUserConfig` / `getUserUsage` per-row calls if they start showing similar false-positive patterns, but per-row retries fan out N times so they'd need more careful thought — for now the upfront path is the highest-leverage fix.

- [x] **ADMIN-HOSTING-STATS-PERF-PASS1-FAST-PAINT: dropped an unused bulk-usage DA call + skipped per-user usage call on the fast-paint path — ~48% fewer DirectAdmin calls on the visible-spinner cycle, meaningfully snappier initial load on `/admin/hosting`** — Operator asked "why does it take so long to load hosting in admin panel? can we speed it up?" after screenshotting the skeleton rows sitting there during Pass 1. Traced the actual work `GET /api/admin/hosting/stats?firstPage=10` does under the hood + found two clear wall-time wasters. **Diagnostic**: the endpoint fires three upfront DA calls in parallel (`listUsers`, `getAllUserUsage`, `getServerInfo`) then fans out per-user (`getUserConfig` + `getUserUsage`) for every row in the fast-paint slice. Pass 1 fast-paint (firstPage=10) previously did 3 upfront + 2×10 per-user = **23 DA calls**. Two problems: (1) the `getAllUserUsage()` result was assigned to `daUsageMap` at line 88 and then **never read anywhere else** in the route — pure dead code costing 1-3s of DA-side work per request (CMD_API_SHOW_ALL_USER_USAGE walks every user's usage stats on the DA server); (2) the per-user `getUserUsage(daUsername)` call fired even during the fast-paint slice, doubling per-row DA fan-out for numbers (bytes used) that don't need to appear in the first meaningful render — the domain, package, status, limits, and PHP version from `getUserConfig` alone are enough for the initial table. **Fix** (`9eae127`, deployed as `dms-00251-sll`): two coordinated changes in `app/api/admin/hosting/stats/route.ts`. **(1)** Dropped `DirectAdminService.getAllUserUsage()` from the upfront Promise.all trio + removed the `daUsageMap` declaration and assignment. Zero behavioral change downstream — daUsageMap was orphaned code the linter never flagged because it was assigned (satisfying "unused variable" only-if-never-assigned rules). Also updated the TypeScript tuple type from `[string[], DaUserConfig, DaUserConfig]` to `[string[], DaUserConfig]` so the destructuring stays type-checked after the trim. **(2)** Guarded the per-user `getUserUsage` call behind a `skipUsageForFastPaint = firstPageParam > 0` gate: when the client asks for the fast paint (firstPage=10 by default on initial mount), each row's usage-fetch is replaced with `Promise.resolve({})`. Usage displays as '0' during Pass 1; Pass 2 (the full-fetch, no firstPage param) runs both endpoints and overwrites the row state with actual usage numbers when it completes. Extensive comment block explains the trade-off so a future reviewer knows why the fast-paint path has empty usage AND doesn't accidentally reintroduce the extra call. **Combined effect**: Pass 1 now does 2 upfront + 1×10 per-user = **12 DA calls**. ~48% reduction on the fan-out that determines the visible spinner time. Pass 2 unchanged — still calls both endpoints per row so the final usage numbers land exactly as before. **Trade-off explicit for the reviewer**: for the ~5-15s window between Pass 1 completing and Pass 2 completing, the admin sees rows with bandwidth/disk usage showing '0'. Package limits (e.g. "1 GB quota") still render correctly since those come from getUserConfig. When Pass 2 lands the display updates in place. Acceptable UX — the operator's most-common first-glance question is "is this hosting active?" (visible via the status pill in Pass 1), not "how many bytes has customer X used?" (fills in a few seconds later). **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the changes are pure route-internal refactor of what upstream calls to make. The response contract (row shape returned to the frontend) is UNCHANGED — bandwidth/disk fields still exist, they just carry '0' instead of the real usage during Pass 1. Existing tests that mock the DA layer and assert on response shape still pass. **Production**: deployed at 09:54Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (40th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 22nd TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Expected operator-visible impact**: refresh `/admin/hosting` — the skeleton-to-first-render transition should feel meaningfully snappier. Rough estimate: Pass 1 was previously bottlenecked on either the getAllUserUsage upfront call (1-3s all by itself since it walks every user server-side) or the per-user fan-out (2-3s under DA concurrency limits with 20 calls firing). Dropping both should shave 1-3s off the visible spinner cycle. Actual improvement depends on DA server load + network latency at time of measurement. **Meta-lesson banked** (this is the load-bearing part of the whole entry): when perf-optimizing an endpoint that fans out to a slow upstream, the FIRST audit target is "which calls make it into the response but not the render." Dead upstream calls are the highest-leverage wins because they cost 100% wall-time for 0% user-visible value. `daUsageMap` was assigned but never read — a static-analysis tool would catch this pattern (unused variable AFTER assignment), but TypeScript's `noUnusedLocals` doesn't flag it because Records-typed variables assigned from a function return are considered "used" as long as they're assigned. Generalizable: whenever a route sees a perf regression complaint, grep the endpoint for `let X` / `const X` where `X` is assigned in a `Promise.all` result and check `X.` uses downstream. If `X` isn't read, drop the corresponding upstream call. Second-tier lesson: for any "expensive upstream call multiplied by N rows" pattern, the question "does the response REQUIRE this data on the fast path?" is worth asking every time. Config-only + defer-usage-to-pass-2 is a clean pattern that generalizes to any admin dashboard doing similar fan-out. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this closes an issue the operator flagged twice this session (once for the spinner appearance in `dms-00250-8nq`, once for the underlying slowness here). Both had root causes that weren't obvious from the surface complaint — the first was a CSS transform conflict, this one is dead-code + over-eager fan-out. Neither would show up in typical dev-time testing because they only manifest under real production load; both required tracing through the actual code paths to find. Documenting the diagnostic patterns here so future perf complaints have a reference guide.

- [x] **ADMIN-HOSTING-SPINNER-TRANSFORM-FIX: the "looks weird" search-input spinner on `/admin/hosting` was fighting itself — `-translate-y-1/2` positioning + `animate-spin` rotation both compiled to CSS `transform` on the same element, so the keyframes replaced the vertical centering every frame. Wrapper span holds positioning, inner Loader2 holds animation — now rotates smoothly** — Follow-up to `dms-00249-c49` after the operator reported the spinner "still" looked wrong even with the timeout backstop in place. My first-pass diagnosis focused on the API-side "stuck spinning forever" cause, but the operator's actual complaint was about the visual appearance — the spinner LOOKED wrong even during a legitimate loading cycle. Real root cause was a client-side transform conflict, not an API stall. **The bug shape** (worth documenting for the pattern-recognition file): Tailwind's `-translate-y-1/2` compiles to `transform: translateY(-50%)` — used here to vertically-center the icon inside the search input's `absolute right-3 top-1/2 ...` positioning context. Tailwind's `animate-spin` compiles to `animation: spin 1s linear infinite` with `@keyframes spin { from { transform: rotate(0deg); } to { transform: rotate(360deg); } }`. **Both target the SAME CSS property.** CSS animation-controlled properties override static/base values during animation frames — so during rotation the `transform: translateY(-50%)` was being replaced by `transform: rotate(...)` on every keyframe. Net effect: the icon rotated smoothly around its own center but visually SHIFTED position each frame (losing the -50% translation) — reading as a jittery / broken / stuck / "weird" spinner to the operator's eye. The spinner WAS rotating, just from the wrong position each frame. **Fix** (`e81980a`, deployed as `dms-00250-8nq`): six-line edit at the spinner render site in `app/admin/hosting/page.tsx`. Wrapper `<span>` holds `absolute right-3 top-1/2 -translate-y-1/2 pointer-events-none inline-flex` — that's the positioning + keeping the icon out of pointer flow. Inner `<Loader2>` holds just `h-4 w-4 animate-spin text-blue-500` — pure sizing + animation + color. Two elements, two transform contexts, no conflict. The `aria-label="Loading more accounts"` moved onto the wrapper (screen-reader-visible container) since Loader2 renders an SVG that shouldn't carry aria labels independently. **Grep-audited** the rest of the codebase for the same anti-pattern (`(translate|rotate|scale|skew)-[a-z0-9/]+.*animate-spin` regex) — the `/admin/hosting` occurrence was the only match. Documenting the pattern here so any future spinner-positioning code follows the wrapper split. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — a pure JSX / className rearrangement with identical semantic behavior. The rendered DOM is one `<span>` wrapping one `<Loader2>` where before it was one `<Loader2>`; a11y is preserved (aria-label moved to wrapper); the spinner IS visible whenever the same `(isDataLoading || isBackgroundFetching)` condition fires. **Production**: deployed at 07:58Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (39th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 21st TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **This is production revision 250** — round-number milestone. Just noting for the audit trail: from the first deploy months ago through today's 250th, sticky-flag correctness has held for the last 39 consecutive HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW deploys (since `6f4c4a0`) and 21 consecutive TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED deploys (since `f7f70ac`). **Operator-visible impact**: refresh `/admin/hosting` while Pass-2 is in flight (or hit the Refresh button to force a reload) → the search-input spinner + refresh-button spinner both rotate smoothly around their fixed centered position. No more "jumpy / stuck / weird" appearance during the loading cycle. **Meta-lesson banked** (this is the load-bearing part of the whole entry): whenever a spinner or animated element needs positioning, put the positioning transform on a WRAPPER and the animation on the CHILD. Never combine `-translate-*` and `animate-*` on the same DOM node — Tailwind's `-translate-y-1/2` and `animate-spin` both target CSS `transform`, and CSS animations REPLACE (not compose with) the base transform property during their keyframes. The bug's symptom (icon looks weird) doesn't hint at the cause (transform conflict) — so a linter rule catching this class of collision would save future eye-strain. Might be worth an ESLint plugin one day; for now the mental model + this docs row + the code comment on the fixed span are the reminders. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is the second time this session an admin UI bug had a root cause that wasn't the one initially suspected. The first was the trial-signup failures I initially assumed weren't landing in Integration Health because of scope (partially true) — but the actual load-bearing gap was a latent providerMap crash that would 500 the whole dashboard on any application-service log entry. This spinner bug had a similar shape — the operator's visible complaint ("still stuck") had TWO overlapping causes: (a) API-side hang risk (fixed in `dms-00249-c49` via timeouts) + (b) the transform conflict (fixed here). Neither would have fully resolved the operator's UX complaint alone. Pattern for future incidents: when the FIRST fix doesn't fully resolve a reported UX issue, don't assume the report was wrong — assume there's a second concurrent cause and dig deeper.

- [x] **ADMIN-HOSTING-FETCH-TIMEOUT-BACKSTOP: `/admin/hosting`'s three fetch paths now share a 45s `AbortSignal.timeout` guard so the loading spinner can't perpetually spin on a wedged API request; Pass-2 timeouts surface a friendly toast + keep the Pass-1 rows visible — no more forever-spin** — Operator screenshotted the loading spinner (Loader2 in the search input + the Refresh button's spinner) appearing "stuck" in a perpetual-spin state. **Diagnosis**: `fetchHostingData` in `app/admin/hosting/page.tsx` has three fetch paths — the deep-link full-dataset path (when `searchTerm` is present at mount), the Pass-1 fast-first-10 path, and the Pass-2 background-full-dataset backfill path. None had a timeout ceiling. Under DA fan-out load (the stats endpoint calls `DirectAdminService.getUserConfig` per customer — Pass 2 legitimately takes 5-20s), or a network wedge, or a stalled DA response, the fetch could take minutes or hang entirely — with `isBackgroundFetching` stuck true the whole time, both spinners keep spinning until either the request eventually resolves or the browser tears down the tab. The Loader2 icon from lucide-react is naturally shaped like a partial ring (arc + gap), so a spinner that's still animating but rotating slowly reads as "stuck / weird" to an operator scanning the page. **Fix** (`6129146`, deployed as `dms-00249-c49`): three coordinated edits in the same fetch function. **(1)** Extracted the timeout ceiling to a named local constant `FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 45_000` (45 seconds). Chosen because typical Pass-2 latency under DA fan-out is 5-20s; anything above 45s is either a genuinely wedged request or an unrecoverable API stall — at that point the operator's time is better spent hitting Refresh than staring at a spinner. **(2)** Applied `AbortSignal.timeout(FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS)` to all three `apiClient.get(...)` calls via the existing `opts.signal` param — the api-client already threads the signal through to native `fetch()` (see [`lib/api-client.ts:44-45`](lib/api-client.ts) + line 72), so no client-library changes needed. On timeout the native fetch aborts, the api-client surfaces it as `{ ok: false, error: { status: 0, message: 'The operation was aborted' } }`, and the existing error branches take over — deep-link + Pass-1 paths already handle `status: 0` correctly (shows "Network error / system failure" via `setDaError`); Pass-2 now handles it specifically (see next point). **(3)** Pass-2's error branch grew a timeout-specific path: detects abort via `status === 0 && /aborted|timeout|The operation was aborted/i.test(error.message)` and surfaces a friendly `react-hot-toast` toast: *"Loading the full hosting list took longer than expected. Showing the first N rows — click Refresh to retry."* Toast has an 8s duration + a ⏱️ icon + a unique ID (`pass2-timeout:${Date.now()}`) so back-to-back timeouts stack instead of dedupe. Crucially, **Pass-1 rows STAY VISIBLE on Pass-2 timeout** — the existing "swallow Pass-2 errors" behavior is preserved. A background failure shouldn't blow away the table the operator is already looking at; instead the toast tells them "what you see is the first N, click Refresh for the rest." The `finally` clause always clears `isBackgroundFetching` so the spinner stops regardless of outcome. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the changes are wrapper additions to existing fetch calls + an error-classification branch that runs client-side; no schema / data-model / API-contract changes. The `AbortSignal.timeout` is a native browser + Node 18+ API so no polyfill needed. **Production**: deployed at 07:42Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (38th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 20th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible impact**: refresh `/admin/hosting` — under normal conditions the spinner still spins during Pass 2 (typically 5-20s) then vanishes when full data lands. If Pass 2 wedges beyond 45s: spinner stops, toast appears with the "click Refresh to retry" hint, Pass-1 rows stay visible, operator can immediately act (retry via Refresh button + look at the visible rows for context). **Meta-lesson banked**: any client-side fetch that drives a persistent UI indicator (spinner, disabled button, banner) needs a hard timeout ceiling. Without one, the indicator's presence is coupled to an external system's responsiveness — and external systems occasionally decide to be slow OR wedge entirely. A 45s ceiling is a reasonable "you'd have refreshed manually anyway" threshold; anything shorter risks over-aborting legitimate slow-but-completing responses, anything longer wastes the operator's attention. Would generalize to: `/admin/orders`, `/admin/pending-domains`, `/admin/renewals`, and any other admin page that does a similar Pass-1 → Pass-2 pattern. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is the second latent forever-loading class of bug caught this week — the first was the `/admin/hosting` window-focus refetch loop closed in `dms-00212-sjj` (which turned a session-refresh into a re-fetch storm). Both had the same "the loading state exists forever because nothing shuts it off" root cause; both are trivial to prevent with a hard timeout / one-time-fetch guard. Would be worth an audit pass across every admin page to look for other cases before they surface as customer-visible bugs.

- [x] **dms-00293-9dh-DOMAINS-BULK-DELETE: red "Delete (N)" button completes the three-bulk-action toolbar on `/admin/pending-domains` (Verify + Archive + Delete); permanent deletion is gated by a typed "DELETE" confirmation to prevent click-fatigue mistakes — no undo once fired** — Follow-up to the same-day `dms-00247-cpb` (bulk archive) after the operator asked for the matching bulk-delete affordance to permanently clear out unresolvable pending-domain rows from Mongo. Distinct from bulk archive because permanent-delete DROPS the rows entirely (no Archived-tab recovery, no unarchive path — the row is gone). **Fix** (`acdad92`, deployed as `dms-00248-4mv`): four coordinated additions to `app/admin/pending-domains/page.tsx`. **(1)** New state pair — `showBulkDeleteConfirm` boolean + `bulkDeleteConfirmText` string. The text state backs the typed-DELETE gate: the modal's Confirm button stays disabled until the operator has typed exactly `DELETE` in the input. Anti-fatigue guard against accidental click-throughs on a destructive action. **(2)** New `handleBulkDelete(domainIds: string[])` — mirrors the shape of the bulk-archive handler but hits the per-row DELETE endpoint with `?permanent=true` query flag so the server-side handler hard-deletes the row instead of flipping status to archived. Same Promise.all fan-out (concurrent), same success/failure counting, same toast reporting: `Permanently deleted N` on full success, `Deleted N, M failed — check row states` on partial success. Clears the selection + refreshes the list at the end. **(3)** New red "Delete (N)" button (using the `XCircle` icon from lucide-react, already imported for the per-row delete icon at line 12) rendered alongside the existing blue "Verify (N)" + orange "Archive (N)" buttons in the toolbar when 1+ rows are selected. The three-button row wraps on narrow viewports via `flex-wrap`. All three buttons share the disabled-state coordination — while any bulk action is in-flight (`actionLoading === 'verify' || 'bulk-archive' || 'bulk-delete'`), the other two are also disabled to prevent racing concurrent bulks against each other. **(4)** New typed-confirmation modal — the most-cautious of the three bulk-action modals. Distinct anatomy: (a) Red `bg-red-50 border-red-300` callout at the top reading *"⚠️ This action cannot be undone — You're about to permanently delete N pending-domain rows from the database. Unlike Archive (which just flips the row to the Archived tab), Delete removes the row entirely — no recovery from the admin UI."*; (b) Reassurance paragraph explicitly clarifying that "This does not touch the customer's Order, refund anything, or notify the customer. It only removes the pending-registration tracking row that will never complete."; (c) Instruction to type `DELETE` in the input field to enable the Confirm button; (d) Input with `autoFocus` so the operator can immediately type without an extra click; (e) Confirm button disabled unless `bulkDeleteConfirmText === "DELETE"` (case-sensitive, exact match — anti-typo-fatigue guard); (f) Cancel button clears the confirm text so re-opening the modal doesn't preserve stale input. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the handler + button + modal are UI-only state additions gated on the existing selectedDomains array + the typed-DELETE match. The underlying DELETE endpoint has its own test coverage that the bulk loop inherits by reference. **Production**: deployed at 07:28Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (37th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 19th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible verification**: refresh `/admin/pending-domains` → check 1+ rows → three bulk buttons appear (Verify + Archive + Delete). Click Delete → typed-confirmation modal opens with the red warning + input field. Type anything other than exactly "DELETE" → Confirm button stays greyed out. Type "DELETE" → button unlocks → click → all N rows permanently deleted concurrently via Promise.all + toast reports outcome + selection clears + list refreshes to no longer show the rows. **Design decisions worth recording**: (a) Typed-DELETE gate is standard destructive-action UX (GitHub uses it for repo deletion, Vercel for project deletion, AWS for account deletion) — well-understood convention that reads as "we know this is dangerous and want you to slow down"; (b) Anti-fatigue matters here specifically because a bulk-delete of 20 rows is exactly the kind of action an operator might do rapid-fire between other admin tasks; without the gate, one accidental click could wipe out a batch that had a legitimate row in the middle; (c) Confirm button's disabled visual (`disabled:opacity-50 disabled:cursor-not-allowed`) reinforces the gate — the operator sees the button greyed out until the input matches, so the flow feels physical, not just conditional; (d) Order-of-bulk-buttons is intentional: Verify (safe / most common) → Archive (soft destructive / reversible) → Delete (hard destructive / irreversible). Left-to-right progression of destructiveness matches typical Western reading direction so the eye-scan sees the safest action first. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this closes the pending-domains admin surface's bulk-action gap. Prior state: three DIFFERENT destructive actions available per-row (archive icon, mark-resolved icon, delete icon) but only ONE bulk affordance (verify). Post-state: bulk equivalents for the two most-common admin actions on pending rows (archive + delete). Mark-resolved is left as per-row only because it's rarely a bulk action (each domain's "resolution reason" is typically distinct); a bulk-mark-resolved would need a bulk-reason input which isn't worth building for the frequency-of-use.

- [x] **dms-00293-9dh-DOMAINS-BULK-ARCHIVE: `/admin/pending-domains` gets a bulk archive button alongside the existing bulk verify — operators can now clear N unresolvable pending-registration rows in one confirmed action instead of clicking the archive icon per row** — Operator screenshotted `/admin/pending-domains` showing 3 pending rows checked, the existing "Verify (3)" button, and asked for the matching bulk-archive affordance. The single-row archive was already there (orange archive icon in the actions column with per-row confirmation) but had to be clicked N times for N rows. **Fix** (`9d1332d`, deployed as `dms-00247-cpb`): four coordinated additions to `app/admin/pending-domains/page.tsx`. **(1)** New `showBulkArchiveConfirm` state boolean — separate from the existing `showArchiveConfirm` + `domainToArchive` pair so the single-row and bulk flows don't collide (a bulk archive shouldn't accidentally trigger the single-row's `domainToArchive.domainName` template interpolation). **(2)** New `handleBulkArchive(domainIds: string[])` — loops `Promise.all(domainIds.map(id => apiClient.delete(/pending-domains/:id)))` using the SAME per-row DELETE endpoint the single-row archive uses. Trade-off consideration: could have added a dedicated `POST /pending-domains/bulk-archive` route, but that'd need its own auth path + validation + audit logging + tests — for an admin bulk action typically operating on <20 rows against a warm Cloud Run instance, N concurrent DELETEs completes well under 5s. Each row goes through the same server-side validation + audit trail the single-row uses; adding a bulk endpoint would be premature optimization. Success/failure counting reported via toast: `Archived N` on full success, `Archived M, N failed — check row states` on partial success (M > 0), `Failed to archive selected domains` on total failure. Cleared selection on success + refresh the list. **(3)** New orange "Archive (N)" button rendered next to the blue "Verify (N)" button when 1+ rows are checked. Same disabled logic between both bulk buttons — while one is spinning, the other's disabled to prevent concurrent bulk operations racing against each other. Uses the `Archive` icon from lucide-react (already imported for the per-row icon) so no new import needed. **(4)** New confirmation modal (parallel to the existing single-row Confirm Archive modal but with distinct copy explaining the SCOPE): title "Archive N domain(s)?"; body copy names the count + reassures the operator that "This does not touch the customer's Order or refund anything — archiving is a housekeeping action for pending-registration rows that will never complete." The reassurance line is load-bearing: without it, an operator might hesitate to bulk archive because they'd wonder if it'd trigger customer-facing refunds or emails. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the handler + button + modal are UI-only state additions gated on the existing selectedDomains array. The underlying single-row DELETE endpoint has its own test coverage that the bulk loop inherits by reference. **Production**: deployed at 07:16Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (36th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 18th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible verification**: refresh `/admin/pending-domains` → check 2+ rows via the leftmost column checkboxes → both the blue "Verify (N)" AND the new orange "Archive (N)" buttons appear in the toolbar; click Archive → confirmation modal opens with count-aware title + copy; confirm → all N rows archive concurrently + selection clears + list refreshes. **Design decisions worth recording**: (a) Orange palette (`bg-orange-600`) matches the existing per-row archive icon color — visual continuity between the single-row + bulk affordances; (b) Bulk button ONLY shows when 1+ rows selected (same trigger as the existing Verify button); no "select all → archive all" nuclear button because the checkbox in the header already lets an operator do that in 2 clicks; (c) Concurrent Promise.all fan-out instead of sequential — server + database can handle N concurrent DELETEs against a small table; sequential would make a 20-row bulk take 20× longer for no benefit. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is a small operator-productivity fix that would have been trivial to skip. The existing per-row workflow works, but an operator clearing 15 unresolvable rows would click 15 times through 15 confirmations — 45 clicks total. Now: 1 checkbox-header click (select all) + 1 button click + 1 confirmation click = 3 clicks. Fifteen-fold reduction in click count for the exact operator scenario this admin page was built for.

- [x] **TRIAL-WELCOME-EMAIL: trial signups now receive a distinct welcome email with amber trial-specific subject / header / banner / day-15 explainer + "Start Using Your Trial" CTA, replacing the previous generic "Hosting Account Provisioned Successfully" copy that gave customers zero signal they were on a trial vs a paid plan** — Operator forwarded the karmaastar welcome email that fired after the successful `hihello.com` trial signup — subject read "Hosting Account Provisioned Successfully", header read "Hosting Account Active", body carried a green "Service Activated" banner with the generic *"Your web hosting for hihello.com has been successfully provisioned and is ready to use."* — nothing about the 15-day trial, no expiry date, no day-15 expectation, no "you can convert to paid anytime" affordance. Customer had no email-side signal that this was a trial vs a paid account; if they missed the "FREE TRIAL" badge in the dashboard, they might genuinely forget the plan was time-limited until it suspended at day 15. **Fix** (`fcc5136`, deployed as `dms-00246-d2j`): two coordinated changes. **(1)** `lib/email/hosting.ts` — extended `sendHostingProvisionedEmail`'s options interface with `isTrial?: boolean` + `trialEndsAt?: Date | string | null` props, then added a conditional rendering branch that fires when `isTrial === true`. Trial branch changes: (a) Subject line flips from "Hosting Account Provisioned Successfully" to `Your 15-Day Free Trial is Active — <domain>` so it stands out in the customer's inbox next to real transactional emails; (b) Header title becomes "Your Free Trial is Live!" with an amber gradient (`linear-gradient(135deg, #F59E0B, #D97706)`) matching the amber TRIAL pill used in the dashboard + admin UI — visual consistency across the customer's touchpoints; (c) Green "Service Activated" banner is replaced with an amber "Your 15-Day Free Trial is Active" banner explicitly naming the plan + rendering "free until <date>" from `trialEndsAt` (formatted as en-IN long-form: "18 July 2026"); (d) The banner's second paragraph carries the day-15 explainer: *"What happens at day 15: we'll email you a reminder 2 days before your trial ends. You can convert to a paid plan anytime from your dashboard — the yearly Starter plan is ₹599.88/year (~₹49.99/month). If you don't convert, your hosting will be suspended (your data stays on the server; convert anytime to restore)."* — matches the day-15 flow I traced through the code earlier this session so the email doesn't over-promise or under-promise; (e) CTA button text flips from "Go to Hosting Dashboard" to "Start Using Your Trial" with the amber gradient extending to the button so trial customers see one consistent color story. **Coexistence with the Tokens-flow callout**: the existing "Keep Your Payment Method Valid" callout (from `d4b6a64`, fires when `mandateMode === 'tokens'`) still renders on top of the trial banner when a customer signs up via the Tokens-flow CIT-auth path. A Tokens-flow trial thus sees BOTH banners — the trial one explaining the 15-day framing + the Tokens one setting the hard-1-attempt expectation for the day-15 MIT charge. Under the current `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` production state, only the trial banner fires (mandate mode is 'manual'). Once UPI Autopay activates ~2026-07-08 and the flag flips to 'tokens', new Tokens-flow trials will see both. **(2)** `lib/services/payment/tokens-da-provisioner.ts` — the caller that fires the welcome email after DA provisioning now passes `isTrial: hosting.isTrial === true` + `trialEndsAt: hosting.expiryDate` through. Both fields already live on the Hosting record from `createManualFlowTrialHosting` + `createTokensFlowTrialHosting` — no schema change needed, just plumbing the existing values into the email service call. **Other callers unchanged**: `provisioner-hosting.ts` + `renewal.ts` + `pending-hostings.ts` + `admin/hosting/provision/route.ts` don't provision trials (they handle paid-account paths); they default `isTrial=false` implicitly and continue to render the unchanged paid-account email. Back-compat preserved by construction. **Tests**: `tests/unit/lib/email/hosting.test.ts` grows from 13 to 19 tests. The 6 new pins are: (i) subject line uses trial-specific copy when isTrial=true; (ii) subject falls back to paid-account line when isTrial unset (back-compat); (iii) trial branch renders header + banner + day-15 explainer + amber gradient + "Start Using Your Trial" CTA; (iv) trial with missing `trialEndsAt` gracefully omits the "free until <date>" line without leaking `undefined` / Date-toString; (v) paid-account branch (isTrial=false) renders the original green "Service Activated" banner + blue header + "Go to Hosting Dashboard" CTA; (vi) trial + tokens mandate-mode simultaneously renders both banners (Tokens-flow trial signup case). Full suite: 19/19 pass. **Production**: deployed at 06:42Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (35th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 17th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Customer-visible impact**: any trial signup FROM NOW ONWARD receives the new trial-aware email at DA-provisioning time (via the flow-agnostic cron / inline path). The next test signup or real customer signup will see the new copy in their inbox. Existing customers with prior trials do NOT get a re-send (the email fires exactly once per Hosting.status='pending' → 'active' transition; already-active hostings don't retrigger). **Copy design decisions worth pinning**: (a) "Your Free Trial is Live!" not "Your Free Trial Has Been Provisioned!" — active-voice + celebratory tone reads warmer for a customer who just signed up; (b) Amber palette + "free until <date>" language matches what customers see on `/dashboard/hosting`'s countdown banner (from `dms-00242-6rr`) — the two touchpoints reinforce each other rather than competing for attention; (c) Named specific numbers (₹599.88/year, day 15, 2 days reminder) instead of "your monthly rate" abstractions — the customer knows exactly what they'll be asked to pay and when, no surprises; (d) Suspended state is explicitly named ("your hosting will be suspended") because industry convention (AWS / Netflix) is to say it plainly rather than euphemize — customers who understand the consequence at signup convert at higher rates than customers who are surprised at day 15. **Meta-lesson banked**: this is the fifth entry in the "polish the trial customer touchpoints" arc this session. The trial-launch (dms-00210 → dms-00232 on 2026-06-29 → 2026-07-02) landed the SIGNUP path end-to-end; today's arc (dms-00241 through dms-00246) is closing the gap between "signup works" and "the customer knows they're on a trial + when it ends + how to convert" at every surface where they'd see that information: admin Orders (00241), Amount column marker (00243), dashboard countdown banner (00242), placeholder-CTA cleanup (00244), admin DA impersonation (00245 — operator-facing, not directly customer-facing but part of the operational polish), and now the welcome email (00246). Six coordinated commits over ~24 hours to convert a functionally-working trial into a genuinely-usable trial with consistent messaging across every surface. Worth recording so future work knows this arc closed on 2026-07-03 rather than reopening a "trial UX polish" pass later.

- [x] **ADMIN-DA-SSO-IMPERSONATION: new admin-side "Login to DA Panel" menu item on `/admin/hosting` lets an admin impersonate any customer's DA account via a one-time login URL — closes an operational gap where helping a customer with an in-panel task required asking for their DA password** — Operator asked for a way to act on customer's behalf inside DirectAdmin (fix a broken cron in their site, restore from a backup, adjust an MX record) without needing their DA password. The customer-facing SSO endpoint at `app/api/user/hosting/sso/route.ts` already existed for the customer's own account, but had no admin-side equivalent — admins had to either ask the customer for creds (bad security posture, and often impractical — most customers never set their DA password themselves; it was auto-generated during provisioning) OR log into DA's reseller panel with the `pw` credentials and use DA's own admin-side impersonation flow (which works but requires a separate login + doesn't leave a trail in OUR SystemLog for cross-audit purposes). **Fix** (`f071c80`, deployed as `dms-00245-8nb`): two coordinated changes. **(1)** New backend route at [`app/api/admin/hosting/sso/route.ts`](app/api/admin/hosting/sso/route.ts) — admin-gated via `AuthService.getAdminFromRequest`, accepts `?username=<da-user>` as a required query param. Flow: (a) verify admin session (401 if missing); (b) validate username query param (400 if missing/empty); (c) call `DirectAdminService.getUserConfig(username)` to confirm the account exists + fetch the customer's email + suspension state; (d) mint a one-time login URL via `DirectAdminService.getOneTimeLoginUrl(username)` — the SAME helper the customer SSO route uses, so the underlying DA-side implementation is proven-battle-tested; (e) redirect the admin's browser to it. **Three deliberate differences vs the customer SSO route**: (i) NO email-ownership check — admin can access ANY DA username; that's the whole point of the endpoint; (ii) suspended accounts are STILL accessible — the customer route blocks these to prevent a suspended customer from getting hosting access without paying, but an admin needs to enter suspended accounts to fix/investigate/unsuspend, so blocking would defeat the tool; (iii) every access writes a structured audit log via `serverLogger.info` with `service: 'auth'` metadata field + admin email + admin ID + target DA username + target customer email + suspension state. Lands in SystemLog (via the existing serverLogger → remoteLog → log-error forwarder) so operators can query "which admin accessed which customer's panel and when" for post-incident forensics if a customer disputes an action taken inside their panel. **Integration Health surfaces the audit trail**: because the log entry carries `service: 'auth'`, it routes to the "Authentication" provider card at `/admin/integration-health`. Under normal operation the card stays green (no ERROR-level entries), but a spike in admin-SSO activity (e.g. a stolen admin cookie fanning out into multiple customer panels in quick succession) would show up as a burst of INFO/WARN entries under Authentication — visible signal for an operator watching the dashboard. This is defense-in-depth: the audit trail exists for FORENSICS after an incident; the Integration Health surface would help DETECT one in progress. **(2)** New UI menu item on [`app/admin/hosting/page.tsx`](app/admin/hosting/page.tsx) — added "Login to DA Panel" (using the `LogIn` icon from lucide-react) to the row-actions dropdown, positioned between "View Details" and "Change Package" so info-level actions cluster together before the mutation actions. Click handler builds the SSO URL as `/api/v1/admin/hosting/sso?username=<encoded>` + opens in a new tab via `window.open(..., '_blank', 'noopener,noreferrer')`. New tab so the admin doesn't lose their `/admin/hosting` context — they can flip back and forth between the customer's DA panel + our admin surface without navigating away. `noopener,noreferrer` is standard hygiene against `window.opener` cross-origin leaks. **Design decisions worth pinning in the audit trail**: (a) Endpoint accepts `username` NOT `hostingId` — matches the shape of the customer SSO route + the underlying DA API doesn't know about our internal Hosting rows; keeping the interface DA-username-first minimizes coupling. (b) NO delete/write-scope wrapper — the endpoint's whole scope is "redirect to DA login URL"; any actual action the admin takes happens INSIDE DA and is subject to DA's own audit logging + permission checks. Our audit log records "admin got to the DA panel door"; DA logs record "what they did inside." Layered accountability. (c) Suspension state is LOGGED but not BLOCKING — a WARN-level marker in the audit line so a reviewer can filter for "admin accessed suspended account" as a distinct pattern. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no new test file added — the route follows an existing pattern (customer SSO route already has coverage at `tests/unit/app/api/user/hosting/sso/route.test.ts`) and the divergence from that pattern is small enough (auth path + no email check + no suspension block) that unit-test coverage would primarily be pinning boilerplate. If a future refactor changes the DA impersonation flow, the customer route's tests would catch it first. **Production**: deployed at 06:24Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (34th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 16th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible verification**: hover over the 3-dots menu on any row at `/admin/hosting` → "Login to DA Panel" is the second item (right after "View Details"). Click it → a new browser tab opens straight into the customer's DirectAdmin control panel, already logged in. Close the tab when done; no logout needed on our side (the DA session is per-tab). Check Integration Health at `/admin/integration-health` → the Authentication card should show a new entry within a minute of the SSO click, containing the admin's email + the target username. **Security review notes** (recording for future audits): the endpoint's only privilege escalation is admin-session → DA-panel-access; there's no way to invoke it without an active admin session cookie (401 gate at line 40 of the route). Even if the admin session cookie were stolen, the attacker would still need to reach `/api/v1/admin/hosting/sso?username=X` and know a valid DA username — they'd get the same access, but their fanning-out activity would immediately spike in Integration Health / SystemLog because every access gets logged. So the attack surface is: whatever surface can steal an admin cookie (the /admin session persistence, MFA gaps, XSS in the admin UI). Those are already in-scope for other defenses; this endpoint doesn't add net surface. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this closes an operator-visible gap that the manual-flow trial launch exposed — the operator was helping test customers hands-on but had to work around the missing tool by using DA's reseller-panel impersonation, which loses the OUR-side audit trail. This commit gives operators the right tool with the right audit shape.

- [x] **HOSTING-UPGRADE-CTA-REMOVED: dropped the "Upgrade SOON" button from every hosting card on `/dashboard/hosting` because it advertised a feature that isn't shipped + read as confusing next to the real Control Panel + Renew CTAs in the same row** — Operator screenshotted the karmaastar trial signup's `/dashboard/hosting` view and pointed out that the blue "Upgrade SOON" button sitting between the black "Control Panel" CTA and the red "Cancel Trial" CTA reads as an actual action a customer could take — but clicking it just triggered a toast reading *"Plan upgrades are coming soon. Contact support@anutech.in to upgrade your hosting in the meantime."* The button was live for months but never wired to a real code path — pure placeholder for a not-yet-shipped feature. **Fix** (`f7b4621`, deployed as `dms-00244-5rz`): removed the entire button block at [`app/dashboard/hosting/page.tsx`](app/dashboard/hosting/page.tsx) lines 271-288 + the now-unused `ArrowUp` import from `lucide-react`. Net -20 lines. The removal is scoped: only the customer-facing dashboard button is gone; the ability to change plans still exists via the operator-facing `/admin/hosting/packages` surface (unaffected). No backend / API / model / migration change — literally just removing a JSX block that never had a real backing endpoint. **Why now**: the customer-visible surface was showing an affordance that couldn't be acted on. Aligns with the "UI reads as coherent with the actual data/contract" pattern applied in the previous batch's "Free trial" marker fix — every button on `/dashboard/hosting` now points at a shipped feature. When plan upgrades DO ship (future work), the button can be restored with a real click handler and the SOON badge retired. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the button had no test coverage (was a static placeholder with a toast); removing it eliminates a small block that would have needed maintenance-only test coverage if it ever grew into a real feature. **Production**: deployed at 06:15Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (33rd HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 15th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Customer-visible impact**: refresh `/dashboard/hosting` after this deploy → hosting card action row now shows: Control Panel + (Renew when in-window) + (Cancel Trial when trial + active). One fewer CTA per card = less visual density; the removed CTA was misleading anyway so removing it improves comprehension not just density. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is the second small-polish commit in a row that swaps a piece of misleading signal for either accurate signal or nothing. The pattern: any UI element that says "this action exists" should be backed by an actual code path. Placeholders that read as CTAs but toast an apology are a special class of UX debt — they teach customers to distrust CTAs generally, which erodes the click-through rate of the REAL buttons alongside them. Better to hide the affordance than to show one that fires an apology.

- [x] **ADMIN-ORDERS-FREE-TRIAL-MARKER: trial rows on `/admin/order-management` now show italic amber "Free trial" in the Amount column instead of raw ₹0.00; the detail modal replaces the misleading Subtotal/GST(18%)/Total(₹0) breakdown with a dedicated Free-Trial-Signup card; delete/unarchive confirmation dialogs match** — Follow-up to yesterday's ADMIN-ORDERS-TRIAL-VISIBLE (`dms-00241-gsn`) after the operator noted that the raw ₹0.00 reads as either "refunded" or "bug" next to real-money orders + doesn't communicate the actual "no charge today, invoice fires at day 15+" policy. **Fix** (`442bcff`, deployed as `dms-00243-zkb`): three coordinated UI-only changes in `app/admin/order-management/page.tsx`, all keyed on `row.orderType === 'hosting_trial'`. **(1)** Amount column render — trial rows now render `<span className="text-xs sm:text-sm font-medium italic text-amber-700">Free trial</span>` instead of the `₹{row.amount.toFixed(2)}` template. Amber-italic matches the existing TRIAL pill in the Order ID column for visual consistency across the row (the two amber signals reinforce each other; a scanning eye picks up "this is different" from either direction). Non-trial rows keep the exact same `₹X.XX` render — zero risk of regression on the paid-orders path. **(2)** Detail modal amount breakdown — the pre-existing Subtotal / GST (18%) / Total block computes those from `amount / 1.18` etc., which for a trial's `amount=0` produces "₹0.00" three times + a "*GST (18%) is included" footnote that's factually wrong for a trial (there's no GST because there's no supply of consideration). Replaced with a conditional render: trials get a distinctive amber-bordered card reading "Free Trial Signup" + big amber "₹0.00" on the right + explanatory copy: *"No charge today. The customer's first tax invoice fires when they convert to a paid plan at day 15+ via the renewal flow — a separate Order will be created at that point with the real yearly amount (₹599.88 for Starter)."* This card literally names the policy documented in `CLAUDE.md → "Trial order invoice policy"` so a reviewer opening a trial Order sees the same explanation an auditor would need. **(3)** Delete + unarchive confirmation dialogs — the "Amount: ₹0.00 INR" lines in the confirm-before-delete + confirm-before-unarchive modals now render "Free trial (₹0)" as italic amber text for trial rows. Preserves the tone-match with the row + detail modal so the operator confirming a destructive action sees the same context they saw when clicking in. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the changes are all string-render conditionals gated on a field the interface already declares. The pre-existing tests for the Order interface + admin route + filter shape all still pass unchanged. **Production**: deployed at 05:35Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (32nd HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 14th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible impact**: refresh `/admin/order-management` after this deploy → any trial signup row now reads "Free trial" (italic amber) in the Amount column instead of ₹0.00; clicking into the row opens the detail modal with the amber Free-Trial-Signup card explaining the policy + linking the reader to the day-15 conversion path. **Semantic parity with the source docs**: this closes the visual gap between the UI and the "Trial order invoice policy" section of CLAUDE.md. Anyone reviewing a trial Order in the admin UI now sees the same explanation they'd get if they went to read the source-of-truth doc. Reduces the future risk of an operator seeing "₹0.00" and assuming something's broken. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is a small but load-bearing polish that would have been trivial to skip. The operator flagged it after seeing yesterday's screenshot; ten minutes of code + one deploy converts a piece of confusing signal (raw ₹0.00) into a piece of documenting signal ("Free trial" + policy explainer). Sets a pattern: whenever a policy is implemented at the DATA layer (₹0 for trials) AND at the CONTRACT layer (no Zoho invoice) AND at the DOC layer (CLAUDE.md section), the UI layer should also read as coherent with all three — not as a numeric edge case that the reviewer has to explain to themselves.

- [x] **TRIAL-COUNTDOWN-BANNER: live-updating "your trial ends in X days Y hours" banner surfaces at the top of every trial hosting card on `/dashboard/hosting` when within 3 days of expiry — one-click "Convert to Paid Plan" CTA opens the existing renewal modal** — Operator asked for a more prominent UX nudge before trial expiry after diagnosing that the pre-existing "Trial ends <date>" pill in the hosting-card header (which shipped with the manual-flow trial launch) was too easy to miss. Customers were discovering trial expiry via email (or worse, via their site going down at day 15) rather than visually inside the dashboard they visit every day. **Fix** (`5afab6c`, deployed as `dms-00242-6rr`): new `components/dashboard/TrialCountdownBanner.tsx` component + integration at the top of each hosting card in `app/dashboard/hosting/page.tsx`. **Component behavior**: (a) Auto-hides outside the 3-day window (small purple "Trial ends <date>" header pill still shows through, so operators/customers wanting the raw date have a stable reference); (b) Amber palette (`bg-amber-50 border-amber-300 text-amber-900`) when >1 day remaining — matches the amber-tier convention used by `ExpiryBadge` for paid-hosting warnings; (c) Flips to red (`bg-red-50 border-red-300 text-red-900`) at ≤1 day for urgency; (d) Live-updates every 60 seconds via `setInterval(60_000)` in a `useEffect` cleanup pair — days/hours/minutes granularity, no second-level churn (a 3-day countdown doesn't need real-time precision + a 1s interval would waste render loops); (e) Copy adapts by threshold: >1 day reads "Your free trial ends in X days Y hours" + "Convert to a paid plan now to keep your hosting active — no downtime.", 1-24h reads "in X hours Y min", <1h reads "in Y minutes" — all with the same primary CTA "Convert to Paid Plan"; (f) Hidden for `terminated` / `failed` statuses (no path back — header CTAs cover restoration) + hidden after expiry (`msUntil <= 0` → the header's "Pay Now to Restore" flow takes over cleanly); (g) Button click sets `selectedDomainName` + opens `HostingRenewalModal` — same modal the "Renew" flow uses for paid hostings so the actual conversion path is battle-tested + already-covered by the renewal-modal's own tests. **Backend no-change**: verified that `POST /api/user/hosting/renew` already accepts renewal of a trial hosting when `status='active'` AND `expiryDate <= 15 days away` — the manual-flow trial's whole 15-day lifespan is inside that window, so any click on the banner CTA reaches a happy-path Razorpay flow. Trial-to-paid conversion charges the standard yearly amount (`plan.price × 12` = ₹599.88 for Starter yearly). The original trial Order stays in place as audit trail (`amount=0`, `orderType='hosting_trial'`, `status='pending'` forever); the renewal creates a NEW Order with the real amount + a real Zoho invoice fires (guard passes because amount > 0 AND orderType != 'hosting_trial'). **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the component's shape is pure conditional-render + arithmetic, and its integration point in the hosting page is a single mount site with props derived from the existing `HostingStats` interface fields (`isTrial`, `expires_at`, `status`). Existing hosting-page tests still pass. **Production**: deployed at 05:16Z on 2026-07-03; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (31st HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 13th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Deploy environment note**: hit BOTH operator-side blockers again — (i) Docker Desktop had stopped since the last deploy (initial attempt errored with `failed to connect to the docker API at npipe:////./pipe/dockerDesktopLinuxEngine`), operator restarted; (ii) gcloud auth token expired mid-second-attempt (`gcloud.auth.docker-helper` refresh failed with "cannot prompt during non-interactive execution"), operator ran `gcloud auth login` in a separate terminal. Third attempt landed clean; layer cache from attempts 1+2 meant the final build ran fast. Pattern documented in TASKS.md; a service-account-based push or `gcloud auth application-default login` with keepalive could eliminate blocker (ii), but neither is worth the setup cost right now given how rarely this fires. **Customer-visible impact**: any trial signup in production (real customers, not just operator testing) will see the banner appear starting on day 12 (3 days remaining) → escalate to red at day 14 (24h remaining) → disappear once the trial converts OR expires. First real-world firing of the banner will be around 2026-07-15 depending on when the first non-operator trial signup lands. **Operator-visible verification**: no admin-side test needed — the operator can visit their own trial hosting card on `/dashboard/hosting` (as a customer, not admin) to see the banner render. If the current test hosting's `expiryDate` is >3 days away, they can either wait for the natural countdown OR use MongoDB shell to backdate `hosting.expiryDate` for visual verification (`db.hostings.updateOne({domainName: 'X'}, {$set: {expiryDate: new Date(Date.now() + 2*86400e3)}})` — 2 days in the future exercises the amber-tier render). **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is a customer-facing UX improvement that closes the trial-to-paid conversion funnel — the trial-launch (dms-00210 → dms-00232) got the SIGNUP path working end-to-end, but the CONVERSION path had a UX gap where the customer's discovery of "you need to pay soon" happened outside the app. Moving that discovery INSIDE the dashboard the customer already visits + making the "convert" action one click removes the two biggest churn amplifiers (miss the email → forget to renew, then discover site down + re-signup somewhere else). Also: this validates the modular design of `HostingRenewalModal` — a component originally built for paid renewals worked verbatim for trial-to-paid conversion because the underlying `/api/user/hosting/renew` endpoint had already been designed to accept both cases.

- [x] **ADMIN-ORDERS-TRIAL-VISIBLE: `/admin/order-management` now surfaces trial + Tokens-CIT signup Orders that were previously hidden by a blanket `status='pending'` exclusion; adds amber TRIAL pill on each row for at-a-glance triage** — Operator asked "is our trial orders not recorded in orders at all? if not why?" after noticing that fresh trial signups (which DO create Order documents in MongoDB via [`app/api/payments/create-order/route.ts:282-312`](app/api/payments/create-order/route.ts) — `amount=0`, `status='pending'`, `orderType='hosting_trial'`, `mandateMode='manual'`) were never visible on the admin Orders dashboard. **Diagnosis**: the trial Orders ARE persisted correctly; they were being filtered out by [`lib/services/orders.ts:343`](lib/services/orders.ts) — `if (!opts.includePending) baseQuery.status = { $ne: "pending" }`. That filter was written under a pre-manual-flow-launch assumption that `pending == bailed-checkout` (customer-abandoned intents cluttering the admin view). The manual-flow trial launch (dms-00210 on 2026-06-29) silently invalidated that assumption — trial Orders live in `status='pending'` from signup until day-15+ conversion, at which point the RENEWAL creates a new Order with the real amount + the trial Order stays as audit trail. So every fresh trial signup was invisible to operators unless they queried MongoDB directly. **Fix** (`2701e0d`, deployed as `dms-00241-gsn`): three coordinated changes. **(1)** `lib/services/orders.ts` — re-shaped the `listOrdersForAdmin` filter from a blanket `status: { $ne: "pending" }` to a `$or` that keeps stale bailed-checkout intents hidden while surfacing legit trial + Tokens-CIT signups: `$or: [{ status: { $ne: "pending" } }, { status: "pending", $or: [{ orderType: "hosting_trial" }, { mandateMode: { $in: ["manual", "tokens"] } }] }]`. Two selectors under the pending arm because both are load-bearing — `orderType='hosting_trial'` catches manual-flow + Tokens-CIT trial signups directly, `mandateMode IN ('manual','tokens')` catches any future non-trial recurring-mandate signup that lands in pending as a legit state (defence-in-depth). Old bailed-checkout Orders (status='pending' + no marker) stay hidden — no regression on that front. Extensive comment block above the filter documents the intent + cites the 2026-06-29 date when the old assumption broke, so a future reviewer knows why the shape is what it is. **(2)** `app/admin/order-management/page.tsx` — amber TRIAL pill added next to the Order ID on rows where `orderType === 'hosting_trial'`. Same amber-100 palette as the `/admin/hosting` TRIAL pill from `5f93f85` for visual consistency across admin surfaces. Tooltip on the pill distinguishes manual-flow ("no charge today; first invoice fires at day 15+ conversion via the renewal flow") vs Tokens-flow ("₹2 CIT-auth charge already refunded; first MIT charge fires at day 15+") so operators triaging a trial know exactly what to expect at the day-15 mark without opening the detail modal. Also extended the Order interface's `mandateMode` union to include `'manual'` alongside `'subscription' | 'tokens'` (previously missing — likely a TypeScript oversight from the manual-flow launch batch that didn't cause runtime issues because the DB accepted the value fine, but would have shown as a type error on any code path that narrowed the union). New `orderType?: 'hosting_trial' | string` field also added to the interface so the pill's conditional render is type-safe. **(3)** `tests/unit/lib/services/orders-readers.test.ts` — the pre-existing "excludes pending (default)" test at line 331 expected `filter.status: { $ne: "pending" }` at the top level, which would have broken after the shape change. Rewrote to pin the NEW `$or` shape (both branches, the trial-marker sub-`$or` containing both `orderType` AND `mandateMode` selectors) AND added a defensive assertion that the OLD blanket `filter.status` MUST NOT be at the top level — if a future refactor accidentally reintroduces it alongside the `$or`, MongoDB's implicit `$and` semantics would AND them together and re-hide the trial rows. Also updated the `includePending:true` test to also pin that `$or` is unset in that path. Full suite: 39/39 pass. **Production**: deployed at 11:51Z on 2026-07-02; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (30th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 12th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); health 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-visible impact**: `/admin/order-management` now shows every trial signup from the launch cutover onwards — the karmaastar / rajus2cbb5 / testing-domain retries + any real customer signups going forward. Rows carry the amber TRIAL pill so an operator scanning the list can triage at a glance. **Where trial signups DO already surface** (context for the reviewer): the operator was NOT completely blind to trials — they were visible at `/admin/hosting` (with the same amber TRIAL pill from `5f93f85`) AND `/admin/renewals` (via [`app/api/admin/renewals/route.ts`](app/api/admin/renewals/route.ts)). But `/admin/order-management` is the canonical "who paid what when" view + the reviewer dashboard consumes its data for the day/week/month rollup metrics, so a missing category there is a real gap. This fix closes it. **Meta-lesson banked (2nd of the day)**: filters written under a specific assumption about what a status value means (`pending == bailed checkout`) turn into landmines when a NEW code path introduces a legit reason for that same status. This is the second such landmine caught this session — the first was the integration-health providerMap crash where a category-mapping table had entries with no matching consumer. Pattern: whenever a status field / enum / mapping table gains a new legitimate value, grep for every consumer that made assumptions about the OLD value set + audit them for the new value. Add tests that pin BOTH the new behavior AND that the old blanket-assumption code doesn't creep back in via refactor. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this closes the last operator-visible blind spot in the trial-signup surface. The trial launch (dms-00210 → dms-00232) landed on 2026-06-29 → 2026-07-02, and this row means the operational triage surface for those signups is now complete: `/admin/hosting` shows the DA-side status, `/admin/order-management` shows the payment-side state, `/admin/renewals` shows the conversion pipeline, and `/admin/integration-health` (from dms-00240-lfd earlier this session) shows any internal errors from the same flow. Four surfaces, complete coverage.

- [x] **INTEGRATION-HEALTH-APPLICATION-SURFACE + LATENT-CRASH-FIX: `/admin/integration-health` now has an "Application" provider card + hint signatures for the common internal-code 5xx shapes; a latent crash where any `service: 'api'` SystemLog entry would silently 500 the dashboard is closed** — Operator asked why the 7-layer manual-flow-trial chain (dms-00222 → dms-00232, ~15 customer-facing 500s across 2026-06-30 → 2026-07-02) never showed up on Integration Health. **Diagnosis** — three overlapping reasons: **(1) Scope mismatch**: Integration Health is designed for UPSTREAM providers (DA/Zoho/RC/Razorpay/SMTP). The trial-chain bugs were internal — Mongoose enum ValidatorError, case-mismatch DB lookups returning null, missing schema fields, silent try/catch swallows. From the dashboard's perspective, no upstream service returned an error (Razorpay was never called, Zoho was never called). **(2) Timing**: the retries happened 2026-06-30 05:04Z → 2026-07-02 04:56Z; the just-flipped default view (Last 24h from `dms-00239-vhd` earlier today) reaches back to ~12:33Z 2026-07-01, so only ~7 hours of that failure window is in scope + post-fix there were no more failures. **(3) Latent bug in the route**: `serverLogger.error(...)` calls DO reach SystemLog with `service: 'api'` (via `remoteLog → POST /api/v1/admin/log-error → SystemLog`). The route's `SERVICE_TO_PROVIDER` map at [route.ts:73](app/api/admin/integration-health/route.ts) maps `api → application` — but `application` was NEVER seeded in `providerMap` at line 569-572. The subsequent `providerMap.get(b.provider)!.totalErrors += b.count` at line 575-576 would throw a TypeError swallowed by the outer catch as a generic "Failed to load integration health" 500. Meaning even if the trial-chain 5xx were IN scope, the dashboard would crash trying to render them. Rendering was working ONLY because no such SystemLog entries existed in the current window — the dashboard was one bad entry away from a hard failure. **Fix** (`8ba4583`, deployed as `dms-00240-lfd`): three coordinated changes in [`app/api/admin/integration-health/route.ts`](app/api/admin/integration-health/route.ts). **(a)** New `application` entry in the `PROVIDERS[]` array with FIVE signatures for the shapes observed during the trial chain + adjacent incidents: (i) `ValidatorError|validation failed|is not a valid enum value|Cast to \w+ failed` (the ORDER-MANDATEMODE-MANUAL shape from dms-00227) — hint reminds operators to grep ALL callers + schemas when adding a new enum value; (ii) `Failed to (generate payment targets|create payment order)` (the outer razorpay-catch fall-through) — hint points at `[CREATE-ORDER]` prefix search + names the specific latent-error patterns; (iii) `Cannot read propert(y|ies) of (undefined|null)|is not a function|TypeError:` — hint calls out the "same-shape across multiple requests = regression" heuristic; (iv) `\[CREATE-ORDER\]|\[CART\]|\[CHECKOUT\]|\[TRIAL-|\[PROVISIONER\]` — catches any tagged internal error that doesn't match a more specific signature; (v) `buffering timed out|MongooseError|connection.*closed|MongoServerError` (PROVISIONER-CONNECTDB-COLD-START-FIX from dms-00235) — hint identifies the cold-start pattern + points at `await connectDB()`. Each signature carries a hint that names the fix path + cites the past incident so operators recognize the shape. **(b)** New `hintForProvider(text, providerId)` helper — when `log.service` has already told us the provider bucket, look up the hint from ONLY that provider's signatures. Avoids the razorpay-generic signature (`/razorpay|BAD_REQUEST_ERROR|...`) stealing an application-code error just because the string "Razorpay" appears in the message (was the exact shape of a test that caught this — "Razorpay order creation failed: Failed to generate payment targets" was routing to razorpay's generic hint instead of application's targeted "no-payment-target throw" hint). **(c)** Latent crash fix at [route.ts:575](app/api/admin/integration-health/route.ts) — `providerMap.get(b.provider)!.totalErrors += b.count` (non-null assertion + runtime landmine) replaced with `providerMap.get(b.provider) ?? providerMap.get("unknown")!.totalErrors += b.count`. Any bucket with an unseeded provider id now routes to `unknown` instead of throwing. Defensive fix for future SERVICE_TO_PROVIDER changes — adding a new mapping without a matching PROVIDERS[] entry would silently break the endpoint under the pre-fix code. **Tests**: new `Application provider` describe block adds 5 pins — SystemLog with `service='api'` classifies to application card without crashing; `[CREATE-ORDER]` prefixed error clusters under application with the create-order hint (specifically pins that the more targeted hint wins over razorpay-generic); MongooseError buffering timeout gets the cold-start hint; application always in response (card renders even at 0 errors so operators see the category exists); unseeded provider id falls back to `unknown` without crashing. Suite: 10/10 pass. **Production**: deployed at 08:00Z on 2026-07-02; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (29th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 11th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); health 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Retroactive utility**: because SystemLog retains 500 entries by default, once the operator visits `/admin/integration-health` with a longer window (7d/30d) they'll now see the trial-chain 500s clustered under the Application card with the targeted hints — retroactively useful for confirming the class of bug has stopped firing since the chain closed 04:56Z on 2026-07-02. **Meta-lesson banked**: whenever a categorized-observability surface (integration health, error monitoring, structured logging) has a category-mapping table (`SERVICE_TO_PROVIDER` here), audit the receiver (`providerMap` roll-up here) for an entry PER key the mapping can produce. A category producer with no matching consumer is a runtime landmine — the code compiles fine (the TS `Record` type doesn't enforce the ProviderId enum against the consumer's key set), and the failure mode is a silent 500 that looks identical to "actual bug" to operators. Adding a defensive fallback (`??` at the get() site) prevents the class entirely regardless of future mapping additions. **Worth recording explicitly because**: the operator's "why didn't X show up?" question turned out to be TWO different reasons (scope + latent bug) + only ONE was fixable by a code change — but the fixable one had been sitting there for at least a week (since the last integration-health commit) as a hard-fail-on-first-application-error time bomb. The fix is small (net +80 lines including tests) but the discovery pattern generalises: any "why doesn't this show data I expect?" operator report deserves an audit of both the scope contract AND the routing/rendering pipeline for silent-failure modes. Also — the "Application" surface itself is a modest but permanent quality-of-life win: any future internal 500 shape becomes triage-able from the dashboard without needing Cloud Logging access.

- [x] **INTEGRATION-HEALTH-DEFAULT-WINDOW: `/admin/integration-health` initial view now shows "Last 24h" instead of "Last 30 days"** — Operator screenshotted the page on a healthy state ("All upstream providers are healthy — no failures in the last 1 day") and pointed out the initial load should default to 24h. Current shape: 30d was baked into `useState(30)` at `app/admin/integration-health/page.tsx:73` — useful as a "any recurring pattern?" retrospective but on first load it dilutes the "is anything on fire right now?" signal because an error from 3 weeks ago sits alongside a fresh error and both look equally alarming until the operator narrows. **Fix** (`b01a628`, deployed as `dms-00239-vhd`): one-line flip `useState(30)` → `useState(1)`. Dropdown options (1/7/30/90) unchanged so the older windows are one click away — operator can still hop to 30d for a retrospective on demand, but the first-paint view is now the "does anything need action right now?" slice. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes — the page's window selector isn't pin-tested and the semantic change is purely which numeric literal seeds the useState. **Production**: deployed at 07:05Z on 2026-07-02; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (28th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 10th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); health 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Customer-visible impact**: zero — this is an admin-only surface. **Operator-visible impact**: `/admin/integration-health` first-paint now shows the recent-24h slice + the summary banner reads "in the last 1 day"; existing operators expecting the 30-day view will notice the change on their next visit and can flip the dropdown back to 30d if needed (bookmarks / URL don't carry state — the dropdown value is local component state, so it doesn't persist across page loads regardless of default). **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is a small but meaningful UX signal — the default view of a monitoring dashboard should answer the operator's most-common question first ("what's broken right now?") rather than their occasional-retrospective question ("has this been happening?"). Small change, one-line diff, but the shape of the ask is generalisable: for any dashboard with a time-window selector, the shortest window (or "live" / "last hour") is usually the correct default because it maximises actionable signal per pixel on first load.

- [x] **ADMIN-ACTIONS-CRON-AUTH: `/api/v1/admin/hosting/actions` now accepts `x-cron-secret` header as an alternative to admin session cookie; `scripts/purge-test-users.js` rewired to route DA deletes through the production endpoint instead of calling DA directly — closes the class of "purge script silently orphans DA accounts because operator IP isn't whitelisted" bugs AND corrects the 2026-06-30 `pw` permission-boundary misdiagnosis** — Operator retried the admin-hosting Delete button via a DevTools fetch through the production `/api/v1/admin/hosting/actions` endpoint on 2026-07-02 and got a clean `{status:200, body:{success:true, message:"Action 'delete' completed successfully for user rajus2cbb5", data:{outcome:"deleted"}}}`. This directly contradicted my 2026-06-30 diagnosis for the `tryprd941e` orphan, where I told the operator the `pw` reseller credentials had a permission boundary preventing DA deletes — turned out the delete works fine when the request originates from Cloud Run's whitelisted NAT IP `34.14.59.128`. **Root cause reframe**: DA has 4 filter layers (hosting-provider firewall + CSF + BFM skip + login-key Allow Networks — see auto-memory `project_da_whitelist_layers`). Only Cloud Run's egress IP is whitelisted at all 4 layers; operator laptops aren't. So ANY direct DA API call from local returns HTTP 401 "Not logged in" regardless of endpoint or credentials. That's what the `purge-test-users.js` script was hitting when its `daDeleteUser(username)` step "failed with a permission error" — the script was orphaning DA accounts on every run. **Fix** (`9ff614c`, deployed as `dms-00238-bkt`): two coordinated changes. (1) `app/api/admin/hosting/actions/route.ts` — auth path extended from single-check `AuthService.isAdmin` to dual-check `authorizeCronRequest || AuthService.isAdmin`. Same pattern the `/api/workers/*` + `/api/cron/*` routes already use (they call `authorizeCronRequest` first + fall through to admin session if the header's absent). New docstring block on the POST handler explains the dual-auth rationale + cross-references `[[project_da_whitelist_layers]]`. (2) `scripts/purge-test-users.js` — the direct `CMD_API_SELECT_USERS` fetch that used to build a Basic-auth-header POST to the raw DA endpoint is now replaced with a JSON POST to `https://app.anutech.in/api/v1/admin/hosting/actions` carrying the `x-cron-secret` header. Same outcome dispatch as the admin UI's Delete button — matches on `outcome === 'deleted'` / `outcome === 'user_not_found'` (both clean successes for a scrub script) and surfaces `warning` field verbatim for `da_unreachable` / `hard_failure`. Script docstring's cascade-order comment rewritten to reflect the new step-3 shape (routed through prod endpoint; NB: local Hosting row + PendingHostings + Razorpay-sub-cancel + User.directAdminUsername all cleaned server-side, so the script's later cascade steps run idempotently against already-cleaned data). CRON_SECRET fetch instructions embedded in the error message when the env var isn't set: `gcloud secrets versions access latest --secret=CRON_SECRET --project=speedy-unison-453807-e9`. **Tests** (`tests/unit/app/api/admin/hosting/actions/route.test.ts`): grow by 2 pins. (a) cron-secret valid + admin session absent → 200 (verifies `isAdmin.mockResolvedValueOnce(false)` isn't consulted, since cron path short-circuits). (b) cron-secret invalid + admin session absent → 403 FORBIDDEN (defence-in-depth pin — the fallback still enforces admin). Full suite: 27/27 pass. **Production**: deployed at 06:57Z on 2026-07-02; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (27th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 9th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); health 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Immediate operator-visible impact**: `node scripts/purge-test-users.js --apply <email>` runs on a laptop now actually deletes the DA account instead of silently orphaning it.
