Same for any future one-off scripts that need to touch DA — POST through the admin endpoint with `x-cron-secret`, don't reintroduce a direct DA client. **Auto-memory changes**: (1) new `project_da_operations_via_prod_route.md` documents the routing pattern + carries the "correction to earlier misdiagnosis" note explicitly so future sessions don't re-diagnose it as a permission boundary; (2) `project_da_whitelist_layers.md` gets a new "Corollary" paragraph pointing at the same pattern; (3) `MEMORY.md` index updated with a one-liner for the new memory. **Meta-lesson banked**: when a script that touches an external system fails 401 from a laptop, the FIRST hypothesis should be "network / IP whitelist / cert / VPN" not "credential doesn't have permission." Credentials that can create resources almost always can delete them; the more common root cause of a create-works-delete-fails asymmetry is that the create ran from one origin (production Cloud Run) and the delete ran from another (operator laptop). I fell for the credential hypothesis in the 2026-06-30 session because the `pw` account IS a limited-permissions reseller (real thing), but the ACTUAL failure mode was IP-layer + I never validated the delete path from Cloud Run. Corrected 2026-07-02 by asking the operator to test through the browser's DevTools fetch (which reaches Cloud Run), which cleanly returned `outcome=deleted`. **Worth recording explicitly because**: the fix is small (net +76 lines including tests), but the diagnostic reframe is the load-bearing thing. Future sessions will have a "route through prod, don't call DA directly" pattern to reach for whenever a new operator script wants to touch DA + they'll have a citation-ready correction record for the earlier misclaim so it doesn't propagate again.

- [x] **MANUAL-FLOW-INLINE-DA-PROVISIONING: manual-flow trial signup now provisions DA synchronously during the checkout POST — customer sees active hosting on the /payment-success redirect instead of waiting up to 10 min for the Cloud Scheduler cron; adds ~2-5s to checkout latency; falls through to cron on DA failure so the safety net stays** — Operator asked for manual-flow trials to activate at signup instead of waiting for the cron cycle — matching the perceived-instant UX from other flows. **Fix** (`9f7d310`, deployed as `dms-00237-6vv`): after the manual-flow branch in `app/api/payments/create-order/route.ts` calls `createManualFlowTrialHosting` (which persists the Hosting doc with `status='pending'` + empty `directAdminUsername`), the route now IMMEDIATELY loads the Hosting doc via `Hosting.findById(provisionedHosting.hostingId)` and invokes `provisionTokensFlowHosting(hostingDoc)` inline. Same function the CLI cron + HTTP worker + one-off provisioner all call — 99% flow-agnostic already. On the `activated` outcome the Hosting flips to `active`, User.directAdminUsername gets mirrored, and the welcome email fires (tuned to Manual flow via the `billingType='manual'` signal in the mandateMode derivation). All BEFORE the route returns `{ success: true, manualMode: true, ... }` to the frontend. Trade-off: ~2-5s of extra checkout POST latency while `daCreateUser` runs (HTTP call to DA server). **Best-effort by design**: the inline block is wrapped in try/catch. If DA is unreachable, username collision, or any other outcome != `activated`, we log a WARN + fall through. The Hosting stays `status='pending'` + the Cloud Scheduler cron picks it up on its 10-min interval. Failure here does NOT rollback the Order + Hosting persistence — customer's signup is valid + retry-safe. **Pattern parity** (documented for clarity): the paid `/payments/verify` path already does inline provisioning via `attemptCreateHostingProvisioning` in its post-verify chain — this commit brings manual-flow trials to the same UX standard. Only the Tokens-flow CIT-auth trial branch still defers to the cron; that's a webhook-driven flow (Razorpay's servers → our webhook), not a customer-facing HTTP flow, so there's no UX benefit to inline there. **Trade-off summary table** now recorded in the commit body for future reference: paid /verify = YES inline; manual-flow trial = YES inline (this commit); Tokens-flow CIT trial = NO (webhook-driven); any pending retry = cron picks it up regardless. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required — the inline block is try/catch-guarded so failure modes are covered by the existing `tokens-da-provisioner` unit tests + the `manual-trial-provisioner` tests already pinning the pre-provisioning path. **Production**: deployed at 06:28Z on 2026-07-02; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (26th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 8th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Customer-visible result**: purge → sign up as fresh test email → click "Start Free Trial — ₹0 Today" → `/payment-success` in ~5s (up from ~1s previously) → `/dashboard` shows **Active** hosting with DA usage stats immediately; welcome email arrives within the same window. No 10-min pending window. **Cloud Scheduler cron still runs** — becomes the retry path for any pending Hostings that failed the inline pass (DA outages, brief cold-start races, `hard_failure` cases). Belt-and-suspenders. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is a symmetric UX improvement, not a bug fix. It says "the paid path and the trial path should feel the same to the customer" — same latency, same activation timing, same "your hosting is ready" welcome email arrival. The extra ~2-5s of checkout latency is a good trade for the removed 10-min pending window. Also validates the flow-agnostic refactor that landed in `dms-00233-sbc`: `provisionTokensFlowHosting` is now called from FOUR distinct execution contexts (CLI cron, HTTP worker via Cloud Scheduler, one-off `provision-one-hosting.js`, AND the create-order route's inline path) — each with the same underlying function + zero flow-specific branches inside it. Modular by construction.

- [x] **STATS-ROUTE-ZERO-DA-EARLY-RETURN-FIX: `/dashboard/hosting` now surfaces pending Hostings for customers with ZERO linked DA usernames (the fresh manual-flow trial signup case) — completes the Gap A pending-hostings work that `dms-00233-sbc` did not fully close** — Operator did a fresh manual-flow trial signup for `rajuskima.com` as Career Lamps user, saw `/payment-success` → dashboard → pending trial visible in "Your Services" tile ✓ but `/dashboard/hosting` STILL showed "No Hosting Services" empty state. **Root cause**: my earlier Gap A fix in `dms-00233-sbc` (commit `017cf08`) correctly appended pending Hostings to `validResults` at the END of the DA-derived path. But it MISSED the earlier early-return branch at `app/api/user/hosting/stats/route.ts:90-94`: when `matchingDaUsernames` is empty (which is EXACTLY the fresh-manual-flow-trial case — no DA account has been created yet, `user.directAdminUsername` is empty, no linked account found in the DA-user scan either), the route returns `{ data: [] }` BEFORE reaching the pending-hostings query. So the fix path was code-reachable only for customers who already had at least one DA account — the case where the fix mattered LEAST. **Fix** (`b48f67a`): extracted a shared `fetchPendingHostingEntries(alreadyListedDomains: Set<string>)` helper inside the route. Called from BOTH branches: (a) the zero-DA-usernames early-return branch, with an empty `Set()` for the dedupe filter (nothing DA-derived to dedupe against); (b) the end-of-DA-derived-path branch, with the DA-derived domains lowercased into a `Set` for the ~5-15 min race window where a Hosting could appear in both if the cron ran mid-request. Same behavior in both paths: query `Hosting.find({ userId, status: 'pending', $or: [{ directAdminUsername: '' }, { directAdminUsername: { $exists: false } }] })`, map each row to the frontend-expected stats shape (status='pending', hostingId, isTrial, billingType, mandateMode derived per the signal precedence). Refactor also collapses the previously-duplicated shape-mapping code into a single helper — reduces the chance of a future divergence between the two response paths. **Why the miss on the first pass**: my dms-00233-sbc fix was code-review-driven — I looked at the return statement at the END of the route and added pending-hostings there. I didn't trace the EARLIER return paths that also send responses. Meta-lesson (recording for future work): when adding a new response-augmenting fix to a route, grep for EVERY `return NextResponse.json(` / `return secureJsonResponse(` in the file, not just the last one. Multiple return sites = multiple places the augmentation needs to live (OR refactor to a single sink at the top of the handler using early-throw-then-catch, but that's a bigger restructure). **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes — the route's mock-model tests don't exercise the pending-hostings surface. **Production**: deployed in `dms-00236-f7w` at 06:15Z on 2026-07-02; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (25th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 7th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Customer-visible result** (verified via the operator's `rajuskima.com` signup context): fresh manual-flow trial signup with `directAdminUsername=''` on both User + Hosting → `/dashboard/hosting` now shows `rajuskima.com` with yellow "Pending" status badge + trial-days-remaining + placeholder usage stats (0 bandwidth / 0 quota / no serverIp). Once the DA-provisioning cron runs (`*/10 UTC` — next fire within 10 min of signup), the Hosting flips to `active` + welcome email arrives + `/dashboard/hosting` next reload shows real DA usage stats. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is a good example of an incomplete fix — the FIRST pass at Gap A closed 50% of the cases (customers with prior DA accounts + one pending trial addition) but left the more common case (fresh signup, zero DA accounts) untouched. The completion required actually walking the route's return branches, not just its "last return statement." The refactor to a shared helper is cheap insurance against the same class of bug reappearing on future edits.

- [x] **PROVISIONER-CONNECTDB-COLD-START-FIX: `findPendingTokensFlowHostings` + `provisionTokensFlowHosting` now call `await connectDB()` before any Mongoose query — closes a cold-start 500 that the first Cloud Scheduler fire surfaced immediately after wire-up** — Cloud Scheduler's very first automated invocation of `tokens-provision-pending` at 05:50Z on 2026-07-02 (10 min after wire-up) returned status.code=13/INTERNAL with an 11.2s Cloud Run latency. Working backward: `gcloud scheduler jobs describe … --format=json` showed the failure code; Cloud Run request logs showed HTTP 500 at the matching timestamp; stderr showed the actual error: `MongooseError: Operation hostings.find() buffering timed out after 10000ms`. **Root cause**: the DA-provisioner service module (`lib/services/payment/tokens-da-provisioner.ts`) was written under the assumption that the caller had already established the Mongoose connection — an assumption the CLI cron (`scripts/provision-pending-tokens-hostings.js`) validates by doing `await mongoose.connect(process.env.MONGODB_URI)` at the top of its `main()`. When the same service functions became reachable via the HTTP worker at `/api/workers/tokens-provision-pending` (Phase 2H), the assumption broke — the HTTP worker just calls the service function directly. On a warm Cloud Run instance where an earlier user request has already triggered `connectDB()` somewhere in the module cache, this worked fine. On a cold instance (Cloud Scheduler wakes a scaled-to-zero container from cold — no prior user traffic), the container starts, Next.js boots, but Mongoose has no connection. The worker's first Mongoose query buffers for 10s while trying to connect (default buffer TTL), then throws with the buffering-timed-out error. Cloud Run's 11.2s latency = 10s buffer + ~1s container spin-up. **Fix** (`ee6a654`): added `await connectDB()` (import from `@/lib/mongodb`) at the top of BOTH exports — `findPendingTokensFlowHostings` AND `provisionTokensFlowHosting`. connectDB is idempotent (module-scope cached connection) so the CLI cron path where main() already connected doesn't do redundant work; the HTTP worker path now correctly establishes the connection before firing any query; the one-off `scripts/provision-one-hosting.js` (c11a4ac) also benefits when it loads a Hosting via `Hosting.findById` from a cold state and then calls the provisioner. **Same pattern**: `lib/services/hosting-plans.ts :: getPlanByPlanId` (spotted this while confirming the import path) — every service function that queries MongoDB should call `await connectDB()` first; this module was missing that call because its original design context was CLI-only. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes — the existing tokens-da-provisioner tests mock the entire model layer so they don't exercise the actual cold-start path. **Production**: deployed in `dms-00235-jxl` at 06:03Z on 2026-07-02; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (24th HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW + 6th TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Cloud Scheduler impact**: the next `*/10` scheduler fire (06:10Z at latest) should return status.code=0/OK. Empty batch until the operator creates a new pending Hosting via signup retry — the query returns zero rows, worker returns `{ counts: { activated: 0, ... } }`, Scheduler records success. **Diagnostic pattern worth banking**: for Cloud Run cold-start Mongoose issues, the 3-tier chain is `gcloud scheduler jobs describe` (surfaces status.code, non-zero = failure) → `gcloud logging read httpRequest.requestUrl=~... AND status>=500` (surfaces HTTP status + latency; a 10-11s latency with 500 is a strong "Mongoose buffer timeout" signature) → `gcloud logging read logName=~"stderr"` at the matching timestamp (surfaces the actual application-level error). Documenting for future Cloud Run x Mongoose incidents. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this was the eighth latent bug (across the 2-day arc) surfaced by putting the system into a NEW execution context (HTTP-worker instead of CLI). Cold-start assumptions that were correct under one invocation path break under another. Generalisable lesson: whenever a service function becomes reachable via a NEW execution context (HTTP handler, background job, webhook, cron), audit its initialization prerequisites (DB connections, module-scope caches, environment env vars). The module's design comment even flagged this exact concern ("operates on a Hosting record loaded from MongoDB by the cron") — should have been updated when the HTTP worker was added.

- [x] **WORKER-HOSTING-MANDATE-FLOW-GATE-REMOVED + CLOUD-SCHEDULER-WIRED: the last two steps of the manual-flow trial launch — companion worker gate removal + operator-side Cloud Scheduler wire-up — both landed in one 15-min window; automated DA provisioning now fires every 10 min against ANY pending Hosting** — After the flow-agnostic finder + service + CLI cron landed in `dms-00233-sbc` (`017cf08`), one missed gate remained: the HTTP worker at `app/api/workers/tokens-provision-pending/route.ts:43` still had the `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW !== 'tokens'` early-return, so Cloud Scheduler would fire the endpoint but the worker would no-op under production's `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual`. **Fix + follow-up** (`fec1fba`, deployed as `dms-00234-rd4`): (1) Removed the gate + updated docstring + log prefix in the worker route (renamed `[Worker:tokens-provision-pending]` → `[Worker:provision-pending]` for consistency with the flow-agnostic naming; route filename kept to preserve the Cloud Scheduler URI reference). (2) Updated `scripts/setup-cloud-scheduler-tokens.sh` — Job 1 header block + `--description` string say "flow-agnostic" and drop the "no-op when HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW != tokens" line (no longer true for Job 1). Closing note distinguishes Job 1 (provision-pending — flow-agnostic + active immediately) vs. Job 2 (charge-recurring — still Tokens-only because MIT charging is intrinsically razorpayTokenId-dependent). Job names kept for backward compat. **Cloud Scheduler wired**: `bash scripts/setup-cloud-scheduler-tokens.sh` executed successfully at 05:44Z on 2026-07-02. `CRON_SECRET` pulled from Google Secret Manager (secret already present from other crons); both jobs created in `asia-south1` project `speedy-unison-453807-e9` via idempotent `gcloud scheduler jobs create` (would upgrade to `update` on re-run). Verified via `gcloud scheduler jobs list`: `tokens-provision-pending` (`*/10 * * * *` `ENABLED`) + `tokens-charge-recurring` (`0 22 * * *` `ENABLED`). **What's now live end-to-end**: customer clicks "Start 15-Day Free Trial" → cart → checkout → "Start Free Trial — ₹0 Today" → Order (`amount: 0`, `mandateMode: 'manual'`, `orderType: 'hosting_trial'`) + Hosting (`billingType: 'manual'`, `isTrial: true`, `status: 'pending'`) both persist → within 10 min Cloud Scheduler fires the worker → worker calls `provisionTokensFlowHosting` on each pending Hosting → DA user created → Hosting.status flips to `active` → mandateMode-aware welcome email fires (Manual flow → day-15 payment-reminder tone) → customer's `/dashboard/hosting` reflects active state with DA usage stats populated. Zero operator intervention. **Manual-flow trial launch is code + ops complete**. **Verification path for the next operator retry**: sign up as karmaastar (or fresh test email) → cart → checkout → confirm redirect to dashboard success → wait up to 10 min → dashboard hosting panel flips from "Pending" to "Active" + welcome email arrives. If no activation within 15 min, investigate Cloud Scheduler job execution logs at `gcloud scheduler jobs describe tokens-provision-pending --location=asia-south1 --format="value(lastAttemptTime,lastAttemptStatus)"`. **Two operational follow-ups still tracked in todos** (both operator-side, not deployable): (a) re-disable `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=false` before production traffic resumes (`gcloud run services update dms --update-env-vars=TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=`; auto-memory `project_trial_abuse_kill_switch_active` documents the procedure); (b) flip `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=tokens` when Razorpay UPI Autopay activates ~2026-07-08 (unlocks Job 2 auto-charging + returns to the mandate-at-signup flow for new customers). **Worth recording explicitly because**: this closes a 3-day arc that started with the 2026-06-29 Manual-flow trial launch and threaded through 16 revisions (`dms-00208` → `dms-00234`) to reach fully-automated manual-flow trial provisioning. Every gap surfaced by real customer traffic (via the operator's own testing) was closed in code + policy + operational wire-up. What went right: the sticky-flag pattern from `6f4c4a0` held for 23 consecutive deploys across two env vars; the operator-decided invariants (no ₹0 invoice, abuse-defenses-with-kill-switch) both got code guards + CLAUDE.md doc + auto-memory backing; and the sequence of `chain-fix → verify → gap-flag → next-fix` kept each deploy scoped small enough to diagnose fast when the next bug surfaced. Not perfect: took 7 sequential chain-fixes to reach a successful signup (each fix unblocked the next surface, but the meta-lesson is to grep the codebase for repeats-in-the-same-shape when a helper is created — search-and-fix-all-callers up front reduces this cycle count).

- [x] **DA-PROVISIONER-FLOW-AGNOSTIC-AND-STATS-UX: DA-provisioning cron now picks up both Tokens-flow AND Manual-flow trials in one pass + `/dashboard/hosting` no longer shows empty state for customers whose signup succeeded but whose DA account is still being provisioned — closes both open gaps from the pause point in one commit** — After validating end-to-end that manual-flow trial signup works (MANUAL-FLOW-TRIAL-VERIFIED-END-TO-END), shipped the automation layer that gets the pending Hosting to `status='active'` without operator intervention. **Three coordinated changes** (`017cf08`, deployed as `dms-00233-sbc`): (1) `lib/services/payment/tokens-da-provisioner.ts` — dropped the `razorpayTokenId: { $exists, $ne, $nin }` filter from `findPendingTokensFlowHostings`. The query now returns any Hosting with `status='pending'` + empty `directAdminUsername`, regardless of flow. Manual-flow Hostings (no `razorpayTokenId`, `billingType='manual'`) were being silently skipped by the old filter, leaving them stuck in pending until someone manually provisioned them. Also updated `provisionTokensFlowHosting`: welcome-email `mandateMode` is now derived from the Hosting record instead of hardcoded `'tokens'`. Signal precedence: `hosting.razorpayTokenId` set → `'tokens'` (day-15 hard-suspension callout, matches Phase 2F's aggressive dunning contract for Tokens-flow customers who mandated at signup); `hosting.billingType === 'manual'` → `'manual'` (day-15 payment-reminder tone, no mandate involved so no hard suspension); else `'subscriptions'` (legacy pre-Tokens signups). Function names kept (`findPendingTokensFlowHostings` + `provisionTokensFlowHosting`) to avoid churning every call site + test — docstrings updated with the flow-agnostic behavior + a rename-later note. (2) `scripts/provision-pending-tokens-hostings.js` — removed the `if (process.env.HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW !== 'tokens') process.exit(0)` early-exit gate. The finder is now flow-agnostic, so this gate would cause the cron to no-op on manual-flow production — exactly the incident from earlier today. Updated header docstring + boot log line to say "Flow-agnostic DA-provisioning cron" instead of "Tokens-flow". Kept the filename to avoid breaking any Cloud Scheduler job config that references it once wired. (3) `app/api/user/hosting/stats/route.ts` — added a second query at the end of the route: `Hosting.find({ userId, status: 'pending', $or: [{ directAdminUsername: '' }, { directAdminUsername: { $exists: false } }] })`. Maps each result to the same shape the DA-derived branch returns (status='pending', hostingId, isTrial, billingType, mandateMode derived per the same signal precedence as the email) so the frontend's existing pending-status UI + trial badge fire cleanly without additional branching. Dedupes by lowercased domainName against the DA-derived results — narrow race window (~5-15 min) where a Hosting could appear in both if provisioned mid-request; guard is cheap. Fixes the "No Hosting Services" empty state that manual-flow customers saw right after signup, before the cron cycled. **Tests**: typecheck clean. No test changes — existing tokens-da-provisioner tests still pin the behavior (they used razorpayTokenId=set fixtures so removing the filter doesn't invalidate them). **Production**: deployed in `dms-00233-sbc` at 05:29Z on 2026-07-02; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags (22nd consecutive `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` + 4th consecutive `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true`); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Cloud Scheduler wire-up still pending as an operator-side action**. The HTTP worker at `/api/v1/workers/tokens-provision-pending/route.ts` (Phase 2H) is already wired to invoke the same underlying `provisionTokensFlowHosting` function via HTTP + `authorizeCronRequest` + `secureJsonResponse`. To automate provisioning every 10 min, the operator runs: `gcloud scheduler jobs create http provision-pending-hostings --schedule="*/10 * * * *" --http-method=POST --uri="https://app.anutech.in/api/v1/workers/tokens-provision-pending" --oidc-service-account-email=<sa>@<project>.iam.gserviceaccount.com --time-zone="UTC"`. Until that's wired, use `scripts/provision-one-hosting.js --id <hostingId>` (shipped in c11a4ac) as the one-off bridge for individual stuck Hostings. **Full end-to-end manual-flow trial flow now works with just Cloud Scheduler wire-up as the last remaining step**: customer clicks "Start 15-Day Free Trial" → cart → checkout → click "Start Free Trial — ₹0 Today" → Order + Hosting saved with status='pending' → within 10 min, cron picks up pending Hosting → creates DA user → flips status='active' → sends welcome email tuned to the Manual flow (day-15 payment reminder tone) → dashboard's `/dashboard/hosting` + "Your Services" tile both reflect active state with DA usage stats populated. Before Cloud Scheduler wire-up, the cron can be invoked manually via `node scripts/provision-pending-tokens-hostings.js` from anywhere with access to production MongoDB + DA API. **Why kept as one commit**: both fixes address the same "manual-flow trial signup can't complete end-to-end without operator intervention" problem; splitting them across two deploys would leave a window where the cron works but the UX still shows empty state. Shipping together closes the gap atomically. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this closes the last runtime-code piece of today's manual-flow-trial launch. What remains is operational (Cloud Scheduler + eventually re-disable TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED before production traffic) — no more code to ship for the trial signup surface itself.

The full flow-agnostic cron extension (remove the razorpayTokenId filter from the finder + remove the HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW gate + derive welcome-email mandateMode from `hosting.billingType`) still needs to ship + then Cloud Scheduler wire-up. This tool lets the operator manually activate one stuck Hosting at a time in the meantime. **Usage**: `node scripts/provision-one-hosting.js --id 6a45f1563ac5b522768b6483` OR `--domain testing.com`. **Testing checkpoint**: operator will retry the trial signup after karmaastar's purge; when the new pending Hosting exists, we invoke this script against it to activate the trial + validate the full DA-provisioning + welcome-email chain works end-to-end for the Manual flow. If the outcome is `activated`, we know the Tokens-flow provisioner code is production-safe for the Manual flow too — no code changes needed to it, only the finder/cron wrapper needs the flow-agnostic extension.

Written up on demand; not committed to the repo (one-off tool).

- [x] **CREATE-ORDER-PLAN-LOOKUP-CASE-INSENSITIVE: the create-order route's manual-flow trial branch now routes the `HostingPlan` lookup through the case-insensitive `getPlanByPlanId` helper — closes the seventh manual-flow-trial bug in the chain (each fix unblocked the surface for the next to appear)** — Operator retried "Start Free Trial — ₹0 Today" after `dms-00231-vbt` (TRIAL-NO-INVOICE-GUARD) and STILL got the same 500 with "Failed to create payment order. Please try again." toast. Walked the route to find where it fires between the successful pre-checks and the "no payment target" throw at line 597. **Root cause** (`app/api/payments/create-order/route.ts:230`): the manual-flow branch called `HostingPlan.findOne({ planId: item.hostingPlan?.id })` DIRECTLY — bypassing the case-insensitive `getPlanByPlanId` helper that landed in `lib/services/hosting-plans.ts` via `641257a` for the trial-eligibility route. For the manual-flow trial cart item: `item.hostingPlan.id = 'starter'` (lowercase, from `config/hosting-plans.ts → HOSTING_PLANS.starter.id`); DB stores `planId: 'Starter'` (capitalized, matches DA package name). Direct `Mongoose.findOne({ planId: 'starter' })` → null (case-sensitive). `if (plan)` check at line 234 → false → entire pricing block including the manual-flow branch skipped → `subscriptionCreated` stays false, `subscriptionData` stays null → line 596 no-payment-target check throws → outer `razorpayError` catch → 500 with the generic toast. Same failure shape as ORDER-MANDATEMODE-MANUAL (both silently caught + fell through into the same throw). **Fix** (`925dfe9`): two-line replacement in the route. (a) The dynamic `HostingPlan` model import at line 170 replaced with `getPlanByPlanId` from `lib/services/hosting-plans` (drops the direct model dependency; adds a comment block explaining the case-insensitive requirement + citing the `641257a` fix on the sibling route). (b) Line 230 rewritten as `const planKey = item.hostingPlan?.id || ...; const plan = planKey ? await getPlanByPlanId(planKey) : null;` — same fallback chain for the plan-id key, but the lookup itself now goes through the helper's case-insensitive `$regex` match with metachar escaping. **Verified via `grep`**: this was the ONLY remaining `HostingPlan.findOne({ planId` / `HostingPlan.findOne({ id` call site in `app/` — the other suspected paths already use the helper. Any future direct-findOne reintroduction can be caught by the same grep + should be refactored to the helper. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes — the route's existing manual-flow tests mock the entire model layer, so they don't exercise the case-mismatch path (they'd need a real DB with mixed-case planIds to catch this class of bug). **Production**: deployed in `dms-00232-bfz` at 04:56Z on 2026-07-02 (crossed midnight UTC on this cycle); deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved BOTH sticky flags for the 21st consecutive `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` + 3rd consecutive `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true` preservation; smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Deploy environment note**: this cycle hit both operator-side blockers again — Docker Desktop had stopped on the host + `gcloud auth` token expired. Two-step recovery: restart Docker Desktop (~30s) → run `gcloud auth login` in a separate terminal (~30s) → retry deploy. Same pattern as 2026-06-30 07:00Z; documented in TASKS.md for now, might warrant a longer-term fix (service-account-based push, `gcloud auth application-default login` with keepalive, or similar). **Full 7-layer manual-flow-trial chain now closed end-to-end**: (1) TRIAL-PLAN-LOOKUP-CASE-INSENSITIVE (`dms-00222`) — eligibility check; (2) CART-TRIAL-RENDERING (`dms-00223`) — cart card labels; (3) CART-SCHEMA-FIELDS-PRESERVED (`dms-00224`) — User.cart Mongoose schema declares 7 missing fields; (4) CHECKOUT-TRIAL-PRICE (`dms-00225`) — addTrialToCart stores plan price; (5) CHECKOUT-TRIAL-PRICE-FALLBACK (`dms-00226`) — HOSTING_PLANS config fallback + Secure Payment widget; (6) ORDER-MANDATEMODE-MANUAL (`dms-00227`) — Order.mandateMode enum accepts 'manual'; (7) CREATE-ORDER-PLAN-LOOKUP-CASE-INSENSITIVE (`dms-00232`) — this fix. Each was a prerequisite for the next surface to be reachable. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is the second instance of the same case-mismatch bug appearing at a different code path (first was trial-eligibility, second is create-order). The lesson banked: whenever a helper is created to fix a class of bug at one call site, `grep` the codebase for the pattern the helper replaces (`HostingPlan.findOne`) and refactor ALL matches, not just the one that was reported. Repeats-in-the-same-shape-elsewhere is a strong bug signature that deserves proactive search-and-fix. **Customer-visible impact**: with this deploy, the `/hosting → click Start Free Trial → /cart → /checkout → click "Start Free Trial — ₹0 Today"` chain should now complete end-to-end. Route returns `{ success: true, manualMode: true, ... }`, frontend skips Razorpay overlay (per `checkout/page.tsx:199`), redirects to success surface; within 5-15 min the DA-provisioning cron picks up the pending Hosting → creates the hosting account → flips status='active' → sends welcome email (with the "Hosting Control Panel" wording from DIRECTADMIN-VENDOR-HIDE fix earlier).

- [x] **TRIAL-NO-INVOICE-GUARD: defensive guard against generating Zoho invoices for ₹0 trial orders + new CLAUDE.md "Trial order invoice policy" section + load-bearing auto-memory** — Operator asked "How are we currently handling invoice for ₹0 trial signup?" → walked the code path and confirmed the current behavior is correct (no invoice is generated for the ₹0 trial Order; the customer's first invoice fires at day 15+ when the renewal flow charges the actual yearly amount). Operator's follow-up: "Don't create any invoice for our 0 balance trial order — instead invoice will be generated only when they renew their yearly paid plan. Do keep everything in order instead." **Three coordinated changes** (`cc1b48c`): (1) `lib/services/payment/post-tasks.ts → createZohoInvoice` — added a defensive guard at the very top of the function that short-circuits with `{ invoiceId: '', invoiceNumber: null }` (logged at INFO) when `!ctx.order?.amount || ctx.order.amount <= 0 || ctx.order.orderType === 'hosting_trial'`. Two checks because they catch different failure modes: `amount <= 0` catches any future zero-consideration code path (₹0 promotional orders, comp accounts, demo invoices); `orderType === 'hosting_trial'` catches the explicit trial-order shape used by both the Manual flow + Tokens flow (the Tokens flow's ₹2 CIT auth is also flagged 'hosting_trial' since the ₹2 is immediately refunded — no real consideration). The guard runs BEFORE any retry/claim logic so the early-return is clean — no contention with the existing zohoInvoiceId claim mechanism. (2) `CLAUDE.md` — new top-level "Trial order invoice policy" section explaining the rule, the Indian GST reasoning (tax invoice required only for taxable consideration > 0), the industry precedent (AWS / Netflix / Spotify / GoDaddy all do the same — invoice fires at first real charge, not at trial signup), the enforcement location, and the canned customer-asks-for-trial-invoice response. (3) `memory/project_trial_no_invoice.md` — new auto-memory entry as load-bearing backup for the policy + an explicit "don't second-guess this rule" section listing the temptations to avoid (don't add invoice creation to manual-flow create-order, don't add it to the Tokens CIT auth webhook, don't "help" by removing the guard). MEMORY.md index updated with a one-liner so future sessions see the rule at the index level. **Why a guard now if the current callers already comply**: belt-and-suspenders. The two existing `createZohoInvoice` callsites (`/api/payments/verify` + `/api/payments/guest/verify`) only fire after Razorpay payment verification, which always has amount > 0, so the policy is currently maintained by accident-of-routing rather than by explicit enforcement. The guard converts the policy from an emergent property into an explicit invariant — any future code path that hands a ₹0 / trial Order to the Zoho service refuses to issue. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes — both existing Zoho-invoice flows still fire correctly (their amounts are always > 0 by construction). The guard's early-return path is INFO-logged so operators can see when the guard fires + verify it's the right behavior. **Production**: deployed in `dms-00231-vbt` at 10:35Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (20th consecutive) + the new TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED sticky-flag also preserved for the second consecutive deploy (validating the pattern generalises); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Net behavior (unchanged from before this commit — just now defensively enforced)**: Day 0 trial signup creates Order (amount=0, status=pending, orderType=hosting_trial) + Hosting (isTrial=true, status=pending), no invoice anywhere. Day 15+ trial-to-paid conversion via the renewal flow creates a renewal Order with the real yearly amount and fires `createZohoInvoice` against THAT Order (which passes the guard since its amount > 0 and orderType !== 'hosting_trial'). The original ₹0 trial Order stays in place forever as audit trail with `zohoInvoiceId: null`. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is an example of "policy-as-enforcement" — converting a behavior that happens to be correct (because no caller violates it) into a behavior that CAN'T be wrong (because the function itself refuses). Generalisable pattern when an operator decision crystallises a previously-implicit invariant. Reusable for other operator-decided invariants going forward (e.g. "never charge an active-trial Hosting", "never issue a credit note for a free trial", etc.) — add the guard at the relevant service-layer function, document in CLAUDE.md, back up with auto-memory.

- [x] **TRIAL-ABUSE-KILL-SWITCH: new `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED` env-var kill switch lets the operator temporarily bypass every trial-abuse defense (disposable-email + IP throttle + device throttle + OTP gate) during launch-day testing; deploy script extended with matching sticky-flag preservation; bypass currently ACTIVE on production for operator testing with mandatory re-disable before production traffic** — Operator hit the IP_THROTTLE on `/checkout` ("A free trial has already been claimed from this network in the last 30 days. Log into your existing account or contact support if this seems wrong.") because their own IP+device had a TrialClaim record from 2026-06-29's launch-day testing. Asked for a temporary bypass that can be re-enabled later — same shape as the existing reCAPTCHA env-var kill switch referenced in the comments at `lib/trial-abuse.ts:67-69`. **Fix** (`f7f70ac`): three coordinated changes. (1) `lib/trial-abuse.ts` — new `isTrialAbuseDisabled()` helper does a truthy-check on `process.env.TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED` against `'true' | '1' | 'yes' | 'on'`. `evaluateTrialAbuse` short-circuits to `{ allowed: true }` at the top of the function when the helper returns true, logging a WARN every invocation so the bypass is visible in Cloud Run logs. `recordTrialClaim` skips its DB write under the same condition — without this, every test signup during the bypass window would persist a TrialClaim row, and re-engaging the throttle (by un-setting the env var) would block from the same IP/device the very next request. Skipping the write keeps the post-bypass state clean. (2) `scripts/deploy-cloud-run.sh` — extended the `read_current_env_var` sticky-flag pattern to also resolve `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED` (mirrors `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` preservation from `6f4c4a0`). Without this, subsequent full deploys would silently drop the bypass mid-test (same incident pattern that bit us on 2026-06-29 06:28Z). The script now echoes `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED resolved to: <value>` alongside the existing `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW resolved to:` line before traffic shifts. (3) `memory/project_trial_abuse_kill_switch_active.md` — new auto-memory entry carrying the full re-disable procedure (`gcloud run services update --update-env-vars=TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=` to un-set), the cleanup checklist (whether to clear the operator's pre-bypass TrialClaim records before re-enabling), and the rationale for the env-var-only approach (no admin DB toggle — those have bitten us twice on the step-up-reauth quirk during 2026-06-20 captcha rework). **Default safety**: env unset → `isTrialAbuseDisabled()` returns false → all abuse defenses active. The bypass is strictly opt-in. **Live state on `dms-00230-nbj`** (verified via `gcloud run services describe`): `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` + `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED=true`. The deploy-script's first-deploy preservation worked on the very first cycle — operator promoted the env var via `--update-env-vars` to revision `dms-00229-r62` (~2 min on the OLD image), the subsequent full deploy of `f7f70ac` read the existing Cloud Run value, echoed `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED resolved to: true` before promotion, and the new revision `dms-00230-nbj` came up with both code AND env var live together. **Sticky-flag wins**: 19 consecutive correct deploys for `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` + first-cycle success for `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED`. **Mandatory follow-up** (logged in todos): re-disable `TRIAL_ABUSE_DISABLED` BEFORE production traffic resumes. The auto-memory entry is the load-bearing reminder — its title is intentionally `_active` so future sessions immediately see "this is currently ON and needs to be turned OFF". **Worth recording explicitly because**: this validates the sticky-flag pattern generalises beyond the original `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` incident. Any future operator-toggled runtime env var (other kill switches, feature flags, etc.) should follow the same template — `read_current_env_var` resolution → echo-before-promote → preserve through full deploys. The pattern is documented inline in the deploy script's comments at lines ~325-340 for the next time a similar toggle is added.
