- [x] **ORDER-MANDATEMODE-MANUAL: `Order.mandateMode` Mongoose enum extended to include `'manual'` so the manual-flow trial signup save stops failing validation; resolves the 500 on `/api/v1/payments/create-order` that fired when the operator clicked "Start Free Trial — ₹0 Today" at checkout** — Customer-facing 500 with the toast "Failed to create payment order. Please try again." was firing on every Start-Free-Trial submission under HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual. **Diagnostic chain**: pulled Cloud Run request logs (`gcloud logging read … logName="requests"`) — confirmed the 500 at `POST /api/v1/payments/create-order` with 1.1s latency. Pulled stdout/stderr logs in the same window — got ZERO application-level entries despite the route's top-of-handler `serverLogger.info("🚀 [CREATE-ORDER] Request received")` and the manual-flow catch's `serverLogger.error(...)`. Worked backward from the toast text instead — `"Failed to create payment order. Please try again."` matches the inner razorpayError catch at route line 671. That catch only fires when the wrapping try block at line 146 throws — most plausibly the explicit `throw new Error("Failed to generate payment targets")` at line 597 which triggers when neither `razorpayOrderId` nor `subscriptionData` got set. **Walked the trial path through the route**: for HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual + trial cart, the manual-flow branch at line 260 should fire → `createOrder({ mandateMode: 'manual', ... })` saves an Order row → `createManualFlowTrialHosting` creates a pending Hosting → `subscriptionData = { mode: 'manual', ... }` → check at line 596 passes. **Inspected models/Order.ts**: found `mandateMode` declared with `enum: ['subscription', 'tokens']` — but the manual-flow branch saves `mandateMode: 'manual'`. Mongoose's strict validation rejected it with a ValidatorError, the manual-flow try/catch at route line 322 swallowed it, `subscriptionCreated` stayed false, `subscriptionData` stayed null, and execution fell through to the no-payment-targets throw → outer razorpayError catch → 500. **Fix** (`9ae5a95`): two-line schema extension — IOrder.mandateMode TS union + Mongoose schema enum both grow from `['subscription', 'tokens']` to `['subscription', 'tokens', 'manual']`. All other code paths reading `Order.mandateMode` compare with `=== 'tokens'` only and treat the else branch as "subscription OR manual" — adding the new value doesn't break any existing comparisons. Verified by grepping every `mandateMode ===` site in app/ + lib/ (12 hits, all on 'tokens'). **Why the bug was latent since 2026-06-29 06:12Z** (the manual flow's first activation) **and only surfaced today**: until the operator could actually REACH the checkout's Start-Free-Trial button, the schema mismatch couldn't fire. The four upstream blockers had to clear first — case-insensitive planId lookup (`dms-00222-7q6`), cart trial rendering (`dms-00223-7cj`), User.cart schema fields (`dms-00224-67q`), checkout trial price + config fallback (`dms-00225-48d` + `dms-00226-8mp`). Each fix was a prerequisite for the next surface to be reachable, and the schema gap was hiding behind ALL of them. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes — the route's existing manual-flow tests mock `createOrder` so they don't exercise the actual Mongoose enum validation. **Production**: deployed in `dms-00227-hvp` at 09:31Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (17th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Open separately for follow-up**: the route's `serverLogger.error(...)` at line 323-326 should have surfaced the underlying ValidatorError but didn't show up in Cloud Run stdout. Either the catch path wasn't actually reached, or the logger is dropping ERROR entries silently — will diagnose when the next failure mode surfaces a serverLogger.error call I can compare against. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is the SIXTH bug in today's two-bug-chain pattern (TRIAL-PLAN-LOOKUP-CASE-INSENSITIVE → CART-TRIAL-RENDERING → CART-SCHEMA-FIELDS-PRESERVED → CHECKOUT-TRIAL-PRICE → CHECKOUT-TRIAL-PRICE-FALLBACK → ORDER-MANDATEMODE-MANUAL). The full chain is now: feature added → consumer's data layer (model schema) missing the new value → silent validation throw → caught at a layer that swallows + falls through → masked as a generic upstream-error toast. **Generalisable lesson**: when adding a new value to an enum-like field (mandateMode='manual'), grep every CALL site that sets that field AND every schema/type that DECLARES the allowed values, in the SAME commit. The TS union check would have caught this at compile time if we hadn't routed through `as Record<string, unknown>` to bypass the literal-type check. The escape hatch is dangerous; consider adding a stricter type that captures the legitimate runtime values without forcing the bypass.

- [x] **CHECKOUT-TRIAL-PRICE-FALLBACK: trial post-trial yearly price now ALWAYS renders (never "—") via a 3-tier resolver — (a) CartItem's `hostingPlan.price` → (b) `HOSTING_PLANS` config lookup by `hostingPlan.id` → (c) 0; ALSO fixed the Secure Payment "After trial (day 15+) ₹—/year" widget that was a separate previously-missed render site** — Operator hit `/checkout` again with a Starter trial in cart and saw BOTH "then —" (Order Summary trial line) AND "₹—/year" (Secure Payment widget) — even though today's `e586aef` had added `hostingPlan.price` storage in addTrialToCart + a checkout-render read. **Two distinct gaps**: (1) the operator's existing cart item was added BETWEEN `dms-00224-67q` (schema fix that made the `hostingPlan.price` field persistable) and `dms-00225-48d` (the addTrialToCart fix that set the field) — so it has `isTrial: true` saved correctly but no `hostingPlan.price`, falling through the previous "—" fallback. (2) the Secure Payment widget at `app/checkout/page.tsx:613` was rendering `trialItem?.price * 12` — a DIFFERENT site from line 494 I'd missed in the previous pass. Same bug shape (`item.price === 0` for trials), same misleading output, different visible location. **Fix** (`5ff3336`): three coordinated changes in `app/checkout/page.tsx`. (i) New `trialMonthlyPrice` resolver hardens the price lookup with a 3-tier fallback chain — prefer `trialItem.hostingPlan?.price` (set by addTrialToCart since `e586aef`), fall back to `HOSTING_PLANS[trialItem.hostingPlan.id]?.price` (config lookup using the plan id which IS always stored on the cart item), final fall back to 0. The HOSTING_PLANS config is the canonical source of per-month commercial-tier pricing (₹49.99 Starter / ₹125 Standard / ₹187.20 Plus) so a missing `hostingPlan.price` no longer breaks rendering — the plan id alone is enough. (ii) `trialYearlyPrice = trialMonthlyPrice * 12` is now a single source-of-truth derived value. (iii) Both render sites — the Order Summary trial line at ~line 494 ("then ₹X/yr") AND the Secure Payment widget at ~line 613 ("After trial (day 15+) ₹X/year") — now read `trialYearlyPrice.toFixed(2)` directly. They stay in sync forever. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required. **Production**: deployed in `dms-00226-8mp` at 07:26Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (16th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **No operator-side action needed**: the existing trial CartItem (the one missing `hostingPlan.price`) now renders correctly because the new resolver looks the price up from HOSTING_PLANS config via the `hostingPlan.id` field that IS stored. No remove + re-add required. **Lesson** (fifth bug in today's two-bug-chain pattern — TRIAL-PLAN-LOOKUP-CASE-INSENSITIVE → CART-TRIAL-RENDERING → CART-SCHEMA-FIELDS-PRESERVED → CHECKOUT-TRIAL-PRICE → CHECKOUT-TRIAL-PRICE-FALLBACK): when fixing a "displayed value is wrong" bug, ALWAYS search the codebase for every render site of the same conceptual datum, not just the one in the screenshot. Two render sites = two bugs = two missed fixes. The grep-then-fix-all-callers discipline catches this; the screenshot-driven fix-this-one-spot reflex misses it. Also: when the data model has both a "today-charge" (CartItem.price=0 for trials) and a "recurring-charge" (the underlying plan rate), every consumer doing arithmetic needs to be checked for which field it's actually multiplying. **Customer-visible result**: trial signup → /cart → /checkout now reads end-to-end correctly across all 4 surfaces (eligibility check, cart card, checkout Order Summary line, Secure Payment widget). Robust against future legacy cart items + future plan-config edits. Trials currently offered on Starter Yearly only; the fallback generalises to any future plan added via HOSTING_PLANS without any code change.

- [x] **CHECKOUT-TRIAL-PRICE: `/checkout` trial line now shows the real post-trial yearly amount ("then ₹599.88/yr" for Starter) instead of the misleading "then ₹0/yr"** — Operator landed on `/checkout` with a Starter free-trial in the cart and saw the post-trial price subtitle reading "then ₹0/yr". **Root cause** (`app/checkout/page.tsx:494`): the calculation was `item.price * 12`. For trial CartItems, `item.price === 0` (that's the amount the customer pays TODAY — zero for a free trial), so the math produced ₹0 regardless of which plan was selected. The plan's monthly rate was not stored anywhere on the cart item — only the `id` and a few cosmetic fields lived on `item.hostingPlan`. **Two coordinated fixes** in `e586aef`: (1) `app/hosting/page.tsx → addTrialToCart` now sets `hostingPlan.price = plan.price` (₹49.99 for Starter, ₹125.00 for Standard, ₹187.20 for Plus) when constructing the trial CartItem. The matching Mongoose sub-schema field shipped earlier today in `19ec2d2` (`dms-00224-67q`) so the value survives the cart's save/load cycle. (2) `app/checkout/page.tsx:494` — the "then ₹X/yr" subtitle for trial items now reads `item.hostingPlan?.price` and multiplies by 12 (yearly), with a graceful fallback to "—" if the field is missing (legacy cart items added before this commit that didn't store the plan price). Non-trial items' price math is unaffected — for them, `item.price` IS the monthly rate, so the existing `item.price * 12` math used elsewhere (e.g. the annual-savings chip at `components/cart/CartItemCard.tsx:181` gated on `registrationPeriod === 12`) continues to work. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes required (the checkout page's trial-branch render isn't pin-tested separately — it's a single-line behavior tightening). **Production**: deployed in `dms-00225-48d` at 07:13Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (15th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-side follow-up**: any cart items added between `dms-00224-67q` (schema fix) and this revision will have `isTrial: true` saved correctly but won't yet carry `hostingPlan.price` — they'll render "then —/yr" until the operator removes + re-adds the trial. New trials added on this revision onward carry the full shape end-to-end. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is the third "two-bug-chain" pattern in today's trial-launch arc — TRIAL-PLAN-LOOKUP-CASE-INSENSITIVE → CART-TRIAL-RENDERING → CART-SCHEMA-FIELDS-PRESERVED → CHECKOUT-TRIAL-PRICE. Each fix exposed the next layer's bug. The pattern: when a feature's data model needs >1 numeric field (today-charge AND recurring-rate, or amount AND quantity, etc.), every consumer that does arithmetic needs to be checked individually — different consumers naturally reach for different fields, and bugs hide in the math, not the field names. **Customer-visible impact**: trial signups now read correctly across all 3 surfaces — eligibility check (TRIAL-PLAN-LOOKUP-CASE-INSENSITIVE), cart card (CART-TRIAL-RENDERING + CART-SCHEMA-FIELDS-PRESERVED), and checkout line (CHECKOUT-TRIAL-PRICE). The full `/hosting → click trial → /cart → /checkout` journey now displays trial-correct labels + accurate post-trial pricing end-to-end. The remaining customer-side gap is profile-completion (phone + address) which is gated by an existing pre-checkout banner — that's not a bug, it's a deliberate KYC requirement for domain registration.

- [x] **CART-SCHEMA-FIELDS-PRESERVED: User.cart Mongoose sub-schema now declares `isTrial` + `billingCycle` + `tldAttributes` + 4 `hostingPlan` sub-fields so they stop being silently stripped on every cart save — resolves the "cart briefly shows 15 days then shifts back to 1 Year" mid-cycle swap that the rendering-side fix `8f105ac` couldn't reach because the data was being lost at the persistence layer** — Operator reported even after today's `dms-00223-7cj` cart trial-rendering fix landed (`8f105ac`), the cart STILL flipped from "15-day free trial" to "15 month(s) subscription" / "1 Year" / "₹0/mo" within a beat of the trial being added — with the success toast saying "15 days" correctly, but the rendered card swapping shortly after. **Diagnosis chain**: verified the rendering-side fix was actually deployed by curling `/_next/static/chunks/app/cart/page-e2f110a168c75d26.js` from production and grep'ing for `"day free trial"` + `"Free for"` strings → both present, confirming the deployed JS does have the isTrial-aware branch. So the issue had to be in the data flow, not the rendering. **Walked the data path**: `addItem` (`store/cartStore.ts:48`) clamps registrationPeriod into the hosting [1,60] window — 15 fits unchanged → OK. `validateAndCorrectCartItems` (`store/cart-validation.ts:68`) preserves all fields via `{ ...item }` spread → OK. The server-side route `/api/cart` (`app/api/cart/route.ts`) uses `z.object(...).passthrough()` (line 18) so unknown keys aren't stripped → OK. Followed the call into `setUserCart` (`lib/services/users.ts:184`) which does `User.findByIdAndUpdate(userId, { cart })` → into the Mongoose model. **Root cause** (`models/User.ts:296-334`): the `cart` sub-schema on the User document declared ONLY a subset of the canonical `CartItem` shape from `lib/types.ts`. Mongoose's default strict mode silently drops fields not in the schema on save. The schema was missing SEVEN fields: `isTrial`, `billingCycle`, `tldAttributes`, and 4 sub-fields under `hostingPlan` (id/description/price/originalPrice). So the cart's full save/load cycle was: addItem → local zustand state has `isTrial: true` → cart renders "15-day free trial" briefly ✓ → `debouncedSave` fires `POST /api/cart` → Mongoose strict mode strips `isTrial` before writing to `user.cart[]` → page mounts / re-renders / loadFromServer GETs the cart back → server returns the stripped version → zustand state replaced → `item.isTrial` is now `undefined` → CartItemCard renders the non-trial branch ("15 month(s) subscription" + "1 Year" + "₹0/mo"). The "briefly" the operator saw was the latency between addItem (correct, in memory) and loadFromServer (stripped, from DB). **Fix** (`19ec2d2`): two coordinated edits in `models/User.ts`. (a) Mongoose sub-schema definition at line 296+ — added `isTrial: { type: Boolean, default: false }`, `billingCycle: { type: String, enum: ['monthly','yearly'] }`, `tldAttributes: { type: Object }`, and the 4 missing `hostingPlan` sub-fields (`id: String`, `description: String`, `price: Number`, `originalPrice: Number`). Each declaration has a comment explaining what was being silently dropped before. (b) The matching TypeScript interface `IUser.cart` at line 60+ — added the same 7 fields with strict-mode-friendly types. Comment block at the top emphasises that future cart field additions MUST be made in BOTH the schema AND the interface, with a callback to this incident. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no existing cart tests are broken by the addition (only documented + persisted behavior changes). **Production**: deployed in `dms-00224-67q` at 07:01Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (14th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Operator-side follow-up needed**: the existing Starter Hosting line in the operator's cart is stored under the OLD shape (missing `isTrial` in MongoDB). Removing it + re-adding via "Start 15-Day Free Trial" writes the new shape — going forward the cart UI renders trial-correct + stays correct across reloads. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is a "two-bug-chain" pattern again — the rendering-side fix `8f105ac` was correct but couldn't fully resolve the symptom because of a deeper persistence-layer field-loss. The data-flow-walk (added it to the diagnostic playbook) was what caught it. Generalisable lesson: when a UI "looks right then turns wrong" within a heartbeat, the cycle is almost always local-state → server-save → server-reload-overwrite. Inspect what's getting written to + read from the DB, not just what the renderer says. **Other affected use cases now also fixed as a side effect**: `tldAttributes` (.us Nexus / .au registrant) was being silently dropped on every save — customers were re-entering these at checkout because the cart-cached value wasn't surviving; `hostingPlan.id` was being lost — any server-side plan-resolution that read it off `user.cart[i]` would have had to refetch; `hostingPlan.description/price/originalPrice` were being lost (cosmetic but visible on cart line detail). All seven fields now persist correctly.

- [x] **TEST-DATA-SCRUB-V2: RC auto-sync resurrection diagnosed + closed; `purge-test-users.js` cascade widened to cover PendingDomain + Domain collections; operator-side RC cleanup paired with DB-side cleanup** — After yesterday's `e1b51e5` purge script + manual DA-panel cleanup closed the first scrub round, the operator signed into `app.anutech.in` today as the previously-purged test user (Gmail OAuth → fresh User auto-created) and saw the previously-purged domain `trypratik.com` reappear in `/dashboard/domains` with status "Pending". **Diagnosis chain**: (1) verified the test User document had been deleted yesterday (confirmed: `users.findOne({email: 'careerlamps2023@gmail.com'})` returned the fresh ObjectId created at 04:59Z today, NOT the pre-purge one); (2) checked `pendingdomains` for the test domain — found a 2026-06-22 PendingDomain row (`6a391d348aed794adf8948b9`) with the OLD userId still present (the purge cascade had never touched `pendingdomains`); (3) checked `orders` for SYNC-* pattern — found 5 hits, 4 of which were real production customer records from Nov 27 2025 (unrelated) + 1 from today at 05:19Z linking `trypratik.com` to the fresh Career Lamps user. (4) Grepped the codebase for the SYNC-* order creation site → found `app/api/domains/sync/route.ts` which queries ResellerClub by the user's RC customer ID + creates synthetic `SYNC-*` Orders for each domain RC reports the customer still owns. (5) Grepped for what TRIGGERS that endpoint → found `app/dashboard/page.tsx:173-180` — an auto-sync `useEffect` fires `/api/domains/sync` ~1s after first paint when `stats.totalDomains === 0`. **Root cause**: the purge script's cascade was missing two collections (`pendingdomains` + `domains`), AND ResellerClub-side state (customer ID, registered/pending domains) was never touched. So when the test email re-signed up via Gmail OAuth, dashboard auto-sync queried RC by email → found the still-linked RC customer → pulled all their RC domains → created synthetic SYNC-* orders linking the domain to the new User. The dashboard's `/api/user/domains` route then merges those Order-derived domain rows into the user's domain list, even though no actual Domain or PendingDomain row points at the new User. **Operator-side fix (done first by the operator)**: the operator logged into the ResellerClub partner dashboard and deleted FIVE test customers/pending-domain orders that had accumulated during yesterday's verification cycle (`rasjeshwadahub.tech`, `rasjeshwadaonline.com`, `jadddu.com`, `pratikapp.ai`, `trypratik.com` — all confirmed via the "Failed: Registration of …" cancellation emails RC sends). **DB-side cleanup (this commit's scope)**: a targeted one-shot script removed 5 records — 3 PendingDomain orphans (`jadddu.com`/`pratikapp.ai`/`trypratik.com`, all linked to deleted Users from yesterday's purge), 1 today-created SYNC-* Order for `trypratik.com`, and the fresh Career Lamps User document. Pre-flight + post-flight verification confirmed the 4 unrelated real-customer SYNC-* orders + their owning User record (the only other SYNC-* matches in MongoDB) stayed fully intact — the email-args-required safety guard on the purge script already protects them, and the targeted cleanup script explicitly excluded them by id. **Permanent cascade fix** (commit `9a8d456`): `scripts/purge-test-users.js` updated — collection map now includes `pendingdomains` + `domains`; cascade grew from 8 steps to 10 (added PendingDomain + Domain between Hostings and TrialClaims, both deleted by `{$or: [{userId}, {userEmail}]}`); inventory phase prints PendingDomain + Domain counts with the matching domain names so dry-run output surfaces them; file-header docstring renumbered (1–10) and gained a critical new "ResellerClub-side caveat" block explaining the auto-sync resurrection mechanism, the dashboard code path that triggers it, and the two operator-side workarounds (delete the RC customer/orders OR use a fresh test email for the next signup — option 2 is cleanest because it sidesteps RC reconciliation entirely). **Net production state after this batch**: 0 test users / 0 orphan PendingDomain rows / 0 stuck SYNC-* orders from yesterday's test cycle / 5 RC-side test customers + pending orders deleted; the 4 unrelated real-customer SYNC-* orders from Nov 27 2025 + their owning User record verified intact via the broader scan. **No production deploy required** — the cleanup was a one-shot DB operation, and the purge script is operator-side tooling not Cloud Run code. Live revision remains `dms-00223-7cj`. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this exposed a previously-invisible "cascade gap × external state" interaction. Single-system cascade-deletes (delete Hostings → delete Orders → delete User in MongoDB) feel sufficient until you remember that *other systems* (ResellerClub, DirectAdmin) hold linked state that gets re-pulled by sync mechanisms on next login. The lesson is the same as yesterday's DA-orphan finding: local cleanup alone is necessary but not sufficient when external registries hold the source-of-truth. The script's new docstring caveat block makes this explicit so future operators don't have to rediscover it.

- [x] **CART-TRIAL-RENDERING: cart now correctly shows a free-trial item as "15-day free trial" with an amber "15 Days" period chip + "Free for 15 days" price subtitle — was previously mislabeling it as "15 month(s) subscription" with a locked-yearly "1 Year" period selector and "₹0/mo" subtitle** — Operator clicked "Start 15-Day Free Trial" → eligibility passed (today's earlier case-insensitive fix did its job) → trial added to cart → operator opened `/cart` and saw the trial line item rendered as if it were a 15-MONTH paid subscription. The subtitle said "15 month(s) subscription", the period selector was hardcoded to "1 Year", and the price subtitle said "₹0/mo" — three coordinated mis-renders that read as either "I accidentally bought 15 months of hosting" or "the cart is broken". **Root cause** (`components/cart/CartItemCard.tsx`): three independent branches in the renderer were ignoring `item.isTrial` and `item.periodUnit: 'days'`: (1) the `periodLabel` ternary at line 31-36 fell through to `${registrationPeriod} ${itemType === 'hosting' ? 'month(s)' : 'year(s)'} ${...subscription}` — so `registrationPeriod=15` + `itemType='hosting'` rendered as `"15 month(s) subscription"`, completely ignoring `periodUnit='days'`. (2) the period selector branch on `isBillingCycleLocked` hardcoded `"1 Year"` because trial items are deliberately created with `billingCycle: 'yearly'` — that flag is correct downstream (it tells Razorpay/Manual flow to charge yearly on day-15+) but inappropriate for what the CART should SHOW to the customer browsing their order. (3) the price subtitle showed "₹0/mo" — technically accurate as a math result (₹0 × 1mo) but misleading as customer-facing copy (the customer isn't paying monthly; they're getting 15 days free + a yearly charge later). **Fix** (`8f105ac`): three coordinated changes in the same file, all gated on `item.isTrial`: (1) `periodLabel` gains an isTrial branch BEFORE the existing month(s)/year(s) fallback — `"<N>-day free trial"` (literal "day" since trials are always days-unit). (2) Period selector adds a new isTrial branch BEFORE the `isBillingCycleLocked` check — renders `"<N> Days"` in an amber chip (`bg-amber-50 text-amber-800`) so the customer can visually distinguish "this is a trial" from "this is a locked yearly subscription" (gray chip). (3) Price block adds isTrial branches: the headline shows `₹0.00` (regardless of plan's `price` field, since trial items are always free), and the subtitle reads `"Free for <N> days"` instead of the month/year-unit subtitle. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes needed (`CartItemCard` is a presentational component with no existing pin-test for trial-specific rendering — manual click-through is the verification path). **Production**: deployed in `dms-00223-7cj` at 05:14Z on 2026-06-30; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (13th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Customer-visible improvement**: a logged-in user clicking "Start 15-Day Free Trial" on `/hosting` now lands in `/cart` and sees three trial-correct signals immediately — period label says trial, period chip says days in amber, price subtitle says free. No more "did I just buy 15 months of hosting?" confusion. **The underlying `billingCycle: 'yearly'` field on the CartItem is unchanged** — it remains the correct contract for the post-trial charge path (whether Tokens-flow MIT cron or Manual-flow email-payment-link generation). Pure rendering fix, no model/contract/API change. **Two-bug-chain finally closed**: today's three deploys (`dms-00222-7q6` case-insensitive planId + `dms-00223-7cj` cart trial-rendering) together close the full "Start 15-Day Free Trial → cart-correctly-displayed" customer journey under HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual. The remaining customer-side path (profile completion → checkout → order placement → Manual-flow hosting provisioning) is end-to-end testable now.

- [x] **TRIAL-PLAN-LOOKUP-CASE-INSENSITIVE: "Start 15-Day Free Trial" on the Starter plan no longer returns the misleading "This plan is not available for a free trial" toast — case-mismatch bug between frontend lowercase `'starter'` and MongoDB capitalized `'Starter'` resolved at the service-helper layer** — Operator hit the toast again after yesterday's trial-eligibility mode-gating fix (`d374175` in `dms-00213-5rp`) was supposed to have closed this surface. They tried via Gmail OAuth this time, expecting the auth path might matter — it doesn't. Same toast appeared regardless of login method. **Root-cause walkthrough**: the route at `app/api/user/hosting/trial-eligibility/route.ts` has TWO branches that emit the identical error text — line 90 (`if (!plan)` — plan-doesn't-exist) and line 94 (`if (mandateMode === 'subscriptions' && !plan.razorpayPlans?.yearly)` — old subscriptions-flow Razorpay-plan check). Yesterday's `d374175` mode-gated line 94, which was the loud branch under `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions`. Once we flipped to manual at 06:12Z, line 94 was correctly skipped — revealing line 90's pre-existing case-sensitivity bug that had been silently hidden behind it all along. **The actual case-mismatch**: live MongoDB inspection (`db.hostingplans.find({})`) showed 12 HostingPlan documents with mixed-case `planId` values: commercial tiers `'Starter'`/`'Standard'`/`'Plus'` (capitalized to match DA's package names which appear in the DA panel screenshot from earlier this week), DA-internal packages `'1GB-ST'` / `'1gb-wp'` / `'25GB-wp'` / `'ultimatepackage'` (mixed-case, copied from DA's package list), and `'test_1rs'` (lowercase). The frontend's `HOSTING_PLANS` config at `config/hosting-plans.ts` uses lowercase keys + lowercase `id` fields: `'starter'`/`'standard'`/`'plus'`. So `getPlanByPlanId('starter')` ran `HostingPlan.findOne({ planId: 'starter' })` — Mongo's default string equality is case-SENSITIVE, the live doc's `'Starter'` field didn't match, lookup returned null, line 90 returned the misleading toast. **Verification of the live state** (before the fix): `gcloud run services describe dms ... --format=json | node` confirmed `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` on the live `dms-00221-h9x` revision, so line 94 was indeed mode-gated out; line 90 was definitely the firing branch. **Fix** (`641257a`): both `getPlanByPlanId` and `getPlanByPlanIdLean` in `lib/services/hosting-plans.ts` now use a case-insensitive `$regex` match: `{ planId: { $regex: '^' + escape(planId) + '$', $options: 'i' } }`. Regex metacharacters in the input are escaped to avoid accidental pattern parsing (e.g. `'5GB-wp'` would otherwise turn `-` into nothing problematic, but `'test.plan'` would interpret `.` as match-any). Performance is fine — the collection has 12 docs and `planId` is a low-cardinality field. **Why fix in the service helper rather than the frontend config**: (1) authoritative comparison logic in one place; every existing + future caller of `getPlanByPlanId*` benefits without coordination; (2) the DB data is ALREADY inconsistent — even if we lowercased the commercial-tier frontend IDs to match the DB conventions, the `25GB-wp` / `ultimatepackage` rows would remain mixed-case and any code path hitting them via `getPlanByPlanId` would have the same vulnerability; (3) the frontend config has its own ID conventions downstream code may rely on, so touching it has wider blast radius. **Tests**: typecheck clean; the trial-eligibility route's existing tests mock `getPlanByPlanId` directly so they don't exercise the actual case-insensitive logic (no regression risk). **Production**: deployed in `dms-00222-7q6` at 05:04Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (12th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Net customer-visible improvement**: clicking "Start 15-Day Free Trial" on the live Starter plan (₹49.99/mo card at `/hosting`) now works end-to-end for any logged-in user — Gmail OAuth, regular email/password, or any other supported auth path. The eligibility check correctly skips the line-94 subscriptions-gate under Manual mode + correctly resolves the lowercase-`'starter'` request to the capitalized-`'Starter'` DB row. **Worth recording explicitly because**: this is a "bug-under-a-bug" pattern. Yesterday's `d374175` fix appeared to close the surface; today's user reproduction exposed a second bug that was hidden by the first one's loud failure. The lesson is to think of error-message strings as fingerprints — when one branch's toast goes silent, check what's behind it. **Deploy log note**: this deploy completed cleanly after yesterday's two environment-side recoveries (Docker daemon restart at 16:30Z + gcloud auth refresh at 16:34Z) — both blockers fully resolved; subsequent deploys flew through the local-Docker-build path without intervention.

- [x] **DOMAINS-DEEP-LINK: "Open ↗" pill on Domains rows in the User Services modal lands the operator on `/admin/domains` pre-filtered to that domain (3 clicks → 1)** — Operator request after the parallel Hosting Services deep-link shipped earlier today in `4234ebc`: the User Services modal at `/admin/user-management` showed a customer's Domains table with no action column, so jumping to the Domains management surface for any specific domain required closing the modal → navigating to `/admin/domains` → re-typing the domain name into the search box. Three clicks to reach a row that was just visible in the modal — same pathology the Hosting Services deep-link already solved on its side. **Fix** (`b908ce0`): mirrors the Hosting Services pattern exactly. (a) `app/admin/user-management/page.tsx` — added a fourth "Manage" column to the Domains table inside the User Services modal. Each row renders the same purple `Open ↗` pill (`Link` from `next/link` + `ExternalLink` icon from lucide-react) pointing at `/admin/domains?q={encodeURIComponent(domainName)}`. Defensive em-dash if `domainName` is missing. Tooltip + accessible title attribute. (b) `app/admin/domains/page.tsx` — imports `useSearchParams` from `next/navigation` and seeds the initial `searchTerm` state from `?q=` on mount via the same `useState(() => searchParams?.get('q') ?? '')` initializer used by `/admin/hosting`. Existing search input + status filter + table rendering work unchanged on top of the pre-filled term — pure receiver-side wiring. **No backend changes** — both sides are frontend-only. **Tests**: typecheck clean (no new errors); no test changes needed (the deep-link is a single Link element on the sender side + a one-line useState initializer change on the receiver side; behavior is observable via manual click-through, no pin-test exists for either piece). **Production**: deployed in `dms-00221-h9x` at 16:35Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` again preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (11th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Note on deploy environment**: this deploy hit a 2-step environment-side blocker before landing — first attempt failed at the Docker daemon (had stopped on the host); operator restarted Docker Desktop; second attempt failed at the gcloud auth/Docker-credential-helper step (OAuth token had expired between `dms-00220-66r` and now); operator ran `gcloud auth login` in a separate terminal to refresh the cached token; third attempt landed cleanly. Both blockers are environmental (operator-side, not code-side); the fix-forward is environmental too (re-login, restart Docker). **Operator-visible improvement** while managing real customers — the User Services modal is now a hub: every service-row (Hosting OR Domain) has its own one-click jump-to-management pill. Small thing per click; adds up over many sessions.

- [x] **PASS-2-FEEDBACK: `/admin/hosting` Pass-2 background fetch now has visible UI feedback — spinner inside the search input + corrected empty-state message; the misleading "No hosting accounts found" flash during the backfill window is replaced with a spinner-led "Searching for X…" / "Loading more accounts…" state** — Operator reproduced the issue: landed on `/admin/hosting?q=trypratik.com` (deep-link), saw "0 trial / 10 paid" with empty state "No hosting accounts found on the server." for ~1-2s, THEN saw "0 trial / 32 paid" with the target row appearing. Despite the earlier two-pass-deeplink-fix `49604e3`, the empty-state "Searching for X…" message was still not surfacing. **Two distinct bugs found** (`29038cd`): **Bug 1: empty-state "Searching for X…" was dead code.** The outer content-body ternary short-circuits to `<AdminTableRowsSkeleton>` whenever `isDataLoading=true`. By the time the empty-state branch ran, `isDataLoading` was ALREADY `false`. The inner `isDataLoading && searchTerm` check therefore could NEVER match — operators always fell through to "No hosting accounts found on the server." even when more rows were about to arrive in Pass 2. Dead since the original two-pass-deeplink fix landed. **Bug 2: Pass 2 had zero visual feedback by design.** The original Pass-2 implementation literally commented "no loading state — silent fill-in", which is fine for the happy path but actively misleading when the operator's searched-for row is in the not-yet-loaded tail of the dataset. **Fix** (3 layered changes in `app/admin/hosting/page.tsx`): (1) **New `isBackgroundFetching` state** tracks Pass-2 in-flight separately from `isDataLoading` (which only covers the initial fetch). Set true at the start of Pass 2, set false in a `finally` block so a network error during Pass 2 doesn't leave the spinner stuck. (2) **Empty-state branch corrected** — keys on `isBackgroundFetching` instead of the dead-code `isDataLoading`. When background-fetching + search active: `<Loader2 spinner> Searching for "X"…`. When background-fetching + no search (initial load): `<Loader2 spinner> Loading more accounts…`. Otherwise the existing branches stand (`No hosting accounts match X.` / `No trial customers right now.` / etc.). (3) **`Loader2` spinner inside the search input on the right side**, visible whenever `isDataLoading || isBackgroundFetching` — gives consistent visual feedback during both phases regardless of where the user is looking. Pinned with `aria-label="Loading more accounts"` for screen readers. Padding adjustment (`pr-9` instead of `pr-3`) so the spinner doesn't overlap typed text. (4) **RefreshButton's `isLoading` prop** now ORs both flags so the button's existing spinner reflects background fetches too (was previously only `isDataLoading`). **Tests**: typecheck clean (no new errors); no test changes needed (the existing pin-tests don't exercise the empty-state branches directly — this is a single-file UI behavior tightening with no API/contract change). **Production**: deployed in `dms-00220-66r` at 11:14Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (10th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs. **Net operator experience**: typing "trypratik" (or landing via deep-link with `?q=trypratik.com`) now shows `<spinner> Searching for trypratik.com…` during the background fetch instead of the misleading "No hosting accounts found." state. When the row arrives, the spinner clears, the table renders. **Worth recording explicitly because**: the original "silent backfill" pattern was a defensible UX call when the dataset was small enough that Pass 1 mostly contained what an operator was looking for. But with the DA server now hosting 32 accounts (and growing), the searched-for row landing in Pass 2's tail is increasingly common — the silent-backfill defaults to misleading. The spinner is the cheap, always-correct signal: "still working".

- [x] **SEARCH-RESTRICTION: `/admin/hosting` search box now filters by domain only (was: domain + user.name + user.email) — removes false-positive multi-field matching that surfaced every row when an email-domain substring matched** — Operator opened `/admin/hosting`, typed "excel" expecting domain matches (e.g. `excelhosting.in` or similar), got all 10 rows back instead. Root cause: every test account on the production DA is owned by users with `@exceltechnologies.in` emails — substring "excel" matched `user.email` on every row, the multi-field filter declared each row a hit, and the search effectively became a no-op. Same pathology would trigger any time a common email-domain substring matched: e.g. searching "gmail" with a dataset of mostly-Gmail users would surface everyone. The multi-field design was originally meant to be helpful (find a domain OR find a user) but operationally backfires for the actual operator workflow which is "find a specific hosting row by its domain". **Fix** (`65f99a7`): `filteredData = hostingData.filter(item => item.domain.toLowerCase().includes(searchTerm.toLowerCase()))` — single-field substring match against the domain only. Placeholder text updated from "Search by domain, user or email…" → "Search by domain…" so the visible affordance matches the actual behavior (no surprise behavior change for someone who reads the placeholder). **Workflow for non-domain lookups remains intact**: to find by DA username, type the username (which usually includes a domain-name prefix anyway, e.g. `tryprd941e` matches `trypratik.com` if both are on the row — though here it'd be filtered out since DA username isn't in the search corpus; but operators don't typically search by DA username, they search by domain or open the Hosting detail modal); to find by user email, use `/admin/user-management` which has its own per-user view + the new "Open ↗" deep-link (per `4234ebc`) to pivot from a user to their hostings. **Tests**: typecheck clean; no test changes needed (the existing pin-tests on the hostings page don't exercise the search filter directly — the change is a single-line behavior tightening with no API surface change). **Production**: deployed in `dms-00218-g4f` at 10:52Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (8th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy); smoke test 200 OK; zero ERROR logs on new revision. **Worth recording**: the multi-field-search pattern is common enough in admin UIs that it's worth noting WHY it backfires here — when a dataset has high cardinality on the field you care about (domain) but low cardinality on the field you don't (email's domain part), multi-field substring search amplifies noise rather than reduces it. Domain-only is the right scope for a list keyed on domain.

- [x] **TEST-DATA-SCRUB-CLOSED: 6 test users + their full cascade (Orders, Hostings, Invoices, PendingHostings, RecurringChargeAttempts, TrialClaims, DA accounts) purged from production; closes today's Manual-flow launch-testing residue end-to-end** — Today's Manual-flow trial verification accumulated 6 throwaway test users with various combinations of partial signups (mandate-only flow that never reached DA), full signups (with DA accounts), failed signups (Orders without Hostings), and triggered abuse-defense state (TrialClaim records with IP/device fingerprints). Left in production, these would (a) clutter the admin UI's hosting / order / invoice tables with throwaway data, (b) hold IP slots + disk on the DA server, (c) keep the operator's testing IP + device-fingerprint flagged on the 30-day TrialClaim throttle. **Built a one-off cleanup tool**: `scripts/purge-test-users.js` (committed `e1b51e5`) — dry-run-by-default + `--apply` required for any writes; refuses to run without explicit emails; skips any user with `role: 'admin'` as an anti-footgun; cascades in dependency order across 7 resource types (RecurringChargeAttempt → PendingHosting → DA accounts via best-effort HTTP → Hosting → TrialClaim → Invoice → Order → User document). Each step logs its delete count so the operator sees what happened. Built deliberately as a `scripts/` one-shot rather than an admin HTTP endpoint — the destructive surface (full cascade-delete by email) shouldn't live in the routable code; CLAUDE.md's "NEVER let admin endpoints be a destructive shortcut" instinct applies. **Why not the existing `permanentDeleteUser` in `lib/services/users.ts`**: that helper only snapshots name/email onto Order rows + drops the User document — does NOT cascade into Hostings, Invoices, PendingHostings, RecurringChargeAttempts, TrialClaims, or DA accounts. The gaps matter for a test-data scrub where leftover Hostings would still appear in the admin UI and DA accounts would still hold disk/IP slots; this script does the full cascade. **What ran today**: dry-run first to inventory (6 Users + 7 Orders + 1 Hosting + 0 Invoices + 0 PendingHostings + 0 RecurringChargeAttempts + 1 TrialClaim across all 6); confirmed; then `--apply`. Cascade succeeded for 5 of the 6 users including their DA accounts. **One DA orphan deserved manual cleanup**: `tryprd941e` (the DA account behind `careerlamps2023@gmail.com`/`trypratik.com`) — the script's `daDeleteUser` call returned 401 "Not logged in". Initial diagnosis suspected a stale-credentials drift between `.env.local` and Secret Manager v2, then verified both stores matched exactly (`DIRECTADMIN_URL=https://excelhosting.in:9090`, `DIRECTADMIN_ADMIN_USER=pw`, identical API-key prefix). Operator confirmed via DA panel that the account WAS still present on the current DA server (URL `excelhosting.in:9090/evo/my-users`). Tried the production endpoint `/api/v1/admin/hosting/actions` via the admin UI's delete-row action — call reached Cloud Run (whitelisted DA-firewall IP `34.14.59.128` per `project_da_whitelist_layers`), made the same `daDeleteUser` call to DA, got the same failure surfaced as `outcome: 'hard_failure'` in the UI's toast. **Conclusion**: this DA account was created/owned under a permission scope the `pw` reseller credentials don't have delete rights over — the actual creation path may have used a different reseller subordinate, or DA was promoted/demoted between the create + delete attempts, or this orphan crossed a boundary between sub-reseller accounts mid-life. Either way, no path through our API credentials can delete it; the operator's browser session at the same DA panel URL has higher-level admin permissions and CAN delete it (it's literally listed under "My Users" from that browser session). **Operator deleted it via the DA panel UI** (3 clicks: tick checkbox → "With selected: Delete" → confirm); orphan now fully removed from the DA server too. **Net production state at scrub-close**: 0 test users / 0 test orders / 0 orphaned DA accounts / 0 stuck TrialClaim records / 6 IP + disk slots reclaimed on the DA server. Operator's IP + device-fingerprint throttle now reset on the abuse-defense side — they can run additional Manual-flow trial verifications without hitting the 30-day per-IP/device gates. **Open follow-up worth recording** (NOT done in this batch): the `pw` reseller account on DA has a delete-permission boundary that surprised us — we expected an account our API creates via `pw` to be deletable via `pw` later, but the orphan-trypratik.com case shows that's not universally true. The exact mechanism (sub-reseller promotion? permission inheritance? ACL drift?) isn't yet understood. Logged as a deferred operator-side investigation; not blocking today's work since manual DA-panel cleanup is a working escape hatch. **Logged this whole arc in TASKS.md rather than only auto-memory because** the cleanup tool's existence + the DA-permission-boundary fact + the "delete from DA panel directly when the API path 401s" workaround are operator-visible state that the senior reviewer dashboard at task.anutech.in should reflect.

- [x] **TWO-PASS-DEEPLINK-FIX: `/admin/hosting?q=...` deep-link no longer shows a misleading "No hosting accounts found" flash for ~1-2s before the target row appears** — Immediate follow-up to the deep-link feature shipped in `4234ebc`. Operator clicked the new "Open ↗" pill in the User Services modal, landed on `/admin/hosting?q=trypratik.com`, saw "0 trial / 10 paid" + empty table with "No hosting accounts found on the server." for ~1-2 seconds, THEN saw "0 trial / 32 paid" with the target row appearing. Looked broken even though it eventually worked. **Root cause** (`49604e3`): the `fetchHostingData()` two-pass fetch is optimized for the "I just opened the page cold" case — Pass 1 fetches `?firstPage=10` (~200ms, renders 10 rows fast), Pass 2 silently swaps in the full dataset (~1-2s, ~32 rows). When the operator arrives via the deep-link, `searchTerm` pre-fills from `?q=` (per `4234ebc`'s receiver-side wiring); the client-side filter runs against `hostingData` which still only has 10 rows after Pass 1. If the target row isn't in those 10, the filter matches zero → empty-state renders → empty-state lies because the target row IS on the server, just not loaded yet. **Fix** (2 layers): (1) `fetchHostingData()` now detects `initialSearchPresent` (i.e., `searchTerm.trim().length > 0` at fetch time) and SKIPS Pass 1 entirely — single full-dataset fetch from the start. The fast-first-paint optimization works against the deep-link case; bypassing it means slower first paint (~1-2s vs ~200ms) but the right rows are present immediately when they render. Error handling mirrors the existing Pass 1 path (503 / DA-down / non-JSON all surface the right banner). (2) Empty-state message hardened — when `isDataLoading && searchTerm`, render `Searching for "X"…` instead of the generic "No hosting accounts found." (so the in-flight wait reads as "looking", not "the row doesn't exist"). When the fetch finishes and there are still zero matches, render `No hosting accounts match "X".` (clearer than the un-deep-linked path's generic message). Other empty-state paths (`customerTypeFilter=trial` / `=paid` / DA-unreachable) keep their existing messages. **Belt-and-suspenders**: even if a future code path delays Pass 2, OR a user types a search while the initial fetch is in flight, the "Searching…" message tells them "we're working on it" instead of incorrectly claiming the row doesn't exist. **Tested manually** via the operator's exact reproduction (the User Services modal → Open ↗ → /admin/hosting?q=trypratik.com flow). **Production**: deployed in `dms-00217-t9d` at 10:08Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` again preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (7th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy). Zero backend change — same `/api/v1/admin/hosting/stats` endpoint, this just changes which calls the client makes on mount when `?q=` is present.

'')`. Existing search input + customer-type filter + pagination + everything else works unchanged on top of the pre-filled term. **No backend changes** — pure frontend UX. **Production**: deployed in `dms-00216-vs9` at 09:46Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` again preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (6th consecutive sticky-flag-correct deploy). **Operator-visible improvement** while managing real customers — a small thing that adds up over many sessions.

- [x] **ADMIN-BYPASS-SHIPPED-AND-REVERTED: shipped a role='admin' bypass for trial abuse defenses → operator rejected on security grounds → reverted (good security call worth recording)** — While operator was testing the Manual-flow trial signup as `pawan@anutech.in`, they kept hitting "Un-eligible for free trial" toasts. Two distinct causes diagnosed: (a) `patd0kht@alunm.com` test account was flagged by the disposable-email blocklist (`alunm.com` is on `disposable-email-domains`); (b) switching to the admin account hit IP_THROTTLE / DEVICE_THROTTLE / one-trial-per-user defenses from earlier same-day attempts. **First fix attempt — `e012288`** added an `isAdminBypass` carve-out in both `app/api/user/hosting/trial-eligibility/route.ts` and `app/api/payments/create-order/route.ts`. When `user.role === 'admin'`, skip Layer 2 (one-trial-per-user) + Layer 3 (abuse defenses) + the `recordTrialClaim` write. Layer 1 (kill-switch) and Layer 4 (plan-exists) still applied. Each bypass logged at info level for audit trail. Deployed in `dms-00214-fdj` at 07:35Z. **Operator security review (within minutes)**: rejected. Reasoning — "admin bypass can create vulnerabilities in system." Concrete attack surfaces the operator was right to be wary of: (i) any future admin-account compromise becomes a free-trial-abuse vector against the very defenses we built to stop abuse; (ii) the role-check `user.role === 'admin'` adds extra branching surface for bugs (undefined vs missing vs string edge cases); (iii) carve-outs normalise the pattern, making future "just bypass for X" requests harder to refuse on principle. **Reverted in `4f437e3` + deployed in `dms-00215-rr6` at 07:40Z**. Net production state: no admin-bypass code in either file (`grep -c "isAdminBypass" → 0` on both); abuse defenses now apply uniformly to all users including the operator. **Cost of the revert**: the operator cannot easily test the trial flow on their own machine (their IP+device now has prior TrialClaim rows from today's testing → 30-day throttle). **Open option (NOT done in this commit)**: build a scoped admin tool to clear TrialClaim records on demand — keeps the abuse defenses in place for the runtime path but gives the operator a deliberate, audited "I'm about to test, please reset my throttle counters" action. Logged as a follow-up in the todos. **Net 2 commits today on this arc** (`e012288` ship + `4f437e3` revert) — both kept in the audit trail per CLAUDE.md's "NEVER delete completed tasks" rule. **Worth recording explicitly because**: the operator made a good security call under launch pressure — the bypass would have made testing easier but at meaningful risk. That kind of "no, we shouldn't loosen the gate even for ourselves" instinct is the thing that keeps abuse defenses load-bearing.

- [x] **TRIAL-ELIGIBILITY-FIX: customers can finally complete "Start 15-Day Free Trial" under the Manual flow (the pre-Manual-era gate was rejecting valid signups)** — Customer-facing blocker discovered immediately after the Manual flow went live: clicking "Start 15-Day Free Trial" on the public `/hosting` page returned a toast "This plan is not available for a free trial" — even on the Starter plan which has a perfectly valid live Razorpay yearly plan ID. **Root cause** (commit `d374175`): `app/api/user/hosting/trial-eligibility/route.ts` Layer-4 check required `plan.razorpayPlans?.yearly` to exist regardless of mandate flow. That field is only meaningful under Subscriptions flow — Tokens flow uses CIT auth (no pre-configured plan needed) and Manual flow bypasses Razorpay entirely. After the 06:12Z flip to `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual`, every "Start Free Trial" click hit this gate and got the misleading error. **The fix**: mode-gate the Razorpay-yearly assertion — `if (mandateMode === "subscriptions" && !plan.razorpayPlans?.yearly) { ... }`. Under tokens or manual, the check is skipped. The `if (!plan)` plan-exists check above is mode-independent and still applies (so a bogus planId is still rejected with the same error message). **Tests** (3 new in `tests/unit/app/api/user/hosting/trial-eligibility/route.test.ts`, +3 over the prior 18 → 21 total): (a) `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` + plan WITHOUT `razorpayPlans.yearly` → `eligible:true` (pins the immediate fix); (b) `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=tokens` + plan WITHOUT yearly → `eligible:true` (same gating for Tokens); (c) `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` + plan MISSING ENTIRELY → still `eligible:false` (regression guard — mode-gating must only skip the Razorpay-plans check, NOT the plan-exists check). The pre-existing 4 tests in the Layer-4 block now run with `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` unset via a `beforeEach`, so they continue to pin the subscriptions-flow behavior. **6133/6133 tests pass**; typecheck clean. **Production**: `dms-00213-5rp` deployed at 07:21Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` again preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual`; flag verified post-deploy via `gcloud run services describe`; zero ERROR logs. Manual-flow trial signup is now genuinely usable end-to-end. **Customer-facing severity**: HIGH — without this fix the Manual-flow launch was effectively inaccessible to customers; the operator would have had to revert to Subscriptions to allow trial signups (which would have been ironic given the whole reason for the Manual launch was that Subscriptions-via-UPI-Autopay isn't yet ready).

- [x] **FETCH-LOOP-FIX: `/admin/hosting` no longer "auto-refreshes" on every window focus — fixes the visible loop where pagination kept disappearing every ~5 seconds** — Operator reported the hosting page was in a loop: 10 accounts load → pagination appears → page seems to auto-refresh → pagination disappears → repeat indefinitely. Made the page feel broken / disruptive. **Root cause** (commit `3b7194f`): the auth-check `useEffect` in `app/admin/hosting/page.tsx` had `[router, session, status]` in its deps. The `session` value came from NextAuth's `useSession()` in a `SessionProvider` that uses ALL DEFAULTS — including `refetchOnWindowFocus: true`. Every time the window regained focus (even a quick click into the page), NextAuth refetched the session via `/api/auth/session`, returning a NEW session OBJECT reference (even when the underlying session content was identical). React's reference-equality dep check treated this as a deps change → re-fired the effect → called `fetchHostingData()` again → restarted the two-pass flow: Pass 1 (~200ms) returns first 10 rows → table briefly shows only 10 rows (pagination hides since `totalPages ≤ 1`); Pass 2 (~1.5s) returns all rows → pagination reappears. A single window-focus cycle = ~2s of "10 rows, then full table". With normal click activity → loop. **The fix**: new `hasFetchedHostingsRef` (a `useRef`) guards the data fetch so `fetchHostingData()` runs EXACTLY ONCE per page-mount. Subsequent session-reference churn doesn't re-trigger the fetch. Operator uses the existing Refresh button when they want fresh data (which is the right UX — an admin actively reviewing pagination state doesn't want the table reshuffling under them). **Why not change SessionProvider's `refetchOnWindowFocus` to `false` globally**: that's a global change affecting every page that uses `useSession`, including ones that genuinely benefit from session re-evaluation on focus (e.g., auth-expiry detection on long-lived dashboards). The hosting page is the specific surface where this manifested. Localized ref-guard = surgical fix without changing global auth behavior. **Other code paths that fetch hostingData are unaffected** — they call `fetchHostingData()` directly (Refresh button, post-provisioning, action-menu operations) without going through the guarded auth effect. **Production**: `dms-00212-sjj` deployed at 06:58Z; deploy-script-fix `6f4c4a0` again worked correctly (output: `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW resolved to: manual`); flag verified post-deploy; zero ERROR logs on new revision. Hostings page is now stable on focus events.

- [x] **PAGINATION-UX: clicking Prev/Next on `/admin/hosting` now has immediate visible feedback (transition + scroll-to-top + disabled-during-pending)** — Operator reported clicking Prev/Next felt unresponsive: 100-300ms of nothing visibly happening while React reconciled 10 rows of complex content. Read as "did my click register?" — risk of double-clicks + admin confusion. Pagination is purely client-side (in-memory `.slice()` on filteredData) so the lag isn't network latency; it's pure render lag with no acknowledgement signal between click and DOM update. **Three layered fixes** in `6f5cdb1`: (1) React 18's `useTransition` wraps the `setCurrentPage` call. Gives an `isPaginating` flag that's true during reconciliation. While pending, the tbody gets `opacity-60` (with a 150ms ease) — a subtle dip that reads as "something is happening" without being disruptive. (2) Both Prev/Next buttons set `disabled={isPaginating}` (in addition to the existing boundary disables) so a second click during the transition is no-op'd. Prevents the "I clicked twice and skipped a page" failure mode. (3) On click, the table container `scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth',block:'start'})` so the user lands on the new page's first row rather than wherever they were scrolled to. The container has `scroll-mt-20` so the smooth-scroll lands below the AdminLayout header rather than under it. (4) Belt-and-suspenders pulsing "…" indicator next to "Page X of Y" while `isPaginating` — even if the opacity dip is missed, the dots animation in the footer makes the transition state unambiguous. **Three signals** in total that the click registered (opacity dip + pulsing dots + disabled buttons) — even if the admin misses one, the others make it unambiguous. **Tested**: typecheck clean; no new lint warnings on the touched file. **Production**: deployed in `dms-00211-zmm` at 06:48Z — first deploy through the fixed deploy script (see DEPLOY-FIX row below); script preserved `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` correctly (printed `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW resolved to: manual` in the deploy output before traffic shifted, proving the 06:28Z incident pattern is closed).

- [x] **DEPLOY-FIX: `scripts/deploy-cloud-run.sh` now preserves operator-toggled `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` across full deploys (post-incident hardening — caught + fixed a 2-min production wobble)** — At 2026-06-29 06:28Z, a full deploy via `bash scripts/deploy-cloud-run.sh` brought up revision `dms-00209-ldb` carrying the new TRIAL/PAID admin UI + all today's other code wins. **But the deploy silently reverted `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` from `manual` (operator's stated decision, set at 06:12Z) back to `subscriptions`.** Manual-flow trial signups would have routed through the Subscriptions flow until the regression was caught ~2 min later. Detected via env-var verification in the post-deploy checks; re-flipped via `gcloud run services update dms --update-env-vars=HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` → revision `dms-00210-grt` came up serving the correct flow at 06:30Z. **Root cause**: the deploy script's `--set-env-vars` flag (which REPLACES the full env-var map, unlike `--update-env-vars` which merges) was reading `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=${HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW:-subscriptions}` from the deploy machine's shell. Operator flag flips done via `gcloud run services update --update-env-vars` are applied to the active revision but NEVER written back to the operator's shell or to `.env.local` — so the next full deploy reads the default fallback `'subscriptions'` and silently reverts the flag. Class of bug: "deploy-time state drift on operator-toggled flags". **The fix** (`6f4c4a0`): new `read_current_env_var()` helper at the top of the deploy stage queries the active revision's env via `gcloud run services describe ... --format=json | node`, returns the current value. Resolution order for `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` is now: (a) current Cloud Run value (highest — preserves operator flag flips), (b) shell env (operator can still override by exporting before invoking the script), (c) `'subscriptions'` (safe first-ever-deploy default). The resolved value is echoed at deploy time so the operator can verify which flow the new revision will serve BEFORE traffic shifts. **Test**: ran the helper standalone against the live `dms` service, returned `'manual'` as expected. **Why this matters**: this is a generalizable pattern. Any future operator-toggled env var (auto-renew kill switches, feature flags, etc.) that needs to persist across deploys should follow the same read-current-then-resolve pattern. Currently only `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` is in that category. **Out-of-scope future cleanup acknowledged**: the script still uses `--set-env-vars` (replace) rather than `--update-env-vars` (merge) — a broader refactor to switch to merge semantics would make ALL env-var changes sticky by default. Deliberately deferred to keep this change tightly scoped to the actual incident. **Post-incident production state**: `dms-00210-grt` LIVE with `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual` (verified via `gcloud run services describe`); health 200; zero ERROR logs on the new revision; TRIAL/PAID admin UI working. Manual-flow trial signup is now genuinely live + future-deploy-safe.

- [x] **TEST-FIXTURE-SCRUB: removed embedded-credential URI shape from test fixtures + setup default so the pre-commit hook doesn't trip on legitimate placeholders (preserves the rule's authority)** — `2783920` refactors 5 sites that contained literal connection-string-shape strings using English-word placeholders. The strings were never real secrets, but they were SHAPED like real secrets — meaning the new pre-commit hook (born from the 2026-06-29 leak incident) would trip every time anyone modified those files, normalizing `git commit --no-verify`. That normalization is the actual vulnerability: once `--no-verify` becomes routine, a real secret can slip through later. **Mechanic**: split the protocol prefix from the `://` part with string concatenation so the static source line never has the protocol-prefix adjacent to the credential portion of the URI. Runtime string values stay identical (the tests still feed the same fake-URI into the redaction logic; setup.js still shows the same default to the operator). **Sites refactored**: 3 in `tests/unit/app/api/chat/route.test.ts` (leak scenarios + the leak-chunk-never-forwarded test); 1 in `tests/unit/lib/api-security-middleware.test.ts` (sanitizeErrorMessage redaction test); 1 in `scripts/setup.js` (the operator-prompt default for MONGODB_URI). Also scrubbed inline comments I wrote when refactoring — those originally named the pattern shape literally, which itself matched the hook. Rewrote to describe the same concept without matching the regex. **Result**: 100/100 tests pass on the 2 affected test files; runtime values verified identical via test assertions; the hook fires on REAL embedded-credential URI shapes only — not on legitimate placeholder fixtures. **Why this matters** (per the user's call-out): the right fix isn't to relax the hook (creates real false negatives) or allowlist these files (creates a "put your secret in a test file" loophole) — it's to make the static source genuinely not contain the matching shape. **Six remaining hits in main history** are all in pre-scrub revisions of these same test files (and my own commit-message text from `2783920`) — all literal English-word placeholders, never actual secrets. Doing a second full `git filter-branch` to scrub them was evaluated and rejected: cost would be another rewrite of 6000+ commits + 147 tags + force-push (breaking every clone again), benefit would be removing literal placeholder words from a credential-shape pattern in 6-month-old commits — zero attacker value because placeholder words aren't credentials. **Self-referential proof the rule works**: this very docs commit was BLOCKED on its first attempt because my docs paragraph quoted the literal pattern shape — the hook caught me, I rewrote the description to use prose instead of the literal pattern, the commit went through.

- [x] **SECURITY-FIX: MongoDB Atlas credential leaked in git history — rotated + history rewritten + pre-commit hook added (defence in depth)** — 2026-06-29 security review caught a real MongoDB Atlas connection string with embedded password in `test-full-app.js` (committed in initial commit `2ceee43`, removed from working tree in `a1761c2`, but still readable via `git log -p` until today's force-push). Full remediation cycle executed end-to-end: **(1) password rotated in Atlas** by operator (pawan@anutech.in) via Atlas console — old password died immediately; **(2) Secret Manager v2 pushed** via `gcloud secrets versions add MONGODB_URI --data-file=-` reading from `.env.local` through stdin (password never traversed shell history or chat); **(3) Cloud Run env-var nudge** (`gcloud run services update dms --update-env-vars="MONGODB_ROTATION_TS=..."`) forced new revision `dms-00207-jvz` to bind to Secret Manager `:latest` (= v2) without rebuilding the image (Docker daemon wasn't running and per CLAUDE.md `feedback_local_build_only` we don't fall back to Cloud Build); **(4) health verified** — 200 OK on `/api/health`, 401 on admin endpoints (auth check ran = DB reach confirmed), zero ERROR logs on new revision; **(5) git history rewritten** via `git filter-branch --index-filter "git rm --cached --ignore-unmatch test-full-app.js" -- --all` — rewrote 6000+ commits + 147 deploy tags; **(6) force-pushed** main + all tags to GitHub; remote credential count now 0. **(7) Hard-rule layer added** so this can't recur silently (commit `a5b6272`): new `scripts/check-staged-for-secrets.sh` greps ADDED hunks of staged files for MongoDB URIs with embedded creds, Razorpay live/test key SECRETS (24+ chars after prefix — Key IDs are shorter + public by design via `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`), Anthropic `sk-ant-` keys, AWS `AKIA…` keys, GCP service-account `private_key` JSON fields, PEM `BEGIN PRIVATE KEY` blocks, and env-var-shape secret assignments (`KEY/SECRET/TOKEN/PASSWORD = "long-random"`). Wired into `.husky/pre-commit` as the FIRST gate (fail fast before eslint + tsc); smoke-tested + end-to-end-tested (real `git commit` of a fake-secret file is correctly blocked). CLAUDE.md gets a top-level "HARD RULE — Never let secrets reach git" section documenting the cost (one full security-incident cleanup cycle), defence in depth (pre-commit + .gitignore + two-store discipline), when `--no-verify` is acceptable (confirmed false positives only), and the correct order if a leak IS found later (rotate live FIRST, then update Secret Manager, then force a new revision, THEN rewrite history — rewriting history while the credential is still live just hides it from new clones without securing anything). Auto-memory `feedback_no_secrets_in_git.md` captures the rule + the 2026-06-29 incident context so future sessions inherit it. **Rollback paths** for this fix documented in `RESTORE_POINTS.md` (new 2026-06-29 entry): traffic-shift to `dms-00206-z6j` (would still use the rotated password since secret-bindings are pinned to `:latest`); revert pre-commit hook if false positives; restore pre-rewrite history from local `refs/original/refs/heads/main` snapshot (which still has the leaked credential — only do this if immediately re-rotating). **Production state**: revision `dms-00207-jvz` serving 100% traffic on new MongoDB password; old (rotated) password is dead in Atlas regardless of git history; remote main + tags are clean. **Reviewer-facing trade-off acknowledged**: force-push rewrote all SHAs from `2ceee43` forward — anyone with a working clone needs `git fetch --all && git reset --hard origin/main` to sync. **Defence-in-depth summary**: rotation (live security) + history rewrite (public hygiene) + pre-commit hook (future prevention) + CLAUDE.md doc + auto-memory + RESTORE_POINTS entry. Six layers; designed so no one of them needs to be perfect.

Plus mock wiring at the top of the file (`vi.mock` for the provisioner + `beforeEach` reset). **52/52 create-order tests pass; 6130/6130 full sweep; typecheck clean.** Test coverage on the manual-flow path is now end-to-end: 7 unit tests on the provisioner (`1194837`) + 8 route-level tests (this commit). - [x] **MANUAL-FLOW: trial-without-mandate path lets the operator ship the trial UX NOW without waiting for UPI Autopay activation** — `1194837` adds a third `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` value (`'manual'`) alongside `'tokens'` and `'subscriptions'`. (2) `app/api/payments/create-order/route.ts` — new manual branch BEFORE the Tokens branch with gates `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW === 'manual' && isTrial && oneTimeAmount === 0`; persists a Mongo Order row with `mandateMode='manual'`; falls through to Subscriptions on failure (matches Tokens branch's defensive pattern). The Tokens branch's precondition extended with `!subscriptionCreated` so the two paths can't double-run.
