(3) `app/checkout/page.tsx` — new branch BEFORE Tokens/Subscriptions checks: when `manualMode || mandateMode === 'manual'`, skip `razorpay.open()` entirely, write success entry to safeSessionStorage, clear cart, redirect to `/payment-success`. No verify-payment call needed (no payment to verify; Hosting was already provisioned server-side). **Tests** (7 new in `manual-trial-provisioner.test.ts`): manual-flow discriminator fields (`billingType='manual'` + `autoRenew=false` + no Razorpay fields), expiry = now + 15d, `next_action_at` = expiry - 2d (renewal reminder pickup), return shape, planName/serverPackage fallbacks, createHosting error propagation. 7/7 new tests; 6122/6122 full sweep; typecheck clean. **Operator wire-up (when ready)**: `gcloud run services update dms --project=speedy-unison-453807-e9 --update-env-vars=HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=manual`. **To flip back to Tokens after UPI Autopay activates** (~2026-07-08): same command with `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=tokens`. Both zero-downtime; customers keep their original billing path on each flip. **Production safety**: `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` remains the default in prod; nothing changes until the operator explicitly flips. Once flipped to `manual`, new trial signups skip Razorpay; existing Subscriptions-mode customers continue on their existing mandates with no migration or double-billing risk. Cloud Scheduler crons for Tokens-flow keep running but find no rows to process (no Hosting has `razorpayTokenId` under manual mode).

- [x] **AUDIT-2-FIX: HIGH-severity 12x undercharge bug on trial-to-paid first MIT — caught by a SECOND self-audit pass after the renewals dashboard shipped** — `f054c20` fixes a real money-loss bug that would have hit every Tokens-flow trial-conversion customer once the flag is flipped. **The bug**: `lib/services/payment/recurring-charge-service.ts` inferred `isYearly` purely from `monthsBetween(startDate, expiryDate) >= 11`. For a trial Hosting (created with `startDate=now` + `expiryDate=now+15days` per `lib/services/payment/tokens-trial-provisioner.ts:96-97`), that delta is 0 or 1 month → `isYearly=false` → customer charged the MONTHLY amount (₹49.99) when they signed up for the YEARLY plan (₹599.88). **12x revenue undercharge per customer**, compounded by the expiry-extension also using the wrong cycle (30 days instead of 1 year), locking the customer into monthly billing forever. **Why trials are yearly-only** (confirming this is a real bug, not a design ambiguity): `app/api/payments/create-order/route.ts:174-176` explicitly rejects monthly trial orders ("Trial is only available for yearly hosting plans"). **Why the first self-audit (d86dd01) missed this**: the existing test suite's `makeHosting()` default uses startDate=2026-06-01 + expiryDate=2027-06-01 (exactly 12 months), so the date heuristic correctly returns `isYearly=true` for all happy-path tests. The trial-first-MIT case (15-day gap) was never exercised. **The fix**: `isYearly = isTrialFirstCharge || monthsSinceStart >= 11` where `isTrialFirstCharge = hosting.isTrial === true`. On successful first MIT, expiry is hard-reset to `now + 1 year` (matches the Subscriptions-flow handler's trial-transition pattern at `lib/services/payment/webhook-handlers.ts:230-234`) and `hosting.isTrial` flips to false so the admin "TRIAL" badge stops and subsequent renewals fall back to the date-heuristic path correctly. **Tests** (3 new under "TRIAL-TO-PAID transition (audit-2 fix)" describe block): (1) trial first MIT (isTrial=true, 15d gap) → YEARLY ₹599.88 not monthly ₹49.99 (this would have failed pre-fix); (2) trial first MIT success → newExpiry = now + 1y + isTrial flips to false; (3) non-trial yearly renewal → still YEARLY + expiry += 1y from current expiry (regression guard, proves the fix didn't break the ongoing-renewal path). 22/22 service tests pass; 6115/6115 full sweep; typecheck clean. **Self-audit hit rate so far**: 3 real HIGH bugs / 9 audit findings across 2 passes = ~33% real-bug rate. Worth doing self-audit passes after non-trivial code drops. **Production safety**: visible no-op today (HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions default + zero Tokens-flow Hostings exist); when flag flips, the very first trial-conversion customer hits the correct yearly charge from the first MIT rather than the broken 12x-undercharge path.

- [x] **RENEWALS-DASHBOARD: cross-mode `/admin/renewals` page closes the LAST admin-UI audit finding (6 of 6 ✅)** — `3ed1180` ships the cross-mode upcoming-renewals dashboard that the Tokens-flow admin-UI audit raised as Finding 6 (MEDIUM, deferred-as-nice-to-have). Unions Hostings on all three billing rails (Tokens / Subscriptions / Manual) into a single chronological list filtered by expiry-date window. Source of truth is the Hosting collection; chargeDate per-row is computed as `expiryDate - 1 day` for Tokens (matches `CHARGE_LOOKAHEAD_DAYS=1` in the MIT cron) and `expiryDate` for Subscriptions / Manual (Razorpay or operator owns those charge dates). **Why this matters even though `/admin/recurring-charges` already covers Tokens triage**: `/admin/recurring-charges` is per-MIT-attempt and Tokens-only; `/admin/renewals` is per-Hosting and cross-mode, so an operator asking "what renewals fire across our whole book in the next 7 days?" can answer that in a single view rather than mentally merging two surfaces. **Page layout** mirrors `/admin/recurring-charges`: AdminLayout shell + filter bar (mode + window) + 3-card summary (Tokens / Subscription / Manual counts spanning the WHOLE window not the filtered slice) + 7-column table (Charge date with "in Nd" relative + past-charge red highlight, Domain + TRIAL pill, User, Plan, Amount with INR formatting, Mode badge with "attempts" pivot link for Tokens rows linking to `/admin/recurring-charges`, Hosting status). **Implementation**: new `app/api/admin/renewals/route.ts` (admin-gated 403 / `status in [active, expired]` + expiry-within-window filter / batched User + HostingPlan joins / aggregate counts across whole window for the summary cards / hasMore pagination signal); new `app/admin/renewals/page.tsx` (mirrors recurring-charges UX patterns); `components/admin/AdminLayout.tsx` sidebar gains a "Renewals" entry with `lucide-react` `CalendarClock` icon, placed immediately after "Recurring Charges" so the two Tokens-flow-adjacent items group. **Tests** (16 new in `tests/unit/app/api/admin/renewals/route.test.ts`, +16 across +1 new file): 403 anti-info-leak, default 30d window, 7d/90d respected + invalid→30d fallback, mode=tokens / subscriptions / manual filter clause shapes pinned, mode=garbage→graceful, Tokens row chargeDate=expiry-1d (numerically precise to 5 places), Subscriptions chargeDate=expiry, Manual chargeDate=expiry, **migrated-customer edge case (BOTH IDs set) → 'tokens' wins (mirrors the discriminator semantics in 5ea21a6 + d4b6a64 + 89d569b — uniform across all 4 surfaces that derive mandateMode)**, User+HostingPlan joins surface correctly, **aggregate counts span the whole window not the filtered slice** (3 Tokens + 2 Subs + 1 Manual distribution preserved when filter=tokens active), missing User/Plan join → defensive "(deleted)" + null defaults, hasMore=true at limit. 16/16 new tests pass; **6112/6112 full sweep**; typecheck clean. **Audit cycle: 6 of 6 ✅** — the close-out banner at the top of this file has been updated to reflect the final state; the original 5-of-6 narrative is preserved verbatim there for the audit trail. **Production safety**: page renders today against existing customer hostings (Subscriptions-flow rows will appear in the Subscriptions column even before the Tokens-mode flag flip); Manual rows show up too. When the flag flips, Tokens-flow signups start appearing in the Tokens column automatically. Read-only — no mutations or side effects.

- [x] **CUSTOMER-RENEWAL-DATE: dashboard now shows the approximate auto-renewal charge date on each Tokens-flow hosting card** — `3b0ce3c` extends the amber payment-validity callout on the customer hosting card (from `89d569b`) with an inline line: "Auto-renewal charge: around `{date}`." The date is computed client-side as `expiryDate − 1 day`, matching the cron's `CHARGE_LOOKAHEAD_DAYS=1` constant. Under the hard 1-attempt rule, this timing is load-bearing — customers need to know which week to verify their saved card is valid + has balance, because any failure on that day causes immediate suspension. The previous note told them they needed a valid card but left them to compute WHEN it mattered. **Design choices**: (a) "around `X`" not "on `X`" — the cron runs daily at 22:00 UTC and sweeps any Hosting with `expiryDate <= today + 1day`, so the actual charge moment is anywhere in the 24h before the displayed date. "Around" honors that imprecision without overpromising a specific timestamp a customer might set a phone alarm to; (b) date formatted via `toLocaleDateString('en-IN', ...)` → "11 July 2026" (matches the grace-period email pattern); (c) computed client-side from existing `expires_at` — no route-shape change; defensive NaN guard if the parse fails; (d) only renders when `mandateMode === 'tokens'` AND `status !== 'expired'` (the conditional wrapping was already in place from 89d569b); (e) **amount is intentionally NOT shown** — the customer knows their plan price from signup; the safer move is to not commit to a specific rupee figure that could disagree with the actual cron-computed charge if the cycle inference (isYearly) lands differently than the customer's mental model. The date is the load-bearing piece. **No new tests**: pure client-side display from existing data; the underlying `expires_at` is already pinned by `tests/unit/app/api/user/hosting/stats/route.test.ts`. Typecheck clean; lint clean on the touched file. **Production safety**: visible no-op today (`HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` default, no Tokens-flow customers exist); appears the moment a Tokens-flow signup completes their CIT auth + DA provisioning and loads the dashboard.

- [x] **CUSTOMER-COMMS-SUSPENSION: suspension email rebuilt — Tokens-flow customers now get a concrete re-subscribe recovery block instead of a 2-sentence "contact support" stub; closes the customer-comms triad (welcome + dashboard + suspension)** — `1959e27` rebuilds `sendServiceSuspensionEmail` in `lib/email/domain.ts` from a 2-sentence generic stub ("Your X has been suspended. Please contact support.") into a properly-structured template matching the grace-period email pattern (red gradient header band + amber callout + dashboard CTA + support footer), and adds a Tokens-flow-specific recovery block gated on `mandateMode='tokens'`. The previous wording was dramatically under-informative for customers under the hard 1-attempt rule whose service was suspended immediately after their single recurring charge failed — "contact support" didn't tell them the mandate was dead and that re-subscribing was the actual fix. **What the Tokens-flow customer now sees**: (1) red gradient "Service Suspended" header band with the domain name; (2) body line explaining the failed renewal payment; (3) amber "What happened" callout explaining the 1-attempt policy in plain language ("each recurring charge is attempted once — if it fails, service is suspended immediately and the payment mandate is closed") with 3 common-cause hints (card expired/replaced, insufficient balance, UPI mandate revoked) so the customer can self-diagnose; (4) "How to restore service" section with concrete CTA — sign in, re-subscribe with a fresh card or UPI ID, new mandate is set up, service reactivates as soon as the first charge succeeds; (5) big "Restore Service" button linking to `/dashboard/hosting`; (6) support contact in the footer for anything more nuanced. **Anti-misinform guard**: when `mandateMode` is anything else (subscriptions, manual, unset for back-compat), the email renders the upgraded template structure but with the generic "contact support" copy + "Open Dashboard" CTA instead — Razorpay-managed Subscriptions retries don't follow the 1-attempt rule, manual customers don't auto-renew at all, and legacy non-Tokens callers shouldn't see Tokens-specific wording. **Tests** (5 new + 1 preserved in `tests/unit/lib/email/domain.test.ts`; 1 updated in `tests/unit/lib/services/payment/recurring-charge-service.test.ts`): subject pin (preserved), body content pin, mandateMode=tokens → recovery block + "What happened" + "re-subscribe" + 3 common causes + "Restore Service" CTA all present, mandateMode UNSET → generic copy + "Open Dashboard" + no Tokens wording, mandateMode=subscriptions → generic copy, mandateMode=manual → generic copy; existing recurring-charge-service abandonment test now asserts `mandateMode: 'tokens'` is passed through to the email send. 38/38 affected tests; 6096/6096 full sweep; typecheck clean. **This closes the customer-comms triad** started by `26b0f51` (welcome email — discovery surface) + `89d569b` (dashboard note — persistent surface) + this commit (suspension email — recovery surface). All three customer-facing moments — service-activated, viewing-hosting, service-suspended — now set the strict-policy expectation accurately and offer the right recovery path. **Production safety**: visible no-op today; when the flag flips and a Tokens-flow MIT charge fails for the first time, the affected customer sees the new email from the very first send.

- [x] **CUSTOMER-COMMS-DASHBOARD: customer hosting page now shows the same payment-validity note for Tokens-flow customers (parallel to the welcome-email callout — persistent surface vs one-shot)** — `89d569b` adds an amber payment-validity note to each Tokens-flow customer's hosting card on `app/dashboard/hosting/page.tsx`. The welcome email from `26b0f51` is one-shot and easy to lose; the dashboard is the persistent surface customers return to whenever they check their hosting. Both surfaces should set the strict 1-attempt expectation. **Discriminator**: new derived `mandateMode: 'tokens' | 'subscriptions' | 'manual'` field on `/api/user/hosting/stats` per row, computed server-side from `hostingRecord.razorpayTokenId` / `.subscriptionId`. 'tokens' wins when both are set (covers the rare customer-migrated-from-Subscriptions edge case where both IDs sit on the record). **Note placement**: between the main card body and the footer info bar — only when `mandateMode='tokens'` AND `status !== 'expired'` (no point telling an already-expired customer to keep their payment valid; their service is already suspended). **Wording matches the welcome email** so both surfaces tell the same story; uses lucide-react `AlertTriangle` (already imported) + amber-50 background / amber-200 border matching the rest of the app's warning-banner pattern. **Anti-misinform guard**: Subscriptions-flow customers (whose retries are managed by Razorpay server-side) and manual customers (no auto-renewal) never see this note — their `mandateMode` resolves to 'subscriptions' or 'manual', conditionally gating the render. **Tests** (5 new in `tests/unit/app/api/user/hosting/stats/route.test.ts`, +5 over the previous 34 → 39 total): tokens-discriminator-set → 'tokens'; subscription-only → 'subscriptions'; manual (neither set) → 'manual'; both set → 'tokens' wins (migrated customer); no matching hosting record → 'manual' (defensive default). 39/39 customer-stats tests pass; 6091/6091 full sweep; typecheck clean. **Production safety**: visible no-op today since `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` is default + zero Tokens-flow customers exist in prod; every existing customer's `mandateMode` resolves to 'subscriptions' or 'manual' so the dashboard note never renders. When the flag flips, new Tokens-flow signups see the note on their hosting card from the very first dashboard load with no operator action needed. **Together with 26b0f51**, the strict 1-attempt policy expectation is now communicated on BOTH the discovery surface (welcome email) AND the persistent surface (dashboard) — customers can't say they weren't told.

- [x] **CUSTOMER-COMMS: Tokens-flow welcome email now sets the 1-attempt-then-suspend expectation up front (so customers know the strict policy before their first MIT charge fires)** — `26b0f51` adds an amber payment-validity callout block to `sendHostingProvisionedEmail` in `lib/email/hosting.ts`, gated on a new optional `mandateMode: 'tokens' | 'subscriptions' | 'manual'` field on the `hostingDetails` object. When `mandateMode='tokens'`, the callout renders between the green "Service Activated" banner and the account-details table — the natural attention moment when customers are reading the welcome and payment expectations are top-of-mind. **Wording is matter-of-fact** (not alarmist): "Keep Your Payment Method Valid" heading; explainer "Your hosting renews automatically. We will charge your saved payment method once when your billing cycle is due."; the strict-policy note "If that single charge fails (declined card, insufficient balance, revoked mandate), your service will be suspended and you'll need to re-subscribe with a new payment method to restore it. Please ensure the card or UPI ID on file stays valid — especially when it's close to expiry or you've recently changed banks." **Anti-misinform guard**: callout renders ONLY for `mandateMode='tokens'`. Subscriptions-flow customers (whose retries are still managed by Razorpay's Subscriptions API server-side, NOT our 1-attempt rule) and manual customers (no auto-renewal) explicitly DO NOT see the callout — would be misleading otherwise. Default (omitted field) = no callout, preserving the original email shape for the 4 non-Tokens call sites (provisioner-hosting, renewal, pending-hostings, admin/hosting/provision). **Tokens-flow call site updated**: `lib/services/payment/tokens-da-provisioner.ts` now passes `mandateMode: 'tokens'` when the DA-provisioning cron fires the welcome email after a Hosting goes from `status='pending'` → `'active'`. **Tests** (4 new + 18 preserved in `hosting.test.ts`; the per-mode test matrix is the value here): tokens→callout-present; subscriptions→callout-absent (anti-misinform); manual→callout-absent; UNSET→callout-absent (back-compat for legacy callers). 22/22 hosting.test.ts pass; 6086/6086 full sweep clean; typecheck clean. **Production safety**: visible no-op today since `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` is default + no Tokens-flow customers exist; when the flag is flipped, new Tokens-flow signups see the callout from the very first welcome-email send with no operator action needed. **Why this matters**: the 1-attempt policy is a meaningful change from competitor norms (Razorpay's own Subscriptions API + Google/Netflix soft-grace ~7-day retry windows). Customers reading the policy after their first charge fails would feel ambushed; reading it on day-15-of-trial when service-just-activated is much more reasonable.

- [x] **DOC-REFRESH: `docs/razorpay-tokens-migration.md` updated end-to-end to match shipped state — IMPLEMENTED (dormant) instead of DRAFT; §3.5 supersedes the original soft-grace [T+1, T+3, T+7] retry policy with the current uniform 1-attempt rule** — `b2e0c8a` refreshes the canonical design doc to match the code that landed 2026-06-26. The doc was written 2026-06-25 in DRAFT status while the original design called for a 4-attempt soft-grace window matching Razorpay's Subscriptions-API behaviour. Implementation shipped throughout the day; the retry policy was then superseded by the uniform 1-attempt rule (5ea21a6 first-charge, d4b6a64 extended to renewals). Without this refresh the doc was becoming a misleading reference — a Phase 3 reviewer would have read the soft-grace spec, traced it to the code, and found a 1-attempt policy instead, with no in-doc explanation. **Updates**: (a) Status header rewritten — IMPLEMENTED (DORMANT IN PROD behind `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` flag) instead of DRAFT, with every implementation + audit-fix + tooling + UI + policy commit hash listed so a future reader can trace the code path against design intent; implementation gate removed (code shipped ahead of cutover Steps 4-5; flag-flip rollout still gated on those + operator-side Cloud Scheduler wire-up); (b) §3.3 webhook-flow table — `payment.failed` on MIT row updated to "mark Hosting expired, suspend DA user IMMEDIATELY on the first failure (no retry exhaustion)" rather than the old "after retry exhaustion" phrasing; (c) §3.5 retry-and-dunning section rewritten end-to-end and renamed to "Abandonment + dunning logic" — documents BOTH policies (original soft-grace AND current hard rule) with a side-by-side comparison table so a future reader sees the evolution rather than just the current snapshot, captures the operator's rationale for the policy change, documents the state machine + the three best-effort abandonment actions + the `priorSuccessCount` discriminator (kept for audit-trail differentiation even though both branches share the same technical policy) + the reversibility path (bump `RENEWAL_MAX_ATTEMPTS` back to 4 to re-activate the dormant retry-claim + retry-scheduling branches with no further code changes); (d) §6 test-plan table — "retry-attempt scheduling" → "abandonment-on-first-failure path"; (e) §8 effort estimate — "retry/dunning logic" → "abandonment/dunning logic" for the cron row. **No code changes** — pure doc refresh. **Why this matters for Phase 3**: the design doc is the source-of-truth for what integration tests should verify against TEST-mode Razorpay; if it disagrees with the code, the test plan would be wrong from the start.

- [x] **TEST-PIN: lock in the Tokens-flow billing-fields contract on `/api/admin/hosting/stats` (mutation-verified — refactor that drops any of `razorpayTokenId` / `subscriptionId` / `razorpayCustomerId` / `isTrial` / `billingType` now fails 5 tests immediately)** — `13791f3` adds a new "Tokens-flow / Subscriptions-flow fields contract" describe block (6 tests, +6 over the previous 31 → 37 total) to `tests/unit/app/api/admin/hosting/stats/route.test.ts`. The 5 fields are what the `/admin/hosting` mandate-mode badge column (shipped 5f93f85) AND the hosting detail-modal "Recurring Payment" amber-card section (shipped a570a7d) both read to render. Without this pin, a future refactor that silently dropped any one of these fields from the response would pass typecheck (they're optional on `HostingData` + `HostingRecord`) AND lint, but every admin row would silently revert to the gray MANUAL badge + the detail modal's amber card would show blank — no errors, no signal, no audit trail; operators would lose visibility without knowing. **Scenarios pinned**: (i) LIVE mode + Tokens-flow Hosting (`razorpayTokenId` set, `isTrial=true`) → all 5 fields surface with the right values; (ii) LIVE mode + Subscriptions-flow Hosting (`subscriptionId` set, Tokens fields null) → subscription badge fields populated, Tokens fields **strictly null** (not undefined); (iii) LIVE mode + manual-billing Hosting (no Razorpay IDs) → all Tokens fields null + `billingType=null` (badge renders MANUAL); (iv) LIVE mode + UNLINKED DA account (no matching Hosting record at all) → fields default to null/false safely, defending against a future refactor that could switch the `??` coalesce to a coercion bug returning undefined (which would silently render TOKENS on orphan DA accounts that have no mandate); (v) DB FALLBACK mode (DA unreachable, route walks the Hosting-collection path instead) → billing fields still present + correct, pinning the separate code path; (vi) regression guard via explicit `in` operator — guards against a refactor that deletes the field entirely (which the `=== null` checks above would NOT catch, since `row.razorpayTokenId ? 'TOKENS' : 'MANUAL'` ternary still works coincidentally when the field is absent → silent revert with no test failure). **Mutation verified**: temporarily commented out the LIVE-mode `razorpayTokenId: hostingRecord?.razorpayTokenId ?? null` line in `app/api/admin/hosting/stats/route.ts` and confirmed 5 tests fail immediately (3 LIVE-mode + 1 DB-fallback + 1 regression-guard `in` test); route restored cleanly; final test count 37/37. **Cost**: ~15 min of work for ongoing protection of a contract that 2 admin UI surfaces depend on. **Production safety**: zero impact on running code — pure test surface addition.

- [x] **POLICY-EXTEND: hard 1-attempt rule extended to renewals — every recurring charge now gets exactly one attempt then suspend (uniform policy, no soft-grace anywhere)** — `d4b6a64` expands the policy shipped in 5ea21a6 to cover both branches of the MIT charge service. The trial-vs-renewal split was an early operator decision (5ea21a6 kept renewals on the [T+1, T+3, T+7] day soft-grace 4-attempt window); after a re-think the decision is to apply the strict rule across the board. Rationale: simpler customer expectation (one rule, not two), faster surfacing of mandate failures so operators can reach out to high-value customers proactively, lower cron load (no T+1/T+3/T+7 follow-up sweeps), and trial signups + long-term customers face the same payment-method validity requirement. **Code change is minimal** — `RENEWAL_MAX_ATTEMPTS` was bumped from 4 → 1 (matches `FIRST_CHARGE_MAX_ATTEMPTS`); `RETRY_BACKOFF_DAYS` is preserved at [1, 3, 7] as a dormant constant with a docstring noting it's kept for future-restore use case; the retry-claim + retry-scheduling branches are now unreachable but kept in place (if the operator later wants to restore soft-grace for either branch, bump the constant + the dormant code wakes up). **Trial-vs-renewal differentiation KEPT** — even though both branches share the same 1-attempt technical policy, the `priorSuccessCount` discriminator is preserved because the operational follow-up differs: trial-conversion fails are typically non-paying signups (no outreach needed), while a long-term customer's mandate dying may warrant proactive support contact (Razorpay mandate timeout? card replaced and not updated?). The distinction surfaces visually in the admin UI (purple "1/1" pill for trial-conversion, blue "1/1" pill for renewal) and in log messages + DA suspend reasons + integration-health hints. **Code surface**: `lib/services/payment/recurring-charge-service.ts` (constants block + abandonment log + DA suspend reason), `app/api/admin/recurring-charges/route.ts` (per-row `maxAttempts` always 1; `wasFirstPostTrial` preserved for UI differentiation), `app/admin/recurring-charges/page.tsx` (renewal-row color flipped gray → blue + tooltip rewritten for both branches), `app/api/admin/integration-health/route.ts` (abandonment + retry hint paragraphs rewritten to describe the unified policy). **Tests** (3 updated, 0 net new): "first failure → 'retry_scheduled'" flipped to "RENEWAL first failure → 'abandoned' on attempt 1 (hard rule extended to renewals)"; "RENEWAL (priorSuccessCount >= 1) failure on attempt 1 → 'retry_scheduled'" flipped to "→ 'abandoned' on first attempt (unified hard rule extended to renewals)" + asserts renewal-specific DA reason; DA suspend reason assertion in the legacy 4-attempt-abandonment test updated to match the new wording; integration-health hint pins updated to assert unified-policy phrasing. All 6076 tests still pass; full typecheck clean. **Customer-facing impact**: trial customers unchanged (same as 5ea21a6); **EXISTING PAYING CUSTOMERS: BEHAVIOR CHANGE** — a single failed renewal charge now suspends service immediately rather than triggering the 9-day [T+1, T+3, T+7] retry window. Customers should be made aware their payment method on file must remain valid. Recovery path unchanged: re-subscribe with a new CIT auth + new mandate. **Production safety**: still a visible no-op today (`HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` default; zero `RecurringChargeAttempt` rows in prod); when the flag is flipped, all recurring charges follow the unified 1-attempt rule from the very first cron run with no migration or backfill needed. **Reversibility**: bumping `RENEWAL_MAX_ATTEMPTS` back to 4 (or any value) in `recurring-charge-service.ts` re-activates the dormant soft-grace retry path without further code changes.

- [x] **POLICY: hard rule — first post-trial MIT charge gets 1 attempt then suspend (trial-to-paid conversion failures abandon immediately; renewals keep the 4-attempt soft-grace)** — `5ea21a6` changes the Tokens-flow MIT charge service at `lib/services/payment/recurring-charge-service.ts` to apply a stricter abandonment policy when the FIRST recurring charge after a free trial fails. Operator decision: trial signups with stale/fraudulent mandates or non-paying cards should be filtered out at the conversion gate rather than absorb a 9-day soft-grace window (which would effectively give those signups free hosting beyond the trial). The change is scoped — existing paying customers (those with at least one prior succeeded recurring charge for the same hosting) keep the existing [T+1, T+3, T+7] day soft-grace retry policy, because penalising a 2-year customer for a single transient decline would be worse UX than competitors. **Discriminator**: `RecurringChargeAttempt.countDocuments({hostingId, status: 'succeeded'})` at the start of each charge. Zero prior successes → first-charge → `FIRST_CHARGE_MAX_ATTEMPTS=1` → fail = immediate abandon. Non-zero prior successes → renewal → `RENEWAL_MAX_ATTEMPTS=4` → fail schedules retry. Both branches share the same abandonment-side effects (DA suspend + Hosting.status='expired' + suspension email). **Code surface**: (a) `recurring-charge-service.ts` introduces `FIRST_CHARGE_MAX_ATTEMPTS` constant with full docstring explaining the rule rationale, renames the old `MAX_ATTEMPTS` constant to `RENEWAL_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, computes `maxAttempts` per-call, and the abandonment-threshold check + log messages + DA suspend reason strings now differentiate trial-conversion ("trial→paid conversion failed on first attempt") from renewal-exhausted ("after N attempts renewal retry exhausted"); (b) `app/api/admin/recurring-charges/route.ts` adds a third batched lookup (aggregate succeeded attempts by hostingId → earliestSucceededAt) and computes `wasFirstPostTrial` + `maxAttempts` per row so the UI can render the right denominator; (c) `app/admin/recurring-charges/page.tsx` renders `N / row.maxAttempts` (1 or 4) with a tooltip explaining the policy and purple text for trial-conversion rows so the dual policy is visible at a glance; (d) `app/api/admin/integration-health/route.ts` both Razorpay-recurring hint paragraphs rewritten to describe both abandonment paths (first-charge hard rule + renewal-exhausted soft-grace) — the retry-scheduled hint now explicitly notes "first post-trial charges get no retries", and the synthesised message format drops the hardcoded "/4" suffix since the denominator is no longer fixed. **Tests** (3 new + 2 hint-text updates, no regressions across 6076 tests): "FIRST POST-TRIAL CHARGE failure → abandoned on attempt 1 + DA suspended + Hosting expired + suspension email sent" (pins the policy: `status='abandoned'`, `attemptCount=1`, `nextAttemptAt undefined`, DA reason matches trial-conversion regex); "FIRST POST-TRIAL CHARGE does NOT schedule retry — straight to abandon (regression guard)"; "RENEWAL (priorSuccessCount >= 1) failure on attempt 1 → 'retry_scheduled', not abandoned (existing 4-attempt soft-grace preserved)" — negative control proving the renewal soft-grace policy is intact; updated existing route tests to pin both branches of the dual-path hint text so future regressions surface. **Production safety**: visible no-op today since `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` is default and no `RecurringChargeAttempt` rows exist in prod; when the flag is flipped, behavior matches the new policy from the very first cron run with no migration or backfill needed. **Customer-facing change**: a trial signup with a flaky card will now see "Your hosting was suspended — please re-subscribe" on day 15 instead of day 25; a long-term paying customer with a transient card decline will still get the 9-day window to recover. **Operator UX**: the Attempts column on `/admin/recurring-charges` now shows "1 / 1" (purple, tooltip "First post-trial charge — hard 1-attempt rule") for trial-conversion fails vs "N / 4" (gray, tooltip "Renewal — soft-grace [T+1, T+3, T+7] day retry policy") for renewals — abandonment rows are now distinguishable at a glance.

- [x] **ADMIN-TABLE: mandate-mode badge column on hosting admin table (audit Finding 5 of 6 closed; 3 HIGH + 2 of 3 MEDIUM admin-UI gaps now resolved, only 1 cross-mode-dashboard MEDIUM remains as genuine nice-to-have)** — `5f93f85` adds a small badge cluster to the Package / Server cell of the hosting admin table at `app/admin/hosting/page.tsx`, so operators scanning the list can immediately see which rows are on which billing rail without opening the detail modal. The cluster renders inline alongside the existing PHP-version + IP pills (column count stays at 6 — no horizontal-scroll regression on the wide hosting table): (i) **TOKENS** purple badge when `razorpayTokenId` is set on the Hosting record — the new Google-style ₹2-and-reverse rail driven by `/admin/recurring-charges` cron; (ii) **SUBSCRIPTION** blue badge when `subscriptionId` is set — the existing Razorpay Subscriptions API rail driven by webhooks; (iii) **MANUAL** gray badge when neither is set — no recurring mandate on file, renewals need operator action; (iv) **TRIAL** amber pill rendered alongside whichever rail badge applies when `isTrial=true` — Hosting is inside the 15-day free window, first MIT runs after window closes. Each badge carries a tooltip that explains the rail + where renewals come from, so a reviewer who hovers doesn't have to leave the table to understand the mode. **No backend changes**: the discriminating fields (`razorpayTokenId` + `subscriptionId` + `isTrial` + `billingType`) were already shipped in `a570a7d`'s HostingData interface + `/api/admin/hosting/stats` route return shape — this commit is the last consumer of those fields. Frontend visibility is now COMPLETE across all 5 admin surfaces (hosting table, hosting detail modal, order detail modal, `/admin/recurring-charges`, integration-health). **No new tests**: badge is pure inline-conditional rendering; the data path is already pinned by the existing 31 tests in `stats/route.test.ts`. Typecheck + lint clean on the touched file (zero new warnings). **Audit findings status after this commit**: ✅ Finding 1 (HIGH) Order detail modal — `a570a7d`; ✅ Finding 2 (HIGH) Hosting detail modal — `a570a7d`; ✅ Finding 3 (HIGH) `/admin/recurring-charges` page — `4da1dd3`; ✅ Finding 4 (MEDIUM) integration-health coverage — `1198403`; ✅ Finding 5 (MEDIUM) Tokens badge column on hosting table — this commit; ⏸ Finding 6 (MEDIUM) `/admin/renewals` cross-mode dashboard — genuine nice-to-have (per-attempt feed already covers triage). **Production safety**: visible no-op today on the live hosting list — `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` default keeps `razorpayTokenId` null on every current row, so the gray MANUAL badge will appear on most existing rows + blue SUBSCRIPTION badge on rows with active Razorpay subscriptions; no TOKENS badges will appear until the flag is flipped + the DA-provisioning cron runs.

- [x] **ADMIN-HEALTH: integration-health card now surfaces Tokens-flow recurring-charge failures via direct `RecurringChargeAttempt` query (audit Finding 4 of 6 closed; 3 HIGH + 1 MEDIUM admin-UI gaps now resolved, 2 MEDIUM cosmetic items remaining)** — `1198403` adds a fourth source to `app/api/admin/integration-health/route.ts` alongside the existing Order-error / Zoho-stuck-invoice / SystemLog scans. The new source queries `RecurringChargeAttempt.find({status: {$in: ['failed', 'abandoned']}, createdAt: >=since})` directly — coverage is now BULLETPROOF rather than contingent on serverLogger calls landing in SystemLog with the right keywords. For each failed/abandoned attempt, the source synthesises a message with the `[RECURRING-CHARGE]` prefix + status (`FAILED` or `ABANDONED`) + attemptCount (N/4) + truncated hostingId + lastError, then feeds through the existing `record()` helper — same bucket-grouping the other sources use, so 5 identical "card declined" failures cluster into one pattern row with `count: 5`. Two new specific Razorpay-provider signatures route these messages to status-specific hints: (i) `[RECURRING-CHARGE] ABANDONED` → "abandoned after 4 failed attempts… Hosting is now status='expired' + DA-suspended + customer was emailed. To recover: customer needs to re-subscribe (new CIT auth + new mandate). Open `/admin/recurring-charges` filtered to `status=abandoned`…"; (ii) `[RECURRING-CHARGE]|MIT charge|retry_scheduled|mandate.*revoke` → "retried per the [T+1, T+3, T+7] day backoff… Check `/admin/recurring-charges` filtered to `status=failed` to see how many attempts are still budgeted". Both hints explicitly point at the dedicated dashboard from `4da1dd3` for per-attempt detail — integration-health becomes the discovery surface ("Razorpay: 5 errors") + the dashboard becomes the triage surface ("which 5, in what state, what's the lastError"). **Defensive**: the new source is wrapped in try/catch — RCA query failure (Mongo connection lost / collection deleted) is logged but does NOT crash the route; integration-health degrades to its pre-Phase-2I behaviour (3 existing sources still render). **Tests**: 5 new tests in `tests/unit/app/api/admin/integration-health/route.test.ts` (the existing route had ZERO test coverage; this is the first test file for it) cover: 401 unauthorized gate; failed attempt → razorpay card with retry-scheduled hint; abandoned attempt → razorpay card with abandonment hint mentioning `/admin/recurring-charges`; similar errors cluster via bucketKey (3 identical → 1 pattern row count=3); RCA query failure does NOT crash the route (graceful degrade). **Audit findings status after this commit**: ✅ Finding 1 (HIGH) Order detail modal — `a570a7d`; ✅ Finding 2 (HIGH) Hosting detail modal — `a570a7d`; ✅ Finding 3 (HIGH) `/admin/recurring-charges` page — `4da1dd3`; ✅ Finding 4 (MEDIUM) integration-health coverage — this commit; ⏸ Finding 5 (MEDIUM) Tokens badge column on hosting table — cosmetic, detail modal covers triage; ⏸ Finding 6 (MEDIUM) `/admin/renewals` cross-mode dashboard — nice-to-have. **The remaining 2 MEDIUM findings are genuinely cosmetic / nice-to-have** — operators now have full triage capability via the 4 admin surfaces shipped today (Order detail modal, Hosting detail modal, `/admin/recurring-charges` dedicated page, integration-health card). **Production safety**: visible no-op on integration-health today (no Tokens-mode customers exist; `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` default keeps `RecurringChargeAttempt` table empty in prod).

- [x] **ADMIN-DASHBOARD: `/admin/recurring-charges` — full visibility into Tokens-flow MIT charge attempts (audit Finding 3 of 6 closed; all 3 HIGH-severity admin-UI gaps now resolved)** — final missing piece from the admin-UI audit. The new `RecurringChargeAttempt` collection shipped by Phase 2D today had zero admin surface; operators couldn't see per-attempt state ("attempt 3 of 4, next retry T+3 days") for any failing mandate. Commit `4da1dd3` closes that gap with a dedicated page + API + tests + sidebar nav entry. **Three new files**: (a) `app/api/admin/recurring-charges/route.ts` — admin-only (`AuthService.isAdmin` → 403 otherwise) read-only feed. Queries `RecurringChargeAttempt` within a lookback window (24h / 7d / 30d / 90d; default 7d), optional status filter (`pending | in_progress | succeeded | failed | abandoned`), joins each row with `Hosting` (domainName + status) + `User` (email + name) via batch lookups (one `$in` per join target — no N+1 queries). Returns aggregate status counts that span the WHOLE window (not the filter slice) so the dashboard summary stays meaningful even when narrowing by status. `hasMore=true` when rows hit the limit (default 100, max 500) so the UI can hint "narrow filters to see more". (b) `app/admin/recurring-charges/page.tsx` — React page. Five status summary cards across the top (Pending / In progress / Succeeded / Failed / Abandoned) with live counts. Filterable table beneath: window selector + status selector. Columns: Domain (with token ID font-mono small under it for copy-paste into Razorpay dashboard), User (name + email), Amount (₹), Status badge color-coded, Attempt N/4, Due-date / Next-retry / Abandoned-at all in one column with appropriate label, Last error string truncated with tooltip. Empty-state message explains the table is empty because Tokens-flow is dormant in production until flag flip. Read-only — operator triages by pivoting to Razorpay dashboard via the customer/token IDs shown. - [x] **FIXED + TOOLED: Two HIGH-severity race + ordering bugs in Tokens-flow MIT cron caught by Explore-agent self-audit; one-command Cloud Scheduler setup script added** — two late-session commits closing out today's Tokens-migration push with quality-hardening + operator-tooling rather than new feature work. **The audit** (Explore-agent over today's 9 implementation commits, same audit pattern that earlier in the session caught the dead-code `subscription.payment_failed` branch in commit `055c51a`) surfaced 9 candidate findings, of which **2 were real HIGH-severity bugs** in `lib/services/payment/recurring-charge-service.ts` — both money-affecting, both fixed in commit `d86dd01`. **Bug 1 (race condition in retry-bump)**: the original Phase 2D code used a read-then-update pattern when claiming a retry — read attempt + check `status === 'failed'` + check `nextAttemptAt < now` + bump `attemptCount` + save. Non-atomic. Two concurrent cron passes could BOTH read `status='failed'`, BOTH bump to `'in_progress'`, BOTH call `RazorpayService.chargeViaToken` → DOUBLE-CHARGE. The initial-claim path (`RCACreate` with unique-index dedup on `(hostingId, dueDate)`) is safe — only one cron wins the create. But the RETRY path (caught by E11000 + read existing + bump) is where the race lives. Fix: replace the read-then-update with an atomic `RecurringChargeAttempt.findOneAndUpdate({_id, status: 'failed'}, {$inc: {attemptCount: 1}, $set: {status: 'in_progress', lastAttemptAt: now}}, {new: true})`. If the update returns null, status changed between our read and our update — another cron won the claim, skip safely with `outcome='skipped'`, `reason='retry claim race lost (another cron won)'`. **Bug 2 (money loss if `hosting.save` fails after Razorpay charge succeeded)**: the original Phase 2D code saved the `RecurringChargeAttempt` FIRST (marked status='succeeded' with the Razorpay payment id), THEN saved the Hosting (extended `expiryDate +1yr`). If `hosting.save()` failed (transient Mongo error / write concern violation / etc.), the customer was charged + the attempt was marked 'succeeded' BUT the Hosting.expiryDate was NOT extended. On the next cron run: Hosting still matches the "due for charge" query (expiry unchanged), attempt for this (hostingId, dueDate) shows 'succeeded', idempotency check skips, customer stays charged-but-not-served forever. Fix: flip the save order. Save Hosting FIRST (expiry extension), then save attempt SECOND (mark 'succeeded'). If `hosting.save` fails → throw → cron logs + attempt stays 'in_progress' (not marked 'succeeded'), so the existing 'in_progress' branch on the next cron run prevents double-charge and an operator can manually reconcile. If `hosting.save` succeeds + `attempt.save` fails → Hosting extended (customer-side good); attempt is an audit-trail orphan in 'in_progress' state that an operator can reconcile manually — no customer impact. **Tests**: 2 new + 1 enhanced existing in `tests/unit/lib/services/payment/recurring-charge-service.test.ts` — the happy-path test now tracks `save()` call order via `mockImplementationOnce` and asserts `['hosting', 'attempt']`; the retry-bump test now asserts the atomic `findOneAndUpdate` filter is `{_id, status: 'failed'}`; one new test for the race-lost case (findOneAndUpdate returns null → skip without charging); one new test for the Hosting-save-failure case (throws before marking 'succeeded'). All 16 recurring-charge-service tests pass after the fixes. **Operator-side tooling** (commit `53c50fd`): new `scripts/setup-cloud-scheduler-tokens.sh` wraps the two `gcloud scheduler jobs create http` commands previously documented inline in the worker route headers (Phase 2H). Idempotent — re-runs update existing jobs instead of erroring. Pulls `CRON_SECRET` from Google Secret Manager so the secret never appears in shell history. Configures both jobs against `speedy-unison-453807-e9` / `asia-south1`: (a) `tokens-provision-pending` cron every 10 min (short cadence so trial signup → DA-account-ready in < 10 min for the customer) with 60s attempt deadline; (b) `tokens-charge-recurring` cron daily at 22:00 UTC (~03:30 IST next day — dead of night before banks' batch-rejection cutoffs) with 300s attempt deadline (the MIT charge batch can hit ~100 hostings). Script lists both jobs at the end + reminds the operator how to flip the feature flag (`gcloud run services update dms --update-env-vars=HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=tokens`). Safe to run BEFORE flipping the flag — both worker routes already no-op when `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW != 'tokens'`. **What remains for production usage**: only Phase 3 (integration tests against Razorpay TEST mode), the operator-side one-command setup script invocation, and the operator-side phased flag flip per `docs/razorpay-tokens-migration.md` §5 (Starter tier first). **Today's commit total on origin/main: 25** (10 implementation + 1 audit-fix + 1 operator-tooling + 13 docs).

- [x] **IMPLEMENTED: Razorpay Tokens migration Phase 2H — HTTP worker endpoints for Cloud Scheduler invocation; Tokens flow is now PRODUCTION-READY IN CODE (only operator-side gcloud + flag-flip remain)** — final Tokens-migration code commit of the day (`5fae71a`), bridging the Phase 2D + 2E CLI scripts to Cloud Scheduler (which can only invoke HTTP endpoints, not Node directly). **What changed**: two new worker routes mirroring the existing `app/api/workers/process-hosting-expiry/` pattern — `app/api/workers/tokens-provision-pending/route.ts` invokes the Phase 2E DA-provisioning loop (`findPendingTokensFlowHostings` + `provisionTokensFlowHosting` per row), and `app/api/workers/tokens-charge-recurring/route.ts` invokes the Phase 2D MIT-charge loop (`findHostingsDueForCharge` + `chargeRecurringHosting` per row). Both routes follow the project's existing worker conventions: `authorizeCronRequest` for CRON_SECRET auth (401 if missing/wrong), `secureJsonResponse` + `secureErrorResponse` for response shapes, per-Hosting exceptions caught + counted in an `errors[]` array (batch continues so one failure doesn't block the rest), structured summary counts (`{activated, skipped, da_unreachable, collision_exhausted, hard_failure}` for provisioning; `{succeeded, retry_scheduled, abandoned, skipped}` for charging) returned in the response body. **Defensive guards**: (a) `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW != 'tokens'` → no-op 200 with all-zero counts (matches the CLI scripts' same guard); (b) the charging worker also refuses if `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` doesn't start with `rzp_live_` → 400 NON_LIVE_KEY (this is a real-money endpoint; belt + suspenders against accidental test-mode invocation in prod). **Cloud Scheduler setup documented inline in the route file headers + the commit message** so the operator has the exact 2 `gcloud scheduler jobs create http` commands ready to run: provisioning every 10 min, charging daily at 22:00 UTC (~03:30 IST next day). **Tests**: 9 new tests in `tests/unit/app/api/workers/tokens-workers.test.ts` covering both workers — 401 unauthorized, no-op on wrong flag value, per-outcome counts in the happy path, per-Hosting exception logged + counted in errors[] without crashing the batch, plus the `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` non-live-key guard on the charging worker. **Aggregate Tokens-flow test surface today: 194 tests pass** (Foundation 14 + 2A 6 + 2B 5 + 2C 10 + 2D 11 + 2G 2 + 2E 9 + 2F 3 + 2H 9 = 69 new today across 8 new files; plus 125 pre-existing Razorpay tests). Full project typecheck clean. **Today's commit total on origin/main: 20** (9 implementation + 11 docs). **Tokens flow is now PRODUCTION-READY IN CODE** — the only remaining steps are operator-side: (1) run the 2 `gcloud scheduler jobs create http` commands (documented inline in the new worker route files for easy copy-paste), (2) the phased flag flip per `docs/razorpay-tokens-migration.md` §5 starting with Starter tier, (3) integration tests against Razorpay TEST mode. No more app-code changes required. **Production safety**: still dormant — `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` default makes both new workers no-op on every invocation. Even if Cloud Scheduler were misconfigured to call these endpoints today (it isn't), no Hosting would match the worker queries because `/create-order` returns `mandateMode='subscription'` by default and no Tokens-mode mandates exist in production yet.

- [x] **IMPLEMENTED: Razorpay Tokens migration Phase 2F — DA suspend + Hosting expired + suspension email on abandoned MIT attempts; Tokens flow is now FEATURE-COMPLETE in code** — sixth + final Tokens-migration code commit of the day (`c42237d`), landing after Foundation + Phases 2A + 2B + 2C + 2D + 2E + 2G had already shipped this session. **What changed**: when a `RecurringChargeAttempt` reaches `MAX_ATTEMPTS=4` (one initial + three retries with T+1, T+3, T+7 day backoff per Phase 2D's state machine), the recurring-charge cron now executes three follow-up actions wrapped in independent try/catch blocks so any single failure doesn't cascade: (a) **DA suspend** — calls `suspendUser({username: hosting.directAdminUsername, reason: "Recurring charge abandoned after N attempts"})` so the customer's hosting service stops, with the reason string surfacing in DA's audit log for operator traceability; (b) **Hosting.status = 'expired'** + clears `next_action_at` to prevent the daily scheduler from re-queuing the already-expired service (matches the existing Subscriptions-flow `subscription.halted` handler's behavior in `lib/services/payment/webhook-handlers.ts:316`); (c) **Suspension email** via the existing `EmailService.sendServiceSuspensionEmail(userEmail, {serviceName, serviceType: 'Hosting'})` helper — customer needs to know their hosting was suspended + how to recover (re-add payment + re-subscribe). **Defensive scoping**: DA suspend SKIPPED if `directAdminUsername` is empty (Phase 2C creates Hostings with empty username in status='pending'; in practice unreachable because MIT charges only fire when `status='active'` which implies DA was already provisioned by Phase 2E, but the guard is belt+suspenders). DA suspend failure → log warn + continue (DB state machine is the source of truth; an operator can manually suspend via DA admin if the cron failed it). Hosting.save failure → log error + continue. Email failure → log error + continue (customer will also see the suspension when they next log in to dashboard). **Tests**: 3 new tests in `tests/unit/lib/services/payment/recurring-charge-service.test.ts` + 1 enhanced existing test — happy abandonment now asserts DA suspend called with the right reason string + Hosting.status='expired' + suspension email sent; `directAdminUsername` empty → DA suspend SKIPPED but status flip + email still fire; DA suspend failure does NOT block status flip + email send; email failure does NOT block DA suspend + status flip. **Aggregate Tokens-flow test surface today: 185 tests pass** (Foundation 14 + Phase 2A 6 + Phase 2B 5 + Phase 2C 10 + Phase 2D 11 + Phase 2G 2 + Phase 2E 9 + Phase 2F 3 = 60 new today across 7 new files; plus 125 pre-existing Razorpay tests). Full project typecheck clean. **Tokens flow is now FEATURE-COMPLETE in code**: every state transition from customer "Start Free Trial" click through CIT auth, ₹2 refund, mandate token storage, Hosting record creation, DA provisioning, day-15 MIT charge, retry backoff, abandonment, DA suspension, and customer dunning notification — all exists in code. **Today's commit total on origin/main: 17** (8 implementation + 9 docs). **What still ships before flag-flip in production**: Phase 3 (integration tests against Razorpay TEST mode), Cloud Scheduler config (invoke `provision-pending-tokens-hostings.js` every 5-15 min + `charge-recurring-hostings.js` daily at ~04:00 IST), and operator-side phased flag flip per `docs/razorpay-tokens-migration.md` §5 (Starter tier only first). **Production safety**: still dormant — `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` default. The new Phase 2F code paths are only reachable when (a) a Tokens-mode `RecurringChargeAttempt` exists, AND (b) it has reached MAX_ATTEMPTS. Neither condition can occur in production today.

- [x] **IMPLEMENTED: Razorpay Tokens migration Foundation + Phase 2A + Phase 2B — 4 new `RazorpayService` methods + data-model additions + create-order route Tokens branch + webhook CIT-auth handler — all behind `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=tokens` feature flag (dormant in production by default)** — three commits on 2026-06-26 building out the Tokens migration foundation that the design doc landed yesterday outlines. **Layer 1: Foundation (`dca48b5`, 896 insertions across 7 files)** — adds `createCustomer({name, email, contact, notes})` for idempotent customer creation, `createRecurringTokenOrder({customerId, validationAmountInPaise, maxAmountInPaise, method, frequency?, expireAt?, receipt, notes?})` for the ₹2 CIT auth order with method-specific token shapes (card-token vs eMandate-token vs NACH-token branching), `chargeViaToken({customerId, tokenId, amountInRupees, email, contact, receipt, description?, notes?})` for two-step merchant-initiated transactions (order + createRecurringPayment) with defensive payment-id field fallback, and `refundPayment(paymentId, amountInPaise?, notes?)` with `speed: 'optimum'` for fast mandate-validation reversal. Razorpay API spec verified against razorpay-node v2.9.6 TypeScript definitions — 5 corrections applied to the design doc's draft §4 (`expire_at` not `expire_by`, card NPCI cap ₹15,000 not ₹99,999, `frequency: 'as_presented' | 'monthly'` only — no `yearly`, `payment_capture` is boolean not 0/1, MIT charge uses `createRecurringPayment`). Data model additions: `Hosting.razorpayCustomerId` + `Hosting.razorpayTokenId` indexed fields, new `RecurringChargeAttempt` collection with status enum (`pending` | `in_progress` | `succeeded` | `failed` | `abandoned`) + unique index on `(hostingId, dueDate)` for cron idempotency. Feature flag `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` added to `scripts/deploy-cloud-run.sh` ENV_VARS with default value `subscriptions`. 14 new unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/razorpay-tokens.test.ts` cover happy paths + validation rejections + defensive field-name fallbacks + error-message propagation; all pass. **Layer 2: Phase 2A — create-order route Tokens branch (`b50709d`)** — when `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=tokens` AND the cart item is a 15-day free trial AND there are no other items in cart AND user has phone, `app/api/payments/create-order/route.ts:240-247`'s recurring-hosting branch now calls `createCustomer` + `createRecurringTokenOrder` instead of `createSubscription`. CIT auth order notes carry the full trial intent payload (`type: 'mandate_validation'`, `user_id`, `domain_name`, `plan_id`, `period`, `is_trial: 'true'`, `trial_days: '15'`, `intended_charge_paise`) so the webhook handler can route + provision. New `mandateMode: 'subscription' | 'tokens' | null` field on the response signals which Razorpay Checkout mode the frontend should open (subscription_id vs order_id + customer_id + recurring:1). Tokens-flow API failure cleanly falls through to the existing Subscriptions flow — production never blocked by missing precondition (no phone / Razorpay 500 / customer-creation race). 6 new tests cover the feature-flag gate, happy path, missing-phone fall-through, Tokens-API-throws fall-through, non-trial-bypass fall-through, and the notes payload carrying the trial intent. **Layer 3: Phase 2B — webhook handler for CIT auth (`04c0d0c`)** — new `handleMandateValidationCaptured(order, payment)` in `app/razorpay/webhook/route.ts` fires on `payment.captured` when the Order row has `mandateMode='tokens'` + `orderType='hosting_trial'`. Handler extracts `token_id` from the payment object, **immediately refunds the ₹2 mandate validation charge** via `RazorpayService.refundPayment(payment.id, 200, {speed: 'optimum', reason: 'mandate_validation_refund'})` — this is the "and reverse" half of the Google "₹2-charge-and-reverse" pattern customers expect within minutes on their bank statement. Then persists `razorpayTokenId` + `razorpayPaymentId` on the Order row and marks Order completed. Idempotency: re-delivered webhooks on already-completed orders no-op cleanly. Deferral logic: if `token_id` is missing from the payment object but `razorpayCustomerId` is on the Order, return without action so a later delivery (or token.confirmed webhook) can complete it. Refund-API failure resilience: log loudly but still persist the token (mandate intact even if money-loss; manual refund from Razorpay dashboard is the fallback). 3 new fields on `Order` model — `mandateMode` indexed enum, `razorpayCustomerId` + `razorpayTokenId` indexed strings. The /create-order Tokens branch extended to persist a Mongo Order row at CIT-auth-creation time so the webhook can find it (without this row, the webhook would log "Order not found, returning 200 to stop retries" and silently lose the mandate). Order-persistence failure re-throws — a stray Razorpay auth order with no DB row is worse than asking the user to retry checkout. 5 new tests cover happy path, idempotency on re-delivery, deferral on missing token, refund-failure resilience, and non-tokens-mode bypass to the existing webhook path. **Aggregate test surface**: 144 Razorpay-related tests pass (52 lib/razorpay tests + 44 create-order tests + 47 webhook tests + 1 misc). Full project typecheck clean. **Production safety**: every code path above is DORMANT today — `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW=subscriptions` (default) keeps every customer-facing behavior identical to yesterday. Flipping the flag in production requires Phase 2C+ to complete first — otherwise customers would authorize a mandate that has no Hosting record to charge against. **What's still pending** (Phase 2C+): (i) Hosting record creation when CIT auth completes — three possible placements being weighed (inside the webhook handler, in `/verify` route, or in a separate cron polling completed Tokens orders); (ii) MIT cron `scripts/charge-recurring-hostings.js` for merchant-driven recurring charges firing on the day-15 trial expiry + ongoing renewals; (iii) retry/dunning logic against the `RecurringChargeAttempt` collection (T+1, T+3, T+7 retry schedule + dunning email after exhaustion); (iv) frontend Razorpay Checkout opener — needs to branch on `mandateMode` and pass `customer_id` + `recurring:1` in tokens mode (current frontend only knows the subscription_id path); (v) integration tests against Razorpay TEST mode. **Day-26 commits + no deploy**: 4 commits (3 code + 1 docs) on origin/main; `dms-00206-z6j` from 2026-06-25 remains the live revision since all Tokens-flow code is dormant behind the flag.

- [x] **DESIGN: Razorpay Tokens migration design doc landed (`docs/razorpay-tokens-migration.md`) — closes the "free trial actually charges money" UX gap by switching to the ₹2-charge-and-reverse Google/Netflix pattern; implementation gated on live cutover Steps 4-5 close** — investigation triggered 2026-06-25 when operator asked "why does our free trial charge money? how does Google do it?". Root cause diagnosed via inline code-read + Explore-agent investigation: Razorpay's Subscriptions API (which our checkout flow at `app/api/payments/create-order/route.ts:240-247` uses) ties each customer to a fixed Plan with a fixed price; at mandate authorization Razorpay's checkout overlay reads the plan and shows the customer the full plan amount (₹599.88 for Starter yearly) — there's no parameter to override this. So even though our cart UI correctly says "₹0 today", Razorpay then shows the customer ₹599.88 + a ₹1-₹2 mandate-validation charge that takes 3-5 days to auto-reverse. The customer experiences this as "the trial isn't actually free" and converts at lower-than-expected rates. **The Google pattern (verified to use Razorpay's Tokens API path, NOT Subscriptions)**: at authorization Razorpay shows just the ₹2 validation amount → customer authorizes → ₹2 debited → ₹2 refunded by merchant (us) within minutes → mandate token stored → all future subscription charges happen via merchant-initiated transactions (MIT) using the stored token, on whatever schedule the merchant runs. **Why design-first, not implementation-first**: this is a significant refactor (4-5 days), changes the data model (`Hosting.razorpayCustomerId` + `razorpayTokenId` + new `RecurringChargeAttempt` collection), introduces new merchant-side responsibilities (retry logic, dunning, cron-driven recurring debits — Razorpay no longer handles these), and runs in parallel with the existing Subscriptions flow for 12-18 months under a feature flag. Writing the design first lets the operator (and any other developer who picks this up) evaluate the entire scope before committing engineering days. **Doc contents** (~530 lines): §1 Background + UX gap diagnosis; §2 Current architecture file-by-file map; §3 Target architecture file-by-file map with new methods + webhook branches + cron handler + retry/dunning logic + data model changes; §4 Razorpay API specifics (with explicit "verify against current docs before implementation" gates on parameter shapes — flagged 0.5-day pre-implementation task); §5 Migration plan including the `HOSTING_MANDATE_FLOW` feature flag + 5-phase rollout starting with Starter tier; §6 Testing plan; §7 Rollback plan (single env-var flip); §8 Effort estimate (4-5 days implementation + 14 days phased rollout); §9 Open questions (`max_amount` re-confirmation, `frequency: as_presented` NPCI support, Paper NACH fallback, refund-window race conditions); §10 Related auto-memory entries; §11 Decision log. **Implementation gate**: post Razorpay live cutover Steps 4-5 close (post UPI Autopay activation est. 2026-07-08) — the Tokens migration needs a verified-working Subscriptions-flow baseline before introducing the parallel Tokens flow. Until then, the design doc is the source-of-truth + the In-Flight row above tracks the implementation slot.

- [x] **FIXED: Razorpay LIVE subscription Plans regenerated for all 3 hosting tiers — 6 new live `plan_T5nE…` IDs created + MongoDB `HostingPlan.razorpayPlans` rewritten + pre-cutover state backed up + script committed for audit** — third deliverable of the 5-step Razorpay live cutover, closes the system-state warning that was hanging over Step 2. **What's new in production state** (no deploy needed — pure DB + Razorpay-side write): 6 fresh subscription plans exist in Razorpay LIVE — `Starter Monthly` `plan_T5nEByxe0FKxP9` (₹49.99/mo), `Starter Yearly` `plan_T5nECBkYp3J9CN` (₹599.88/yr), `Standard Monthly` `plan_T5nEBcxzg8xXVz` (₹125.00/mo), `Standard Yearly` `plan_T5nEBowIdGKqIn` (₹1500.00/yr), `Plus Monthly` `plan_T5nEBAXABwtntv` (₹187.20/mo), `Plus Yearly` `plan_T5nEBQ9x8cUomF` (₹2246.40/yr). Each plan's amount is the existing `HostingPlan.renewalPrice` in INR converted to paise via `Math.round(price * 100)` (the same arithmetic the existing admin PATCH route at `app/api/admin/hosting/packages/route.ts:296-331` uses for the in-app rotation flow — chose to mirror it exactly rather than invent new logic). Yearly amount is `renewalPrice × 12` with no yearly-discount applied (matches existing PATCH route behaviour). **MongoDB writes**: the 3 `HostingPlan` rows in the `hostingplans` collection now have `razorpayPlans.monthly` + `razorpayPlans.yearly` pointing at the new live `plan_T5nE…` IDs; the previous test-mode IDs (`plan_SiPzGow78Buoov` / `plan_SiPzHFFxgMZwpP` for Starter, `plan_SiQ0WljcQG2Mos` / `plan_SiQ0XFppyH3jss` for Standard, `plan_SiQ0Vg9HADR210` / `plan_SiQ0W6i2NBgbMu` for Plus) are no longer referenced anywhere in the live system, but **the test-mode plans themselves still exist on Razorpay's test-mode side** and can be deactivated cosmetically from the test-mode dashboard whenever — no rush, they don't affect live mode. **Script details** — `scripts/razorpay-regenerate-plans-live.js` (160 lines, committed to git): runs in dry-run mode by default, requires explicit `--apply` flag to do writes; refuses to run unless `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` starts with `rzp_live_` (safety check against accidental test-mode execution); creates all 6 plans in-memory FIRST and only touches the DB after all 6 succeed (so a partial-failure Razorpay API hiccup leaves the DB untouched + the operator can deactivate any stranded plans manually); writes a JSON backup of the 3 pre-cutover `HostingPlan` docs to `scripts/razorpay-plans-pre-live-backup-2026-06-25.json` BEFORE any write, and refuses to overwrite an existing backup file (guards against accidental double-runs that would create duplicate live plans). The backup file is gitignored under a new `.gitignore` pattern `scripts/*-backup-*.json` — it's a local recovery artefact, not source-of-truth. **Mid-script fix**: the dry-run output initially showed yearly amounts with IEEE-754 float-math display artifacts (e.g. `187.2 × 12 = 2246.3999999999996`) — confusing to the operator reviewing the dry-run output. Fixed via `.toFixed(2)` formatting in both the current-state and would-do print blocks. The wire-level paise calculation was always correct (`Math.round(price * 12 * 100)` collapses the float noise to a clean integer), only the display string was misleading. **Verification post-`--apply`**: re-ran the script in dry-run mode — it now reads the new live `plan_T5nE…` IDs from MongoDB and prints them under "Current state", confirming the DB writes persisted. The operator can cross-check by logging into the Razorpay LIVE dashboard → Subscriptions → Plans → 6 plans should be visible with the right names + amounts. **System impact**: until this row landed, the system was in an inconsistent state — Step 2's deploy had swapped the keys to live mode (one-shot orders worked) but subscription orders would have hit a 404 at Razorpay's `/subscriptions` endpoint because the plan IDs in MongoDB referenced test-mode plans that don't exist on the live API. With Step 3 done, every layer is consistent end-to-end — public Key Id in customer JS is live, server-side credentials in Secret Manager are live, webhook secret is live, plan IDs in the DB are live. The system-state warning paragraph at the top of TASKS.md (added when Step 2 flipped to Done) has been removed in the same commit. **Rollback note**: a `dms-00205-2zv` traffic-shift via `RESTORE_POINTS.md` Path 1 only rolls back the IMAGE, not the DB writes. To fully revert Step 3's DB changes, run the regen script in reverse mode reading from the backup JSON — there's no automated path for this today; if Step 4 verification surfaces a real-money showstopper, the operator can manually edit the 3 `HostingPlan` docs in MongoDB to restore the test-mode IDs from `scripts/razorpay-plans-pre-live-backup-2026-06-25.json`. **Step 4 (₹1 verification) is the natural follow-up** — same image, but now exercising the full live-mode flow end-to-end with real money.

(c) The public build-time var `NEXT_PUBLIC_RAZORPAY_KEY_ID=rzp_live_T5NRBOq7ByM414` was baked into the customer-side JS bundle at build time — confirmed present in `/_next/static/chunks/app/checkout/guest/page-b53d78066180cf2e.js` via direct curl + grep against the live URL. - [x] **FIXED: Razorpay live-mode dashboard prep complete — live API credentials generated + both webhook routes Enabled (one-shot payments AND subscriptions) + dead-code event-name bug discovered + fixed in same pass** — first deliverable of the 5-step Razorpay test-to-live cutover. **What's now live on the Razorpay dashboard side**: (a) Live API keys generated under Account & Settings → API Keys → live tab — Key Id `rzp_live_T5NRBOq7ByM414` is public-by-design (gets baked into the customer-side JS bundle at build time) + Key Secret stored separately for Step 2's Secret Manager rotation; (b) `https://app.anutech.in/` added as an approved website in Business website details, verified ✓ in under a minute (sits alongside the already-approved parent `anutech.in` so the Razorpay risk team has explicit per-subdomain authorisation for the actual payment-origination domain); (c) two webhooks Enabled with `Last Updated: 24 Jun 2026 12:47pm` / `12:54pm` — **Webhook 1 at `https://app.anutech.in/razorpay/webhook`** subscribed to `payment.captured` + `refund.processed` (drives the `handlePaymentCaptured` flow in `app/razorpay/webhook/route.ts` — claims pending order, creates Zoho invoice, fans out provisioning via `finalizePendingOrder`; plus `handleRefundProcessed` for credit-note creation on the Zoho side), and **Webhook 2 at `https://app.anutech.in/api/webhooks/razorpay`** subscribed to `subscription.charged` + `subscription.halted` (drives `handleSubscriptionCharged` for hosting-renewal expiry-extension + `handleSubscriptionFailed` for retry-exhausted dunning). Both webhooks share the SAME signing secret since both routes read from a single `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` env var. **Bonus: dead-code event-name bug discovered + fixed** — while reviewing dashboard event lists to know what to subscribe to, the codebase grep surfaced that the subscription webhook route was listening for `subscription.payment_failed`. Razorpay has **no such event**; their actual taxonomy for failed-subscription scenarios is `subscription.halted` (retry budget exhausted) + `subscription.pending` (charge failed but retrying) + the lower-level `payment.failed`. So the handler's `else if (event === "subscription.payment_failed")` branch in `app/api/webhooks/razorpay/route.ts:87` has never fired in production since it was written. The handler body itself (`lib/services/payment/webhook-handlers.ts:306-344` — marks hosting expired, suspends DA user, flips billing to manual) matches `subscription.halted` semantics exactly. Renamed the event string in 5 places (route dispatch, handler log message, unit route test, unit handler test, integration test fixture); function name `handleSubscriptionFailed` left as-is (it's still a sensible domain synonym for "halted" + renaming would touch more files for cosmetic gain). Code change is uncommitted in working tree and bundles with Step 2's env-rotation deploy. **What this unblocks**: Steps 2–5 can now proceed in order — Step 2 (env-var rotation + redeploy) is the next In-Flight item, requires the 3 live secrets to be `gcloud secrets versions add`'d into Google Secret Manager before the redeploy (per the `project_secret_manager_split` auto-memory: updating `.env.local` alone does NOT propagate the 3 runtime server secrets — Secret Manager is the binding source for `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` / `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` / `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` at container startup). Auto-memory entry to be added flagging the Razorpay-side subscription event taxonomy (`charged` / `halted` / `pending` / `paused` / `resumed` / `authenticated` / `activated` / `completed` / `cancelled` / `updated`) so future webhook integrations don't repeat the `payment_failed` mistake.

- [x] **Rollback infrastructure formalised — `dms-00205-2zv` saved as a known-good restore point + `RESTORE_POINTS.md` catalogue committed to the repo + auto-memory note so future Claude Code sessions check the catalogue first** — operator request following the close of the 20-deploy hosting-chain saga: mark the current image as a known-working state so any future regression can be rolled back instantly, instead of having to scan git log + rebuild a working image from scratch. **What's now in place**: (a) the dms-00205-2zv image (digest `sha256:ec8d429dc59763537cb7203d681b6c3e494c0c4ed3c68c8fb35420e40f5428f9`) is tagged in Artifact Registry with TWO aliases — `stable-2026-06-22` (date-based, auto-discoverable when scanning by date) and `known-good-hosting-chain-closed` (semantic, intent-grep-able). Both resolve to the same digest. (b) New file `RESTORE_POINTS.md` at the project root catalogues this entry plus three rollback paths in order of speed: **Path 1 — Cloud Run traffic-shift** (`gcloud run services update-traffic dms --to-revisions=dms-00205-2zv=100`) is instant + no rebuild; works while the revision is still in Cloud Run's keep-list (~100 most recent). **Path 2 — re-deploy from the stable tag** (`gcloud run deploy dms --image=…:stable-2026-06-22`) works after Cloud Run GCs the revision but the tag is intact. **Path 3 — re-deploy from the digest** (`…:dms@sha256:ec8d429d…`) is the paranoid mode — the digest is cryptographic so it survives even if someone re-tags the image. (c) `RESTORE_POINTS.md` includes a copy-paste template at the bottom — "Adding a new restore point" — so the operator can add to the catalogue without remembering the `gcloud artifacts docker tags add` invocation. (d) Auto-memory entry `project_restore_points` added so future Claude Code sessions on this project look at `RESTORE_POINTS.md` FIRST before designing a rollback from scratch. **What this unblocks for the dev workflow**: future deploys can be made aggressively (small frequent changes, the way this project tends to operate) because the rollback to a known-good state is now a single command instead of a deploy-from-scratch problem. Commit `5200042` pushed; no Cloud Run deploy required (this is repo + Artifact Registry config only). **One follow-up worth flagging** that's still owed: the project doesn't yet have a SEPARATE preview / staging environment — every deploy goes to the live `dms` service. If multiple developers want to test changes simultaneously without stepping on each other's `app.anutech.in` traffic, the right path is Cloud Run's `--no-traffic --tag <name>` revision-tagging pattern (gives each preview deploy its own dedicated URL like `https://<tag>---dms-721945682828.europe-west1.run.app` while production traffic stays pinned to the current revision). The `scripts/deploy-cloud-run.sh` script can grow a `--preview <tag>` flag in a follow-up commit to make that workflow trivial; flagged here because the operator just hit this need on 2026-06-23.
