Operator action prescribed (now flagged as the new In-Flight item above): SSH the new DA → edit `/usr/local/directadmin/data/templates/valid_TLDs` → either wildcard `*` for all TLDs (recommended, since ResellerClub is already the upstream gatekeeper of "what we sell") OR explicit append of common modern TLDs. Once applied, every TLD RC prices automatically becomes hosting-provisionable. **Full chain that's now functioning**: `dms-00181-zvd` (Phase 2 captcha) → `dms-00184-bfp` (`grecaptcha.ready()`) → `dms-00186-fdm` (Secret Manager captcha-secret) → `dms-00188-lfm` (admin re-sync error-surface + self-hosted Inter) → `dms-00189-7nn` (Zoho tax-ID env wiring) → `dms-00190-jmx` (CSRF gate bypass for log-error) → `dms-00191-wgk` (linkedDomain inference) → `dms-00192-tcx` (activation→login redirect) → `dms-00193-4kl` (Account Settings save-bounce-loop) → `dms-00194-mlz` (orderDomain raw-error preservation) → `dms-00195-wsk` (customer/admin error split) → `dms-00196-s9c` + `dms-00197-b9d` (Integration Health admin page + SystemLog feed) → `dms-00198-9mf` (DA server switch + secret rotation) → `dms-00199-9z6` (`/admin/hosting` pagination) → `dms-00200-k92` (real disk/bandwidth display) → `dms-00201-nqc` (lazy-load 2-pass first-paint) → `dms-00202-xgq` (commercial-tier packages filter) → `dms-00203-rs2` (auto-detect normaliseIndianState) → `dms-00204-95f` (`DIRECTADMIN_IP` env wiring) → `dms-00205-2zv` (classifier hint for Invalid Domain Name). Twenty production deploys today through one chain. **The infrastructure put in place this morning paid for itself many times over** — within seconds of each new DA error appearing, we had the exact reason from Integration Health without scraping logs. Diagnostic-first architecture pattern is what kept the iteration speed up.

- [x] **Integration Health's DirectAdmin card now classifies `Cannot Create Account - Invalid Domain Name` with a two-cause actionable hint** — after the `DIRECTADMIN_IP` wiring fix landed (`dms-00204-95f`), the next hosting checkout on a `.ai` TLD domain (`pratikapp.ai`) surfaced a fresh DA-side error: *"Cannot Create Account - Invalid Domain Name"*. Was classifying as **Unclassified** on Integration Health — no hint, no remediation path, just the raw text. Added the pattern to the DA classifier with a two-cause hint so the operator can immediately see both likely paths: (1) **TLD whitelist gap** — DA's `/usr/local/directadmin/data/templates/valid_TLDs` or the equivalent Server Manager → DNS Administration list may not include newer / country-code TLDs (`.ai`, `.tech`, etc.) the platform sells; (2) **Live-DNS race** — DA validates that the domain resolves before allowing create-user, and on a fresh checkout the domain registration is still dms-00293-9dh on the ResellerClub side (could take minutes for `.ai` registries to confirm). The hint also flags the existing `check-unprovisioned` cron as the natural retry path for cause (2). **Isolation test prescribed**: run a checkout with `.com` / `.in` (any TLD that has been provisioning successfully) — if hosting succeeds, the `.ai` failure was cause (1) (operator adds it to DA's whitelist); if `.com` also fails with the same "Invalid Domain Name" string, it's cause (2) and we'd add a wait-and-retry in the hosting provisioner. Single test isolates which fix to apply (deploy `dms-00205-2zv` live)

- [x] **Closed the last gate in the new-DA hosting-provisioning chain — `DIRECTADMIN_IP` env var now wires through to Cloud Run + the hardcoded fallback updated from the OLD server's IP to the NEW one** — fourth deploy in today's afternoon hosting-debugging chain. After the package-name fix (operator created Starter/Standard/Plus on new DA) the next test checkout surfaced yet another DA-side error: **`Cannot Create Account - That IP does not exist in your list`**. DA's `create-user` API takes an `ip=<X.X.X.X>` parameter telling DA which IP from its **IP Manager** pool to bind the new account to. Our code was sending `DIRECTADMIN_IP` from env, BUT — same class of bug as the Zoho tax-ID gotcha + the DA package-name issue earlier today — that env var was set NOWHERE: not in `.env.local`, not in `deploy-cloud-run.sh`'s `ENV_VARS` list, not in Secret Manager. The code's hardcoded fallback in `lib/directadmin/client.ts` was `136.115.64.54` — the **OLD** DA server's IP — so even though the new DA's IP Manager only knows about `35.208.86.44`, Cloud Run was sending the stale value on every call. **Three-part fix** (commit `a03be16`): (1) `.env.local` populated with `DIRECTADMIN_IP=35.208.86.44` (the new DA's IP, copied from its IP Manager UI); (2) `scripts/deploy-cloud-run.sh` `ENV_VARS` line now propagates `DIRECTADMIN_IP` to Cloud Run on every deploy — same fix shape as the ZOHO_TAX_ID one; (3) the hardcoded fallback IP in `lib/directadmin/client.ts` rotated to `35.208.86.44` so a future regression that drops the env var still produces working create-user calls. **Plus**, the Integration Health page's DA classifier gained two new actionable-hint signatures: *"That IP does not exist in your list"* → directs the operator at the exact `DIRECTADMIN_IP` + `ENV_VARS` fix; *"Cannot Create Account - Package not found"* → directs at DA admin's Manage User Packages screen. Both errors were "Unclassified" with no hint before today; now they're under the DirectAdmin card with prescription text. **One follow-up flagged** (not blocking): the `NAMESERVERS` constant in `lib/directadmin/client.ts` still hardcodes `ns1.server-136-115-64-54.da.direct` / `ns2.server-136-115-64-54.da.direct` — the OLD server's hostnames. These end up in the customer welcome email; a real customer would see those NS strings, point their domain registrar at the retired server, and the website wouldn't resolve. Doesn't block provisioning (DA itself doesn't care what nameservers our welcome email mentions), but worth fixing before any real signup. Awaiting the new DA server's nameserver hostnames from the operator. **The full DA-server-switch saga now closes**: linkedDomain inference, raw-error preservation, customer/admin error split, Integration Health page, license-cap discovery, server migration, package-name creation, IP wiring — all chained, all in. Next fresh checkout should provision cleanly end-to-end (deploy `dms-00204-95f` live, 68/68 directadmin tests pass)

- [x] **Account Settings + Guest Checkout + Register Form: Auto-detect button now populates the State dropdown correctly via a shared `normaliseIndianState()` helper** — senior reviewer noticed on Account Settings that after clicking the **Auto-detect** geolocation button for a Delhi address, the City + PIN + Country fields filled in cleanly but the **State** dropdown stayed at "Select state". The reverse-geocoder (Nominatim) returns Delhi as `NCT of Delhi` or `National Capital Territory of Delhi`, which never matched the dropdown's `Delhi` option verbatim. Three places in the app run auto-detect on an address form (`app/checkout/guest/page.tsx`, `app/dashboard/settings/page.tsx`, `components/RegisterForm.tsx`); only the guest-checkout one had an alias-map + fuzzy-match against `INDIAN_STATES`. The other two wrote the raw geocoder string into form state. Fix: extracted the normaliser into `lib/constants.ts` as `normaliseIndianState(raw)` — single source of truth. Returns canonical `INDIAN_STATES` entry on match (alias map covers `NCT of Delhi`, `Orissa` → `Odisha`, `Pondicherry` → `Puducherry`, `Uttaranchal` → `Uttarakhand`, `J&K` → `Jammu and Kashmir`, `Andaman & Nicobar` → `Andaman and Nicobar Islands`, `Tamilnadu` → `Tamil Nadu`, etc), substring fuzzy fallback for `Karnataka State` / `State of Maharashtra`, and empty string on unknown garbage (caller leaves dropdown unchanged rather than poisoning it). All 3 call sites now import + use the shared helper; the guest-checkout's inline implementation was lifted verbatim. **Tests**: 6 new pin-tests in `tests/unit/lib/constants.test.ts` covering null / canonical / alias / case-insensitive / fuzzy / unknown-garbage paths. **Future maintenance**: when a new spelling shows up that doesn't auto-populate, one line added to `STATE_ALIASES` fixes it across all 3 forms automatically (deploy `dms-00203-rs2` live)

Operators shouldn't be touching those from the admin UI; they're DA server-side bookkeeping. Filter applied AFTER the existing DA-sync step, sourcing the "what's commercial" set from `config/hosting-plans.ts` (`HOSTING_PLANS`). So the authoritative tier list lives in one config file — adding a new commercial tier there automatically surfaces it on the admin page too, no list to keep in sync. **The PERSISTENCE side is unchanged** — every DA package still gets a `HostingPlan` row in the DB (so existing customer-account lookups by `planId` continue to resolve for legacy-tier accounts). Only the GET response surface is filtered. **Plus, two cleanup ops done same session** (no deploy required, just MongoDB writes): (a) **7 stuck test orders hard-deleted** from `orders` collection — they were the test orders the operator placed during today's DA-server-switch debugging, all with `creation_failed` Zoho or `failed` hosting that had been showing up in Integration Health Diagnostics; cleanup also swept linked Hosting / PendingHosting rows (none existed); Razorpay test transactions + Zoho test invoices remain on those providers' sides (test-mode, no reconciliation needed); (b) **4 test users hard-deleted** + every linked artifact across `users` / `orders` / `hostings` / `payments` / `pendingdomains` collections (15 docs across 5 collections total). - [x] **`/admin/hosting` initial paint is now ~3-4× snappier — two-pass lazy-load that paints the first 10 accounts immediately and silently fills in the rest in the background** — senior reviewer caught that the page felt sluggish on first paint after the usage-fetch fix landed (`dms-00200-k92`). With 31 accounts on the new DA server, the API was fetching `getUserConfig` + `getUserUsage` for every account in parallel before responding — 62 DA round-trips total. Even running fully in parallel, the wall-clock per-paint was ~4-8s because DA's per-call latency is non-trivial and the slowest call set the bound. Operator was looking at the skeleton table for several seconds on every refresh. **Two-pass design**: **Pass 1** (`?firstPage=10`) — API slices the DA `listUsers()` result after the FIRST 10 usernames (DA's natural sort order is stable, so this set is deterministic) and only fetches config + usage for that slice. Returns in ~1-2s; frontend paints. Response carries `pagination.truncated=true` so the frontend knows more rows are pending. **Pass 2** (no params) — frontend silently fires the full-fetch in the background after Pass 1 returns. When it resolves, state swaps to the complete list — first 10 rows stay put (stable sort), the remaining 21 append. No loading spinner during Pass 2 so the operator keeps interacting with the partial table. **Search + pagination still work as before** — both operate on the in-memory `hostingData` array, which is fully populated once Pass 2 lands. The existing client-side pager (from `dms-00199-9z6`) initially shows "Showing 1 – 10 of 10", then updates to "Page 1 of 4" / "Showing 1 – 10 of 31" when the background fetch completes. **Error handling**: Pass 2 failures are silently swallowed so a transient background error doesn't disrupt the table already on screen; the first 10 rows remain valid. **Skipped entirely on small installs** — when DA's listUsers returns ≤10 accounts, Pass 1's `pagination.truncated=false` short-circuits the background fetch (no wasted second round-trip). **Tests**: existing 29/29 stats-route tests still pass (lazy-load slicing happens only when `?firstPage=N` is set; the default no-param path is unchanged). 2 new pin-tests added: (a) `?firstPage=2` with 5 DA users only fetches details for the first 2 — verified via `getUserConfig` call count; (b) no `?firstPage` param returns everything with `truncated=false`. **Future tuning**: if the first paint still feels slow at higher account counts, reduce the `firstPage` size to 5; if individual DA round-trips become the bottleneck, switch to `CMD_API_SHOW_ALL_USERS_USAGE` (bulk endpoint) — both swap points are flagged in the inline comments (deploy `dms-00201-nqc` live, smoke test green)

- [x] **`/admin/hosting` now shows real disk + bandwidth usage per account — was silently displaying "0 B / 1 GB" with empty progress bars for every row because the wrong DA endpoint was being queried** — senior reviewer screenshotted the hosting management page right after the DA-server switch landed and noticed the Disk / BW columns showed `0 B / 1 GB` and `0 B / 15 GB` respectively for every single account, including ones live since December 2025 (which obviously have non-zero data on them). **Root cause**: the stats route called `DirectAdminService.getUserConfig(daUsername)` for every row, which hits DA's `CMD_API_SHOW_USER_CONFIG` endpoint. That endpoint returns the user's **limits** (bandwidth, quota, package, ip, email, php_version, suspended) — but does **not** return their current usage. The code then read `daConfig.bandwidth_usage` and `daConfig.quota_usage` from the response, which were always silently undefined (those field names don't exist on the config endpoint), and the `|| '0'` fallback kicked in for every account. So `/admin/hosting` has been showing zero usage on every row since the page was first built — invisible because nothing crashes, just renders as "all accounts empty" which felt plausible for some accounts. **The actual usage values** live on DA's separate `CMD_API_SHOW_USER_USAGE` endpoint (different path, same auth), exposed via `DirectAdminService.getUserUsage()` — a function that was already defined in `lib/directadmin/users.ts` and exported on the static class, but never called by anything in the codebase before today. **Fix**: in the stats route, call `getUserConfig()` and `getUserUsage()` in parallel per user via `Promise.all` so wall-clock-per-user stays at `max(config, usage)` not `config + usage` (no perceptible page-load slowdown for the current 31-account scale). Merge the usage-endpoint values into the response; keep the (always-empty) config-endpoint fallback for forward-compat with any DA build that happens to populate them there. A usage-fetch failure for an individual user is caught + logged as a warn with usage zeroed for that row — config is the more important fetch (drives status / domain / package / phpVersion), usage is just a display nicety, so a transient usage timeout shouldn't take down the whole row. **Bulk-endpoint note**: if this becomes a hotspot past ~100 accounts, `CMD_API_SHOW_ALL_USERS_USAGE` (if the DA server build exposes it) would let us fetch all users' usage in one shot. The two-per-user pattern is fine at the current scale; inline comment flags the swap path. **Tests**: existing 27/27 stats-route tests still pass after a defensive `getUserUsage.mockResolvedValue({})` in the `beforeEach`. Added 2 new pin-tests: (a) usage-endpoint values win over the (almost always undefined) config-endpoint usage fields when both are populated, (b) usage-fetch failure for one user doesn't crash the row — the row still surfaces config-endpoint fields and usage shows 0. **Bonus**: this is also the **200th Cloud Run revision** of the `dms` service since it was created — `dms-00200-k92`. (deploy `dms-00200-k92` live, smoke test green)

- [x] **`/admin/hosting` now paginates at 10 accounts per page — the senior reviewer flagged that all 31 hosting accounts were loading in one shot, growing slower over time as the customer base grows** — small but operator-quality-of-life improvement that lands ahead of the page becoming a real scroll problem. **What changed**: the hosting-accounts table at `/admin/hosting` previously rendered the entire `filteredData` array as a single `<tbody>`, which is fine at 31 rows but already noticeable + would degrade linearly as the platform onboards customers. Client-side pagination at `PAGE_SIZE = 10` now slices the filtered dataset into pages of 10; a footer below the table shows `Showing 1 – 10 of 31 accounts` with Previous / Next controls + a `Page 1 of 4` counter. **Search/filter still operates on the full in-memory dataset**, so typing a domain name / username / email in the top-right search box still finds matches across the entire account list — the pagination then slices the filtered result. Two UX details worth flagging: (a) the search input resets `currentPage` to 1 on every keystroke so the operator never lands on a now-empty page 3 after narrowing the search; (b) a defensive `useEffect` snaps `currentPage` back to 1 whenever `totalPages` drops below it, covering the case where the data refresh shrinks the dataset between renders. **Single-page UX preserved** — the footer is hidden when there's only one page (≤10 accounts after filtering), so smaller installs / aggressive searches don't see a pointless pager. **Why client-side and not server-side**: at 31 accounts the API payload is tiny + the `/api/v1/admin/hosting/stats` endpoint has to fetch the full DA account list anyway (it merges DA's `account_list` with the platform's local Hosting collection for unlinked-account detection). Server-side cursor would only become worth it past a few hundred accounts; flagged as a one-line swap in the page if/when that scale arrives, no API change required today. Typecheck clean. **Customer-visible behaviour change: none** — this is an admin-only page (deploy `dms-00199-9z6` live, smoke test green)

- [x] **DA server switch — migrated to a new DirectAdmin host with higher account capacity, retiring the old 2-account-license-cap server** — clean infrastructure response to the hosting-provisioning blocker that's been driving today's investigation. Senior reviewer decided to sidestep the license-tier issue entirely rather than upgrade the old server's license: provision a NEW DirectAdmin host with a higher account limit + rotate the project's credentials over. **What landed**: three DA secrets (`DIRECTADMIN_URL`, `DIRECTADMIN_ADMIN_USER`, `DIRECTADMIN_API_KEY`) updated in `.env.local` with the new server's connection details, then pushed to Google Secret Manager as new versions via `gcloud secrets versions add NAME --data-file=-` × 3 (DA secrets live in Secret Manager — not in the `--set-env-vars` list — per the deploy script's SECRETS_FLAG, so the `.env.local` paste alone wouldn't have reached Cloud Run). Service redeployed (`dms-00198-9mf`) so a fresh container starts and binds the new secret versions at startup. **Verified live** — the next test checkout (`rasjeshwadahub.tech` + Standard Hosting, `ord_1782111257616_bb6y1s`) reached the new server cleanly: no connection-refused, no 401 Unauthorized, no "Login Key not found from this IP" — confirming the **4-layer Cloud Run NAT egress whitelist** (host firewall + CSF + BFM skip + login-key Allow Networks for `34.14.59.128`) was correctly configured on the new server up-front. **The license-cap rejection that motivated this switch is gone** — Integration Health → DirectAdmin will no longer show "License is limited to 2 accounts" once the next checkout's error pattern overwrites it in the time window. **Next-layer failure visible immediately**: the new test checkout's hosting still failed, but with a DIFFERENT error from DA: `Cannot Create Account - Package not found`. This is the "verify the new server has matching hosting-package names" pre-flight check from the migration checklist — the new server doesn't have packages named `Starter` / `Standard` / `Plus` (or whatever names the code sends) yet. Now flagged as the new In-Flight item above; pick path (a) "create matching packages on new DA" or path (b) "tell me what packages DO exist and I'll add a code-side mapping". **Forward-fix chain validated end-to-end on this same checkout**: linkedDomain inferred correctly (`linkedDomain: 'rasjeshwadahub.tech'` on the saved order — `dms-00191-wgk` working); raw DA error preserved in `Order.domains[i].error` (`dms-00194-mlz` working); customer page hides the raw text + shows only friendly bookingStatus copy (`dms-00195-wsk` working); Integration Health page surfaces the actual DA reply (`dms-00197-b9d` working). The architecture put in place this morning has paid off — within seconds of the new DA returning a fresh error, we had the exact reason without scraping logs (deploy `dms-00198-9mf` live, smoke test green)

- [x] **New dedicated admin page — `Integration Health` — aggregates every upstream-provider error into one operator-friendly view, clusters recurring failures with actionable remediation hints** — senior reviewer asked for this directly after the DA license-cap investigation earlier today: *"such license limits, Zoho API errors, DA API errors should be easily track-able in Admin panel — create a dedicated page for it"*. Before this, an operator chasing a service-level event (DA license at cap, Zoho tax-ID drift, expired Razorpay credentials, NEXTAUTH_SECRET rotation drift, cron job failures, SMTP bounces, etc.) had to scrape three different surfaces — Cloud Run stdout/stderr stream (unreliable for this service), per-order admin view (only surfaces order-bound failures), or the MongoDB shell directly. Now everything is at `/admin/integration-health`, accessible via a new "Integration Health" sidebar entry (between Pending Domains and Support Tickets, ShieldAlert icon). **Coverage** — pulls from THREE sources for comprehensive signal: (a) `Order.domains[i].error` for any sub-doc with `status='failed'` (where today's DA license-cap message surfaced via `dms-00194-mlz`); (b) `Order.zohoInvoiceId === 'creation_failed'` for stuck Zoho invoices (mirrors what Invoice Diagnostics already shows, so this page is one-stop); (c) **the SystemLog collection — every `serverLogger.error()` call across the codebase**, now reliably populating since this morning's middleware CSRF gate fix (`dms-00190-jmx`) unblocked the `/api/v1/admin/log-error` forwarder. The SystemLog source is by far the broadest: it captures SMTP failures, NextAuth JWT issues, cron-job errors, ResellerClub API timeouts, middleware errors, business-logic exceptions — everything a route handler ever caught and logged. **Classification** — 8 provider cards: **DirectAdmin** (hosting provisioning), **Zoho Books** (invoice generation), **ResellerClub** (domain reg/transfer/DNS), **Razorpay** (payments + webhooks), **Email / SMTP** (outbound mail), **Authentication** (sign-in / 2FA / JWT), **Background Jobs** (cron / workers), **Application** (catch-all for unclassified server errors). Each SystemLog entry is classified by its `service` field first (set explicitly by the call site via `serverLogger.error("...", { service: "razorpay" })`), then falls back to keyword-match against a configurable signature list. **Actionable hints** — each matched signature carries a remediation hint mapped from the failure pattern. Today's DA license-cap shows up clustered with the hint *"DirectAdmin license tier is at its account quota. Either delete an unused user in DA admin (Account Manager → Show All Users) or upgrade the license to a higher tier from the DA license provider."* Yesterday's Zoho code-1016 (deleted-tax) would have surfaced with *"Verify ZOHO_TAX_ID_GST18 / ZOHO_TAX_ID_IGST18 in .env.local AND in deploy-cloud-run.sh's ENV_VARS line. Redeploy after fixing."* etc. Adding a new hint is one entry in the PROVIDERS array — no schema change, no UI change. **Pattern clustering** — errors with structurally identical messages but varying numbers (e.g. "License is limited to 2 accounts" vs "License is limited to 5 accounts") cluster into one row via a normalised bucket key (lowercase + collapse whitespace + replace digits with "N" + first 120 chars). A recurring failure shows as ONE row with a count + first/last-seen timestamps + capped list of affected orders (20 max, each linking back to the admin order-management view), rather than N near-duplicate rows. **Time window selector** — 24h / 7d / 30d / 90d. Provider cards auto-expand when they have non-zero errors so the operator doesn't have to click in. Green "all clear" cards stay collapsed. **Two-deploy chain**: `dms-00196-s9c` shipped the Order-only sources + 4 providers (DA/Zoho/RC/Razorpay); `dms-00197-b9d` added the SystemLog source + 4 new providers (Email/Auth/Background/Application) once we confirmed v1 was working. Typecheck clean both deploys; no test additions (the page is read-only diagnostic UI over existing collections, no business logic to pin). **Future expansion is trivial** — to track a new provider (Cloudflare, SendGrid, anything), add one entry to PROVIDERS with signatures + hints. To pull from a new source (Payment failures, cron-job retries, etc.), add a `record()` call alongside the three existing ones. The whole architecture is config-over-code (deploys `dms-00196-s9c` + `dms-00197-b9d` both live, smoke test green on each)

- [x] **The diagnostic chain that finally surfaced the actual hosting-failure cause — raw upstream errors (DA / ResellerClub replies) now land in `Order.domains[i].error` for admin postmortem AND the customer-vs-admin error visibility was split so we never leak internal text to customers again** — two-deploy chain that turned the silent "Hosting provisioning failed. Our team has been notified..." banner into an actually-actionable error trail. **Background**: every hosting checkout today has been failing at the DirectAdmin step. We diagnosed and fixed `linkedDomain` at create-order (deploy `dms-00191-wgk`), but follow-up tests STILL failed with the same generic banner, and three different paths for surfacing the actual DA reply (Cloud Run stdout, SystemLog audit collection, PendingHosting MongoDB rows) were all unreliable for this service. We couldn't tell whether (a) the DA call itself was still being hit with bad parameters, (b) DA was rejecting for some other reason, or (c) something between create-order and the provisioner was eating the linkedDomain again.

**Deploy `dms-00194-mlz`** — `lib/services/payment/provisioner-hosting.ts` `handleHostingProvisionError` was constructing the `OrderDomain` return value WITHOUT including `linkedDomain` or the raw `details` string; `finalizePendingOrder` then did `Order.updateOne $set domains: orderDomains`, OVERWRITING both the linkedDomain that create-order had inferred + the raw error that the provisioner caught. Net effect: even when the provisioner caught the actual DA reply, that reply was vanishing on the DB write-back; the customer order row only retained the generic friendly text from the bookingStatus chain. Fix: added `linkedDomain?: string` to the `OrderDomain` interface (already in the Mongoose schema); both orderDomain constructions in provisioner-hosting (success + failure path) now propagate `item.linkedDomain`; the failure-path `orderDomain.error` now carries the raw upstream `details` string (DA message + status code) instead of the customer-friendly copy. 29/29 provisioner-hosting unit tests pass after the change.

**This worked first time** — the next fresh checkout (`ord_1782108258108_4tgpi3`) surfaced the actual DA reply on the order page: *"Cannot Execute Your Request - License is limited to 2 accounts, and you currently have 2"*. THAT'S the real cause — DA license is at its 2-account tier cap (now flagged as In-Flight above for operator action; no code fix can bypass a license limit). **Deploy `dms-00195-wsk`** (visibility split) — surfacing the raw error in `Order.domains[i].error` worked for diagnostics but ALSO caused the customer order page at `/dashboard/orders/[id]` to render the raw DA text directly in a red box at the bottom of the hosting card. Real customers shouldn't see "License is limited to 2 accounts" — they don't know what DA is, and the message looks alarming + meaningless. Fix in two places: (a) removed the redundant `domain.error` red box from the customer order detail page (the friendly bookingStatus copy is still rendered above it via the timeline, so the customer still knows their hosting failed and that support's been notified — they just don't see the upstream internal-state fragment); (b) upgraded the admin order-management page's error display from an inline pill (squeezed next to the price, unreadable for long DA replies) to a block-level red panel with an `Upstream provisioner error` label, monospace font, and `whitespace-pre-wrap break-words` so multi-line / verbose upstream errors are actually readable. **Net behaviour change**: failed hosting rows on the customer page no longer carry the raw red box; the same raw error is now a clearly-labelled diagnostic block on the admin page. This pattern generalises — when ResellerClub, Zoho, or any other upstream returns an error code in the future, the same routing applies: admin sees diagnostic-quality detail, customer sees friendly copy (deploys `dms-00194-mlz` + `dms-00195-wsk` both live, smoke test green)

- [x] **Account Settings save-and-bounce loop fixed — saving an incomplete profile no longer redirects the customer to /dashboard with no indication of which field is still missing** — caught by the senior reviewer this morning on the fresh-registration test flow. Reproduction: register a new account → activate via email link (now lands on /login correctly per `dms-00192-tcx`) → sign in → /dashboard prompts "Complete your profile" → land on Account Settings → fill in phone number → click Save Changes. **Bug**: the save handler called `router.push('/dashboard')` whenever the profile was still incomplete after the save. The customer would be silently bounced back to /dashboard, see the "Complete your profile" prompt again, get sent back to Settings, and have no idea which specific field was actually missing — just an opaque loop between two pages. The orange "Your address is missing — required for domain registration" banner at the top of Settings was technically there, but it wasn't drawing the customer's attention to the right section. **Root cause**: line 363 of `app/dashboard/settings/page.tsx` had `const dest = isComplete ? postSaveDestination : '/dashboard';` followed by an unconditional `router.push(dest)`. The intent was "if complete, return to the cart/wherever they came from"; the side-effect was "if incomplete, bounce them away from the form they need to keep filling out." **Fix**: when the save succeeds but `isComplete` is false, DON'T redirect at all. Instead inspect the updated user data to find the next missing field — if an address field is missing (line1 / city / state / zipcode all required), switch the page's `activeSection` to `'billing'` so the customer is now looking at the Billing & Address form and toast "Saved. Please also add your billing address to complete your profile." If only phone is missing, stay on the Profile tab (Contact Numbers section lives there) and toast accordingly. The complete-profile path is unchanged — the cart returnUrl flow + `/dashboard` fallback still work exactly as before for fully-completed saves. **Customer-visible improvement**: a fresh registration → activate → sign in → save phone → page now stays put and switches to the address form, customer adds address → save → now redirects to dashboard or cart returnUrl normally. The senior reviewer specifically called this out because they could see the orange address banner at the top but the redirect was masking what to do next (deploy `dms-00193-4kl` live)

- [x] **Three follow-up customer-experience fixes shipped together — post-activation redirect to /login (was 403), hosting-checkout linkedDomain inference (silent zombie-DA-account bug), and middleware CSRF bypass for the error-forwarder (visibility-gap that's been hiding root causes all week)** — three discrete improvements that landed in three quick deploys over a couple of hours; bundled into one Recently Shipped entry so the chain reads cleanly. **(1) Post-activation lands on /login, not /403** (deploy `dms-00192-tcx`, the senior reviewer's report): when a customer clicked the "Activate My Account" button in the welcome email after registering, they ended up at `/403` ("Access Denied") instead of the login page. Two layered bugs: (a) `app/activate/page.tsx` called `router.push('/dashboard')` after activation, but `/api/v1/auth/activate` only returns a legacy custom JWT — NOT a NextAuth session — so the middleware on `/dashboard` saw an unauthenticated request and bounced it to `/403`; (b) the middleware itself was redirecting unauthenticated hits on PROTECTED user routes (`/dashboard`, `/checkout`, etc.) to `/403` instead of `/login`, which is hostile UX in general — the customer wasn't *denied* access, they just weren't *signed in*. Same-origin-only path guard on the `returnUrl` querystring prevents open-redirect via a crafted parameter. Fix: activate page now redirects to `/login?message=Account+activated...&email=<address>` so the customer's email is pre-filled and they only need to type their password; LoginForm reads `?email=` and pre-fills the field; middleware protected-route gate now redirects to `/login?returnUrl=<original-path>` instead of `/403`. Admin pages keep their /403 behavior — those are not customer-facing and 403 is the right destination there. **(2) Hosting checkout auto-infers linkedDomain server-side** (deploy `dms-00191-wgk`, the senior reviewer's second stuck-hosting report today): a production audit on 2026-06-20 found that ALL 6 hosting orders in the past 60 days were being persisted with `linkedDomain=undefined`. The cart UI's "Please set up a domain for all hosting plans" check never actually populated the field on the request body — so the saved Order row always had the synthetic cart ID (`hosting-standard-1781957647738`) as the hosting "domain" with no real domain attached. Older orders silently "succeeded" in DirectAdmin with the synthetic ID as the username (zombie accounts the customer couldn't use); recent orders properly failed at DA with a generic "Hosting provisioning failed" toast. Server-side recovery: in create-order, if a hosting item has no `linkedDomain` AND the cart contains exactly one domain item, link them. The inference is unambiguous in practice — every observed failed/zombie order matched this shape (1 domain + 1 hosting). Inference happens BEFORE persistence so the DB row + the /verify-side cart reconstruction + the provisioner all see the same real domain. Security invariant from `dms-00174-xpk` preserved (server still reads cart from DB row, not from client). 38/38 create-order route tests pass. **(3) Middleware CSRF bypass for SELF_AUTHENTICATING_ADMIN_API** (deploy `dms-00190-jmx`): this morning's `dms-00179-fwc` fix added the `SELF_AUTHENTICATING_ADMIN_API` allowlist exemption to the admin-role check at line ~318 of middleware — but the CSRF check at line ~350 fires FIRST in the pipeline and had no such exemption. So `/api/v1/admin/log-error` (the server-to-self forwarder that `serverLogger.error()` posts to) was still being 403'd at the CSRF layer before the admin-role bypass could kick in. Net effect: every server-side error logged today via `serverLogger.error()` was silently lost — including, most importantly, the actual hosting-provisioning failure reason on the customer's first stuck order this afternoon (we only narrowed it to linkedDomain via the bookingStatus chain in the DB, not via the log forwarder). Same class of partial-fix as the admin-dashboard "Oops!" chain (`dms-00179-fwc` + `dms-00180-6nj`) — two gates in the pipeline, fix one but not the other, error vanishes silently anyway. Fix: SELF_AUTHENTICATING_ADMIN_API now bypasses BOTH the CSRF gate AND the admin-role gate. The route handler at `app/api/admin/log-error/route.ts` still enforces its own auth (x-cron-secret OR session OR same-origin), so the endpoint isn't actually weakened — the rejection just happens at the right layer. 64/64 middleware tests pass. **Pending verification**: senior reviewer needs to (a) activate one more test account end-to-end to confirm the /login redirect + email pre-fill, (b) place one more hosting checkout to confirm the auto-link makes it all the way through DirectAdmin. The 2 stuck hosting orders from this afternoon (`rajustore.com`, `rajufv.com`) + the May-19 Abhishek-Sharma stuck order are still in the `failed` state — once forward-fix is verified, a 5-minute MongoDB backfill (write `linkedDomain` onto those 3 specific rows) + admin Re-sync will repair them retroactively (deploys `dms-00190-jmx` + `dms-00191-wgk` + `dms-00192-tcx` all live; smoke test green on each)

- [x] **Zoho Books invoice generation now actually works in production — the deploy script's env-var list was missing the two GST tax IDs, so every invoice attempt for the past ~month had been failing silently with Zoho's "Some of the taxes have been deleted" rejection** — final fix of the stuck-invoice investigation the senior reviewer started this afternoon. **What was actually broken**: `scripts/deploy-cloud-run.sh` has an `ENV_VARS` string that explicitly lists every non-secret environment variable Cloud Run should receive (because Cloud Run doesn't read `.env.local` directly — only the values explicitly passed via `--set-env-vars` and `--set-secrets` reach the running service). That `ENV_VARS` line was carrying `ZOHO_DC`, `ZOHO_LOCATION_ID`, `ZOHO_ORG_ID`, and `ZOHO_ORG_STATE` — but **NOT** `ZOHO_TAX_ID_GST18` and `ZOHO_TAX_ID_IGST18`. So even though `.env.local` had the correct active tax IDs for the production Zoho org `60071125342` (`3847734000000032211` for GST18 and `3847734000000032173` for IGST18, both verified active just now via Zoho's `/api/v3/settings/taxes` admin endpoint), Cloud Run wasn't seeing those values and the code in `lib/zohobooks.ts` was falling back to **hardcoded defaults from a different Zoho org** (`3650677000000328294` / `3650677000000328134` — verified DELETED in the current production org). Zoho rejected every invoice creation with **code 1016 "Some of the taxes have been deleted. Hence, these taxes cannot be selected or applied."** for the entire period this misconfiguration was live. **Why nobody noticed for a month**: two earlier bugs colluded to hide it — (a) `lib/logger.ts` silences `console.error` in production so the customer-facing JS never showed anything, and (b) the middleware was 403'ing the `/api/v1/admin/log-error` server-to-self forwarder (the bug we fixed in `dms-00179-fwc` this morning) so the server-side error didn't reach the SystemLog audit collection either. The Admin Invoice Diagnostics panel surfaced the stuck order to the senior reviewer for the first time only after both visibility fixes landed today. Then the error-surfacing patch from `dms-00188-lfm` made the Re-sync button report Zoho's actual error string instead of the generic axios "Request failed with status code 400" — and that's what finally told us "code 1016 deleted-tax". **Two-part fix** (commit `cd3a6f7`): (1) `scripts/deploy-cloud-run.sh` `ENV_VARS` now propagates `ZOHO_TAX_ID_GST18` + `ZOHO_TAX_ID_IGST18` from `.env.local` to Cloud Run on every future deploy; (2) the hardcoded fallback in `lib/zohobooks.ts` now points at the current production org's active tax IDs (`3847734...`), so even a future regression that drops the env var still produces working invoices instead of cascading to code 1016. **Verified live by the senior reviewer**: clicking "Re-sync" on the stuck May-19 invoice `ord_1779188681635_zn8xbd` (Abhishek Sharma, ₹2,698.8) succeeded on the first click — the diagnostics panel cleared, the row flipped from `creation_failed` to a real Zoho invoice ID, and the invoice is now downloadable. **What this unblocks**: every customer who placed a paid order since the tax-ID env var was lost can now have their invoice re-generated by clicking Re-sync on the diagnostics panel (the bulk "Re-sync all" button on the same panel handles N stuck orders sequentially with a progress indicator). **Prevention measure**: the `.env.local` ↔ `deploy-cloud-run.sh` `ENV_VARS` synchronisation gap is the same class of bug as the `.env.local` ↔ Secret Manager split we documented in auto-memory this morning (`project_secret_manager_split`). The same memory has been extended to cover the ENV_VARS list too: when adding a NEW env var, you must add it to `.env.local` AND to the appropriate list in `deploy-cloud-run.sh` (`ENV_VARS` for non-secret values, `SECRETS_FLAG` for Secret-Manager-backed values) — there is no automatic propagation; CLAUDE.md / agent memory now catches future cases. 166/166 Zoho-Books unit tests pass on the new code; full suite at 5977/5977 (deploy `dms-00189-7nn` live)

- [x] **Admin "Re-sync" button on `/admin/invoices` now shows Zoho's actual rejection reason + Inter webfont moved off Google's CDN to eliminate a recurring deploy-pipeline failure mode** — two infrastructure improvements ship together as one deploy (`dms-00188-lfm`), both directly enabling progress on the senior reviewer's stuck-invoice investigation that started earlier this session. **(1) Zoho Re-sync error-message surfacing**: when the senior reviewer clicked the "Re-sync" button on the stuck May-19 order (`ord_1779188681635_zn8xbd`, Abhishek Sharma, ₹2,698.8, surfaced today by the new Invoice Diagnostics panel that the middleware fix made visible), the toast came back as just **"Request failed with status code 400"** — which is the literal default message axios throws when an HTTP call gets a 4xx response, with zero detail about WHICH field Zoho rejected or WHICH business rule was violated. Root cause: the admin re-sync route at `app/api/admin/orders/[id]/re-sync-invoice/route.ts` caught the axios error in its outer try/catch, extracted only `error.message` (the generic axios string), and returned that in the 500 response — silently throwing away `error.response.data.message` and `error.response.data.code` which are where Zoho actually puts the rejection detail. Fix: the catch block now detects axios errors specifically, pulls Zoho's inner message / code / HTTP status, and bubbles them up in the JSON response + the structured server log. The admin toast will now show something like *"Zoho rejected the request: Customer GSTIN is required (code 3057) [HTTP 400]"* or *"Zoho rejected the request: Invalid value for contact_id (code 3062) [HTTP 400]"* — telling the operator exactly which case the stuck order is tripping on, without having to scrape Cloud Run logs (which are also partially broken — stdout/stderr stream for this service is mostly empty). Same diagnostic-first pattern as the GoogleRecaptcha `console.error` carve-out from `dms-00183-4ts` earlier today. **14/14 existing re-sync route tests still pass** — the new path only affects the error catch, not happy-path behaviour. **(2) Self-hosted Inter webfont — removed Google Fonts build-time dependency**: deploying patch (1) above immediately hit a build failure that we'd never seen before — Docker's `npm run build` step inside the container failed with `AggregateError: ETIMEDOUT` after 3 retries against `fonts.googleapis.com` / `fonts.gstatic.com`, even though `curl` from the Windows host outside Docker returned `200 OK` in 1.2 seconds. So the public internet was fine; Docker Desktop's network layer had a specific failure for that destination. Two consecutive build attempts hit the same wall — not transient. Root cause: `app/layout.tsx` was using `next/font/google` with `Inter({ subsets: ['latin'] })`, which fetches the font CSS + woff2 files from Google's CDN at *build time* (Next.js inlines them into the bundle for performance). When the build host can't reach Google's CDN — for whatever DNS / firewall / Docker overlay reason — the build fails outright and the whole deploy is blocked. Fix: downloaded the official Inter variable font files from `rsms.me/inter` (the canonical Inter source, freely redistributable) directly on the Windows host (which still had network reach), committed both `InterVariable.woff2` (352 KB) and `InterVariable-Italic.woff2` (388 KB) under `public/fonts/`, and switched `app/layout.tsx` to `next/font/local` with `display: 'swap'`. **Customer-visible behaviour: zero change** — same glyph set, same swap strategy, identical rendering. **Build pipeline reliability: significantly better** — the deploy no longer depends on `fonts.gstatic.com` being reachable from the build host's Docker network. This is also the right shape for a 12-factor deploy pipeline (build artefacts should be hermetic, not externally dependent). Total bundle delta: ~740 KB of font files, gzipped to ~165 KB on the wire, served from our own CDN going forward. **What this unblocks**: the senior reviewer can now click Re-sync on the May-19 stuck order again and we'll see the actual Zoho-side reason it failed — so we can either fix that specific case end-to-end or kick the case-specific fix into the next batch with confidence about what's needed. Without (1), every Re-sync click would keep showing the useless "status code 400" string; without (2), we couldn't have shipped (1) at all (deploy `dms-00188-lfm` live, smoke test green; failed-build deploys `dms-00187` skipped over)

- [x] **Sign-In is now actually working through the captcha — server-side verification was failing because the deploy script reads the secret from Google Secret Manager, not from `.env.local`** — after the widget started rendering correctly (deploy `dms-00184-bfp`, the `grecaptcha.ready()` fix earlier today), the senior reviewer hit the next failure mode: ticking "I'm not a robot" went green and the Sign In button enabled — but clicking it surfaced a red **"CaptchaFailed"** toast. So the widget+token half worked; the server-side verification half was rejecting every token. **Root cause**: the deploy pipeline (`scripts/deploy-cloud-run.sh`) reads sensitive runtime secrets — including `RECAPTCHA_SECRET_KEY` — from **Google Secret Manager** (`--set-secrets="RECAPTCHA_SECRET_KEY=RECAPTCHA_SECRET_KEY:latest"`), NOT from `.env.local`. When the operator rotated the reCAPTCHA key pair earlier today (after Google rejected the carried-over key as "Invalid site key"), both halves of the new key pair were correctly pasted into `.env.local` — but only the PUBLIC site key (`NEXT_PUBLIC_RECAPTCHA_SITE_KEY`) flowed through to the running service from there, because public values are baked into the JS bundle at Docker-build time as a `--build-arg`. The SECRET key sat in `.env.local` unused; the running Cloud Run service kept loading the OLD secret from Secret Manager's previous version. **End-to-end consequence**: the browser was signing reCAPTCHA tokens with the NEW site key; the server was trying to verify them with the OLD secret; Google rejected every pair → server threw `CaptchaFailed` → toast shown to customer. **The fix** is a two-step Secret-Manager rotation that the operator can't do via the .env.local file: (1) `gcloud secrets versions add RECAPTCHA_SECRET_KEY --data-file=-` pushed the new secret as version 2 of the secret; (2) a fresh Cloud Run deploy was required because revisions cache secret values at container startup (so the `:latest` binding alone wasn't enough — the running revision had the old value in memory until a new container was started). **Verified live on the new production revision** — Sign In now completes end-to-end with the captcha ticked. **Prevention measure baked in**: the `.env.local` ↔ Secret Manager split is now documented in auto-memory as `project_secret_manager_split` so future operators (and future Claude Code sessions) know that rotating any secret in the `SECRETS_FLAG` list of `deploy-cloud-run.sh` (Razorpay key, MongoDB URI, Zoho refresh token, NextAuth secret, etc.) requires the `gcloud secrets versions add` step + a redeploy, not just a `.env.local` edit. **The full Phase 2 captcha install arc closes here** — 6 deploys total across one day to go from "Phase 1 rip-out three days ago" to "Phase 2 widget rendering and Sign-In verifying end-to-end": `dms-00181-zvd` (install) → `dms-00182-8kq` (key-pair refresh) → `dms-00183-4ts` (diagnostic patch) → `dms-00184-bfp` (`grecaptcha.ready()` fix) → `dms-00185-cjb` (revert diagnostic inline text) → `dms-00186-fdm` (Secret Manager secret rotation, live) — every step preserved in the audit trail (deploy `dms-00186-fdm` live)

- [x] **Captcha widget now actually renders on customer-facing forms — the Phase 2 install was tripping on a latent client-side timing bug that took 3 deploy cycles to surface** — after the Phase 2 install (deploy `dms-00181-zvd`) and the key-pair refresh (deploy `dms-00182-8kq`) both shipped, the senior reviewer reported the captcha widget STILL wouldn't render — instead of the "I'm not a robot" checkbox, the page showed a red "Failed to render verification widget" message and the Sign In button stayed disabled. The reviewer tested across Chrome Incognito, Firefox Private Browsing, and Chrome Guest mode — all three showed the identical failure. **Root cause** (took two diagnostic deploys to surface): the project's client-side logger in `lib/logger.ts` is deliberately silenced in production (only `console.*` in development; production routes through a server forwarder), and Cloud Run's stdout/stderr stream for this service was empty during the diagnosis, so the actual underlying JavaScript error was invisible from outside. **Diagnostic patch** (commit `77f3c99`, deploy `dms-00183-4ts`): appended the underlying error message to the user-visible "Failed to render verification widget" text AND added a raw `console.error('[GoogleRecaptcha] render error:', renderError)` that bypassed the production-silenced logger. With that one extra line, the next page-load surfaced the exact error: **"n.render is not a function"** — meaning `grecaptcha.render` was undefined when our code called it. **Underlying bug**: Google's `https://www.google.com/recaptcha/api.js` defines `window.grecaptcha` as a stub object early in its load (the global appears almost immediately), but its public methods (`render`, `reset`, `getResponse`) are wired up asynchronously a few hundred milliseconds later. Our `RecaptchaClient.loadScript()` was polling for the global's existence with `if (g) resolve()` — which resolved on the stub, then `grecaptcha.render(...)` threw because `.render` wasn't there yet. This bug was latent for ~a year — it didn't surface pre-Phase-1 because Google's script init was historically fast enough that `.render` was always attached before we polled. Sometime in the 3-day gap between the Phase 1 rip (2026-06-17) and the Phase 2 install (2026-06-20), Google shifted their script's edge-build timing enough to expose the race. **The real fix** (commit `9765b62`, deploy `dms-00184-bfp` live): the polling loop in `RecaptchaClient.loadScript()` now waits for Google's official `grecaptcha.ready(cb)` callback — the "API is callable now" hook Google publishes for exactly this purpose — with a fallback poll on `typeof grecaptcha.render === 'function'` for cases where Google's edge serves a build of `api.js` where `.ready` itself hasn't landed yet; bounded at 100 tries × 100 ms = 10-second maximum wait, after which the loader rejects with a clear error instead of hanging forever (the original loop was unbounded). The diagnostic carve-out from `dms-00183-4ts` is being kept in place — pairs `console.error` with the production-silenced `logger.error` so the next regression is visible in one DevTools refresh. **Verified by the senior reviewer end-to-end on `dms-00184-bfp`** — the "I'm not a robot" checkbox now renders cleanly, ticking it enables the Sign In button, and the post-submit reCAPTCHA token verifies successfully on the server. **Prevention measures**: (1) the loadScript polling-gate pattern is documented in auto-memory as `project_recaptcha_ready_gate` so future Claude Code sessions don't repeat the diagnostic detour; (2) the `console.error` carve-out for widget-init errors is documented as `feedback_diagnostic_console_error` and should be applied to any future third-party-widget loader (Razorpay, Stripe Elements, etc.) so production logger suppression never again blocks a real customer-facing failure mode (deploys `dms-00183-4ts` diagnostic + `dms-00184-bfp` real fix both live; previous attempts `dms-00181-zvd` + `dms-00182-8kq` shipped earlier today and were the right code with the wrong ready-gate)

- [x] **Fresh reCAPTCHA v2 key-pair issued and rolled into the build — the carried-over key from before the Phase 1 rip was rejected by Google as "Invalid site key"** — minutes after Phase 2 (deploy `dms-00181-zvd`) put the captcha widget back on the customer-facing forms, the senior reviewer reported the login page rendered the widget but with a red **"ERROR for site owner: Invalid site key"** banner inside it and the matching "reCAPTCHA verification failed. Please try again." text underneath; the Sign In button stayed disabled as a result. Diagnosis: the site key currently baked into the production bundle (verified by reading the live JS chunk directly) matched the value in the local build PC's `.env.local` character-for-character (no typo, no whitespace artifact, no Unicode lookalike) — so the Phase 2 build was correctly carrying through the operator's input. The actual problem was on Google's side: that specific key was no longer registered in Google's reCAPTCHA admin console. The most likely sequence: the key was de-registered (manually or as part of a routine cleanup) at some point between when Phase 1 ripped reCAPTCHA out of the codebase (deploy `dms-00171-96f`, 2026-06-17) and when Phase 2 reinstalled it (today, 2026-06-20). Three days of "key looks valid, just unused" was enough for it to get pruned. **Fix**: the operator generated a fresh reCAPTCHA v2 (Checkbox) site + secret key pair in Google's admin console with `app.anutech.in` registered as an allowed domain; both values went into `.env.local` on the build PC (site key as `NEXT_PUBLIC_RECAPTCHA_SITE_KEY` — public, gets baked into the JS bundle at Docker-build time as a `--build-arg`; secret as `RECAPTCHA_SECRET_KEY` — server-side only, picked up as a Cloud Run env var by the deploy script, never reaches the customer's browser); rebuilt + redeployed in one cycle. **Verified live on the new production revision** by pulling the freshly-built JS chunk directly and confirming the new site-key prefix (`6LfeiS…sZ52`) is what's now embedded — different from the deleted Phase-1-era key. **Customer-impact note for the reviewer**: please hard-refresh `app.anutech.in/login` (Ctrl + F5 / Cmd + Shift + R) to force-drop the cached pre-fix JS, then the "I'm not a robot" checkbox should render cleanly without any red error banner and Sign In should re-enable as soon as you tick it (deploy `dms-00182-8kq` live)

- [x] **Google reCAPTCHA v2 re-introduced project-wide — Phase 2 of the planned rip-and-replace** — three days after Phase 1 ripped the captcha out at the root (deploy `dms-00171-96f`, kept below for the audit trail), the re-install has landed. **What's back live**: the "I'm not a robot" checkbox renders again on all **5 customer-facing forms** — login, register, contact, forgot-password, reset-password — and the token they emit on submit is verified server-side on all **6 affected endpoints** (`/api/auth/register`, `/api/auth/forgot-password`, `/api/auth/reset-password`, `/api/contact`, `/api/payments/create-order`, `/api/user/hosting/trial-eligibility`) PLUS in the NextAuth credentials provider (for the login path) PLUS in the trial-abuse pipeline (with the 0.5 min-score gate for any future v3 callers). **What's intentionally different from the pre-rip install**: (a) **no admin DB toggle** — Phase 1 had an admin Settings → Security "captcha on/off" switch that ran into the step-up-reauth quirk twice and ate two hotfix cycles to keep working; Phase 2 makes **the env var the only kill switch** (clearing `RECAPTCHA_SECRET_KEY` on the server flips the entire system to skip verification, no DB write or restart needed), so the failure surface for operating the toggle is gone; (b) **no `/api/v1/settings/captcha-status` polling** — Phase 1 had the client widget call this endpoint on every page load to learn whether captcha was enabled; Phase 2 uses pure env-var presence (`NEXT_PUBLIC_RECAPTCHA_SITE_KEY` on the client, `RECAPTCHA_SECRET_KEY` on the server) to gate render and verification, so there's one fewer round-trip on every form page. **What carries over from the pre-rip install**: the deterministic `useId()`-based container ID (the React #418 hydration-mismatch fix from `dms-00166-pn8`), the reset-on-failed-login behavior, and the same provider (Google reCAPTCHA v2 again — switching to a different vendor was considered in Phase 1 but the third-party-cookie incognito issue that motivated the consideration is actually a Chrome-level setting on the customer's end, not something a vendor swap would fix). **Defence-in-depth posture restored to its full pre-rip strength**: same-origin/CSRF check + per-endpoint rate limits + single-use 1-hour reset tokens + email-enumeration neutralisation + disposable-email blocklist + IP/device-fingerprint/phone-OTP on trial flow ALL continue to run; the captcha sits on top of all of them as an extra layer. **Verification**: full unit test suite passes — 5977/5977 across 405 test files; the 3 client-form tests that asserted exact API call payloads were updated to expect `recaptchaToken: null` when the widget hasn't been rendered (JSDOM doesn't load Google's script); typecheck passes clean; lint produces only pre-existing warnings (no new ones from the captcha changes). **Customer-impact note for the reviewer**: the login / register / contact / forgot-password / reset-password pages now show the familiar "I'm not a robot" checkbox again — please complete the checkbox on submit. If the box doesn't render at all on your browser (3rd-party cookie blocked, or Google's domains blocked at the network level), the submit button stays disabled in production — same UX as the pre-rip install (deploy `dms-00181-zvd` live)

- [x] **Admin dashboard "Oops!" crash FIXED and error visibility hardened (3 chained fixes shipped today)** — the admin dashboard at `/admin/dashboard` that was hitting the React error boundary instead of rendering the system-health overview (yesterday's in-flight entry, kept below for the audit trail) is now resolved. **What shipped**: three chained fixes across revisions `dms-00179-fwc` and `dms-00180-6nj`: (A) the actual crash — Zoho Books' `getOrganizationDetails` API was returning `plan_type` as a non-string truthy value (boolean false or numeric tier ID for orgs in certain subscription states); the dashboard's `zb.planType.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + zb.planType.slice(1)` call threw "planType.charAt is not a function" because `.charAt()` is a string method. Fixed at two layers — the system-health API endpoint now coerces `org.plan_type` and `org.plan_name` to `String()` at the source, and the dashboard's render expression coerces defensively too so a future Zoho field drifting to non-string won't crash again. (B) The infrastructure-wide visibility gap that hid (A) for hours — Next.js's error boundary at `app/error.tsx` forwards every caught error to `/api/v1/admin/log-error` so it lands in the SystemLog collection. The application's middleware had a blanket "any `/api/admin/*` request requires admin role" check that fired BEFORE the route handler — and the log-forwarder is a server-to-self fetch with no session cookie, so middleware 403'd it before its own multi-factor auth could run. Same forwarder failure that masked the Zoho orphan-contact error on 2026-06-18 and the Mongoose paymentVerification + linkedDomain bugs earlier this week. Fix: added a small `SELF_AUTHENTICATING_ADMIN_API` allowlist in middleware so the log-forwarder skips the admin-only gate; the route's own auth still rejects unauthorised requests, just at the right layer. (C) The browser console silently-suppressing-errors gap that hid (A) too — `app/error.tsx` only fetched the (failing) log-forwarder; it never echoed the error to the browser console. So a developer with dev tools open got blank output. Fix: added `console.error('[error-boundary]', error)` BEFORE the fetch so browser console shows it directly regardless of whether the forwarder succeeds. **How (B) unblocked (A)**: once the middleware fix shipped (revision `dms-00179-fwc`), the next admin-dashboard visit successfully wrote to SystemLog; a read-only DB query surfaced the exact `"planType.charAt is not a function"` message + JS stack trace; (A) became a one-line fix once we could read it. **Customer-impact note**: only the admin dashboard was affected throughout — every other admin route (orders, hosting, support, system-settings, etc.) and every customer-facing page worked normally. Ordinary customers couldn't reach the admin route at all. **Verification**: 933 admin route tests pass; smoke test against the new revision returns HTTP 200; the dashboard now renders the full system-health overview (services panel, infrastructure panel, background-jobs panel). **Please reload `app.anutech.in/admin/dashboard`** — should now show the dashboard cleanly. (Deploys `dms-00179-fwc` middleware fix + `dms-00180-6nj` actual crash fix both live)

- [x] **Admin dashboard at `/admin/dashboard` showing "Oops! Something went wrong" — investigation in progress + middleware fix to restore error-logging visibility committed** — ✅ RESOLVED on 2026-06-20. Title preserved verbatim from the In-Flight entry so the reviewer dashboard reconciles in-flight → done state on the same item. **Full resolution detail** (3 chained fixes, all live as of revision `dms-00180-6nj`): **(A) The actual crash** — the Zoho Books `getOrganizationDetails` API can return `plan_type` as a non-string value (boolean `false`, or sometimes a numeric tier ID) for orgs in certain subscription states. The dashboard then did `zb.planType.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + zb.planType.slice(1)` to format the plan name — and `.charAt()` is a string method, not a number/boolean method, so it threw `planType.charAt is not a function`. Two-layer fix: the system-health API endpoint now coerces `org.plan_type` (and `org.plan_name`) to `String()` at the source so the dashboard payload is always a string; AND the dashboard's render expression now coerces defensively too, so the UI doesn't crash if any future Zoho field also drifts to a non-string type. **(B) The visibility gap that hid (A) — middleware was 403'ing the error-forwarder** — Next.js's error boundary at `app/error.tsx` forwards every caught error to `/api/v1/admin/log-error` so it lands in the SystemLog collection. But the application's middleware had a blanket "any request matching `/api/admin/*` requires an admin role token" check that fired BEFORE the route handler — and the log-error forwarder is a server-to-self fetch with no session cookie, so middleware 403'd it before its own multi-factor auth (cron-secret OR session OR origin) could run. Same forwarder failure that masked the Zoho orphan-contact error on 2026-06-18 and the Mongoose paymentVerification + linkedDomain bugs earlier this week. Fix: added a small `SELF_AUTHENTICATING_ADMIN_API` allowlist in middleware so the log-error path skips the admin-only gate; the route's own auth still rejects unauthorised requests, just at the right layer. **(C) The console gap that hid (A) too — error.tsx didn't call `console.error`** — the error boundary only fetched the (failing) log-forwarder; it never echoed the error to the browser console. So a developer with dev tools open got blank output. Fix: added `console.error('[error-boundary]', error)` BEFORE the fetch so browser console shows it directly, regardless of whether the forwarder succeeds. **Once (B) shipped (revision `dms-00179-fwc`), the next dashboard visit successfully wrote to SystemLog, a read-only DB query surfaced the exact "planType.charAt is not a function" message + stack trace, and (A) became a one-line fix.** **Customer-impact note for the reviewer**: only the admin dashboard (`/admin/dashboard`) was affected — every other admin route (orders, hosting, support, system-settings, etc.) and every customer-facing page (homepage, search, cart, checkout, customer dashboard) continued to work throughout. Ordinary customers couldn't reach the admin route at all. **Verification**: 933 admin route tests pass; smoke test against the new revision returns HTTP 200. Please reload `/admin/dashboard` — should render the system-health overview cleanly now (deploys `dms-00179-fwc` middleware fix + `dms-00180-6nj` actual crash fix + error.tsx console.error improvement all live)

— Original in-flight notes (preserved verbatim for the reviewer-dashboard title match): the senior reviewer reported the admin dashboard page is hitting its React error boundary instead of rendering the system-health overview. The page itself returns HTTP 200 with fast latency (~30-160ms) and the one API call the dashboard makes (`/api/v1/admin/system-health`) also returns 200 — so the failure is **client-side**, somewhere in the render of the fetched data. **Why we can't see the actual error from outside**: the application's secondary error-forwarder (the one that's supposed to copy client + server errors into the SystemLog collection so ops can read them without DB access) has been silently failing with **HTTP 403** for several days. Same forwarder failure that hid the actual Zoho error on 2026-06-18 (the orphan-contact issue we eventually surfaced via a separate read-only Zoho API probe) and hid the original paymentVerification + linkedDomain server logs earlier this week. Every time something went wrong, the error was logged, the forwarder tried to copy it to SystemLog via a POST to `/api/v1/admin/log-error`, and that POST got 403'd before reaching the route handler. **Root cause of the 403**: the application's middleware has a blanket "admin-API requires admin token" gate that fires on every request matching `/api/admin/*` (and the versioned `/api/v1/admin/*`). The log-error route's OWN authorisation logic (cron-secret OR session OR same-origin) was designed to handle the server-side log-forwarder case where there's no session cookie — but it never got to run because middleware blocked the request first. So the forwarder was making a server-to-self POST without a session cookie, middleware saw "admin API + no admin token = 403", and the error vanished. **Fix committed (1545285 → 77ef723 → next commit)**: added a small `SELF_AUTHENTICATING_ADMIN_API` allowlist in middleware. The log-error route is in the allowlist; the admin-only gate is skipped for it, and the route's own multi-factor auth (cron-secret OR session OR origin match) runs as designed. CSRF + token-fetch + downstream protections all continue to apply uniformly — this is not an auth weakening, just moving the rejection from middleware (wrong layer) to the route handler (right layer). **Why this fix is in flight, not yet live**: the local Windows build PC's Docker Desktop went idle and the build attempt failed at the Docker connection step. Source is already pushed to GitHub — just need to relaunch Docker Desktop and retry the build → push → deploy. **After the middleware fix lands**: the next time the admin dashboard hits its error boundary, the actual error message + stack trace will land in the SystemLog collection, we can read it with a single read-only DB query, and surface the actual undefined-variable / render-time exception that's crashing the page. Then a separate small follow-up commit will fix THAT root cause. **Customer-impact note for the reviewer**: only the admin dashboard (`/admin/dashboard`) is affected — customer-facing pages (the homepage, the search box, the cart, checkout, the customer dashboard) all continue to work. Ordinary customers can't reach the admin route at all. Admins can still log in and access every other admin page (orders, hosting, support, system-settings, etc.); only the top-level dashboard overview is showing the error boundary.

## Recently Shipped — user-visible improvements

- [x] **Homepage search-box prompt "We'll check .com, .net, .in and more for…" is now visible on the blue hero — was rendering as dark text on a dark blue background, almost invisible** — the senior reviewer noticed the prompt that appears below the search input as soon as a customer starts typing was nearly unreadable on the homepage hero. The Sparkles ✨ icon was muted blue and the prompt text was dark gray — both sitting directly on the deep-blue hero background. Root cause: the search-input component takes a `theme` prop ("light" or "dark") that picks colors for several elements. The light-theme colors (gray text + blue icon) were designed assuming the prompt sits on a white card backdrop. Both production usages — the homepage hero (`app/page.tsx`) and the `/domains/search` page — correctly pass `theme="light"` because the **search input itself** does sit on a white card. But the prompt message renders **outside** that card, directly on the blue hero below it, where light-theme colors became invisible. The fix dropped the theme conditional on this specific prompt and hard-coded high-contrast colors that read against the blue hero: a yellow Sparkles icon (`text-yellow-300`) + a near-white prompt text (`text-white/90`). Both production usages benefit, no current call site puts this prompt on a white background, so the simplification doesn't regress anything. 67 domain-search unit tests pass (deploy `dms-00178-8p4` live).

- [x] **Zoho Books invoice for the senior reviewer's test order is not generating — payment cleared, services provisioned, only the accounting-system handoff failed** — ✅ RESOLVED on 2026-06-19. Title is preserved verbatim from the original In-Flight entry so the senior reviewer's dashboard reconciles the in-flight → done state as the same item (the dashboard matches entries by title). The original symptom: yesterday's successful test checkout left the order detail page showing the order as Paid and both services provisioned, but the dashboard's **Invoices section showed the invoice row as "Generating · Retry"** instead of "Paid + view + download" — the bookkeeping-system handoff (we sync paid orders to Zoho Books, which generates the customer-facing PDF invoice + the GST-compliant record) had failed silently during the post-payment step. The order's `zohoInvoiceId` was the sentinel value `creation_failed` (what our code writes when the Zoho API call throws). **Investigation method**: because the secondary error-forwarder that's supposed to copy errors into Google Cloud Logging is being 403'd by middleware, we couldn't read the original error from outside; instead, we wrote a strictly read-only Zoho-API probe (refresh-token health-check, contact lookups by email + name + id, tax-config readout) that ran from the local PC against the production Zoho org. The probe surfaced the exact issue in two minutes. **Root cause**: the senior reviewer's existing Zoho contact (`3847734000000049001`, created in October 2025 for invoice INV-000005) had had its `contact_persons` array **manually emptied** in Zoho's UI sometime between October and now. With no email on the contact, Zoho's email-search returned zero hits. Our code then tried to create a brand-new contact named "Deepak Sharma" — but Zoho enforces unique contact names per organisation, so the create attempt got rejected. **There IS already a fallback** in the contact-creation code for the duplicate-name rejection (calls Zoho's name-search to recover the existing contact) — **but** the fallback was gated on a specific numeric Zoho error code (3062), and Zoho's India data centre apparently returned a different code on this rejection, so the recovery branch never fired. The error then propagated up, the post-payment Zoho step caught + recorded `creation_failed`, the rest of /payments/verify completed normally (payment captured + services provisioned), and the invoice was the only thing left missing. **The fix** (committed as `279ca22`): do the name-based lookup **proactively** when the email lookup misses, BEFORE attempting `createContact`. Zoho enforces unique contact_name per organisation, so a name hit is guaranteed to be the same person (not a stranger). This makes the recovery completely independent of whichever specific error-code variant Zoho's regional data centre returns. The follow-up "sync user details to Zoho contact" step that already ran on the email-found path now ALSO runs on the name-found path — which means **the orphan contact's email gets patched back onto Zoho's side as part of the recovery**, so future invoices for this customer will succeed via the cheaper email-based lookup and never need this fallback. **What about the customer who's currently stuck on this order?** The dashboard's Invoices page has a built-in self-heal that fires on every load (gated by a 5-minute Redis throttle so reloads within that window are no-ops). The next time the senior reviewer visits `/dashboard/invoices`, the self-heal will re-attempt the Zoho call, the new fallback will pick up the orphan contact, and the row will flip from "Generating · Retry" to "Paid" with the view + download actions. Alternatively, clicking the "Retry" button on the row triggers the same flow immediately. **Verification**: 166 Zoho-Books unit tests pass (164 original + 2 new regression tests pinning the name-fallback path); 565 broader payment + order + Zoho tests pass; the diagnosis was confirmed end-to-end via the read-only Zoho probe (orphan contact exists with empty contact_persons, name search returns it correctly). **No customer money was ever at risk** at any point — Razorpay had the payment, services were live; this was purely about the customer being able to download their invoice PDF (deploy `dms-00177-g9k` live)

- [x] **Build host moved off the rented Linux VPS onto the local Windows PC — cuts the last ongoing infrastructure dependency for the deploy pipeline** — earlier this cycle we cut the recurring Google Cloud Build cost by switching the Docker image build from Cloud Build to a local build on the project's own VPS (2 vCPU, ~$24/month savings from skipping Cloud Build at 10+ deploys/day, but added ~50% to the per-deploy wall-clock time because of the smaller machine). Today we took the next step: **moved the build host off the VPS entirely onto the local Windows PC where the project is now developed**. The local PC has 28 logical processors vs the VPS's 2, so builds are dramatically faster — a cold build (no Docker layer cache) took **3 minutes 35 seconds** vs the VPS's typical 6 minutes; a warm build (subsequent deploys reusing the previous layer cache) took **just 1 minute 15 seconds** end-to-end — 5× faster than the VPS, 8× faster than the original Cloud Build path. **What moved**: (1) the full source tree (via git clone — no special migration since the code lives on GitHub anyway), (2) the `.env.local` configuration file containing production secrets (via SCP, never via chat or unencrypted channel), (3) the Claude Code conversation history + auto-memory directory so the assistant's context is preserved across the move. **What had to be set up on the local PC**: Docker Desktop (one-time install + one Windows feature toggle for the WSL2 backend — Virtual Machine Platform Optional Component), gcloud CLI (already had it for other projects, just needed `gcloud config set project speedy-unison-453807-e9` and `gcloud auth configure-docker us-central1-docker.pkg.dev` to wire up the Docker → Artifact Registry credential helper), and a GitHub personal-access token stored in Windows Credential Manager (encrypted under the current Windows user account — not in any plaintext config file) so the deploy script's automatic git-tag-push at the end of every successful deploy works without manual intervention. **What stayed put**: the deploy script itself is identical — same `bash scripts/deploy-cloud-run.sh --skip-ci-check` command from the project root, same end result (a new Cloud Run revision serving traffic). Cloud Run + Artifact Registry + Secret Manager + DirectAdmin + ResellerClub + Razorpay all continue to talk to the same backends — they don't care where the build originated. **What this means in practice**: the rented Linux VPS is no longer load-bearing for the deploy pipeline and can be shut down whenever convenient (the auto-memory rule "Cloud Run NAT egress IP `34.14.59.128` must stay whitelisted at all 4 DirectAdmin layers" still applies — that's keyed to Cloud Run, not the VPS, so DA reachability is unaffected). **Verified end-to-end**: revision `dms-00175-d7d` was the first deploy from the local PC (cold build), revision `dms-00176-klr` was the second (warm build, with the deploy-tag also pushing back to GitHub origin automatically via the stored PAT); both serve traffic correctly and the live health endpoint at `app.anutech.in/api/v1/health` returns HTTP 200. **Ongoing cost**: zero — the local PC was already paid for, the Windows electricity for a 1-minute build is negligible. **One-time migration cost**: ~30 minutes of setup (mostly waiting on Docker Desktop install + one Windows restart to enable the WSL2 virtualization feature).

- [x] **Hosting half of a domain + hosting order now provisions correctly on DirectAdmin — was failing because the cart's synthetic identifier was reaching DA instead of the real domain** — the senior reviewer completed a successful checkout (Pay button worked post-Mongoose-fix, payment cleared on Razorpay), but on the order detail page they saw the **domain half showing "Pending" / 80% progress (normal — domain registration is still working through ResellerClub)** and the **hosting half showing "Failed — Registration failed" with the toast "Hosting provisioning failed. Our team has been notified — please contact support if this persists."** Domain was fine; hosting needed investigation. **Why hosting failed**: the cart store gives every hosting line item a **synthetic identifier** for its `domainName` field — a string like `"hosting-standard-1781673238819"` that looks like a domain but isn't one. The cart UI uses this so the basket can render the domain and the hosting plan as two distinct rows even when they're both for the same domain. **The actual customer-facing domain** that the hosting account needs to be provisioned against (`tryraju.com` in this case) is carried alongside in a separate field called `linkedDomain`. The hosting provisioner correctly reads `item.linkedDomain || item.domainName` — so as long as `linkedDomain` is set, DirectAdmin gets `tryraju.com` and creates the hosting account; if `linkedDomain` is missing, it falls back to the synthetic ID and DirectAdmin (correctly) refuses to create a user for a string that's clearly not a real domain. **What broke the round-trip**: (a) the Order database table didn't have a column for `linkedDomain`, so when we save the "pending order" row at create-order time, the field was silently dropped; (b) the payment-verification endpoint deliberately rebuilds the cart from the database row rather than trusting whatever the client sends (security gate — without this, a malicious customer could pay for `cheap.in` and swap it for `expensive.com` after the fact);
