**reopens a resolved ticket back to "open" when the customer replies with the resolved-at cleared** (so the ticket re-enters the support queue); emails the admin with the customer's HTML-escaped reply body and the user's HTML-escaped name+email; one known interaction pinned: zod doesn't reject extra body fields (e.g. a customer sending `priority: "high"` alongside `status: "closed"`), so the extra field is silently ignored rather than 400'd (the priority itself is never written to the ticket since the route only consumes status — future hardening to add `.strict()` would flip this) (deploy `dms-00143-pgj` live)
- [x] **Admin payments dashboard + admin hosting actions (suspend / unsuspend / delete)** — added automated safety checks: the admin payments dashboard requires admin login (non-admins blocked before any Razorpay call); supports optional ?from / ?to date-range filtering; **fetches 5× the requested page size (minimum 25) so domain-only payments can be reliably filtered out of mixed Razorpay traffic** (without the over-fetch, a small ?limit=5 page might fetch 5 mixed payments and only show 1-2 domains); each Razorpay payment is enriched by joining with the local order data AND with the user record by email; the customer-name resolution uses a three-step fallback chain (populated order user → Razorpay payment-details name → user-by-email lookup → "Unknown" if all fail); amounts, fees, and tax are converted from paise to rupees correctly; refund status is surfaced as both the boolean flag and the refund amount; the response carries a pagination summary with hasMore math; the hosting-actions endpoint is admin-only (non-admins blocked with 403 before any DirectAdmin call); accepts a strict discriminated-union body where suspend/unsuspend require a username (1-100 chars) and delete requires EITHER a username OR a hosting ID — pinned at the schema layer with a `.refine()` check so a delete with neither is rejected at 400 before any service call; each action maps the DirectAdmin probe's four typed outcomes correctly — server-unreachable → 503 with a "try again" message, hard-failure → 500 "see server logs", other (suspended/unsuspended/user_not_found) → 200 with the outcome name; **the delete action is multi-step and resilient — first matches local hosting rows (snapshotting them so the loop survives the row deletion), then per-row cancels the Razorpay subscription if one exists (per-row failures are SWALLOWED so one bad subscription doesn't strand the rest of the cleanup), then permanently deletes the local Hosting rows, then clears any PendingHosting rows for the same username, then tries to delete the DirectAdmin user (a NOT_FOUND or anything-else outcome still leaves the local cleanup committed — the response carries a warning field but the local rows have already been cleared so the state stays consistent), and finally clears the DirectAdmin username from any User records**; outer catch always returns a generic 500 ACTION_FAILED with the upstream error fragment NOT leaked into the response (deploy `dms-00142-vvr` live)
- [x] **Zoho-invoice creation worker + hosting-expiry suspension worker** — added automated safety checks for two background workers fired by Cloud Tasks: the Zoho-invoice worker creates the customer's invoice in Zoho Books AFTER payment has already confirmed (so a Zoho outage never blocks customer service activation), requires the cron-secret on every call (machine-only), idempotently skips orders that already have an invoice ID (returns 200 success — Cloud Tasks stops retrying); **uses an atomic claim so two parallel retries can't double-create the same invoice — if the claim fails, returns 200 with "Already claimed" so Cloud Tasks stops retrying**; treats "order not found" as a permanent skip (200, no retry — the order genuinely doesn't exist); releases the claim AND returns 404 (Cloud Tasks retries) if the user lookup fails; releases the claim AND returns 500 (retry) if Zoho returns a null invoice; correctly maps duration to period unit (12 months → 1 year; otherwise N months); only looks up the hosting plan record when serviceType is hosting AND a plan ID was supplied; the Zoho invoice creation is called with payment-mode "Razorpay" and shouldApplyPayment=true so the invoice lands already-paid; on success, records the Zoho invoice ID + number back on the local order; the hosting-expiry worker handles the once-daily "your hosting expired" suspension flow, requires the cron-secret, refuses to run if the hosting isn't found OR isn't active OR hasn't actually expired (200 success in all three skip-cases — idempotent and safe to fire multiple times); suspends the customer's DirectAdmin account with an explicit "Expired Subscription" reason but skips the DA call if no DA username is linked; **three-step renewal-price fallback chain — looks up the customer's original order first (matching by exact domain name, then by hosting-plan ID as a fallback), then asks the plan record, then defaults to the starter plan's price as the last resort**; period+period-unit are pulled from the original order item when available; creates a pending renewal Order with locked fields (currency INR, status pending, itemType hosting, hostingPlan metadata, bookingStatus step="suspended" / progress 10); sends the customer a renewal notification email **WITHOUT an invoice number — the invoice is created only after the customer actually pays, so Zoho Books doesn't accumulate due invoices for unpaid renewals**; flips the hosting status to suspended only after all the side-effects succeed; the user-not-found edge still suspends the hosting locally but skips the email (so a half-deleted customer doesn't bounce the worker into a retry loop); inner-catch returns 500 PROCESSING_FAILED so Cloud Tasks retries on transient DA/Mongo errors (deploy `dms-00141-mj2` live)
- [x] **Customer hosting renewal order + admin user CRUD (read/update/delete)** — added automated safety checks: the customer hosting-renewal endpoint requires login and is **rate-limited per-user (5 attempts per minute keyed on user ID, not IP)** so a multi-customer office network isn't throttled by one bad actor; scopes the hosting lookup to the calling customer's own accounts (anti-IDOR — hostile body fields cannot redirect the lookup); refuses if the hosting isn't found (404), refuses if the hosting is terminated (clear "contact support" message — 400 with a distinct code from the generic "not renewable"); refuses if the status isn't renewable (only active / expired / suspended are eligible — pending/cancelled rejected); for active hostings refuses if more than 15 days from expiry with the actual days-remaining surfaced to the customer (boundary inclusive — exactly 14 days = eligible), expired and suspended skip this guard so the customer can pay even after expiry; refuses if the plan record can't be found; **the renewal period is locked to 12 months exactly with the price computed server-side as plan price × 12 — a hostile body amount or period override is completely ignored** (pinned with a probe); the Razorpay order metadata carries exactly 3 fields (type, domain_name, user_id); the admin user-CRUD endpoint requires admin login on all three methods — the read returns a single user by ID with a 404 on missing; the update enforces zod-strict roles (only "user" or "admin"), **refuses an admin attempting to deactivate their own account (anti-lockout) while still allowing the admin to deactivate OTHER users** and returns a curated 6-field user shape with password hash + 2FA secret asserted absent; **the delete requires step-up password re-authentication before any destructive action — the reauth check fires BEFORE the user lookup, so a stolen session cookie alone can't even probe whether a given ID exists**; refuses admins deleting their own account (anti-lockout); **refuses deleting the last admin (the system always has at least one admin in place — pinned with an explicit countAdmins ≤ 1 probe)**; non-admin user deletes skip the count check entirely; uses soft-delete + session invalidation instead of a hard wipe (deploy `dms-00140-9qd` live)
- [x] **Public AI chat widget + customer registration flow** — added automated safety checks: the public AI chat widget (anonymous-friendly, no login required) is **rate-limited to 10 requests per IP per minute BEFORE any body parse** so a single abuser can't drain the Anthropic API budget (a hostile payload can't even reach the JSON parser when throttled); accepts only message arrays with strictly-typed role (user/assistant only — system or tool roles rejected), bounded content (1-8000 chars per message), and non-empty arrays; truncates conversation history to the last 20 turns to bound token cost (pinned with a 25-message probe asserting only the last 20 reach the SDK); **pins the Anthropic model to the dated Haiku 4.5 release `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`** so a future alias re-point doesn't change the persona or token-spend profile; locks max_tokens to 1024; uses an ephemeral cache_control on the system prompt to reduce per-request token cost; returns a Server-Sent Events stream with text-delta chunks (non-text-delta events like message_start, ping, message_stop, tool input_json_delta are filtered out); includes a final `data: [DONE]` sentinel; mid-stream errors emit a single `data: {error: 'Stream error'}` then close; gracefully maps the Anthropic SDK's distinct error types (authentication-failure → 401, upstream rate-limit → 429, anything else → 500); the customer registration endpoint runs through six defense layers — (1) cross-site forgery check, (2) rate-limit 5/IP/min BEFORE body parse, (3) strict zod schema validation, (4) optional Google reCAPTCHA verification with the caller's IP from x-forwarded-for first / x-real-ip fallback / "unknown" if neither, (5) business-logic check rejecting existing users with a 400 USER_EXISTS instead of overwriting, (6) server-side `crypto.randomBytes(32)` 64-char hex activation token with 24-hour validity; the profileCompleted flag is set true ONLY when phone + address.line1 + address.city are all present (any missing → false — pinned per-field); the response is curated to exactly 8 user fields (id, email, firstName, lastName, role, isActivated:false, profileCompleted, provider) — password hashes, the activation token itself, and any other sensitive fields are explicitly absent; the activation email send is fire-and-forget with its own .catch (SMTP failure doesn't block the response); ResellerClub + Zoho Books background sync is also fire-and-forget after the response so a customer doesn't have to wait on external providers — any sync failure is swallowed and the customer is registered regardless (deploy `dms-00139-6ll` live)
- [x] **Customer hosting-plan upgrade order + admin full-database backup download** — added automated safety checks: the customer hosting-upgrade endpoint requires login and is **rate-limited per-user** (5 attempts per minute keyed on the customer's user ID rather than their IP — so a multi-customer office network isn't throttled by one bad actor); refuses the upgrade if the hosting isn't found (404 NOT_FOUND), isn't currently active (400 NOT_ELIGIBLE), or has already expired (400 HOSTING_EXPIRED — customer must renew first); refuses downgrades and sidegrades (target plan must be priced strictly higher than current — 400 INVALID_UPGRADE); **the prorated charge is computed strictly server-side as "(target price − current price) × remaining days ÷ 30" rounded with a ₹100 minimum floor — the client-supplied amount field is completely ignored** (pinned with a hostile-body probe sending amount=9 alongside the legitimate plan IDs; the server still computed and used the correct prorated amount); the Razorpay order metadata carries hosting_id, domain_name, from_plan, to_plan, user_id (5 fields exactly) for downstream webhook verification; the internal pending Order record mirrors the Razorpay order with a "payment_verified" booking-status step initially showing 10% progress, and order IDs use a `upg_${timestamp}_${random}` pattern; the admin database backup endpoint requires admin login AND **step-up password re-authentication** (a stolen session cookie alone can't download the entire production database — pinned with a wrong-password probe returning 403 with NO audit log entry and NO stream); the response is a streaming gzipped JSON export of every Mongo collection EXCEPT system collections (`system.indexes`, `system.views` etc. are explicitly skipped); **sensitive fields are stripped at the projection layer — the password hash, TOTP secret, pending TOTP secret, TOTP backup codes, password-reset token + expiry, and pending-email token (7 fields total) are projected OUT of the users collection, and razorpaySignature is projected OUT of orders** — pinned with explicit field-by-field projection assertions; non-sensitive collections receive no projection (no accidental over-filtering); an immutable audit log entry is written BEFORE the stream begins (so even an aborted download leaves a forensic trail with the requestId UUID, admin email, source IP — IP resolved via x-forwarded-for first / x-real-ip fallback / "unknown" if neither header is present); response carries `Content-Type: application/gzip` and a dated `Content-Disposition` filename (deploy `dms-00138-xm8` live)
- [x] **Admin "create the default hosting packages" + admin registered-domains list** — added automated safety checks: the default-packages bootstrap endpoint creates the starter / standard / unlimited hosting plans in both DirectAdmin AND the local database, is admin-only (non-admins blocked with 403 before any DA call or DB write), **idempotent — safe to re-run** (plans already in the database are skipped with a "skipped: Already exists" status rather than failing); normalises "unlimited" quotas (≤0) to the literal "unlimited" string for the DirectAdmin API but to 0 for the local database schema (because the schema rejects negative numbers); recovers gracefully when DirectAdmin reports "already exists" mid-bootstrap (proceeds to DB sync instead of erroring — closes a DA/DB drift vector when a previous partial run left packages server-side); other DirectAdmin errors are caught and counted per-plan, not propagated; per-plan failures are fully isolated so one bad plan doesn't kill the rest of the run; the overall response distinguishes three distinct outcomes — total failure (500 with full error details for ops), partial success (200 with "with some errors" message + per-plan results array), and clean run (200 with "complete" message); skipped-only runs (everything already exists) also count as success; the registered-domains admin list pages over all orders and surfaces only fully-registered domain items (filters out pending/processing/failed/cancelled status, filters out hosting line items — and items without an explicit itemType are treated as 'domain' for legacy back-compat, AND **filters out anything in the pending-domain queue — a registered-status row that's still in pending manual review must not show in the active DNS-management list, even if the order says "registered"** — pinned with case-insensitive trimmed match); per-domain dedup applies a three-step priority chain (DNS-activated wins over not-activated, then ResellerClub-order-ID-present wins over absent, then newer createdAt wins) so the most-authoritative copy of a duplicated domain is shown to admin; customer name+email are joined from the populated user reference with an "Unknown" fallback when the user is missing; ResellerClub order ID has a three-step fallback chain (per-domain field → domain.orderId → legacy top-level order field); pagination is capped at 100 entries per page (anti-DoS); page numbers below 1 are clamped to 1, limit values are clamped into [1, 100] (deploy `dms-00137-qzw` live)
- [x] **Admin outbound-IP check (with audit trail) + nightly DirectAdmin status sync** — added automated safety checks: the admin's IP-check endpoint requires admin login (non-admins blocked before any external probe fires — preventing IP-check log spam), queries the same four external IP-lookup services as the public diagnostic, but **also persists every check to the database with the admin's user ID** (audit trail for ops debugging — who checked the IP, when, and what they saw — and the record is saved even when all four services fail so the audit log captures the failure too); the response only carries the detailed services payload when at least one service succeeded (failure-only payload is suppressed to keep the dashboard clean); per-service failures are isolated; the database record coerces a null primary-IP to empty string at the boundary so the schema constraint is satisfied; serverInfo headers (host, x-forwarded-for, x-real-ip) absent in the request → undefined in the DB record (NOT null — Mongoose ergonomics); the nightly DirectAdmin status sync worker requires cron-secret-only auth (NO admin-session fallback — machine-only by design, so an admin can't accidentally trigger a mass-sync from the browser), takes a user ID via POST body (zod-required), syncs every one of that user's hostings with no truncation cap, only checks active or suspended accounts (terminated/pending hostings are skipped to keep the sync focused), hostings without a DirectAdmin username are also skipped (legacy/manual rows), maps the DirectAdmin probe's four outcomes distinctly — **user-not-found → mark hosting terminated + disable auto-renew; server-unreachable → log and skip this run; hard-failure → log and skip; found → proceed** (critical: server-unreachable does NOT terminate — closes a mass-termination vector during a DA outage); when the DirectAdmin account exists and is suspended, the local status flips to suspended (but only saves on actual change — idempotent); when active and the domain is found in the DirectAdmin domain list, status flips to active (case-insensitive match); **anti-false-positive guards: if the DirectAdmin account is active but the specific domain isn't on it AND the user has at least one OTHER domain on DA → terminate the hosting (the userDomains-non-empty precondition guards against terminating when the domain-list probe itself failed or returned empty — a flaky probe doesn't mass-terminate); if the domain-list probe THROWS, the error is caught and the route proceeds as if the list were empty, again NOT terminating**; per-hosting failures are isolated via Promise.allSettled so one row's blow-up doesn't kill the rest of the sync (deploy `dms-00136-8zh` live)
- [x] **Admin settings dashboard + TLD-pricing cache controls** — added automated safety checks: the admin settings endpoint requires admin login for both read and write (non-admins blocked before any database read); on write, security-critical setting keys (2FA flags, payment-provider live + test keys, DirectAdmin admin credentials, ResellerClub + Zoho secrets, SMTP password, Anthropic API key, cron secret, auth/NextAuth secrets, field-encryption key — all 18 keys hard-coded into an allowlist) require step-up password re-authentication BEFORE the setting is changed; **THE BIG ONE: the step-up trigger uses the SERVER-STORED category of the existing setting PLUS the hardcoded allowlist — never the body-supplied `category` field** (so an admin can't sneak a security-key update through by labelling the request as `category: "branding"` to skip the password challenge — pinned with an explicit hostile-probe test where the body says one thing but the route enforces the right thing); the step-up check fires even on a first-time write of a security key (when the setting doesn't yet exist server-side, the allowlist alone is enough to trigger reauth); the response after a successful write echoes the freshly-re-read setting back to the UI; missing description defaults to empty string and missing category defaults to "general"; the updatedBy audit field is always set to the admin's email (never their ID, never anonymous); the TLD-pricing cache controls are admin-only across all three actions — read current cache status (returns enabled/keys/isRedis/TTL), purge the cache (manual reset with admin-email audit log), and update cache settings (enable/disable + TTL in minutes, capped at 30 days / 43200 minutes); **disabling the cache automatically purges it so a stale cache can't keep serving after disable** (pinned per-branch); updating TTL only fires when the value is a positive integer (zero, negative, floats, and strings are all zod-rejected); the PUT zod schema uses `.refine()` to require at least one of `enabled` or `ttlMinutes` per call so no-op empty bodies are explicitly rejected (deploy `dms-00135-8mc` live)
- [x] **Customer "my domains" list + admin invoice-conflict diagnostics** — added automated safety checks: the "my domains" list accepts either JWT login or NextAuth session (JWT checked first; session fallback only when JWT absent), **refuses deactivated accounts even with a valid token**, all three source queries (recent orders, pending-domain queue, active Domains collection) are scoped to the session user ID (anti-IDOR — a hostile body/query field cannot redirect the lookup), aggregates the three sources with case-insensitive name deduplication so the same domain never appears twice (the active Domains row wins over the pending-domain row over the order row, so the most-authoritative status is shown), applies a 14-day window on the orders source to keep the list focused, skips deleted orders and hosting line items (so the list is domain-only — and the hosting filter uses both the itemType field AND the isHostingItem helper as a defence-in-depth pair), failed and cancelled domain registrations are also skipped, empty/missing domain names are filtered out, results sort by registration date newest-first (missing dates go last), supports optional pagination via limit+page query params (defaults to "show everything" for back-compat; page is clamped to a minimum of 1 to handle URL-tampering or NaN page values), and supports an optional status filter applied AFTER aggregation; the admin invoice-conflict diagnostic dashboard is admin-only (non-admins blocked before any database read), surfaces two distinct classes of bad state (duplicate Zoho invoice numbers AND paid orders with a Zoho invoice ID stuck in pending/failed state for too long), joins the user email/name onto each affected order with a shared user-cache reused across both diagnostic legs (so the same user is only fetched once even if they appear in both lists), missing-user-in-cache surfaces as `undefined` (not a runtime crash), orphan order IDs that exist in the conflict aggregation but were soft-deleted are filtered out, the stuck-order list is capped at 100 entries (anti-DoS for an admin diagnostic), and the response carries a summary block with conflict-group count, conflicted-order count, and stuck-order count for the dashboard widget (deploy `dms-00134-4xx` live)
- [x] **Admin "show me a customer's hosting details" + customer domain renew flow** — added automated safety checks: only admins can fetch hosting details (non-admins blocked before any DirectAdmin probe fires), the customer's DA username is required as a query parameter (no listing-all defaulting — a missing username returns 400 before any work happens), three DirectAdmin probes (account config, usage, server info) run in parallel for speed, the live nameservers are fetched from the registrar's DNS records with trailing dots stripped (falls back to the hardcoded ResellerClub defaults if the lookup throws or returns no NS records — so a flaky probe doesn't blank the panel), the "unlimited" string from DirectAdmin is normalised to "Unlimited" for the UI (case-insensitive), the PHP-version selection follows a three-step preference chain (per-account override → DA selector → server default → "Default" fallback), the on/off feature flags are strict-case ("ON" only, "on" doesn't count), and the response is shaped down to a curated 11-field stats object (no raw DA fields leak through — pinned with a sentinel field injection test); the customer domain-renew flow has two methods — GET fetches renewal pricing + current expiry (year count is a positive integer 1-10, defaults to 1 if omitted, coerces string years from URL params), POST actually renews after payment confirms; the three registrar outcomes are mapped distinctly (renewed → 200 with new expiry date, balance-pending → 202 "queued" message and **NO order written**, hard-failure → 500 generic "team notified" copy and **NO order written** — pinned per-branch so a failed renewal never leaves a phantom completed order in the customer's history); the order's new-expiry-date is computed as years × 365 days from now and stamped on both the order record and the customer's domain list; missing customer first/last names produce an empty-string userName (not the "undefined undefined" template-literal quirk seen in the test-plan endpoint — this route uses `firstName || ''` explicit fallback) (deploy `dms-00133-lln` live)

- [x] **Customer shopping cart server-sync + admin "₹1 test plan" toggle** — added automated safety checks: the cart endpoint requires login for read, write, and clear (all three methods checked independently); the cart envelope is capped at 50 items via zod (anti-DoS);

**restricted TLDs (.gov.in etc.) are silently dropped from the cart on every read AND write so a customer can never check out something we can't actually fulfil**, with the dropped names surfaced back to the UI in plain English ("Removed 1 restricted domain we cannot register: bad.gov.in" — pluralised for 2+ drops); domain-item registration periods are automatically clamped to the per-TLD min/max (e.g. .ai requires 2-year minimum, so a stale 1-year period silently bumps to 2 on the next read); the read path persists any corrections back to the database (so the next page-load sees the cleaned version) but only when corrections actually happened (no needless writes); hosting items pass through the cart validator untouched (no domain-side policy applied), and legacy items without a domainName also pass through; zod uses `.passthrough()` so price/currency/custom fields the UI tacks on are preserved; the write path is keyed on the session user ID (a hostile body field can't override it); the ₹1-test-plan toggle is admin-only with the gate enforced per-method, the GET returns a strict-boolean `enabled` flag (defaults to false even if the setting is missing OR truthy-but-not-true — closed-by-default for a public payment surface); enabling with an explicit Razorpay plan ID uses it verbatim, enabling without one falls back to the DB record first then auto-creates via Razorpay with locked args ("₹1 Test Hosting Plan", 1 INR, monthly);

**a Razorpay API failure during auto-create surfaces with the upstream error and writes NOTHING locally — no partial state where the plan exists upstream but our DB and flag are out of sync**; the local plan record is written BEFORE the feature flag flips on (pinned ordering); disabling cleanly flips the feature flag false and marks the plan inactive (no Razorpay-side call); one known interaction pinned: an admin record with `undefined` firstName/lastName logs as "undefined undefined" (template-literal coercion quirk — fallback only fires for empty-string names, future hardening tracked) (deploy `dms-00132-t7m` live)

- [x] **Customer "turn on DNS management" for a domain I own + daily domain-watch worker** — added automated safety checks: the customer-side activate-DNS endpoint requires login, scopes the domain lookup to the calling customer's own orders so one customer can never activate DNS on another customer's domain (anti-IDOR — the user ID is always taken from the session, never from the request body); refuses if the domain isn't fully registered yet (clear message about waiting), refuses to re-activate one that's already active unless an explicit "force" override is sent; **the customer-side version is intentionally the OPPOSITE of the admin version when the registrar call fails — the customer version returns 502 and does NOT mark DNS as locally-activated, because customer-side correctness matters more than admin convenience** (the admin twin can retry manually); on success the domain is marked locally activated, a timestamp is recorded, and a "dns_activated" audit row is appended to the booking-status log; falls back to a legacy top-level order field when the per-domain registrar order id is missing; the daily domain-watch worker requires the cron-secret (NO admin-session fallback — it's machine-only by design, so an admin can't accidentally trigger 100 registrar calls from the browser), processes up to 100 watches per run in chunks of 5 (registrar rate-limit friendly), per-watch failures are isolated so one network hiccup doesn't kill the rest of the batch, when a watched domain becomes available the customer is emailed and the watch is removed so they only get one notification (no daily spam), and the email-send failing doesn't roll back the removal — the customer will see the watch is gone and can re-create it if needed (deploy `dms-00131-4fm` live)
- [x] **Admin "reset a customer's password" + public bulk domain search** — added automated safety checks: only admins can reset another customer's password, and even logged-in admins must re-enter their own current password (step-up authentication) before the action proceeds — **a stolen admin cookie alone can't change passwords (the step-up gate fires BEFORE any target lookup or password mutation)**; admins are explicitly blocked from using this endpoint to reset another admin's password (privilege protection — no using customer-service tooling to wipe a peer admin's access — no database mutation on the block) and from resetting their own password through it (a separate self-service flow exists for that); the new password is hashed with bcrypt at 12 rounds, any pending password-reset token is cleared so a previously-issued reset link can't be replayed against the new password, and a notification email is sent unless the admin explicitly opts out — mail-server hiccups don't roll back the password change (the password is already persisted by the time the email attempt runs); the public bulk domain-search endpoint is rate-limited (5 requests per IP) BEFORE any body parsing (so a hostile payload can't even reach the JSON parser when throttled), accepts up to 60 domains in a single payload but caps actual registrar lookups at 20 after trim/lower-case/dedupe normalisation, restricted TLDs (.gov.in etc.) are flagged as unavailable without ever hitting the registrar, per-domain failures are isolated (one network hiccup doesn't blank the rest of the results — each failed domain comes back with "Availability check failed"), the lookup prefers an exact lowercase match in the registrar response before falling back to the first result (anti-typo-squatting in the response selection), and entries that lack a TLD entirely are surfaced with a friendly "must include a TLD" message rather than a generic error (deploy `dms-00130-zln` live)
- [x] **Admin order archive / un-archive + customer trial-eligibility check** — added automated safety checks: only admins can delete or un-archive orders (non-admins blocked before any database read); the delete action defaults to a reversible soft-archive (audit-trail preserved) but the `permanent=true` query flag escalates to a hard delete with a clearly distinct "permanently deleted" response message; **the `permanent` flag is strictly case-sensitive — only the literal `permanent=true` triggers permanent delete, while `permanent=TRUE`, `permanent=1`, `permanent=yes`, and a bare `permanent` all stay safe-by-default soft-archive** (pinned per-variant so a future query-param-parsing refactor can't silently weaken the guard); the order-not-found case returns 404 before any delete fires; the customer trial-eligibility endpoint requires login, refuses if the global trials kill-switch is off, refuses if the customer has already used their lifetime free trial (lookup is keyed strictly on the resolved user ID — a hostile body field can't override it), runs the anti-abuse defenses (disposable-email check, reCAPTCHA, IP+device throttles, optional OTP gate behind a per-environment flag) with abuse signals (deviceFingerprint, recaptchaToken, otpToken) passed through verbatim to the evaluator, refuses if the chosen hosting plan lacks a yearly Razorpay subscription mapping, and finally responds with eligible plus the standard 15-day trial length and the otpRequired flag; both GET (legacy URL-param form) and POST (canonical body form, so abuse signals never appear in URLs or referrer headers) share the same eligibility pipeline — symmetry pinned with a direct GET/POST comparison (deploy `dms-00129-q6d` live)
- [x] **Daily expiry/renewal scheduler + customer "do I have any services?" check** — added automated safety checks: the daily scheduler accepts either cron-secret or admin session, fetches up to 500 due hostings AND domains in parallel, processes each in chunks of 20 with all-settled isolation (one row blowing up doesn't kill the rest of the batch), uses an atomic database lock so the same row can't be picked up twice by overlapping runs, **releases that lock if the downstream Cloud Tasks dispatch fails — pinned per-branch so a refactor can't introduce abandoned locks that hold rows hostage for 10 minutes**, supports a "simulated time" header that gets forwarded into the task payload for QA fast-forward, fires the domain-watch worker fire-and-forget with a 60-second hard timeout (so a hung worker can't camp on the Cloud Run slot indefinitely), and finally checks the ResellerClub wallet balance against a configurable threshold (default ₹1000) — emails admin a low-balance alert if below, surfaces an unparseable balance as "checked:false reason='unparseable balance'", and the balance check failing is swallowed so the main scheduler still reports success; the "do I have any services?" check used by the customer dashboard accepts either a JWT login or a NextAuth session, **deactivated accounts are refused even when carrying a still-valid token — verified independently of the token's validity**, returns three fields exactly (hasDomains, hasHosting, hostedDomains) where domains in cancelled/failed/terminated state are excluded from the active count, the hosting flag is set if the user has a DirectAdmin username OR an active hosting line item (cancelled hosting alone doesn't count), and the hostedDomains list is deduped via Set with hosting lines that lack a domainName excluded (no empty strings in the list) (deploy `dms-00128-xht` live)
- [x] **Customer domain-transfer initiation + nightly stuck-provisioning sweeper** — added automated safety checks: the customer transfer endpoint is rate-limited (100 attempts per IP) BEFORE the login check (so an unauthenticated attacker can't probe the auth gate), requires login, validates the EPP auth code as a bounded 1-128 character string, ensures the customer has a ResellerClub profile before initiating (refuses on a missing customer ID or contact ID with a 500 instead of crashing later), then maps the registrar's four outcomes to distinct HTTP responses: initiated → 200 with success message, balance-pending → 202 with a customer-friendly "queued" message, registry-rejected → 400 with a customer-actionable hint about EPP code / unlock status / 60-day rule, and hard-failure → 500 with a generic "team has been notified" copy that never leaks registrar internals; **only the initiated path writes the pending Domain row + appends to the user's domain list — the three failure branches NEVER touch the database, pinned per-branch**; the nightly stuck-provisioning sweeper accepts either cron-secret or admin session, scans pending domains AND pending hostings stuck more than 24 hours (excluding admin-resolved archived rows), escalates to CRITICAL when age exceeds 7 days OR verification attempts exceed 5 (boundary pinned: exactly 5 attempts is still WARN, the rule is strict > 5), sorts CRITICAL ahead of WARN ahead of age, emails admin a single digest with all stuck rows ordered worst-first, the subject line distinguishes between "N CRITICAL + M stuck" vs plain "M stuck" so a glance at the inbox tells admin whether to act now, and a digest-send failure is swallowed (the cron still returns 200 — so the external scheduler keeps its daily cadence even when the mail server hiccups) (deploy `dms-00127-bmb` live)
- [x] **Admin Razorpay mode switch + system-health dashboard** — added automated safety checks: the admin's Razorpay-mode endpoint requires admin, accepts two strictly-typed actions (save-keys vs switch-mode) so a switch-mode payload can't smuggle stray key fields into the wrong branch; saving keys only persists the fields actually supplied (admin can update just the webhook secret without re-typing keys); switching mode refuses with a 400 if the target-mode keys aren't already saved (no accidentally blanking out the live keys);

- **the GET response is locked to which-keys-are-set booleans + public key IDs only — secret keys NEVER reach the browser, pinned with a negative-leak guard**; the server-restart kick after switching mode parses the PID file as a strict positive integer (a PID file containing `99 ; rm -rf /` produces `kill -TERM 99` — never the injection — and PID=0/negative/missing all gracefully degrade to a "re-deploy to apply" response); the system-health dashboard runs seven independent probes (database, queue backlog, failed jobs, ResellerClub, DirectAdmin, Razorpay, Zoho Books, server metrics) each individually try/catch isolated so one upstream outage doesn't blank the whole page; the JWT auth path is checked first (App Router-reliable) and falls through to the session check on JWT failure; the Zoho health side has a five-state diagnostic (misconfigured / DB-flagged-expired / live-expired / trial-expiring / trial / active); ResellerClub's NoBilling credit accounts hide the wallet balance instead of showing a misleading zero; response is `Cache-Control: no-store` so admin always sees a fresh state (deploy `dms-00126-wb5` live)

- [x] **Customer "complete your profile" + domain booking-status read/write** — added automated safety checks: the "complete your profile" save endpoint requires login, only writes a field when something was actually supplied (so a customer who just updates their phone doesn't accidentally wipe their saved address), but the GST number is the one field that intentionally CAN be cleared with an empty string; defaults a brand-new customer's country to India when their record didn't have an address yet; recomputes the profile-completion flag against all eight required fields on every save (one known interaction pinned: a brand-new customer who omits country still gets flagged complete because the "IN" default fills in first); the response is curated down to twelve named fields, with the password hash, password-reset token, 2FA secret, and internal DirectAdmin username explicitly pinned ABSENT; the public domain booking-status endpoint (used by the registration progress widget that polls before the customer is even logged in) handles both reading and writing — orderId beats domain-name when both are sent, only the nine listed step names are accepted (anything else 400s before any database write), message length is capped at 2000 characters, progress is a 0-100 integer, and three of those step names automatically flip the underlying domain's status to registered / failed / processing; non-mapping steps leave the status alone (deploy `dms-00125-ggn` live)

- [x] **Public "forgot password" flow + outbound-IP diagnostic** — added automated safety checks: the public "forgot my password" endpoint runs through seven defense layers (cross-site forgery check first, rate limit BEFORE any input parsing — so hostile payloads can't slip through, schema validation, Google reCAPTCHA with the caller's IP attached from `x-forwarded-for` first / `x-real-ip` fallback / "unknown" if neither, 

**anti-email-enumeration response that's byte-identical whether the email is registered, unregistered, or an admin account** — pinned with triple-comparison so a future logging tweak can't reintroduce the side-channel, admins are explicitly blocked from resetting via this public path with zero database side-effects on probe, and a single-use 64-character hex reset token good for one hour that's saved BEFORE the email is sent so admin can re-issue if the mail server hiccups); the outbound-IP diagnostic queries four external IP-lookup services with each failure isolated (one slow or 403-ing service doesn't blank the page); server-info echo is locked to exactly four header fields (user-agent, host, x-forwarded-for, x-real-ip) so a refactor can't widen it to leak cookies or auth headers; one known quirk pinned: when services disagree, the "voting" picks the last-distinct-IP seen rather than actual mode (works fine in normal operation where all 4 agree, future hardening tracked) (deploy `dms-00124-bjk` live)

- [x] **Admin "turn on DNS management" for a domain + internal test-automation trigger** — added automated safety checks: the admin's "activate DNS management" endpoint refuses non-admins, won't act on a domain that's still being registered (clear "wait for registration to complete" message instead of a generic 500), refuses to re-activate one that's already active unless an explicit "force" override is sent, and if the registrar's DNS-activation call hiccups the row is still flagged locally as activated (so admin can retry the registrar side later without losing the click); the registrar's raw error message never reaches the admin's screen; falls back to a legacy top-level order field when the per-domain registrar order id is missing; the internal QA-only "trigger the daily scheduler at this simulated time" endpoint requires an admin in production but is open in non-prod for QA, refuses to run unless the time-simulation feature flag is on, blocks payloads with garbage service-types before any database lookup, lets QA fast-forward a specific hosting or domain to "due now" by id, then fires the scheduler with the simulated-time header (deploy `dms-00123-kp2` live)

- [x] **Admin manual hosting-assign + nightly stuck-order watchdog** — added automated safety checks: only admins can reach the manual hosting-assign endpoint (gate runs first, before any input parsing — so a non-admin probing with garbage payloads still gets a clean 403, not a 400); for customers who don't already have a DirectAdmin account a safe username is generated by stripping the domain to alphanumerics, capping at 8 characters of domain + 4 characters of random suffix, lower-cased and capped at the 14-character DirectAdmin limit; the post-create step that points the domain's nameservers at the registrar's DNS is best-effort — if just that step fails the hosting account still saves and the customer record still updates; an "already exists" collision on the hosting server returns a clean 409 instead of a generic error; the nightly stuck-order watchdog accepts either the cron-secret header or an admin session, auto-retries any hosting accounts that got deferred at checkout (in chunks of 5 at a time so the upstream hosting server isn't overwhelmed), one row blowing up doesn't kill the rest of the batch (the cron always reports back), then emails admin if any paid orders are still stuck 30+ minutes after payment — and the alert-email failing doesn't roll back any of the retries that DID succeed (deploy `dms-00122-x8p` live)
- [x] **Customer one-click hosting-panel login + upgrade-pricing info** — added automated safety checks: the SSO endpoint that one-click-redirects the customer to their DirectAdmin panel verifies ownership by case-insensitive email match (so a customer can't generate a one-time login URL to another customer's panel), refuses access if the account is suspended, and gracefully recovers from a flaky suspension check; browser clients land on a themed error page while API clients get clean JSON; the upgrade-info endpoint hides downgrades entirely (only plans more expensive than the current one appear), computes prorated charges as "(target − current) × remaining-days ÷ 30" with a ₹100 minimum floor (so a tiny prorate doesn't get wiped by payment-gateway fees), and refuses to compute when the hosting has already expired (must renew first) (deploy `dms-00121-fh2` live)
- [x] **Deep health-check probe + test-automation status read** — added automated safety checks: the deep health-check pings the database and cache layer in parallel with a 3-second timeout per dependency, returns "degraded" with HTTP 503 when any dep fails (so Cloud Run can pull traffic from a broken pod), and surfaces per-dep latency so a slow dependency stands out from a dead one; when the cache is unconfigured (e.g. early-bootstrap before REDIS_HOST is set), the check reports "not configured" instead of crashing; the internal test-automation status endpoint requires admin only in production (QA can use it freely in non-prod), supports a "simulated now" parameter so date-based logic is replayable in tests, and projects the right expiry field (hosting vs. domain) per service type (deploy `dms-00120-4j5` live)
- [x] **Admin DirectAdmin diagnostics + bulk cleanup** — added automated safety checks for two admin support tools: the read-only DA diagnostics page gathers users / resellers / license / system info in parallel — a single source failing wraps that card with an error message instead of blanking the whole page; the optional inline cleanup runs only when explicitly requested (strict `?cleanup=true` flag) and iterates a hardcoded orphan list pinned in tests so any future change requires explicit review; the standalone bulk cleanup endpoint is capped at 100 usernames per request, treats "already deleted" the same as "deleted just now" (operationally identical), surfaces "DA temporarily unreachable" as a distinct retryable signal, and one failed delete doesn't abort the rest of the batch (deploy `dms-00119-6z5` live)
- [x] **Admin service-users overview + bulk verify pending domains** — added automated safety checks: the "users with services" admin overview deduplicates the main aggregation with the directadmin-username fallback list (so customers who have a hosting account but no Hosting DB row still show up, but only once), uses DB state only (no live DirectAdmin call — list never times out), and uses a Set-based dedup that stays fast as the user base grows; the bulk verify-pending-domains action is capped at 100 domains per request (anti-abuse) and maps three distinct registrar outcomes — success → marked completed; still pending → status preserved with refreshed reason; anything else → marked failed (deploy `dms-00119-6z5` live)
- [x] **Trial-claim phone-OTP send + verify** — added automated safety checks for the two-step phone verification on the free-trial signup: the "send OTP" endpoint is rate-limited to 3 attempts per IP (no SMS-bomb of a customer's phone) BEFORE any parsing, normalises Indian phone numbers (strips spaces/dashes/country-code, requires exactly 10 digits), and treats SMS-provider failure as a separate "upstream temporarily down" signal (not an internal bug); the "verify OTP" endpoint is rate-limited to 10 attempts per IP (looser for legitimate typos), the code is single-use (already-used codes are rejected), and on success the response carries a signed token (not the raw OTP) that the downstream trial-claim flow trusts (deploy `dms-00119-6z5` live)
- [x] **Public password reset + hosting trial-eligibility check** — added automated safety checks: the public "reset my password" flow runs through five layers (CSRF check, schema validation, reCAPTCHA verification with IP capture, token verification, AND a hard refusal for admin accounts — so admins can't bypass their own step-up-auth admin-reset flow through the public form) and clears the used reset token before saving the new password (the link can't be replayed); a security-alert email is sent on success (failure swallowed so a mail-server hiccup doesn't roll back the reset); the trial-eligibility check enforces one-trial-per-user across BOTH the hosting collection AND the order history (so a deleted hosting row doesn't let a customer claim a second free trial), and the domain conflict check is scoped to the calling customer (no cross-tenant peeking) (deploy `dms-00118-m5v` live)
- [x] **Customer pay-invoice endpoint + admin invoice re-sync** — added automated safety checks: the customer pay-invoice endpoint verifies the invoice belongs to the customer BEFORE talking to the payment provider, returns "not found" rather than "forbidden" so another customer's invoice can't be enumerated by id, and refuses to start a new payment on an already-paid invoice (anti-double-pay); the admin re-sync action clears the "stuck pending creation" marker before retrying so a single retry can actually unblock a stuck invoice, and tells admin two distinct outcomes (success with the new invoice id vs soft-failure with a "check logs" message) (deploy `dms-00118-m5v` live)
- [x] **Account-activation endpoint + customer domain-status check** — added automated safety checks: the account-activation endpoint is rate-limited (10 attempts per IP per minute), distinguishes "token expired" from "token unknown" so the customer knows whether to request a fresh activation email, refuses to re-activate an already-active account, and clears the used token from the database before issuing the login JWT (so the activation link can't be replayed); the customer domain-status check is scoped to the customer's own domains, uses a strict "Active" match against ResellerClub's status field (case-sensitive), and surfaces three distinct outcomes (registered / still pending / upstream failure with reason) (deploy `dms-00117-f7f` live)
- [x] **Customer invoice list (with auto-heal) + cancel-trial action** — added automated safety checks: the customer invoice list automatically retries any paid orders whose invoice never finished generating (the retry runs inline so it survives Cloud Run's CPU-after-response throttle; if no retries recover, the route skips a pointless second DB read); the cancel-trial action terminates a trial in three steps (cancel auto-charge, suspend hosting account, mark trial terminated) where each upstream step's failure is logged but doesn't abort the others, AND the trial order record is intentionally kept so the customer can't claim a second free trial (deploy `dms-00117-f7f` live)
- [x] **Customer 2FA confirm + disable endpoints** — added automated safety checks completing the 2FA lifecycle: confirming the initial setup activates 2FA only after the first code verifies, then issues 8 one-time backup codes (shown to the customer once, stored hashed at rest — the plaintext codes never reach the database); disabling 2FA requires BOTH the current password AND a current authenticator code or one of the unused backup codes — neither alone is enough, and the password check runs first so a stolen authenticator code can't probe whether disable succeeded (deploy `dms-00116-blx` live)
- [x] **Resend activation email + admin nameserver-change** — added automated safety checks: the public "resend my activation email" endpoint is rate-limited to 3 attempts per IP (anti-spam) BEFORE the email is even parsed; returns the same generic "if that email has a pending activation, we've resent the link" response whether the email exists, is already activated, or is a fresh resend (anti-enumeration); the admin nameserver-change endpoint reaches any customer's domain but still validates the nameserver format and surfaces the registrar's real reason on failure (deploy `dms-00116-blx` live)
- [x] **"Who am I" endpoint + customer invoice PDF download** — added automated safety checks: the "who am I" status check used by the front-end on every page-load returns only a boolean "has-password" flag, never the password hash; the response carries a curated field set (no TOTP secret, no internal DA username, no pending-email leaks); the customer invoice PDF download is rate-limited (10 per minute per customer), ownership is verified locally in MongoDB BEFORE any provider call (saves a Zoho round-trip on bad IDs and prevents Zoho-side enumeration); placeholder invoice IDs from the creation flow are rejected with a clean message; the response is a real inline-PDF (renders in browser, not forced download) (deploy `dms-00116-blx` live)
- [x] **Customer 2FA enrollment QR + hosting auto-renew toggle** — added automated safety checks: the customer 2FA-setup endpoint refuses to generate a new enrollment QR if 2FA is already enabled (must be explicitly disabled first — closes a vector where an attacker could re-issue a fresh secret); the secret returned is stored as "pending" until confirmed via the separate confirm step (a half-finished enrollment can't lock the user out); the hosting auto-renew toggle only works on active hosting accounts (no flipping auto-renew on suspended / terminated / pending accounts) and is scoped to the calling customer's own hosting; response carries only the new state + billing type, no internal fields (deploy `dms-00115-xbl` live)
- [x] **Admin self-reset password + admin domain-sync** — added automated safety checks: the admin's "reset my own password" endpoint requires step-up re-authentication (current password) on top of being logged in, so a stolen session cookie alone can't change the admin password; the new password is bcrypt-hashed (12 rounds) before storage; the domain-sync endpoint accepts either a single domain or a batch of up to 100 (hard cap to prevent abuse), with a 500ms pause between each item to avoid rate-limiting the registrar (deploy `dms-00115-xbl` live)
- [x] **Daily hosting-expiry auto-suspend job + server-error logging endpoint** — added automated safety checks for two system-internal endpoints: the daily job that finds expired hosting accounts and queues them for suspension (accepts either the cron-secret header OR an admin session; a failure on one hosting doesn't abort the whole batch — successes and failures are tracked separately so admin can see exactly which ones need re-running); the server-side error logger accepts cron secret, logged-in session, OR a same-origin browser request, and on failure uses raw console output instead of the logger itself so a logging crash can't snowball into a request storm (deploy `dms-00115-xbl` live)
- [x] **Admin user-management actions (reset 2FA, reactivate deactivated user)** — added automated safety checks: only admins can run either action; **the 2FA-reset endpoint specifically refuses to operate on other admin accounts** (closes a privilege-downgrade vector — no admin can wipe another admin's 2FA via a customer-service flow); the reactivate endpoint refuses to "reactivate" a user who isn't actually deactivated (no misleading audit-log entries); the response shape never leaks internal fields like the TOTP secret or password hash (deploy `dms-00115-xbl` live)
- [x] **Admin hosting package change + customer hosting renewal info** — added automated safety checks: only admins can change a customer's hosting package, with distinct messages for "no such user", "no such package", "hosting server unreachable" (treated as a temporary outage, not an internal bug), and "something else broke" (with the real reason surfaced for admin debugging); the customer renewal price is computed strictly as "plan price × 12 months" with the 1-year period locked in (no half-year or two-year option leaks through); the renewal endpoint returns only the customer-safe fields, never internal plan / hosting flags (deploy `dms-00115-xbl` live)
- [x] **Admin invoice PDF download + clear-invoice-number maintenance action** — added automated safety checks for two admin-only endpoints: only admins can download a customer invoice PDF (the response is a real PDF file, with the invoice ID baked into the filename, not a JSON wrapper); the recovery action that frees up a stuck invoice number is idempotent (calling it again on an already-cleared order returns a polite "already done" message instead of erroring) (deploy `dms-00114-hh6` live)
- [x] **Admin ResellerClub balance check + customer invoice-sync** — added automated safety checks: only admins can read the ResellerClub reseller account; credit-mode (NoBilling) accounts return wallet figures as "not applicable" rather than a misleading zero; the customer's manual "sync my missing invoice" button only touches that customer's own orders; provider-side error fragments (Zoho access tokens, retry tokens, ResellerClub API-key fragments) never leak into client responses (deploy `dms-00113-tkg` live)
- [x] **Admin retry / delete on a stuck hosting provisioning** — added automated safety checks for the two admin actions on a stuck hosting row: deleting (clears the row) and retrying (re-runs provisioning via the same code path the cron uses, so admin-triggered and cron-triggered retries stay in sync); the retry surface tells admin two different things depending on whether the customer already has hosting elsewhere vs whether a fresh account was created (deploy `dms-00113-tkg` live)
- [x] **Three more admin / public endpoints (eligible-user picker, ₹1 test-plan feature flag, admin invoices)** — added automated safety checks: non-admin users can't reach the eligible-user picker or admin invoices; the public ₹1 test plan stays disabled until BOTH the feature flag AND the active plan record exist (strict boolean check, fail-closed on any error); the eligible-user picker returns only the customer's name and email (no internal fields like phone, role, or password hash) (deploy `dms-00112-6x7` live)
- [x] **Client log-forwarder + admin IP-whitelist status endpoints** — added automated safety checks: the public log-forwarder caps message / URL / timestamp sizes so it can't be abused as a free log writer; logger failure stays silent (no error body sent back) to prevent client-side logging loops; the admin IP-whitelist status check is admin-only and returns a stable empty-state shape so the dashboard widget doesn't break on first run (deploy `dms-00111-d6x` live)
- [x] **Three admin list endpoints (deactivated users, support tickets, pending hostings)** — added automated safety checks so non-admin users can't reach any of the three lists; page numbers in the support-tickets list are clamped to a safe minimum; the test pinned one current quirk where a garbled page parameter still leaks through (flagged for a future hardening pass) (deploy `dms-00110-k29` live)
- [x] **Customer order list + order lookup endpoints** — added automated safety checks for the three customer order endpoints (list all orders, fetch one by ID, customer-safe order detail): one customer can never read another's order (the lookup is keyed on the logged-in customer); the user-facing endpoint applies a field allow-list so internal admin notes and raw payment-provider responses never reach the customer (deploy `dms-00109-tjv` live)
- [x] **Public status endpoints (health, maintenance, captcha, account-status)** — added automated safety checks for the 4 small read-only endpoints used by Cloud Run probes and the login UI: the health check stays fast and never crashes (no auth, no database); captcha never silently turns off on a settings error (the check defaults to "captcha required" — anti-bypass); maintenance mode auto-expires when the scheduled end-time passes (no manual flip needed); the account-status endpoint returns a stable shape (deploy `dms-00107-fgm` live)
- [x] **Customer support ticket endpoints** — added automated safety checks for the customer ticket-creation and ticket-list endpoints: rate-limited to 5 new tickets per hour per customer; user-typed text (subject, message, name) is HTML-escaped before being embedded in the admin notification email (anti-XSS in admin inbox); attachments are validated before the ticket is saved; mail-server failure doesn't roll back a successful ticket creation (deploy `dms-00107-fgm` live)
- [x] **Customer DNS-management list endpoint** — added automated safety checks so the DNS management page only shows the customer's own domains, and only those that are fully registered (pending / failed / cancelled domains are filtered out and not editable); same domain on multiple orders is shown once (deploy `dms-00106-fw8` live)
- [x] **Subscription create / cancel endpoints** — added automated safety checks for the customer subscription lifecycle: only the customer who owns a hosting can cancel its subscription (others get a "not found" without any hint that the hosting exists); payment-provider errors are masked from the client so account / key fragments never leak (deploy `dms-00105-8mw` live)
- [x] **Customer dashboard page** — added automated safety checks for the dashboard data feed: one customer can never see another's orders or hosting; a deactivated account holding a still-valid login token can't reach the data; broken background-sync jobs don't blank the dashboard; the upcoming-renewal list only shows real registered items inside a 30-day window (deploy `dms-00104-7qx` live)
- [x] **Payment provider webhook (Razorpay)** — added automated safety checks to the webhook endpoint that receives payment-status updates from the provider: only requests cryptographically signed by the provider are accepted; messages older than 24 hours are rejected (anti-replay); duplicate deliveries within a 15-minute window are deduplicated at the cache layer; the database is the long-term safety net (deploy `dms-00103-dmg` live)
- [x] **Customer domain-watch list (add / list / remove)** — added automated safety checks: per-customer cap (20 domains) to prevent abuse; duplicate-add attempts return a clean "already watching" message instead of a crash; one customer can't see or remove another customer's watches (deploy `dms-00103-dmg` live)
- [x] **Public contact form** — added automated safety checks: bots are filtered by reCAPTCHA before any email is sent; user-supplied HTML is stripped before being rendered into the admin email and the user's confirmation email (anti-XSS); validation errors are surfaced cleanly to the user (deploy `dms-00103-dmg` live)
- [x] **Customer nameserver-change endpoint** — added automated safety checks so customers can't change nameservers for domains they don't own, and malformed nameservers are rejected before being sent to the registrar (deploy `dms-00103-dmg` live)
- [x] **Email-change flow (request + confirm)** — added automated safety checks: password is re-verified before a change is started; social-login accounts (e.g. Google) can't bypass that check; the confirmation link expires after 1 hour, signs the user out on every device, and is safe against email-enumeration probes (deploy `dms-00102-7q5` live)
- [x] **Customer hosting-stats page** — added automated safety checks for the customer-facing hosting dashboard, mirroring the admin version (per-customer rate limit, slow DirectAdmin server handled gracefully) (deploy `dms-00102-7q5` live)
- [x] **Public TLD pricing display page** — added automated safety checks so the live pricing API stays fast (cached), and a specific-TLD lookup always gets a fresh price (uncached) (deploy `dms-00101-xr2` live)
- [x] **Admin order-management page** — added automated safety checks for the order list, archive, and unarchive flows (deploy `dms-00101-xr2` live)
- [x] **Admin pending-domains list page** — added automated safety checks so the page handles invalid filters, big page sizes, and broken inputs without crashing
- [x] **Reorganized this task tracker** so it reads in plain English for the senior reviewer (technical details moved to `docs/AUDIT-TECHNICAL.md`)
- [x] **Admin can manually provision a hosting account from the dashboard** — safety checks added so a slow hosting server doesn't lock up the form, and failed attempts are queued for retry instead of vanishing
- [x] **Admin can create / list / edit hosting packages** — package price changes now automatically sync with the payment provider for renewals
- [x] **Customer payments now go through stricter security checks** so the system can't be tricked into accepting underpayment, or sneaking unpaid items past the verification gate
- [x] **Guest checkout (no account needed) is hardened** against email-claim attacks where someone tries to bind a paid purchase to another customer's account
- [x] **Domain search is faster on repeat queries** thanks to caching, and won't lock up if the upstream registry is slow
- [x] **Profile edits are locked down** so a regular user can't accidentally (or intentionally) promote themselves to admin
- [x] **Set-password flow works correctly for users who originally signed in with Google** (previously it asked them for a "current password" they never had)
- [x] **Admin hosting-stats page degrades gracefully when DirectAdmin is slow** — shows cached data instead of a blank page after 5 seconds
- [x] **Admin TLD-pricing dashboard** loads faster (cached), and refuses to cache an empty result so a brief upstream outage can't make it look like "no TLDs available"
- [x] **Admin can mark pending domains as completed / failed / cancelled**, with proper sync back to the customer's order and the registrar
- [x] **Payment confirmation and renewal-reminder emails** (and WhatsApp messages) verified end-to-end
- [x] **One-time-use backup codes for two-factor login** work as expected; admin-initiated 2FA reset properly revokes existing sessions

## Backlog

- [x] **✅ SAFE PASS DONE 2026-08-01 (`dms-00450-sp1`+`sp2`) — Split the largest admin pages into smaller pieces** — Operator chose the *safe-mechanical-only* scope (these are 1k–2k-line operator-critical pages that can't be runtime-verified in this environment). Extracted co-located **types** + already-module-scope **pure presentational primitives** into sibling files — zero runtime change, tsc-verified: hosting → `types.ts` (1994→1951); user-management → `types.ts` (1648→1637); dns-management → `types.ts` (1071→1034, dropped a dead `NameserverInfo`); settings → `types.ts` + `primitives.tsx` (previewTrackingId + 7 UI primitives, 1017→893). system-settings had nothing top-level to extract (all inline). **Deliberately NOT done (deferred):** the deeper stateful-component split (extracting tables / detail+create modals into components) — real regression risk, needs the admin app run to verify each page, so it's a separate step gated on that. See "ADMIN-PAGE-SPLIT" in Recently Shipped.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-01 (`dms-00451-a11`) — Run accessibility checks automatically in the build pipeline** — `jsx-a11y/recommended` was configured but every rule was `warn`, and CI runs `next lint --quiet` (warnings suppressed), so a11y was configured yet never reported or enforced. Added a **ratchet gate**: `scripts/check-a11y.mjs` (`npm run lint:a11y`, new CI step) runs the jsx-a11y rules, lists every violation, and fails the build only if the count exceeds a frozen baseline — so **new a11y regressions can't merge**, the existing debt is visible (not hidden), and the number can only ratchet down. Baseline started at 141; fixed 2 singletons (global-error `<html lang>`, CardTitle heading-content false-positive) to land at **139**. Verified both directions (exit 1 on regression, exit 0 at baseline). Remaining 139 (mostly 104× `label-has-associated-control`) is now visible, capped, and drivable down over time. See "A11Y-CI-GATE" in Recently Shipped.
- [x] **✅ DONE 2026-08-01 (`dms-00452-pid`) — Tighten one legacy database field (pending-domain identifier)** — Did the required one-time data audit first (read-only, prod): the `pendingdomains` collection holds **0 docs**, and the sole create path (`new PendingDomain(...)`) never sets a custom `_id` (Mongoose auto-generates an ObjectId). The legacy `_id: Schema.Types.Mixed` ("support both ObjectId and String IDs") only ever mattered for the **synthetic, order-derived list rows** the admin view builds in memory (`_id: order_<id>_<domain>`, `source:'order'`) — plain objects typed `SyntheticPendingDomain`, never persisted through the model. So: dropped the Mixed override (default → auto ObjectId), tightened `IPendingDomain._id` + the `Document<>` generic to ObjectId, and hardened `getPendingDomainById` to return `null` for non-ObjectId ids (preserving today's behaviour exactly while avoiding a CastError on the now-typed path). 151 pending-domains tests pass; tsc clean; zero new failures. See "PENDING-DOMAIN-ID-TIGHTEN" in Recently Shipped.
